# CLAUDE.md

## Workflow

- **Merge automatically.** When a task is finished and tested, commit, push, open a pull request against `main`, and merge it without asking. Then tell the user what was merged and link the PR.
- **Don't merge when:**
  - tests fail or a change couldn't be verified;
  - the result is uncertain or only partly works;
  - the user says to wait.

  In those cases, push the branch, report the problem, and leave the PR unmerged.
- If the working branch's earlier PR is already merged, start the next change on top of the latest `main`. Merge `main` into the branch rather than force-pushing.
- The app is served from `main` by GitHub Pages, so a merge is a deploy.
- **Other sessions may be merging at the same time.** Right before merging, fetch `main`; if it moved since the branch was made or last updated:
  1. merge `origin/main` into the branch and resolve any conflicts;
  2. if `main` changed `CACHE` in `sw.js` or `PLAN_RULES` in planner.js, and this branch bumps it too, bump it again to one above `main`'s value (two sessions bumping to the same number is a single bump, so phones would miss the second change);
  3. re-run `npm test` on the merged result, push, and only then merge. If tests fail after the merge, apply the "Don't merge" rule.
- **The re-run after merging `main` is small.** It runs only the tests that both `main`'s incoming change and yours can affect, and says so ("main has moved since `npm test` last passed on this branch").
- When `main` brought only docs, or code that none of your tests uses, it prints "Nothing more needs to run" and stamps the map for the merged files: commit the map, push and merge.
- The re-run is your whole selection again, as before, when both sides changed the same file (a second `CACHE` bump in `sw.js` counts), when `main` changed `tests/run.js`, `tests/impact.js` or `package.json`, or when you edited a file after the merge.
- The small re-run needs `npm test` to have passed on the branch before `main` was merged in, with its map committed: so test and commit first, then fetch and merge `main`.
- Measured: a branch that selects 89 tests (100 s); `main` moved by a docs change: 0 run again (1 s); `main` changed `openStats()`: 6 run again (7 s).
- **No GitHub test run.** GitHub doesn't run the tests on pull requests (the user dropped it to save the wait). Your own `npm test` on the final branch is the only gate: merge right after it passes, without waiting on GitHub checks or subscribing to the PR.
- **Nightly full test run** (2026-11-14, the audit; since 2026-11-18 `.github/workflows/full-tests.yml`, which replaced `nightly-tests.yml`): every check (`npm run test:all`, Chromium and the WebKit halves) on `main` at 01:00 UTC on GitHub's Ubuntu runners, nothing deployed; a failure emails the owner, its failing checks, each with its shard, are in the run's summary and the logs are kept 14 days (`test-log`, and `shard-<i>` per shard); it never gates a merge and is never a pull request check. Tests run on Linux there: a test that leans on Windows (`tasklist`, paths) must work on both (the GPU guard's `alive` uses `process.kill(pid,0)` off Windows)
- **Cloud full run on demand:** `npm run test:cloud` (`tools/cloud-tests.js`, needs `gh`) starts `full-tests.yml` on the current branch as pushed, waits, and prints the summary (every failure with its shard), the wall-clock time and the runner minutes; exit code 0 when all passed; `-- --ref <branch|sha>` tests another, `-- --no-wait` only starts it
- the test server (`tests/run.js` `server`) maps a folder's address to its `index.html` by the address, not the joined path: on Windows `path.join` ends in a backslash, `./` was a 404 there only, so sw.js's install (its `addAll` has `./`) failed on Windows and the service worker took over every test page from its second load on Linux alone (found 2026-11-24: modload failed every night from 2026-11-23 and passed on Windows)
- a test that holds, refuses or swaps a page file with a route (`ctx.route`, `page.route`) opens its context with `serviceWorkers:'block'` (as modload, shellfix, connrole and every WebKit context do): once sw.js controls the page it answers from its cache and the route never sees the request; `tests/modload.test.js` checks that `./` is served and that no service worker is registered
- since 2026-11-24 the runner blocks service workers in every Chromium context it hands a test (`tests/run.js` `file`: `serviceWorkers:'block'` merged under the context's own options; the built-in suites too), as `tests/webkit.js` does for WebKit; a test about the service worker says `module.exports.serviceWorkers=true` (`sw.test.js`, which has its own server); before, tabload's reloads on Chromium all came from sw.js's cache and its route held nothing after the first load (4 of its 5 loads tested a finished page)
- `tests/tabload.test.js` checks on each load that safety.js (the script after start.js; ui.js until the restructure S3a moved it before add.js) reached its route once and was held (`held`): with the service worker in control the loads after the first ask the network 0 times
- a test file that can't be loaded (a syntax error) fails "threw" at once (`tests/run.js` `run`); before 2026-11-24 the worker never answered and the run waited out `TEST_FILE_TIMEOUT` (15 minutes)
- `tests/safeport.test.js` asks Node's fetch and Chromium for `bad.invalid:<port>`, which can't resolve, so its sweep of ports 1-10,100 connects to nothing on this machine (it swept 127.0.0.1 before 2026-11-24), and loads the app in a fresh page: on the page that tried the refused ports a failed goto's error page commits late and broke the next goto on Windows every run ("interrupted by another navigation to chrome-error://chromewebdata/"; a `goto('about:blank')` between doesn't help)
- `tests/impact.js` `scan` blanks an ES module's `import …;` and `export … from …;` statements to spaces (newlines kept, offsets unchanged) before compiling, so worker.js, worker/*.js and the module workers are read function by function (2026-11-24; before, 15 of 19 such files didn't parse and every edit in them counted as top-level code: an edit inside `cleanTag` selected 34 tests); an edit to an import line is top-level code; `tests/impact.test.js`
- speed budgets are scaled by `pace(page)` (`tests/pace.js`) in scoredials (the rank at 4x CPU, 900 ms; a flat budget failed the 11-18 nightly at 1039 ms), mqcloth (50 ms) and ocpblouse (4000 ms), as in mannequin, colnew and back
- by hand: `gh workflow run full-tests.yml --ref <branch>` (the branch's own workflow), or `gh workflow run full-tests.yml -f ref=<branch|sha>` (main's workflow on that checkout); GitHub > Actions > Full test run > Run workflow
- the cloud run is split into `SHARDS` (10) runners at once, the matrix in full-tests.yml (change both together; `tests/shard.test.js` checks they match); each runs `node tests/run.js --shard i/N` (or `TEST_SHARD=i/N`) with `TEST_JOBS=3`, `TEST_LOCK=0`, and a summary job joins the logs (`node tests/shard.js --summary`) and fails the run when any shard failed or never finished
- the split (`tests/shard.js` `plan`): every test of `run.js --keys` (files, built-in suites, WebKit halves) in exactly one shard, the same on every runner; largest first, each to the shard whose estimate grows least (the longer of its seconds over 3 and its longest test, +40 s for a shard with a WebKit half, whose runner installs WebKit's apt packages); `node tests/shard.js --plan 10` prints it and checks the partition
- shards are balanced by `tests/shard-times.json` (per-test seconds from a cloud run; a test not in it counts as the median); refresh it with `npm run test:cloud -- --save-times` and commit it when tests were added or grew; without it the split is equal counts
- `ALONE` in tests/shard.js (`asmgl.test.js`) gets a runner to itself: it paints on SwiftShader and took 88 s alone, 226 s beside two other tests
- only the shards with a WebKit half install WebKit and its apt packages (25–60 s); without the apt packages every WebKit half fails to start (tried 2026-11-18); node_modules and the Playwright browsers are cached (actions/cache), so the rest of a runner's setup is about 25 s
- measured on the branch (2026-11-18): 3:49 from start to summary (229 s run) against 15 min for the old single-runner run (909 s), about 27 runner minutes against 15; the same checks and results as the single runner (6424 checks, the same 4 failing, plus shard.test's 14)
- measured on main (2026-11-18, `npm run test:cloud -- --ref main`, twice): 4:25 and 3:49 from start to summary (runs 4:15 and 3:36), slowest shard 3:58 and 3:21, 30.6 and 28.9 runner minutes (35 and 34 billed); 6558 checks both times, as a local full run, with only `back.test.js`'s two Linux-font checks failing
- backgesture was split in three (2026-11-18): the slowest shard's Tests step went from 162–188 s to 136–140 s (asmgl 85–160 s alone, editorrework's WebKit half about 125 s); the critical path is now the WebKit apt step (`install-deps webkit`), which took 23–109 s on most runners that night and hit its 300 s limit on one runner in two of four runs (that shard's WebKit halves then fail "the browser starts"; main's run failed the same way): the split branch's runs took 4:28 (old shard times, and a shard retrying 3 flaky tests), then 5:12 and 7:16 from start to summary, those two set by an apt step, not the tests (3:49–4:25 before)
- the next step to go faster is apt, not the tests: fewer shards with a WebKit half (`SHARD_WEBKIT`), or the packages cached
- WebKit's apt packages are cached (2026-11-18): `tools/ci-browser-deps.sh list <browsers>` reads Playwright's package list (a stand-in `sudo` on PATH records the command `install-deps` would run), `… install <dir> <secs> <browsers>` installs the .deb files kept in `~/.cache/browser-apt` offline (`apt-get install --no-download`) and only when that fails or nothing is kept goes to apt (update + install, `Acquire::Retries=3`, 30 s per stalled request, up to 3 tries within `<secs>`), then keeps the downloaded .deb files
- the cache key is `browser-apt-<ImageOS>-pw<Playwright version>-<browsers>-<hash of the package list>-<ImageVersion>`, restored also from another image version of the same list (`restore-keys`; the offline install falls back to apt if it doesn't fit); `actions/cache/restore` and `actions/cache/save` (saved only after a set installed and verified, `dpkg-query` "ii" for every package)
- the offline install: apt with no package lists and no sources (`Dir::State::Lists` an empty folder) first, then `dpkg -i` of all the kept files at once, which is what installs them on ubuntu26 runners (apt refuses them alone; with the image's stale lists it tried to fetch the archive's copies, "Unable to fetch some archives"); `sudo -E` is refused on the runners, so the scripts pass `env DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive`
- measured (2026-11-18, ubuntu26 image 20261111.196.1, Playwright 1.63.0): WebKit's set is 61 packages, 186 .deb files, 133 MB; a hit installs in 13–21 s (step 14–21 s, restore 1–2 s) where apt took 22–109 s and twice over 300 s the night before; a miss (apt) took 25–28 s that morning
- measured: three warm `npm run test:cloud` runs on the branch, 3:27, 3:05 and 3:26 from start to summary (3:49–4:25 on main before, 5:12 and 7:16 when apt was slow), all 6580 checks passing
- the deploy's check run (`chromium webkit`, 72 packages, the same 186 files): miss 59 s, hit 18 s
- the step never fails: without the packages the WebKit halves fail "the browser starts" and the run fails (a deploy stops), as before
- the miss budget is eight minutes with WebKit (`480`; it was five, which two runs hit on 2026-11-17/18), two for Chromium alone (fonts)
- GitHub caches are per branch, readable from the default branch: a branch's first run uses main's cache, a cache saved on a branch isn't seen by main
- `SHARD_WEBKIT` is 2 (2026-11-18): the 13 WebKit halves run on the last two shards (`editorrework`'s alone on one), so 2 runners install WebKit instead of 4, at the same estimated slowest shard (163 s, `asmgl` at 160 s); `tests/shard.test.js` checks it stays no slower than spreading them
- a test that counts sync requests waits for the devices to be still first (`tests/sync.test.js`: `quiet` = no sync running and `stateSig` unchanged over three checks 120 ms apart, after `recordRun`; `settle` syncs both devices until they hold the same wardrobe; `hush` calls off a device's scheduled backup): a sync that brings changes redraws Today, which records the day and re-locks the week in the background (`recordRun` in today.js) and schedules a backup, and either device's late write landing during the count made it flaky (about 1 run in 4 under load, 2026-11-14; 0 in 40 after)
- **`npm test` runs only the checks the change can affect** (the user's rule: never the whole suite for a small change). It compares the branch with `origin/main`, prints what changed and why each test runs, and runs nothing for a docs-only change.
- `npm run test:all` runs every check. It is not needed before a merge; use it only when the selection looks too small for what you changed.
- Test runs rewrite `tests/impact.map` (what each test uses). Commit it with the change: later sessions choose their tests from it.
- **The map must be in the commit (enforced).** `git push` is refused while `tests/impact.map` has uncommitted changes; commit it and push again. Never bypass it with `--no-verify`.
- **A changed app file needs a new `CACHE` name (enforced).** The service worker serves one version at a time, so phones take changed files only when `CACHE` in sw.js changes; `git push` is refused when the branch changed a file in sw.js's `ASSETS` while its `CACHE` number is not above `origin/main`'s (`cacheStale` in tests/impact.js, part of the pre-push check, which fetches `main` first for up to 8 s and falls back to the local copy offline): merge `origin/main`, bump it to one above `main`'s, run `npm test`, commit and push again.
- If a change reaches `main` without its map, or without a passing `npm test` on the final files, the next session's `npm test` notices and runs every check. So run `npm test` after your last code edit, then commit everything it changed.
- A conflict in `tests/impact.map` needs no thought: `.gitattributes` merges it by keeping both sides' lines (`merge=union`).
- Never tell the user to download a backup file before a change: the worker keeps daily backups and Settings → Roll back undoes a bad change (see `sync.js`).
- **Run only what you need while working.** `node tests/run.js <name>` (or `TEST_ONLY=<name>`) runs just the `tests/*.test.js` files whose name contains `<name>`. Run `npm test` once before pushing, and once more only if `main` had to be merged in.
- **CLAUDE.md: one fact per line.** Add a new bullet under the file's heading instead of extending an existing one, so sessions working in parallel don't edit the same line and conflict.

## Project

Static app served from `main` by GitHub Pages (no build step): classic scripts loaded in index.html's order, sharing one global scope; module Web Workers (asm-worker.js, analysis-worker.js) import some of them (since 2026-11-24 every page script but merge.js is an ES module that hands its shared names to `globalThis`: see `index.html`). The server is a Cloudflare Worker (worker.js and its ES modules in worker/), deployed with Wrangler. The sections below go by file, in load order; a long section groups related facts under #### headings. Each bullet is one current fact; when something changes, add a bullet under the file's heading (or replace the fact when you are sure it is superseded) rather than appending "(replaces … above)" notes. (Rewritten 2026-11-14 from the bullets as they stood: superseded history removed, each fact stated once.)

- **American English** (the owner, 2026-11-15): every word a person reads (UI text, toasts, errors, the manifest, worker messages, what Claude writes for the owner) is American: color, gray, pants (not trousers), vest (not waistcoat), check (not tick), canceled; dates fall back to `en-US` (locale.js `APP_LOC`)
- machine names stay as they were, British included, since saved wardrobes, sync, the worker API and caches use them: color keys (`grey`, `lightgrey`, `darkgrey`, `midgrey`), the describe kind and request `what` `'colour'`, `colourHint`, band/figure `colour`, pattern.js `colours`/`centre`, the weave's `colours`, localStorage keys (`spec_recolour`), ids (`st-colours`), identifiers (`COLOUR_RULES`, `colourSchema`); a key shown to people goes through a display map (core.js `COLOR_NAMES`: `grey` → gray, `lightgrey` → light gray, `slate` → dark slate blue-gray; the type `pants` is "Pants" in `TYPES`)
- the Claude prompts (worker/tag.js `CLAUDE_SYSTEM`, worker/describe.js `DESCRIBE_SYSTEM`) end with "Write every word the owner reads (name, why, ask, hints) in American English."; their prose says color/gray, but a sentence naming a color key keeps the key ("between charcoal and grey"), and tailoring words in the prompts stay (trousers, mac, trainers as a synonym)
- the spreadsheet import (kit.js) still reads British headers and words (Colour, Second colour, trousers, waistcoat, trainers, grey)

### `index.html`

- markup and script tags
- the load model (the ES-module move, step 2, 2026-11-23): every classic page script is `<script src="…" defer>` and the page's last script is the inline module `<script type="module">startUp()</script>`; deferred scripts and modules run in document order once the page is parsed, before `DOMContentLoaded`, so the order among scripts and "startUp() after every script" are as they were
- what `defer` changes: the page is drawn and takes taps before its scripts have run, and `document.readyState` is `'interactive'` (not `'loading'`) while they run; nothing a script does at load may assume it runs before the first paint
- what must run before the first paint stays a plain inline script in `<head>` (three, none deferred): the saved theme (`data-theme` from localStorage `theme`, before the stylesheet), the text size (`--tscale`, `html.bigtext`) and the start-up catcher; styles.css stays a blocking `<link>`; nothing else has to: the header's theme glyph (theme.js, ◐ until then), `--navx` (app.js `fitNav`, after Today is drawn), `body.owner` and the rest are set by scripts that ran after the first paint on main too
- the start-up catcher (`<head>`, needs no other script): it hears a script that didn't load (the `error` event on the `<script>`, capture) and every error or rejection nobody caught before `window.appReady`; when `startUp` is missing once the scripts have run (at once), or when something was heard and Today is still empty 4 s after `load`, it writes "The app couldn’t start. Your wardrobe is safe on this phone: nothing was deleted. Check your connection, then try again." with Try again (a reload) into `#today` (`#start-fail`, `role="alert"`) and notes it in localStorage `err_log` ("Start-up: the app didn’t start: …"); it never writes over a drawn Today or the no-storage page, and a start-up that does come overwrites it; start.js `startFailed` still handles a failure inside start-up (CR-7-03)
- the catcher never takes a slow start for a failed one: while `window.startBusy` (start.js `startUp` sets it; false again only when `startFailed` itself couldn't finish) it looks again every 4 s, a minute at most; measured (`C:\code\c2-modload-lab\falsecard.js`, both engines): scripts held 8 s with no error, a caught error or an uncaught one, an uncaught error with the stored wardrobe 7 s late, a first run signed out: never shown; start.js refused: shown at about 90 ms
- once files are modules a failed import stops the whole graph before any of it runs: that case is the catcher's (nothing else is left to say so)
- the first page scripts that are ES modules (the ES-module move, step 3, the bridge, 2026-11-23): back.js and locale.js, each `<script type="module" src="…">` (no `defer`: a module is deferred by itself) at its old place in the list, so the order of execution is unchanged and every file is still fetched in parallel from its own tag (iOS 16.4 has no modulepreload); merge.js and srgb.js stay classic (the Web Workers `importScripts` them)
- a module's top-level names are its own; what classic scripts and the tests' `page.evaluate` still use as globals it hands over in its last lines (the bridge): `Object.assign(globalThis, {name})` for a function or constant, `Object.defineProperties(globalThis, {name: {get: () => name}})` for a `let` others read, with `set: v => {name = v}` only when another file or a test assigns it; then `export {…}` of the same names for the files that are modules too; no helper file, the statements are written out so `tests/modscope.js` can read them
- converting a file: the recipe, its traps and the proof list are `C:\code\cr-2026-11-16\MODULES-RECIPE.md`; `tests/bridge.test.js` holds each module's row
- a module that can't be loaded, doesn't parse or doesn't link (an import of a name the other file doesn't export) shows the start-up card like a classic script that failed (`tests/modload.test.js`, back.js, both engines)
- **the load-failure contract** (the restructure, step 2, 2026-11-26; the owner's choices of 2026-11-25): `tests/load-contract.json` lists every page script with its class and feature group; a new page script gets its entry in the same PR (`tests/modload.test.js` fails on a page script the contract doesn't list)
- `required`: the app can't start without it, so its failure may show the start-up card; it imports only required files. These are bus, core, back, settings and start (the card shows today) and toast, ui and act (the owner's choice (c): today their 404 still shows Today; a later import of them may show the card)
- `today-no-picture`: without it Today is drawn with no outfit picture, never the card; it imports required and today-no-picture files, never an optional one. The 14: app, planner, today-plan, today, history, photos, month, scorecard, missed, stats, assemble, draw, detect, srgb (the owner's choice (a): they stay as they are, not required)
- `optional`: only its feature is lost and Today is drawn with its picture; it imports required and today-no-picture files and files of its own group, and no required or today-no-picture file imports it: they reach it through bus.js (an announcement, or a socket `ask`)
- a feature group fails together (the owner's choice (b)): `add` (the Add page and its readings: add*, checklist, colpick, look, own-lists, measure, tagging, tag-request, pattern-read, migrate), `item-sheet` (item-photo, item-view, itemsheet, wardrobe), `backup` (sync, sync-photos, sync-status, snapshots, backupfile), `painter` (draw, assemble, mannequin, mq/*, asm-*); every other optional file is a group of its own
- how a new file picks its class: optional unless the app truly can't start without it (then required, and only if everything it imports is required); a screen, a sheet, a Settings part or a painter helper is optional; the class says what its 404 shows (`on404`: `card`, `no-picture`, `picture`); an entry's own `on404` records where the measured outcome differs (ui shows `picture`; toast and act did until S5e; mannequin, mq/vest and sync show `painted`)
- `painted` (mannequin, mq/vest, sync): without the file Today has no mannequin, only the painted picture, which comes once the painter has painted: within seconds on Chromium and Linux's WebKit, late on a busy runner (the first cloud run, 2026-11-26: Chromium showed no figure for mannequin and sync), not on Windows' WebKit (its healthy page showed no painted picture in 10 s in the lab survey); so modload takes `picture` or `no-picture` for it, never the card
- `tests/modload.test.js` checks the contract: every page script listed, only a required file's 404 may be the card, the imports (static, `export … from` and `import('…')`, read with acorn) keep the class rules (planted imports show each rule catches its case), and, on Chromium and WebKit, each of the 85 files 404'd with a seeded 50-piece wardrobe and the 512 test masters shows its contract's outcome (four pages at a time; a case ends when the card or the figure is there, else 8 s after Today was drawn; the day is held on Monday 2026-11-16, since on WebKit the missing file of the day's mannequin pose leaves no figure); modload runs with the real planner and the painter on (`realPlanner`, `assembled`)
- merge.js stays a classic script (optional, its own group: backups stop without it); calendar.js's `import('./calparse.js')` is in the contract's `lazy` list (not a page script)
- **the order, restructure step 3 (S3a, 2026-11-26)**: the ui kit and logic files load before the screens that read them: tag-request.js after srgb.js; toast, ui, sheet-swipe, measure and pickscore right after act.js; boxfold before scorecard; tagging after own-lists; fav after stats. Forward reads (a file reading a name of a file that runs later; `node C:\code\r0-lab\inventory.js <checkout>`) 704 → 609
- **the data out of the screens (restructure S3b, 2026-11-26)**: four parts moved down into data files, their lines' text unchanged (but the few named below): `days.js` (the day records `idsOf`, `keyDate`, `saveDay`, `notAhead`, from history.js; right after act.js), `photo-keys.js` (the kept pictures `renders` and their store `loadRenders`, `keepRender`, `shareRender`, the keys `comboSig`, `comboKey`, `fnv64`, `workerBase`, `workerList` and its list state, `picsDrawn`, and `esc`, `jsq`, `srcOk`, from photos.js; right after who.js, before the painter), `who.js` (who is connected: `acct` with `setAcct`, `acctBase`, `acctCall`, `acctEsc`, `hasInviteHash`, `rememberRole`, `acctRole`, `isTester`, `applyRole` from account.js, and an ended invitation `ENDED`, `endedAnswer`, `inviteEnded` from sync-status.js; right after srgb.js) and the backup's state into sync.js (below); forward reads 609 → 571
- S3b's announcements (bus.js): who.js `inviteEnded` says `emit('invite-ended')` where it called sync-status.js `showSyncStatus` and account.js `acctEndedUI`, which now pin for it (in that order, their load order); sync.js says `emit('backup-changed')` where it called `showSyncStatus` (setSyncStatus, `synced`, the redraw after a pull), and sync-status.js pins `showSyncStatus` for it; account.js assigns `acct` with who.js's `setAcct`
- the classes of the three new files (`tests/load-contract.json`): days.js and photo-keys.js `today-no-picture` (group `today`, as history.js and photos.js), who.js `optional` (its own group)
- **the painter's order (restructure S4, 2026-11-26)**: the painter's tags are one block right after pickscore.js (after the ui kit, logic and pattern-read.js and tagging.js, which it reads), before nav.js and the screens: asm-keys, assemble, asm-watch, asm-pocket, asm-color, asm-gl, asm-kinds, mannequin, mq/*, draw, asm-ocp; the order with the fewest forward reads among the painter files that keeps what runs at load: asm-color.js and asm-gl.js register their handlers with `acts` at load (`typeof acts` guard), so they stay after act.js; asm-kinds.js's and asm-ocp.js's set-up below their bridges reads asm-keys/asm-color's `ASM_CM_KINDS` and `ASM_CM_BTN`, asm-gl's `ASM_GL_KINDS` (a `typeof` guard: before asm-gl.js the new kinds would never reach the graphics chip) and asm-kinds' `ASM_NK`, so asm-gl < asm-kinds < asm-ocp; the screens that read the painter (today, today-picture, itemsheet, settings, start, photos, look …) now run after it; forward reads 454 → 425 (main after S3c)

- their bridges: core.js 202 names, with getters for `CUSTOM_COL` and `CUSTOM_PAT` (only `applyCustom` writes them) and getters and setters for the seven lets others assign: `items` (analysis.js, analysis-worker.js, 128 tests), `dayRecs` (today.js, pack.js, the worker, tests), `settings` (snapshots.js, the worker, tests), `weather` and `climate` (today.js, weather.js, the worker, tests), `TODAY_OVERRIDE` (the worker, 67 tests) and `clockSkew` (sync.js, tests/clock.test.js); `setWardrobe` stays the one writer the app's files use; planner.js 90 names (`LOOK_K`, `lookLeather` and `planLook` only for a string tests/lookplan.test.js runs in the page), analysis.js 11; `tests/bridge.test.js` has each row and who reads it
- analysis-worker.js is a module worker (2026-11-24): scorecard.js (`rankWorker`) and shop.js (`analyzeWardrobe`) start it with `new Worker('analysis-worker.js', {type: 'module'})`, and its `import './merge.js'; import './core.js'; import './planner.js'; import './analysis.js';` run the four files in the order its `importScripts` did; each module's bridge puts its names on the worker's `globalThis`, where the other files and the worker's own lines read them (its writes of `items`, `settings`, `weather`, `climate`, `TODAY_OVERRIDE` and `dayRecs` go through the setters); merge.js, run as a module there as worker/backup.js runs it, gives only `DW_MERGE`, so the worker copies it onto its globals before any message (`Object.assign(globalThis, globalThis.DW_MERGE)`), as importScripts left them
- module workers are Safari 15 / iOS 15 and later, and the app already needs iOS 16.4 (ES modules), so no classic copy of the worker is kept; a browser that can't run one, a worker file that can't be loaded or doesn't parse, an import that fails and a throw as it starts all fire the Worker's `error` event, and the rank and What to buy are worked out on the page as before (`rankW=false`, `onMain`)
- a module worker that can't load (its file or an import missing or not parsing) fires a plain `Event('error')` on the Worker that never reaches the page's error listener (both engines, `C:\code\c2-core-lab\wkerr.js`), where the classic worker's exception did; so scorecard.js `rankWorker` and shop.js `analyzeWardrobe` note it in `err_log` themselves when the event isn't an `ErrorEvent` ("The background check (analysis-worker.js) didn’t start: it or a file it imports didn’t load", one line per worker, as before); a throw as it starts is an `ErrorEvent` that reaches the page and is logged there as before
- the browser's own words for a load failure aren't given to a module worker's page, so the line is the app's; it is also written when the worker file itself is missing (404) and, on WebKit, when it doesn't parse, where the classic worker left no line
- measured at this conversion (`C:\code\c2-core-lab`, main beside the branch, Chromium and WebKit): `planyear.js`, a year planned (Nov 2026 to Oct 2027, 261 workdays) on 8 bench wardrobes (realistic, awkward, leather, realistic without jackets, without ties, awkward with neither, 149 and 300 generated pieces): every day's ids, waistcoat, notes, score and checks byte for byte main's (16 of 16 hashes, the same on both engines); `workerproof.js`, a seeded 60-piece wardrobe with the clock held: the score card's rank and What to buy byte for byte main's from the worker, and from the page when the worker file is missing, doesn't parse, misses one of its files or throws as it starts (10 of 10, and the same answers as a working worker)
- measured too: `failload3.js`, each of the three files missing, not parsing or 3 s late, 18 cases a side: what a person sees is main's in all (the start-up card without core.js, Today, the picker, a pick, the rank, Settings, Insights, What to buy failing without analysis.js), the error log aside (with core.js missing Chromium logs the same lines in another order; with analysis.js missing or not parsing the worker's failed load isn't logged, as above); with core.js missing planner.js stops at its line 24 (`const lumOf=lumCol`) before its bridge, so its names aren't the page's where main kept its function declarations, and the card shows either way; `offline.js` (Chromium, the service worker in control, the server stopped, the network off): a reload and a new load of Today, Wardrobe, Insights and Settings, the rank from the worker and What to buy, every module held in the cache under its tag's name, as main
- `tests/planner-repeats.test.js` counts the days the shared simulation dresses where `outfit()` reads `settings.vestMode` (once a call, read nowhere else in planning): a wrapped `window.outfit` no longer sees planner.js's own calls; on main both counts are the same (102 and 293)
- `lab/gl-bench.html` runs core.js (it loads the files from blobs in order) as a module script when its text has `export {`, so its bridge hands detect.js, draw.js and the painter its names there too (`tests/labbench.test.js`; as a plain script it stopped at "Unexpected token 'export'")
- core.js's `bg` is used by nothing (ESLint says so now that the file is a module); left in, since this move changes no code line
- first paint, measured (2026-11-23, `C:\code\c2-modload-lab`, iPhone 17 Pro viewport, before = main 9fdc0f2): with the service worker's cache nothing changes (Chromium Today at 55 → 50 ms, WebKit 212 → 224 ms, the same frames); on a cold load over a slow connection Chromium is the same (it already drew the empty page while scripts came), but WebKit now draws the header, empty page and tab bar at once (first paint 505 → 111 ms with 20 ms per file) where it drew nothing until the last script had run; Today itself comes at the same time (586 → 576 ms)
- `tests/modload.test.js` (Chromium and WebKit): the tags, `afterDom`, taps during loading, a script that can't be loaded or doesn't parse, an Arabic-language phone's dates, the saved theme before the first script
- sync.js and today.js are ES modules too (step 4, 2026-11-24), their code unchanged, each tag at its old place, importing nothing; sync.js's bridge is above its top-level listeners (`connRestored.then` throws without app.js), today.js's above `acts({…})`
- sync.js gives `restoring` (snapshots.js), `syncing` and `syncDue` (tests) a getter and a setter, `syncTimer`, `syncRun` and `syncLater` a getter; today.js gives `openRow`, `belowOpen`, `listOpen` (history.js), `dayShown` and `BLOUSE_ROLL_ON` a getter and a setter, `recordRun` a getter; boxfold.js gives `foldQuiet` a getter (the restructure S3a moved it there from today.js)
- no test replaces `syncNow`, `syncOnce`, `scheduleSync` or `renderToday` (a replacement misses the module's own calls): daysoff and setup answer the real sync's requests, syncwrite counts the requests, fieldmerge, materials and sockpatterns hold or count the timer on `syncNow`, settingsui, asmahead and assemble count what is written into `#today`, shellfix writes its failure into `renderToday` as today.js is served
- `tests/actsindex.test.js` watches `goToday` (`todayTabTap`) and `syncTap` (`syncNowTap`) at their handlers (`VIA`); `tools/visit-measure.js` also names a traced function a module keeps to itself (`planSnapCheck`)
- measured at this conversion (`C:\code\c2-mod16-lab`, main beside the branch, Chromium and WebKit): `syncproof16.js`, two phones on the repo's worker with a store in memory and the clock held: merge, deletion and Undo, offline and reconnect, Sync now, the pause, Roll back, Restore and `connRestored` give byte-identical wardrobes, requests and KV writes; `todaytaps.js` (56 steps) and `touch.js` (108) the same by real taps; `failload.js`: 16 load failures show what main shows
- the painter and its drawings are ES modules too (Round C, wave C3, 2026-11-24): detect.js, srgb.js, mannequin.js, mq/*.js, draw.js, assemble.js and the asm-*.js files, each tag at its old place, importing nothing; asm-worker.js is a module worker that imports its eight files; only merge.js is still a classic page script (the server imports it); mannequin.js and the pose files put their names on `self` themselves and end with `export {}`
- the painter's two inline handlers became `data-act` (C3): Painted plain's "Read N patterns" (`asmReadAllTap`, asm-color.js) and the graphics-chip switch (`data-act-change` `asmGlPick`, asm-gl.js), registered with `if(typeof acts==='function')acts({…})` below each file's bridge (the painting worker has no act.js); no file has an inline handler left (`tests/inlinehandlers.test.js` `LEFT` is empty)
- no inline handlers (C2-4, 2026-11-18): its controls name their handlers with `data-act` / `data-act-change` / `data-act-keydown` / `data-act-focus` (see `act.js` for which file holds each); tests find them by id or by `data-act` (`header .hbtns button[data-act="setOpenTap"]`, `#settings-modal .scat-head[data-cat="backup"]`)
- `#sr-live` (after `#toast`, `.vh`, `aria-live="polite"`): the one live region for what changed without a toast (ui.js `announce`; fix round 3, 2026-11-16)
- the Settings categories' head buttons sit in `<h3 class="scat-h">` (VoiceOver's heading rotor; W2-19-16); `#cfg-workdays` / `#cfg-blazer` are `role="group"` named "Workdays" / "Blazer days" (W2-19-10)
- the head script also sets `html.bigtext` when `--tscale` is 1.3 or more (styles.css gives the clamped names a third line, W2-19-17); the 150% cap stays (the owner, W2-19-09)
- Add's `#add-for` (under `#note`, `role="status"`, hidden unless asked): "Adding a belt: …" after Today's "Add one" (add.js `renderAddFor`)
- the tabs are Today, Wardrobe and Insights (`TABS` in start.js; `#n-stats` shows the `#stats` page, titled Insights, with no ‹)
- Add is a page of the Wardrobe (`openAdd` in start.js: the Wardrobe's round + `#ward-add`, the setup's buttons, a pasted photo), with a ‹ back to where it was opened (`SUBS` has `add`; `subOpen`/`subBack`)
- while the Wardrobe's + shows, a toast sits above it (`bottom: --navh + 84px`, `body:not(.iv-on):has(main:not([inert]) #wardrobe:not([hidden])) #toast`; 2026-11-17, the owner: the Undo message floated over the +); elsewhere it stays 12px above the tab bar; `tests/wardfab.test.js`
- on the Wardrobe (shown, not under Settings or a sheet, no item sheet open: `undoHere`) a `toast` with Undo is instead the round Undo button (`#undo-fab`, toast.js `undoFab`; 2026-11-17, the owner's pick from mockups `C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\undo-fab\`, "A with the glowing countdown", no line): 56px like the +, 18px above it, the undo arrow over "Undo", a glowing gold ring (`.ufc`, `ufRun` over `--ud`, the toast's 8 s) running down; a tap undoes and it goes; its label is "Undo: <the toast's words>" and the words are announced (`announce`); another Undo replaces it; the keyboard on it keeps it up; `toastDrop` takes it too; elsewhere, and from the item sheet, Undo is the toast as before; tests read `#undo-fab` there (`tests/wardfab.test.js`, wardrobe, editorrework)
- the Wardrobe's + (`#ward-add`, 2026-11-15, the owner's ask; it replaced the header row's small "+ Add"): a 56px ink circle with a drawn + (`aria-label` "Add to wardrobe"), in index.html's main after the pages (not inside `#wardrobe`, whose page-in animation transforms it), fixed `16px` above the tab bar (`--navh`) at the right edge of the content column (main's 560px, or 1080px from 600px, centered, less its 16px padding; the screen's right edge less 16px on a phone and the Duo unfolded); shown by `#wardrobe:not([hidden]) ~ #ward-add`, hidden under every layer (`main[inert]`: Settings, the sheets, the setup; `body.iv-on`: the item sheet); pops in only under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`; `#wardrobe` has 76px bottom padding so the last row scrolls clear of it; bottom right, not top right: thumb reach; `tests/wardfab.test.js`
- Insights starts with "What to buy" (`shopButton()`), then the stats
- What to buy waits for a wardrobe big enough to judge (`shopGate`, `SHOP_MIN` in shop.js: 3 jackets, or 3 pairs of trousers when the dress code has no jackets; 5 shirts; 4 ties when ties are worn; 2 pairs of shoes, all in rotation): until then the card says what to add and doesn't open (`openShop` returns)
- under the "Your wardrobe" heading: the Packing list link (`.slinks`, a page) and Laundry & cleaning, a fold like the other categories (2026-11-17, the owner's ask; it was a page, `#care`): `#sc-laundry` (first in `SET_CATS`, its head `#care-btn`), its panel `#care-in` drawn by care.js `renderCare` as it opens and after a send or clean (`careRedraw`), its line `#sum-laundry` ("1 due · 2 nearly due · 1 away", `careSum`); Today's "See what is due" (`openCare`) opens Settings on it; `tests/care.test.js`
- Settings' panels have a fixed padding with two spacers inside the clip (`.mpanel-in::before/::after`), not a padding animated beside the panel's height, their fade on its own layer, and `toggleCat` writes the summaries after the fold has moved (480 ms), not in its first frames (2026-11-17, "a little choppy")
- wording: nothing a tester reads says worker, Cloudflare, KV, token or benchmark ("the server", "Connect in ⚙ Settings first.", "it needs an update"); `tests/ui.test.js` reads all of Settings for those words
- error texts the code tests by pattern: `/Connect in|OPENAI_API_KEY|forbidden|Today’s limit/` in `startItemPics`
- Privacy: outfit pictures are drawn or painted by the app and never go to OpenAI (the server can no longer make one, 2026-11-14); an item's photo and description go to OpenAI only on a tap of Generate item pictures

#### Settings

- what only the owner needs is in "Advanced · For the person who runs the server" folds (`.adv`: `#adv-photos`, `#adv-connection`), closed every time Settings opens (`advClose` in ui.js); the server address and the code (`#cfg-url`, `#cfg-token`, still saved by Done) are under Connection
- tests must open a fold before clicking inside it (`document.querySelector('#adv-connection .adv-head').click()`); Advanced sections other than Connection's show only with `body.owner` (a test adds the class, or connects as the owner)
- Outfit pictures (`#sc-photos`) → Advanced: Pictures ahead, "Painted plain" (`asmGapUI`, which lists a textured shirt with no reading) and the graphics-chip switch
- Pictures ahead (`asmCmUI`, `asmCmStatus`, `#cm-status`): of the looks of the next `ASM_AHEAD` (60) days' workdays painted on a colour-coded master, how many this phone holds (`asmFor`): "Next 60 days: X of N looks painted on this phone" (until 2026-11-16 it counted the looks the PC builder had uploaded, "built on the computer")
- the Accuracy check (benchmark) was removed and Photo tagging has no Advanced fold; `benchEstimate` stays (the colour picker's cost)
- the Colour-coded masters switch is gone; a phone's stored `asm_cm` is cleared at start-up; `asmCmSet` stays for the tests; Item photos (the earlier assembler's) is gone too
- Schedule → "Start the plan over" (`replanButton` in history.js, `#replan-btn`; 2026-11-14, the owner's ask: "completely redo the plan, throwing out my locks and changes"), for everyone, not in a fold: today and every coming day not marked worn lose the week's locks and the owner's changes (`dayChanged`: swaps, picks, the vest, a piece taken off, the suit or blazer chosen; `replanFuture()`); occasions, days off, worn days and the past stay; it asks first
- a rules change (`replanFuture(true)`) only releases locks after today and keeps picks
- the Suit vest row hides when ties are off too (`renderDressUI`), with a note: no waistcoat is planned without a tie
- a Settings row whose dropdown doesn't fit beside its label wraps it onto its own line (Dark shirts was cut off at 390px)
- About: Send is switched off while not connected
- Outfit pictures starts with "Today’s outfit" (`#pics-box`, `#cfg-pictures`, `renderPicturesUI`/`setPictures` in settings.js; 2026-11-14, the owner: "build an option in settings to let the user turn off the painter and only use the mannequin"): "Painted pictures" (the default) or "Drawing only", for everyone, not in a fold, with a line under it saying what the choice does (`PICS_HELP`); drawn from settings.js, not in index.html
- the choice is `settings.pictures` ('drawn'; absent or 'painted' = painted), synced setting by setting like the others, so all of a person's devices follow it; a sync that changes it calls `picsSwitched` (start.js `onSyncedData`)
- with "Drawing only" the Advanced fold of Outfit pictures is hidden (`renderAsmUI` hides `#asm-group`, and `#adv-photos:has(#asm-group[hidden])` the fold): Pictures ahead, Painted plain and the graphics-chip switch are the painter's; the item sheet's "Outfit pictures: …" line (`asmGapNote`) goes too
- the Outfit pictures summary is the choice: "Drawing only", "Painted pictures", or "Painted pictures · needs a connection" (the masters come from the server)

#### Today (today.js)
- the uniform blouse's Sleeves row (2026-11-17, the owner: sometimes worn with the sleeves rolled): under the blouse, "Down"/"Rolled up" with ↑/↓ (`rollRow`, `setRoll`), per day (`rec.roll`, merged field by field like any day field; cleared by Reset day and an occasion); planner.js sets `o.rolled` (outfit and frozenOutfit) and the painting takes the rolled master; the row's drawing shows the sleeves rolled (`rolledOf`)
- a casual jacket taken off is called by its type on its "Taken off" row (`offTop`: the day's pinned top), not "Blazer"

- the casual garments on Today (2026-11-16): the picker (history.js `openPicker`) adds "Casual · pick by hand" (`handPool`, `handGroup`, `.pick-hand`) under the scored pieces: casual tops in the shirt's picker, the cardigan and chore jacket in the jacket's, cargo pants in the pants'; not scored, never in Suggest another; a pick is `force[slot]` like any; the row is named by the type ("T-shirt", "Chore jacket": `slotLabel`), and a casual top hides the Tie and Tie clip rows (`needsTie`); `tests/handpick.test.js`
- planner.js `outfit()` wears a hand-pick piece only from the day's pick for its own slot and in rotation that day (`hp`, `hpTop`, `hpShirt`, `hpBot`); a casual top makes `noT` (`casPick`); a casual jacket makes the day a blazer day (odd pants); `PLAN_RULES` unchanged (no existing item plans differently: they never enter `by`); tests/fixedplan.js leaves them out the same way

- the Pocket square row (`pocketRow`, `pocketNone`; 2026-11-16): only with a jacket on and a square owned (or worn/taken off that day); ⇄ (the picker, by score) and − (`setOff('pocket')`, "Taken off" with +); none planned: "Not today" with + (the picker, the only square too); a pick puts a taken-off square back (history.js `pocketOn`)
- Today's date is "Thu, November 12" below 360px (`.dlong`/`.dshort`) so the day never wraps alone
- the folded pieces box (`piecesLine`, `piecesBox`): a jacket, trousers, shirt or tie the owner named is called by its name exactly as typed (`itemName`; "Medium Grey suit", not "mid grey worsted suit"), the others by colour; the line is escaped (`esc`); `tests/pieces.test.js`
- the picture has no share button (2026-11-14, the owner: "the user can just send a screenshot"; it was `shareMark`/`shareOutfit`, `.wshare`, at its top right)
- with "Drawing only" (Settings → Outfit pictures, `picsDrawn()`) Today always shows the traced mannequin on its office background: `renderFor` gives null, so neither a painted picture nor an outfit photo kept from before shows, and there are no swatches (they are for pictures only); `tests/drawnonly.test.js`
- switching back to "Painted pictures" paints or downloads the day on screen at once, while Settings is still open (`picsSwitched`: `asmSync`, `asmEnsure`, `schedulePrefetch`), and Today shows it when Settings closes (`refreshToday` → `todayStale`); building ahead resumes from `asmEnsure`

#### Today's cloth swatches (today.js)

- the picture shows the day's cloth bottom left (`swatchStack`, the owner's choice from mockups): the jacket's, shirt's and tie's swatches top to bottom, no rim, no names; only the pieces worn; above ✕ (`.up`) on days that show ✕ and ✓; hidden while zoomed; pictures only (the drawn figure already shows its cloth); `tests/todayswatch.test.js`
- they are 64 px (radius 13px, 9px apart) with a soft shadow (`0 2px 7px rgba(0,0,0,.28)`): the owner's choice from mockups at 52/64/76/90 px
- `ovlItem`: a jacket's or shirt's stripes in Claude's colours (`patternSpec.across`, as the painter draws them; measured from the photo a thin stripe blurs into the ground), each at least `OVL_LINE` (0.1) of a repeat
- `ovlSvg`: a tie's pattern at half size (`scale(.5)`)
- which source (`ovlPiece`, the owner's choice from a 64 px comparison): a tie, and a plain or finely woven suit or blazer, with a precision swatch (`item.swatch` with `swSrc` 'tex') shows that close-up (`ovlCloseup` and not `ovlDrawn`; painted twill grain read as a flat square at 64 px)
- striped, checked and figured jackets, and those without a close-up, are painted (`ovlSuitPng`): a 260 px PNG (`OVL_PX`), the read bands two repeats across, anti-aliased, a windowpane's crossing lines at least `OVL_CROSS` (.06) of a repeat at `OVL_CROSS_A` (.75), the cloth's grain (`ovlGrain`: twill, herringbone, linen, else plain weave) at `OVL_GRAIN` (2.2); a jacket with a stripe, check or figure and no band reading keeps the drawn tile; cached (`OVL_SUITS`)
- `ovlGrain` also reads corduroy (wales along the stripes), seersucker (a puckered stripe beside a flat one) and velvet (soft pile mottling) from the material (2026-11-15)
- a close-up patch barely shows a pattern at that size (his ink aubergine pinstripe read as plain dark cloth), hence `ovlDrawn` for a band or motif reading, or a stripe, check or motif pattern
- a shirt is always drawn, never its close-up (the owner: as in the mockup he chose)
- a shirt's figured weave is drawn at higher fidelity (`ovlWeave` → `texTileSmooth` in draw.js, the owner's choice "Smooth + thread grain": a soft 6 px tile read blurry, a sharp one as pixel steps)
- a herringbone shirt's swatch is the painter's own cloth (`ovlHbShirt`: `asmCmTile`, its colour held and every other column lighter, `OVL_HB_PAIRS` 5 column pairs across, an `OVL_HB_PX` 512 px PNG; the owner's pick "D"): the drawn tile at 2.2x read as harsh charcoal zigzags; made once per look, asynchronously, a plain patch of the shirt's colour meanwhile (`data-hb`), the drawing if it can't be made; `tests/hbswatch.test.js`
- a herringbone swatch's placeholder id comes from a counter (`ovlHbN`, as `ovlCuN`; CR-8-05, 2026-11-17): it was the cache's size, which repeats after an eviction, so a finished tile could land in another day's placeholder; `tests/asmrelease.test.js`
- a worsted twill with no close-up cloth (a suit, blazer or dress trousers routed 'twill' that `asmCuWants` turns down: the owner's Black suit, near-black) is painted with `asmWorstedTile` (asm-color.js, 2026-11-14, the owner's pick "strong" from mockups): rounded ribs rising about 60° to the right (`ASM_WT.k` 14,8 cycles over a 256 tile, `mm` 24, three times life size), uneven ribs, yarn twist and fleck, light swing `amp` 2.8 (the painter tones detail down on dark cloth); its looks carry `/wt1` in their key (`asmWtTag`); herringbone, chinos and jeans keep `asmTwillTex`; the generated twill read as a crosshatch, a faint check over the jacket; ambientCG's CC0 scans were tried first and lost (no suiting worsted; Fabric005 turned into pinstripes); `tests/asmcu.test.js`
- a twill suit's or blazer's swatch is the painter's own block (`ovlCuSuit`, 2026-11-14, the owner: "show the block of pattern the painter uses for the texture now, not the close up"): `asmCuTile`'s whole 256 px tile in the tagged colour, made once per look asynchronously (a plain patch meanwhile, `data-hb`, `OVL_CU`); only where the painter takes it (`asmCmRoute` 'twill' and `asmCuWants`: a close-up, not near-black), else, or with no clean patch, as before (`ovlSuitOld`); `tests/twillswatch.test.js`
- tapping a swatch opens it up close (`.ovsw`, `swTap`; the owner's pick from mockups): the swatch shows large at the top left (`.swlens`, 46% of the picture) with no name under it (the owner's ask); share, ✕ and ✓ hide; the photo zooms `SW_Z` (2.3) and moves so the piece lands at `SW_AT` (70% across, 40% down), ringed (`.swring`)
- the piece's spot is per colour-coded master (`SW_SPOT`: the point deepest inside each piece, measured from the masters' masks, the jacket's body without sleeves and lapels; `swKind` picks the master, `asmCmUse`, else suit/vest/shirt-tie/shirt-open by the outfit)
- another swatch switches; the same one, a tap on the picture, the double-tap zoom or another day close it (`swClose`, `foldAll`); a redraw of the day keeps it open (`swApply` after `applyZoom`)
- while one is open the small swatches stay where they are at 64 px when the big swatch leaves room, and shrink and move down only as far as they must (the owner: "stay the same size if they don't have to move"): `.fig.photo` is a size container, size `min(64px, max(36px, 18cqw - 16.7px))`, the offset above ✕ kept while `54cqw - 232px` allows it, gaps 9 px; about 48 px on a 390 px phone, 64 px in place on the Duo upright (688 px picture)
- the open small swatch's outline is 1.5 px of white at 70% (the owner: 2.5 px solid white was too thick and bright)
- the picture's frame is `overflow:clip` and `swApply` resets its scroll: the zoomed photo made it scrollable, and a tap's focus on the swatch scrolled it, pushing the big swatch and ring out of place
- `tests/swatchzoom.test.js` checks 390, 375, 330 and 720 px with real taps, and the outline

### `styles.css`

- styles
- design system for every page but Today (`:is(#wardrobe,#item-view,#add,#stats,#pack,#care,#settings-modal)`; Today keeps its own tuned styles): page header `.phead` (22px title, `.pcount`, `.pback`), buttons and inputs 44px (`--ctl-h`/`--in-h`), radius 12px on controls and 14px on cards, bordered cards, `.pri` filled, `.danger` red outline
- the design system's rules start with `:where(#wardrobe,…,#settings-modal)` and keep `:is(button,.btn)` / `:is(select,input…)` inside (the code review's CR-9-01, 2026-11-17): (0,1,0) and (0,1,1), above the global `button{}` / `select,input{}` resets, level with a page's class rule, so the later rule wins by order with no `!important` (with `:is(#…)` they had an id's weight and 22 `!important` fought them; an all-`:where` rule would lose to the resets)
- the select's `padding-right:8px` is written `:is(select,input[type=text]):is(select)` to stay one step above the field rule
- `[hidden]{display:none!important}` near the top is the one rule for the hidden attribute (it replaced eight per-element patches, CR-9-12)
- `tests/stylea11y.test.js` fails on an `:is(#…)` prefix, `!important` back in the freed rules, a per-element `[hidden]` patch, or a class in styles.css no app file uses (classes built as `'prefix-'+x` / `prefix-${}` count as used): delete a feature's rules with the feature
- `tests/uipages.test.js` checks sizes, titles and no sideways scroll at 320/466/626px
- safety: `.nostore` (the full-page message), `.tagfail` (the Add card's tagging failure)

- `--danger` is #e2695f on the dark theme (#c0473f was 3.5:1 on the dark card)

- `html{font-size:calc(var(--tscale,1) * 100%)}`: index.html's head sets `--tscale` from the iPhone's Larger Text (`-apple-system-body` size / 17, its default; kept .85–1.5; measured again on `pageshow` and coming back), 1 elsewhere; to try a size, set `--tscale` on `<html>`

- narrow screens (Safari's page zoom: 150% = 268 CSS px on an iPhone 17 Pro) and large text: in a Today pieces row under 15rem the slot name takes the first line and the piece goes under it (`#today .row` wraps, the label's flex-basis `clamp(4.25rem, (15rem - 100%) * 999, 100%)`; names went one letter per line; a container query on the list broke the unfolding list's scroll on WebKit, `reveal`/`pagescroll`); Settings' day buttons go 4 + 3 below 16.5rem (`.sgroup:has(>.days)` container); category titles wrap; the item sheet's and Add card's fields are `max(58%, min(8.5rem, 72%))` wide; `.pick-nm` breaks a long word; the header title stays one line with an ellipsis

- a phone on its side (`orientation:landscape` and height up to 500px): the picker takes the screen's height and shows six pieces across

- Today's card titles and second lines grow with Larger Text only as far as the width allows (`min(1rem, max(16px, 4.6vw))`, `min(.8125rem, max(13px, 3.75vw))`): never below the default size; "November wear plan" was cut at 150% text on a 402 px phone
