- at 340 px and under the header title may wrap ("Daily" over "Wardrobe") when a wider font does not fit it on one line, and with `html.bigtext` the item sheet's title gets a third line (2026-11-18): Linux's font on the cloud runs cut both at 150% text at 268 px; Segoe UI and Arial still fit as before
- a toast breaks a long unbroken name (`overflow-wrap:anywhere`, the button `flex:none`), so its Undo stays on the screen
- fix round 3 (2026-11-16), accessibility and the older user: a faded `#toast` is `visibility:hidden`; `.ask-danger` keeps #c0473f on the dark theme (white on #e2695f was 3.3:1); `--fline` (#7a8288 light, #6b7883 dark) is every input's, select's and textarea's border (`!important`, 3:1 on card and page; `--line` was 1.3-1.5:1); placeholders `--mut`; the focus ring `--acc-ink`; `--ok-ink` (#1b7043 / #4fb07a) for "Best match", "Very close" and What to buy's "Better outfits" fill #24704a; `.hrow.wk` not faded; 44px for the sample card's buttons, Suggest another and a holiday row
- text over 4.5:1 (CR-9-05): the "Add without a photo" subtitle is `--mut` (it was faded with opacity, 3.3:1); the item sheet's pills are `--mut` mixed 30% with `--ink`, "In rotation" `--ck-good` mixed 20% with `--ink`; `.conf.medium` has dark text on its gold; the connected ✓ (`.conn-tick`) is #24704a
- 44px to tap (CR-9-06): Settings' day buttons `::after` -3px above and below (44px; seven to a row they are 43px wide at 402px, and a hit area past their sides made the name read as cut), the Wore pill -11px, a piece's ⇄ and − 46px wide (`left`/`right` `min(-4px, 50% - 23px)`), a piece's row (`.rowtap` 44px with 2px padding above and below taken back by a -2px margin, the row no taller; not a `position:relative` `::after`: that made todaypants' record of a pants pick fail on WebKit), Insights' type rows `min-height:44px`; the month's day cells (35px wide at 320px with large text) are left as they are: seven to a row
- side safe areas (CR-9-07): `--sal` / `--sar` (`env(safe-area-inset-left/right)`) pad the header, the tab bar, the tour and import sheet's head, body and bar, the setup's head and bar, Settings' header, the no-photo sheet's bar and the no-storage page, with their old padding as the least; 0 on an upright phone
- `tests/stylea11y.test.js` covers the code review's styles and accessibility round (CR-9-01..08, CR-9-12): weights, `!important`, `[hidden]`, unused classes, the no-photo, import and tour focus, What to buy's focus back, contrast and tap sizes in both themes, the side safe areas (set by hand: neither browser has them)
- `tests/a11y.test.js` covers fix round 3's accessibility and older-user fixes (focus, live region, sheets, names, states, toast, contrast, Add one, color sheet order, cleaning banner, tour double tap, theme, weather retry, headings)
- the file's order (Round B, 2026-11-17): the tokens first, then each component as it was written, with a rule written twice merged into one where the cascade allowed it and the later fixes moved to their component; each component starts with a one-line `/* ---- what it is ---- */`; no dated or audit-tagged comments (that history is in git and here)
- the tokens are one `:root` block, one dark block for the device's dark mode (`@media (prefers-color-scheme:dark){:root:not([data-theme=light])}`) and the same values again for the theme button (`:root[data-theme=dark]`): a new dark color goes in both
- dark values that were component rules are tokens: `--fab-shadow` (the Wardrobe's +), `--rd-ok` (the reading sheet's green), `--fr-ring` (the getting-started checklist's empty rings)
- z-index layers are tokens: `--z-fab` 40, `--z-toast-low` 50, `--z-busy` 60, `--z-nav` 900, `--z-item` 950, `--z-over-item` 960, `--z-setup` 980, `--z-settings` 1000, `--z-sheet` 1101, `--z-color` 1201, `--z-reading` 1206, `--z-kit` 2000, `--z-chooser` 2011, `--z-toast` 2050, `--z-ask` 2101; a backdrop is its layer less one (`calc(var(--z-sheet) - 1)`); 1–5 inside a component stay numbers
- text sizes are tokens: `--fs-2xs` .6875rem, `--fs-xs` .75rem, `--fs-12h` .78125rem, `--fs-s` .8125rem, `--fs-b` .875rem, `--fs-m` .9375rem, `--fs-l` 1rem, `--fs-xl` 1.125rem, `--fs-2xl` 1.375rem; nothing was snapped, so rarer sizes are still written out (.7813rem, .7188rem, .78rem, .84rem, 1.03rem …): snapping them to the scale changes text by up to 0.3px and needs its own screenshots
- the bottom sheets share one rule (`#pick-sheet,#ask-sheet,#col-sheet,#cu-sheet,#rd-sheet,#nps-sheet,#shop-sheet`: fixed at the bottom, the 18px top corners, border, shadow, off screen until `.on`), the five short ones a second, the no-photo sheet and What to buy a third; every backdrop one (fixed, `inset:0`, 50% black); Settings, the setup and the tour one (fixed, full screen, the page's colors); each keeps its own rule for the rest
- the fold card (`.mbox`, `.month-link`, `.mltxt`, `.mpanel`, `.mpanel-in`, `.chev`) gives Today's cards and Settings' categories their look and motion; Today's sizes (the 76px head, the 8/20px margins, the panel's 10px padding, the title on one line and its narrow-screen sizes) are under `:where(#today)`, so Settings states its own and no longer undoes Today's (CR-9-14 in CSS only: the class names stay)
- `tools/cssdiff.js` proves a stylesheet change looks the same: every computed property of every element (and `::before`, `::after`, `::placeholder`, `::marker`) and its box, under origin/main's styles.css and then the checkout's, on 34 screens and sheets in 15 cells (light and dark, 402 and 320 px, 100% and 150% text, the theme button against the device both ways, 700 px, a phone on its side, reduced motion, 85% text), on the replay lab's WebKit iPhone; `--empty` runs the first screens with no wardrobe; a difference counts only if two records under the old sheet agree (timers flip some classes); `--show <out>` lists them; `--shots <dir>` saves before and after screenshots

### `merge.js`

- the merge rules, one copy for the app and the server (Round B, the code review's CR-6-12 / CR-5-12, 2026-11-17): items and days field by field (`mergeItem`, `mergeDay`, `dayWins`), settings setting by setting and entry by entry (`mergeSettingsBy`, `SET_SPLIT`, `setEntries`, `setJoin`, `mergeSplit`), whole snapshots (`mergeStateBy`) and their fingerprint (`stateSig`), with their helpers (`stamp`, `fsOk`, `fieldStamp`, `itemBase`, `tombWins`, `sameVal`, `fsPack`, `setHash`, `setKeys`, `setStampOf`, `subOk`, `subStampOf`, `dayFlat`, `dayUnflat`, `dayStamp`)
- a classic script with no DOM, storage or other file's names: index.html loads it right before core.js, analysis-worker.js `importScripts` it before core.js, and sw.js `ASSETS` lists it (offline); its names are page globals as they were in core.js
- worker/backup.js `import`s it: as an ES module its names stay inside the file, so its last line hands them over as `globalThis.DW_MERGE`; keep it free of `import`/`export` (the page loads it as a plain script)
- where the app and the server always differed the caller says so: `mergeStateBy(a, b, o)` / `mergeSettingsBy(a, b, o)` with `o.now` (the clock that forgets 180-day-old deletions), `o.old` (two settings copies without `ks`: the app keys them, `keySettings`), `o.num` (a setting's stamp read as a number), `o.lax` (a record that isn't one passed over, a deletion mark read as a number), `o.drop` (the server's `sampleOnly`); `stateSig(s, lax)`; called without them each function is exactly core.js's
- core.js keeps the app's own parts: `MERGE_APP` ({now: nowMs, old: keySettings}), `mergeState(a, b)` and `mergeSettings(a, b)` (the app's names, calling the shared ones), and the stamps made when something is saved (`itemFields`, `dayFields`, `keySettings`, `stampSettings`, `subStamp`, `keySplit`, `takeSettings`, `stampAllSettings`)
- the move changed nothing (proved at the PR with main's two copies against merge.js on 20,000 random snapshots, junk included: app against app and server against server, the same results, the same thrown errors and the same objects handed back)
- `tests/mergeshared.test.js`: 3,000 seeded random wardrobes merged both ways round give the same result in the app (merge.js + core.js) and the server (`mergeLite`); the server's own ways (sample pieces, junk records, old settings copies, text stamps); no copy of the rules left in core.js or backup.js; the load order
- the vm tests that run core.js alone (clock, fieldmerge, rollback, settingsmerge) and tools/fill-material.js read merge.js first; tests/backup-storage.test.js copies it beside its old-worker copy; tests/deploy.test.js checks the deploy workflow runs for every app file the worker imports
- merge.js stays a classic script after core.js became a module (2026-11-24): core.js reads its names as page globals, and the module worker analysis-worker.js imports it as worker/backup.js does and copies `DW_MERGE` onto its globals; nothing in merge.js or worker/ changed, so that merge deployed nothing to the server
- `tests/replay/scripts/CR-6-12.js` (port 9320): phones on the old apps (26cd8b1, 7a28f96, c4a549b) back up against today's worker and a phone on today's app gets their pieces; `CR612_WORKER=<checkout>` runs it against another worker to compare KV writes

### `bus.js`

- the notice board (the restructure, step 0's pilot, 2026-11-25; plan: `C:\code\cr-2026-11-16\RESTRUCTURE-PLAN.md`): how a lower file tells a screen something without naming it, so it never reads a file that runs after it: `on(topic, fn)` pins fn, `emit(topic, ...args)` calls every pinned fn in pin order (the files' load order) and says how many, `heard(topic)` says whether anything is pinned (where code asked `typeof f==='function'`)
- `emit` runs every listener even when one throws, then throws the first error on to the announcer (as the direct call it replaced would have); a fn pinned while its topic is announced runs from the next announcement
- an ES module that reads no other file and is read by nothing as a global (its `BRIDGE` row is empty): every user imports `{on, emit, heard}` from './bus.js'; its tag is the first module, right after merge.js; a leaf of `tests/deps.test.js`
- a topic is named for what happened (`settings-saved`), not for who acts on it; when the announcer needs an answer it passes an object the listeners fill in (`emit('settings-summaries', S)`, calendar.js `S.calendar = calSummary`)
- `tests/bus.test.js` checks bus.js and lists every topic (`TOPICS`) with the files that announce it and pin for it, in load order: a topic used but not listed, an announcer or listener that changed, or a topic with no listener or no announcer fails; a file pinning through settings.js's `settingsPart(fn)` counts as pinning `settings-parts`
- without bus.js nothing that imports it runs (settings.js, start.js and the 13 parts): the start-up card shows, as when settings.js or start.js is missing on main (measured, both engines, 404 and bad syntax)
- sockets (the restructure, step 2, 2026-11-26): `serve(name, fn)` offers fn under name (the last one served is the one asked), `ask(name, ...args)` calls it and gives back its answer, or does nothing and gives `undefined` when nothing is served (the feature's file didn't load), as the `typeof f==='function'` guards did; an error the served fn throws reaches the caller
- a socket is how a required file calls into an optional one ("show a toast", "open the color picker") without importing it (`tests/load-contract.json`); app.js has its own global `ask` (the server request), so a file that uses both imports bus.js's under another name (`import {ask as askFor} from './bus.js'`)
- `tests/bus.test.js` lists every socket (`SOCKETS`: the one file that serves it, the files that ask); a socket used but not listed, or served or asked by other files, fails; none is used yet (step 3 converts the callers)

### `core.js`

- colours, types, item helpers (no DOM; also loaded by background workers)
- an ES module since 2026-11-24 (index.html's notes): what the other files read is its bridge, the last lines; a `let` another file or a test assigns needs `set: v => {name = v}` there, or the write misses the module's own variable
- `setWardrobe({items, days, settings})` is the one writer of `items`, `dayRecs` and `settings` in memory (Round B, CR-7-07): it sets only what it is given, so each caller keeps its own `applyCustom`/`planDirty` in its own order; the planner's brief swaps call it too, to swap and to put back (analysis.js `items`, pack.js `packPlanOf` and today.js `planWith` `dayRecs`; S1a, 2026-11-25)
- the casual garments (2026-11-16, the owner's approved new types): `tee` T-shirt, `polo`, `henley`, `sweater`, `sweatshirt`, `hoodie`, `qzip` Quarter-zip (`CASUAL_TOPS`, worn in the shirt slot in place of the shirt), `cardigan`, `chore` Chore jacket (`CASUAL_JACKETS`, the jacket slot), at the end of `TYPES`; their colours (`COLS_BY`, a block after it), patterns (`PATS_BY`: solid, stripe, heather, cable on the knits, other; `CASUAL_PAT_ALIAS`), materials (`MATERIALS_BY`, polo 'piqué'; `MAT_ALIAS` per type), all equal to worker/tag.js's lists
- cargo pants: `trouserKind` 'cargo' (`TROUSER_KINDS`, `isCargo`; `trouserKind(i)` reads them as chinos for the rules that ever see them; "Khaki cargo pants")
- the uniform blouse (2026-11-17, the owner's OCP coat): type `blouse` "Uniform blouse" in `CASUAL_JACKETS` (hand-pick only, the jacket slot, − takes it off), patterns camouflage/solid/other (camouflage is OCP: `isOcp`), its colors (`COLS_BY.blouse`, khaki first) and materials (ripstop first, `MAT_ALIAS.blouse`); care.js washes it after 3 wears; Wardrobe search, import words, Pack and Insights nouns
- what the owner sees uses `trouserKindShown(i)` (cargo stays cargo): the item sheet's Kind select and Details summary, and the Wardrobe grid's label "Cargo pants" (picker audit 2026-11-17 #4: they read "Chinos"); the planner keeps `trouserKind`
- hand-pick only (the owner's decision): `isHandPick(i)` = a casual type or cargo pants (`HANDPICK_TYPES`), `handPickSlot(i)` its Today slot; the one check the planner (`planSim`'s `by`), Suggest another (`swapSlot`), the picker's scored grid (`slotPool`), the rotation stats (`buildStats` rows), What to buy (`shopList`, `evalPlan`'s idle), `shopGate` and `setupNeeds` ask; a later Uniform flag can extend it
- combat boots (`shoeStyle` 'combat', 2026-11-17) are hand-pick only too: `isHandPick` holds shoes of that style, `handPickSlot` gives 'shoes'; they go only with camouflage pants (`camoPants(b)`: pants whose pattern is a camouflage, `camoPat`)
- `casualTop(o)`: a casual top in the shirt slot; `needsTie` is false then (no tie, no tie clip that day; the top is the top layer, a sweater over a shirt is not modelled)
- `hasPat(t)` = FABRIC or a casual type (pattern and second colour in the Add card, item sheet, colour picker, tag stats, import, search; no close-up, swatch or reading); `TAG_TYPES` (the nine types and 'cargo') is sent as `types` with the plain tag and /tag-many (not the picker's freeColors/freePattern calls, which stay byte-identical: tagging.js `tagExtras`)
- pocket squares (2026-11-16, the owner's approved feature): type `pocketsquare` ("Pocket square", after the tie clip in `TYPES`), its colours (`COLS_BY`, white first), patterns (the tie's prints plus `border`, a contrast edge in the second colour; `PAT_ALIAS.pocketsquare`), materials (silk, linen, cotton, wool, silk blend, polyester), the slot `pocket` (`SLOTS`, `SLOT_TYPE`); `hasPat(t)` = FABRIC or a pocket square (pattern and second/third colours, no close-up, swatch or reading); `dressOut` leaves it out without jackets; `TAG_TYPES` (['pocketsquare']) is sent with every tag (`types`), and the worker offers the type only then; `tests/pocketsquare.test.js`, `tests/tagger.test.js`
- `isWorkday(d)` = a weekday in `settings.workdays` that is not a day off (`offName(d)`: a public holiday in `settings.holidays` `{country, list:[{d,n}], skip}` that is not skipped, or one of the user's own `settings.daysOff` `[{d,n}]`; dates as `YYYY-MM-DD`, `isoKey`)
- the planner skips days off like weekends
- `itemSeason`: the item's own season (one set in the item sheet, 'all' too, wins), else tweed pattern, else its material (`matSeason`: `MAT_SEASON` flannel, tweed, corduroy, velvet, cashmere, cashmere blend, moleskin, melton cold; linen, linen blend, seersucker, tropical wool warm; by type `MAT_SEASON_BY` a wool tie cold (a wool suit stays all year); any case; season types only; mohair blend and hopsack set none: the owner's decisions on the materials audit, 2026-11-15)
- `dayClimate` with a location but no usual weather (`climate` null: its history failed to load) uses the location's country (`location.cc`, else the holiday country) the same way, Gulf highs and all (fix round 3, W1-1-05 = W2-16-03: a Kuwait December was planned as winter with an overcoat); the hemisphere still comes from the location's latitude
- `dayClimate` with no location: the holiday country (set from the time zone at setup) gives a Gulf climate's usual highs (`CC_TMAX`: KW SA AE QA BH OM IQ; a Kuwait December is mild, no overcoat) or the southern hemisphere (`SOUTH_CC`); not the phone's time zone itself, so tests stay the same on any machine
- `inRotation(i)`: false for an item the user took out of rotation (`item.paused`, synced with the item); it stays in the wardrobe and in past outfits
- bought, on the way: `item.order` with `item.paused`, optionally `item.arrives` (YYYY-MM-DD); `inRotation(i, n)` is true for day number `n` (`di`) after the arrival date (`arriveN`), so the plan includes it from then on
- `inRotation(i, n)` / `arriveN` serve pieces away being cleaned too: `item.arrives` holds `item.away.back`
- `item.shade` (−6…+6 steps of `SHADE_STEP` 4 L*, `shadeHex`/`shadeFor`/`shadeName`): the owner's darker/lighter for the drawn main colour (`colOf`); the tag, the planner (`planCol`, `lumOf`) and photo keys keep the tagged colour
- shade is set on the Add card and in the editor (`shadeRow`, `stepShadeQ`/`matchShadeQ`, `stepShadeE`/`matchShadeE` in add.js); "Match close-up" uses the close-up's measured lightness
- `desc(i)` names a metal only for `METAL_TYPES` (watch, belt, cufflinks): an Add card carries `metal` 'silver' for every type, and a suit's or shoes' caption read "· silver"
- `desc(i)` names cufflinks by colour and metal ("light blue · gunmetal"; silver or gold links only their metal, `linkFace`), says "gunmetal" for the metal 'other' (`metalWord`), a sneaker's leather "leather" (`leatherWord`), and a season only for `SEASON_TYPES` (2026-11-15, the new-user audit: "Saved: Other.", "calf" sneakers)
- `MATERIALS_BY`: the materials offered per item type for `item.material` (suit, blazer, pants, shirt, tie, socks, overcoat, raincoat; lower-case names, shown with `cap1`); shoes and belts (their `leather`), watches (band and metal), cufflinks and tie clips have no list
- shirts also offer polyester, polyester blend, silk, rayon, modal, lyocell, bamboo, wool, jersey, denim and corduroy (2026-11-16, the owner: "add polyester to dress shirts and any other materials a shirt could be made of"); the shirt alias polyester → performance stretch went (polyester is its own material now; nylon still reads as performance stretch, and the one-time rename is unchanged); shirt aliases viscose → rayon, Tencel → lyocell, merino → wool, poly-cotton / cotton-polyester → cotton blend, poly → polyester; the worker's list and its "low for a fiber the eye cannot tell" line name them too; a corduroy or flannel shirt is cold-weather by `MAT_SEASON` like a jacket
- the lists follow the materials audit and the owner's decisions (2026-11-15, `C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\materials-audit\`): shirts hold the fabric only (cotton, poplin, linen, linen blend, flannel, cotton blend, performance stretch, seersucker; their weaves twill, oxford, pinpoint oxford, end-on-end, chambray are patterns), ties lost "knitted silk" (the knit pattern says it) and gained shantung; added: tropical wool (suit, blazer, pants), silk blend (suit, blazer), moleskin (suit, pants), camel hair and jersey (blazer), gabardine and seersucker (pants), mercerized cotton (socks), melton, loden, alpaca (overcoat), rubberized cotton (raincoat); every list at most 20 names (the reader's schema); `tests/materials.test.js`
- a stored name that left a list is never lost: it shows as Other with its name (`matRow`), and search, the season, the painter and import still read it (an own material removed in Settings too)
- the person's own materials (`settings.ownMaterials = {type: [name]}`, 2026-11-16): added from Claude's offer, listed per type in Settings → Photo tagging → Your materials (`#ownmat-list`, tagging.js `renderOwnMatUI`, Remove: `removeOwnMaterial`; items using one keep it), synced entry by entry (`SET_SPLIT` 'mats'); see "own materials" under the Add card
- `MAT_ALIAS` (word -> listed name, `'*'` and per type) and `matAlias(type, word)`: the audit's alias table (fresco/tropical/high-twist -> tropical wool, worsted -> wool, merino -> merino wool, lisle/fil d'Écosse -> mercerized cotton, broadcloth -> poplin, wool-linen/linen-cotton/silk-linen -> linen blend, wool-silk -> silk blend, camel -> camel hair, mohair -> mohair blend, nylon-spandex/stretch -> performance stretch, nylon on socks -> synthetic blend, on shirts -> performance stretch…), read by the import (kit.js) and the search (wardrobe.js `matSyn`); `SHIRT_WEAVE_MAT`: a shirt weave in an import's Material column -> its pattern key
- the owner's stretch shirts (74% nylon, 26% spandex, typed "nylon/spandex", then moved to "nylon") become "performance stretch" once in the app (wardrobe.js `matMigrate`)
- `matMigrate` stamps only the material, by the corrected clock (`nextStamp`, `itemFields`, as app.js `put`), not the whole item at `Date.now()` (CR-5-08)
- a shoe's material by style (`leathersFor`): a sneaker in leather (stored `calf`, shown "Leather"), suede, nubuck, canvas or knit (`SHOE_TEXTILES`, textured in draw.js `leatherLayers`, finish `textile`); a boot in calf, suede, nubuck, the grains or cordovan; a loafer in calf, suede, patent or cordovan; a derby in every leather and nubuck; oxfords in every leather; belts in every leather, braided leather (`braided`, said "braided leather" by `leatherWord`) and canvas (`BELT_MATS`; 2026-11-15, the owner's options)
- a combat boot's material: suede, nubuck or smooth leather (`leathersFor`), each shown "… and nylon" (`COMBAT_MATS`, `leatherWord`); coyote (#8f6a3c, the photo's suede) is a shoe colour and a sole colour (`COLS_BY.shoes`); a combat boot's or a sneaker's second colour is its sole (`soleStyle`, colpick.js "Sole")
- a combat boot added by hand is suede and nylon: choosing the style Combat boot on an Add card makes the material suede unless a material was chosen there first or Claude read another (add-card.js `setq`; the owner, 2026-11-18)
- nubuck (`SHOE_NAP`) is drawn with suede's nap, finer, and planned as suede (planner.js `finish`); a braided belt as smooth leather; a canvas belt (finish textile) goes with sneakers, canvas or knit shoes and suede-type shoes (`beltFit` 0)
- the Add card and the item sheet label it "Material" on every pair of shoes and every belt (`leatherLabel`, since 2026-11-15: belts can be canvas), right under Style and above More details / Notes; a style change to one that can't be in the material makes it plain leather
- a watch's band (`BANDS`): metal bracelet, integrated bracelet, leather, rubber or a fabric NATO strap (2026-11-15); a strap (`STRAP_BANDS`, `strapBand`) has the item's colour ("Strap color"), is drawn in it (rubber plain, NATO with fine ribs, leather stitched) and painted on the picture (asm-watch.js); only leather is matched to the shoes
- the metal `two-tone` (2026-11-15; added to `METAL` after its statement, `METAL_TONES`, asm-watch.js `ASM_WATCH_TONES`): planner.js `metalSame` lets it agree with gold and silver (not gunmetal)
- cufflinks may be silk knots: the metal `silk` ("silk knot", `metalWord`), offered to cufflinks only (`metalOptsFor`, `LINK_METAL_OPTS`; not in `METAL`); drawn as two knotted balls in the item's colour; planner.js `metalOf` gives null, so they go with any metal (as an enamel clip)
- shoes can be white or cream (sneakers), and the second colour is a sneaker's sole
- `stone` (pale grey-beige chinos, family tan) and `denim` (indigo, family blue) are trouser colours; "stone" doesn't read as khaki
- coats' patterns (2026-11-17, the Look beta): `PATS_BY.overcoat` / `raincoat` = solid, herringbone, houndstooth, check, glen-check, windowpane, tweed, other (= worker `PATTERN_BY_TYPE`); `hasPat` is unchanged, so only the beta shows a coat's pattern and second colour (look.js `lookHasPat`); a tweed coat plans as cold weather (`itemSeason`), and no coat saved before has a pattern, so plans are unchanged
- `tagTypesNow()`: `TAG_TYPES`, plus 'coatpat' while the Look beta is on (look.js `lookOn`); `tagExtras` and /tag-many send it, so with the beta off every tag request is as before
- one title style: `.cap` doesn't capitalise every word; `sentence(t)` capitalises a generated description's first letter (`itemTitle`, the item sheet, the piece picker, Today's pieces line, Laundry, Stats, What to buy); a name the user typed is shown exactly as typed
- titles (the owner's title audit, 2026-11-17; `tests/titles.test.js` caps them on a varied wardrobe): `shortDesc(i)` is the main colour, the pattern and, for shoes and belts, the leather (a belt's and cufflinks' metal after " · "); a watch "Gold chronograph · white dial"; `itemTitle`, `savedTitle` (add.js; a shoe's style or trouser kind as the noun), `careName`, `statName`, the score's swap buttons, Today's chips and below-row labels, the import's lines and month.js `shirtWords` use it; `nm()` is `shortCol` + pattern + `typeWord` ("navy argyle socks"); `desc()` keeps everything (second and third colours, toe cap, season, French cuffs, the watch band) for the "As tagged" caption, alt text and search; no picture key reads either
- `itemName(i)` / `itemTitle(i)` / `titleCls(i)`: an item's own name (`item.name`), what it is called on screen (the name, else `desc(i)`), and the class for it (`named`: shown as typed; `cap`: a description, capitalised)
- a described title carries a shoe's style and a trouser's kind (`styleWord` in `itemTitle`: "Oxblood calf loafers", "White leather sneakers", "Stone chinos"; W2-17-15); oxfords, dress pants and items with no style read as before; `desc` and `nm` are unchanged
- they are in core.js, not wardrobe.js, because Today is drawn at start-up, which can be before the later scripts have loaded: in wardrobe.js the start-up render threw now and then and the app came up with an empty wardrobe
- the planner's texts (`desc`, `nm`, `rateOutfit` notes) never use the name
- `avgText(v)`: an average score with one decimal, rounded down ("99.9"), "100" only when every day scored 100; Stats' "What costs you points" is "Small deductions" and What to buy says "days with a warning"
- the dress code: `settings.dress = {jacket, tie}` (synced; a missing object or key means yes); `wearsJacket()` / `wearsTie()` read it
- `needsJacket(o)` / `needsTie(o)`: is an outfit without that piece short of one (not on a `casual` day, not when the dress code leaves it out); the score and Today's pieces list use them
- `dressOut(type)`: an item type the dress code leaves out (tie and tie clip; suit and blazer): not counted as never worn, not suggested
- trousers have a kind (`item.trouserKind`: chinos or jeans; none = dress trousers) and shoes a style (`item.shoeStyle`: derby, loafer, boot, sneaker; none = oxford): `trouserKind(i)`, `shoeStyle(i)`, `TROUSER_KINDS`, `SHOE_STYLES`; `typeWord(i)` names them in `nm()` ("denim jeans", "brown loafers")
- items from before have neither kind nor style and plan exactly as before (`tests/dress.test.js` GOLD unchanged)
- `hasVest(i)`: a suit has a waistcoat only with `vest:'yes'`; while the wardrobe has no `settings.vestDef` (it is from before that rule) a suit with no value still has one, so nothing changes until its suits were given their value (dress.js)
- swapping the belt brings shoes that match it, as swapping the shoes brings a belt (`FOLLOW.belt` in pickscore.js: shoes and socks; the owner: with one belt per pair of shoes a belt swap took the belt off and said "None of your belts match")
- when the other belts' shoes don't suit the outfit (a black suit wants black shoes), the swap or pick changes nothing and Today says why (`beltless` in pickscore.js: the day planned with the record in memory only); `tests/picker.test.js`
- the type `pants` is "Trousers" on screen (`TYPES`, packing list, Care); photo prompts still say "dress trousers"
- colours always come from the item's tagged colours, matched by brightness rank, never from the photo, except an item's `sett`: it is read from the white-balanced sharp photo or close-up at the user's request, so its bands keep their measured colours
- `PATS_BY` must stay in sync with the worker's prompt (worker/tag.js), and so must the per-type colour lists (`COLS_BY` / `DIAL_COLS` here = `TYPE_COLORS` / `DIAL_COLS` in worker/tag.js; `tests/colors.test.js` checks it)
- `colBaseFor(hex, type, accent)` (moved here from colpick.js, 2026-11-15): an own shade's base when Claude gave none, from the type's own list by colour family (`BASE_HUE` 45°, else `BASE_HUE2` 80°; a near-neutral the list's neutrals), then within `BASE_L` of its lightness, then ΔE00; by ΔE alone over every key petrol blue planned as charcoal and cognac as rust; worker/describe.js `baseFor` is the same rule
- `labOf(c)` is `rgbLab` of the colour's hex (Round B, 2026-11-17: it had its own copy of the formula; the same numbers)
- what an own colour's or pattern's name may contain (2026-11-15, the re-audit's N4): letters of any script, digits, spaces and - ’ . , ( ) / + (`OWN_NAME_OK`); a straight apostrophe becomes ’ and & "and" (`ownNameTidy`); `addCustomColor`/`addCustomPattern` (tagging.js) refuse a typed name with anything else ("Use only letters, numbers, spaces and - ’ . , ( ) / + in the name.", `ownNameBad`) and store it cleaned; `applyCustom` cleans every name it applies (`ownNameClean`, a pattern's desc with `ownDescClean`), so a name arriving by sync or saved before can't carry < > " ' & or a backtick into the page; `CUSTOM_COL`/`CUSTOM_PAT` hold cleaned copies, `settings.custom` stays as synced; Claude's suggested names are cleaned before they are added (colpick.js `colAddNew`/`patAddNew`)
- own colour and pattern keys for a name in any script (fix round 3, 2026-11-16, W2-12-03 = W2-16-01): `ownColKey`/`ownPatKey` drop accents (`ownKeyNorm`: NFD without marks, Arabic-Indic digits as 0-9, no tatweel), keep a-z and 0-9, and add a short FNV-1a hash of the name (`ownKeyHash`, base 36) when it has letters outside a-z; a Latin name without digits gets the key it always had; keys stay ASCII: `OWN_COL_RE` `^own[a-z0-9]{1,28}$` (digits are new: "Rot 1" and "Rot 2" were both ownrot) and `OWN_PAT_RE` `^own-[a-z0-9-]{1,28}$`, in `applyCustom` and the worker's `KEY_RE` (worker/tag.js, worker/describe.js)
- one own-key rule: the app's `OWN_COL_RE` / `OWN_PAT_RE` (core.js: `applyCustom` and `cleanRecord`'s colour and pattern keys) and the server's `worker/ownkeys.js` (tag.js and describe.js lists, records.js `recCheck`) are the same regexes; an `own…` colour or pattern key outside them is dropped by the app and refused by the server; `tests/ownnames.test.js` checks they agree
- a stored key is never derived from its name again: `ownColFind(name)` / `ownPatFind(name)` find the owner's colour or pattern by name (colpick.js Claude's New chips, Use all, the reading sheet); a new name whose key an older name holds gets 2, 3… after it (`ownFreeKey`); a name needs two letters or digits of any script (`ownNameShort`); `normCol` takes an exact own key with digits
- `patNameTaken`: a new pattern named like a built-in one ("Birdseye", "glen check") or one of the owner's is refused (W1-3-05: the check compared `own-birdseye` with `birdseye`); `colNameTaken` compares letters and digits of any script (`colLetters`)
- the Arabic comma ، is allowed in own names (`OWN_NAME_OK`, `ownNameClean`, worker `ownTxt`, which keeps own names in any script in tag and describe requests: `txt` emptied an Arabic name)
- `nm()` and the month's lines (month.js `shortTop`, `shirtWords`, `piecesParts`) and analysis.js name an own pattern by its name (`patName`, W1-1-04: "own basketweave"); photos.js `itemDesc` keeps `patLabel`, since it is in `itemKey`
- right-to-left names (W2-16-04, W2-16-10): `nameHtml(t)` escapes and wraps a name with a right-to-left letter (`RTL_RE`) in `<bdi>`, `nameIso(t)` puts it between U+2068/U+2069 for plain text; both return a Latin name unchanged; used by the packing list, Today's pieces line (`piecesParts`), Insights' pairings, Laundry (`careName`), the days-off chips and the Saved / Deleted / is back toasts; `.named` has `unicode-bidi:plaintext`; the name, brand, notes, material, search, day-off and new colour/pattern name fields have `dir="auto"`
- `colBaseFor` (and worker/describe.js `baseFor`) takes the nearest hue pool (`BASE_HUE`, then `BASE_HUE2`) that holds a colour within `BASE_L` of the shade's lightness before a hue alone (W2-16-12: a dark petrol tie was based on light blue, now slate)
- each item type offers only colours it really comes in; an off-list tag moves to the closest allowed colour (`fitCol`), and a colour an existing item already has stays selectable for it
- settings merge setting by setting (2026-11-14, the audit's "settings sync as one block": phone A's workdays and phone B's city, the later save dropped the other): each setting has its own stamp `settings.ks[k]` and the fingerprint of the value it was stamped with `settings.kh[k]`; `keySettings` runs where settings are read (start.js start-up and `onSyncedData`, settings.js, safety.js) and gives a copy from before every setting its one stamp, and a setting only the defaults filled in stamp 0; `stampSettings` (in `saveUserSettings`) stamps only settings whose value changed; `mergeSettings` (in `mergeState`) takes each setting from the newer stamp, ties to the newer copy, and makes the result's `updated` one above both when it took anything from the older copy, so the phone holding it uploads it (sync.js sends settings newer than the server's); two copies without `ks` merge whole as before; a roll-back stamps every setting (`stampAllSettings` in sync.js `rollBackState`) and marks ones the snapshot lacks removed; the server merges with the same `mergeSettingsBy` (merge.js); `tests/settingsmerge.test.js`
- items merge field by field (fix round 3, 2026-11-16, the audit's W1-5-01: a rename on one phone and "Take out of rotation" on the other, the later whole item won): `put()` (app.js) works out `item.fs` = {u: the item's stamp, b: the stamp of every field not listed, s: {field: stamp}} against the stored copy in the same transaction (`itemFields`; a removed field is stamped too; an edit stamped at or behind that copy is made one above it); `mergeItem` (in `mergeState`) takes each field from the copy with the newer stamp for it, ties to the newer copy, and gives the result a stamp one above both when it took anything from the older copy, so it uploads and wins elsewhere (still one backup per phone)
- a copy whose `fs.u` isn't its own stamp (an older app's save, a whole restamp: `putRaw`, Undo of a delete, a roll-back, kit.js's sample and import writes) counts its one stamp for every field, so two such copies merge whole as before; a new item, or one stored again after a deletion, gets no `fs`
- a deletion wins over a copy whose oldest field is no newer than it (`tombWins`, `itemBase` = `fs.b`, else the stamp): an edit made on a copy from before the deletion (an offline phone, a sheet left open, a roll-back) no longer brings the piece back (W1-6-06, W1-5-05); Undo of a delete restamps the item whole after it, so it still comes back; the phone whose edit was dropped is told (sync.js `applyMerged`)
- settings made of entries merge entry by entry (W1-5-06, W1-5-03): `SET_SPLIT` = own colors and patterns (`custom`, by key), `care` and `dress` (by name), `workdays`, `blazerDays`, `shopSkip` (each in or out), `daysOff` (by date) and own materials (`ownMaterials`, type 'mats', by 'type/name', 2026-11-16); each carries `settings.kse[k]` ({'': the stamp of unlisted entries, entry: stamp}) and `settings.khe[k]` (each entry's fingerprint, '-' removed, '#' the whole value's, so a copy an older app changed is seen and counted whole); `keySettings` keys them (`keySplit`), `stampSettings` stamps the entries that changed (`subStamp`; a setting that didn't exist before has no entries), `mergeSettings` merges such a setting entry by entry (`mergeSplit`) when the copies differ on it; two phones each adding a different day or own color keep both, the same entry changed on both takes the later; `stampAllSettings` drops them (a roll-back or restore wins whole)
- favorites (`settings.fav`, fav.js, 2026-11-18) merge entry by entry too: `SET_SPLIT` 'fav', a look as `l/j|sh|tie`, a piece as `i/id`; `setJoin` gives `{looks}` in the order loved (`at`) and `{items}` sorted; cleaned by `SET_CLEAN.fav` (`favClean`: `{j, sh, tie, at}` of `ID_RE` ids, a shirt and a jacket or tie)
- days merge field by field (W1-5-02): `saveDay` and the week's lock (history.js `lockUpcoming`) work out `dayFields` (fs as an item's) against the copy in `dayRecs` (a record changed in place: against the stored one, through `atomic`; reading storage first cost each WebKit day write about a fifth more); the picks, skips, pieces taken off and pieces recorded count slot by slot (`DAY_SUB`: 'force.tie'); a pick fixes every other slot to what the day showed, and such an entry (the piece already in `ids` for that slot) is not a change; `mergeDay`: a copy without fs counts its stamp for every field, except the week's lock (`lockOnly`), whose other fields are its `base`; the lock field keeps `dayWins`' rule; `tests/fieldmerge.test.js` (the rules, app and worker, then two devices through the real worker)

- cleaning reminders on the phone (2026-11-14, the owner: "a phone reminder when a suit is due"): Settings → Schedule → Morning reminder → "Cleaning reminders" (`#care-rem-sw`, `remindSwitch('care')` in remind.js; follows the morning switch until set by itself, `remindPrefs().care` undefined); `careReminders` (care.js) gives `{date: text}` for the coming workdays: a suit's or blazer's last wear on the morning planned, the first morning it is planned past its limit (the owner, 2026-11-14: only the first), and once due one reminder the next workday morning to come, neither again until cleaned (`localStorage.care_told` {c, d, o}); kept as `care.json` beside `remind.json` in `dw-keep-remind`; the dates sent to the server are those of either kind (no server change); sw.js shows the cleaning text under the outfit line, or alone titled "Due for cleaning"; `tests/care.test.js`, `tests/remind.test.js`
- the cleaning frame on Today's picture (`careFrame`/`careDayState`/`careBandFit` in care.js; the owner's design 2026-11-14, "B" from mockups, tweaked): a suit or blazer one wear short of its limit has a yellow frame and a banner along the picture's bottom (wear dots left, "**1 wear left** · Suit – wear 4 of 5" right, no icon), its last wear red ("**Last wear** · Suit – to the cleaner today"), past it red ("**Overdue** · Suit – worn 6 of 5", the dots +1); on today and coming days, the wears planned between today and the day counted (so yellow today is red the next time it is planned); both inside the picture (frame inset 5 px, banner 40 px over its bottom); the banner is cut round the tie swatch when the swatches reach into it, measured each time (ResizeObserver and every frame through `swApply`'s transition), so the cut follows the swatches when a tap shrinks them; on today and past days the ✕/✓ row and the swatches sit above the banner (`.fig.cb`); words 13 px, down to 11, then the bold words on their own line, fitted once for the narrowest space (beside a full-size tie swatch) and kept while the cut moves (the owner, 2026-11-14: refitted every frame the text jumped), again only when the picture's width changes; trousers keep the grey line under the outfit (`careNote`); `tests/care.test.js`

- the Sync now button is `syncTap()` (sync.js): a tap clears a backup pause (`sync_pause`, the day's saves used up) and tries; if the server still refuses, the pause comes back; while paused, `showSyncStatus` shows the pause's message and the last backup's time, not only "Backed up …" (2026-11-14: the owner's Sync now did nothing silently after Cloudflare's plan was upgraded); `tests/server-limits.test.js`

- records coming in are checked (`cleanRecord`/`cleanDay`/`cleanState`, fix round 3 2026-11-16, the audit's Critical W2-13-02 = W2-12-01: a crafted Restore file, sync or server answer put an item's id, photo, color key and sett band color into the page as markup): only an item with an id of `ID_RE` (letters, digits, `_ . : -`, 80 at most) and a known type comes in
- `cleanRecord` keeps color and option keys only of letters, digits, `-` and `_` (`REC_KEY`; an own key whatever its script; a known color's word, "light blue", becomes its key), photos/photoSm/texture/swatch only as whole `data:image/jpeg|png|webp;base64` strings (`REC_PHOTO`, else dropped), `added`/`updated`/`shade` only as numbers, a `sett` only of numbers, hex colors and plain words (`recStrict`), free text (name, brand, material, notes, details, patternText) as typed but without bidi controls (`noBidi`), and every other field with `< > " \`` taken out of its strings (`recFree`); a clean record comes out equal (idempotent)
- `cleanDay`: a date of `REC_DATE`, and a day's `ids`/`lock`/`force` keep only item ids of `ID_RE`; `cleanState` also keeps only numeric tombstones of `ID_RE` ids, and cleans a snapshot's settings with `cleanSettings`
- where it runs: Restore file, every sync pull (lite and whole) and roll-back copy (sync.js), what each sync sends (so the server's check never refuses the app's own records), and the stored wardrobe once per phone at start-up (app.js `cleanStored`, localStorage `rec_clean`); `tests/records.test.js`
- settings coming in are checked too (`cleanSettings`, fix round A 2026-11-17, the code review's Critical C1 = CR-2-02 + CR-4-01 + CR-7-02: a crafted Restore file, sync or holiday list put an own color's key and hex, a holiday's or day off's date and a hidden What to buy key into the page as script): each setting is kept only in its own shape (`SET_CLEAN`), an entry of the wrong shape left out
- `SET_CLEAN`: workdays and blazer days 0-6; holidays' country two capital letters (else ''), dates of `REC_DATE`; days off a `REC_DATE` date; names (`n`) without bidi controls or `< > " \``; `shopSkip` keys `SHOP_SKIP_RE` (type|group|class of letters, digits, - and _); own colors and patterns only with a key of `OWN_COL_RE` / `OWN_PAT_RE`, a color also a 6-digit hex, base and kinds plain keys (kinds always a list), a look through `recStrict`; own materials as `ownMatName` gives them; `tagStats` counts as numbers; every other setting through `recFree`
- `cleanSettings` never touches the stamps (`updated`, `ks`, `kh`, `kse`, `khe`) and returns a clean copy as the same object, so the app's own settings are never restamped; a cleaned setting differs from its `kh`, so the next save stamps and syncs the clean value
- where it runs: `cleanState` (Restore file, every sync pull, roll-back), `takeSettings` (start-up, after a sync, the safety restore, the holiday refresh: a setting of the wrong shape leaves the phone's own), and the stored copy once per phone at start-up (app.js `cleanStoredSettings`, localStorage `set_clean`); `tests/settingsin.test.js`
- `cleanStoredSettings` keeps the original in meta `set_kept` ([{rec, at}], appended) and replaces the stored copy in the same transaction, stamps unchanged, only while it is still the copy read; a one-time toast says "Some of this phone’s settings couldn’t be shown safely and were set aside. Nothing was deleted: the originals are kept on this phone."
- the connection is kept in the database too (`connKeep`/`connRestored` in app.js; 2026-11-14: on the owner's iPhone the Home Screen app's localStorage lost the server address and code after a forced close during an update, while the database kept the wardrobe and Safari kept both): `worker_url`, `app_token` and `remind` are copied to meta `conn` (after the start-up restore, when Settings closes, on connecting, whenever the app goes to the background) and put back at start-up where localStorage lost them; start.js and sync.js wait for `connRestored`; cleared on purpose in Settings, the copy is cleared too; `tests/connkeep.test.js`
- the two automatic copies of the connection (`connKeep(true)`: after the start-up restore and going to the background) only add what localStorage holds, in one transaction; only `connKeep()` (Settings, connecting, Delete my data) replaces the copy, so a failed read at start-up or keys lost while the app is open can't write the loss over it (2026-11-23)
- account.js `acctStart` sets the body's connection classes again once `connRestored` has settled (2026-11-24): a connection put back from the database came after the first `applyRole`, and the phone stayed `nocon` until Settings opened (WebKit on every such start, Chromium about 1 in 6); `tests/connrole.test.js`
- `page.waitForFunction` doesn't wait for an async predicate (its promise counts as true at once, both engines): a test waiting on IndexedDB polls with `page.evaluate` (`until` in `tests/connkeep.test.js`)

### `planner.js`
- an enamel tie clip (black, red, blue, navy: `clipMetal` null) is chosen by the tie (`enamelPref` in planner.js, 2026-11-14, the owner: 7 of his 9 clips were never planned, an enamel clip scored 0 against a silver watch-and-buckle match's 9): picking up the tie's second or third colour (by family) `ENAMEL_ACCENT` 11, beating silver; the tie's own colour or a near one (red with burgundy, blue with navy) `ENAMEL_TONE` 8, just under, so it comes round when silver has been worn; anything else `ENAMEL_CLASH` -5; gold, rose gold and gunmetal stay off against a silver watch and buckle (the owner: "keep gold off"); no clip on a waistcoat day, as before; on his wardrobe over six months: blue 5, navy 2, black 2, red 1, silver 5, grey 6, gold/rose gold/gunmetal 0, scores unchanged (99.77); `tests/planner-rules.test.js` (realPlanner)

- outfit rules, `outfit()` / `buildPlan()`, `rateOutfit()` (no DOM)
- `PLAN_RULES` 21 (2026-11-16): pocket squares (`pocketScore`): only with a jacket on; never the tie's own colour family and pattern (`psSameTie`); a square whose colours (accents too) pick up a non-white colour of the tie, shirt or jacket `PS_ECHO` 8 (+`PS_ECHO_ACC` 2 for the tie's second/third/accent, −`PS_TONE` 3 in the tie's own colour); white or cream plain/border/woven `PS_WHITE` 6 (the safe default); else `PS_CLASH` −6; planned when ≥ `PS_MIN` 0 (so with a white square owned every jacket day has one; with only clashing squares none); formal day white ±`PS_FORMAL` 8; funeral only a plain white one; rest `rb` and `PS_AGAIN` 4 for yesterday's; a pick or lock is worn whatever its score; `rateOutfit` adds a 'Pocket square' check (5) only when one is worn
- `takeOff`: the square goes with the jacket, or alone (`rec.off.pocket`, Today's −), and is untaken (not worn); `o.off.pocket` only when taken off by itself; `FOLLOW` re-picks it after a jacket, tie or shirt swap; `ADVICE_SLOTS` has 'pocket'
- `tests/dress.test.js` GOLD lines leave the `pocket` slot out (the plan without squares is byte-identical)
- the new-user audit's planner fixes (2026-11-15, Fixer 6; `tests/planner-rules.test.js`):

- the week's locks carry `rec.lockAt`; a lock made while a type in `LOCK_TYPES` (suit, blazer, trousers, shirt, tie, shoes, belt) had fewer than `LOCK_THIN` (3) in rotation is let go once one is added after it (`lockStale`, used by `planSim` and `fixedAhead` through `lockOf`, and by `lockUpcoming`, which relocks it): a new user's first week stayed on his one suit, its blazer day too; old locks (no `lockAt`) and wardrobes with three of each keep theirs

- `beltWait(shoes)`: the belt a pair waits for when belts are owned and none matches it ("black smooth leather belt"); named in the Variety line's "same shoes as yesterday", Insights' never-worn reason and its shoes-two-days-running line (stats.js, dress.js)

- **Planner rules:** whenever a change alters how outfits are chosen (planner.js scoring, groups, rotation), bump `PLAN_RULES` in planner.js
- on the next start a bump releases only the week's locks and the app's own recorded pieces on days after today (`checkPlanRules()` → `replanFuture(true)` in history.js); the owner's swaps, skips and vest choices stay, so there is no reason to skip a bump; Settings' "Re-plan upcoming days" also clears them, on demand

#### How `outfit()` is built (Round B, 2026-11-17, CR-3-13 / CR-3-15; code moved only: plans identical)
- `outfit()` runs six steps in order: `dayPools` (dress code, occasion, rest, picks, skips, lock, wash, each slot's `pool`, `take`), `chooseJacket`, the waistcoat (in `outfit()`), `chooseClothes` (pants, shirt, tie), `chooseLeather` (shoes, belt, watch), `chooseAccessories` (socks, clip, cufflinks, pocket square, coat), then `outfitNotes`
- the steps share the day through the object `dayPools` returns (`c`); each step copies what it needs into locals first (`const {day,suitDay,…}=c`), so the hot loops read locals as before
- the step order is the planner's: each step `take`s its pieces, so rest counts the earlier steps' pieces as worn; don't reorder them
- each real mistake is one test in planner.js, read by the planner, the score and the notes: `patsHard`, `shirtJacketHard`, `tieFlat` / `tieAsShirt` / `tieShirtHard`, `blackOnBlack`, `pantsAsJacket`, `oneColor`, and `shoeSuits` (`shoeScore` above `SHOE_LINE`, -50); `clothesHard` is built from them, and `rateOutfit`'s checks take their `hard` flag from them (their points and words stay in the check)
- `accMetal(c, tie, watch, belt)`: a clip's or cufflinks' metal against the watch and buckle (enamel by the tie), used by `outfit()` and by the tests' fixed plan (pickscore.js `fitRule`, which used `===` and no enamel rule before)
- `occTop(oc, j, season)`: a jacket's fit for an occasion (`formalTop` or `OCC[oc].top`), used by `jscore` and `fixedAhead`
- stats.js `shoesWhyUnused` is the never-worn shoes sentence for `whyUnused` and dress.js `dressWhyUnused`
- measured on the benchmark wardrobes (Chromium, a year planned, median of runs): planning time unchanged within noise; 63 plan-and-score hashes (9 wardrobes x 7 settings) identical before and after

#### Simulation
- every month from two months back is simulated from one shared start so months agree
- the simulation is resumable (`planSim(start).upTo(day)`; `buildPlan(y,m)` runs one from `planStart`)
- `planSim`'s day loop compares day numbers (`di`), not dates: where the clocks go forward at midnight (Cairo, Santiago, Beirut) the month's last day was never planned
- the planner picks only items in rotation (`by()` in `planSim` filters `inRotation`; belt/coat notes in `rateOutfit` too); the what-to-buy analysis runs the same planner
- frozen past days look their pieces up in the full wardrobe (`frozenOutfit`), so a paused item stays in outfits already worn
- a past day whose piece was deleted since is rated without it (`frozenOutfit` sets `o.gone[slot]`; 'Complete outfit' doesn't ask for a gone piece), so deleting an item doesn't fill Insights' trend with real mistakes
- `planSim` keeps on-the-way items with a date in `by()` and gives `outfit()` a per-day pool (`byOn(n)`), so an item joins the plan the day after it arrives
- `planSim` gives every day `o.ctx` = `{prev: the last two workdays' sumUp, prev3, n}`; `prev` stays two days long, since the Variety score reads every entry of it
- a past day marked "Not worn" is not the previous workday for repeats (`prev`/`prev3` skip it, as `last` does)
- `planSim` keeps `prev`/`prev3` on every worn workday, one without a jacket too (taken off, a casual day, jackets off in the dress code; the code review CR-3-04: the next day repeated its tie and shirt): its summary has no jacket group, which `colourRepeat` and `lookRepeat` skip; `remember()` keeps each jacket's own history (`hist`) to days with that jacket
- a day with no jacket of its kind (a blazer day with no blazers) takes the other kind
- a wardrobe with no suit and no blazer is planned as trousers, shirt and tie
- speed: `withColRun` keeps each item's colour family (`pcol`) and colours (`colsOf`) for one run (`planSim`'s `upTo`, `runAnalysis`) in WeakMaps (`colRun`; only while the run lasts, so an edited item is read afresh next time)
- speed: in `outfit()` each trousers', shirt's and tie's own terms are worked out once (`P1`/`S1`/`X1`) and the rotation part only for outfits inside the window, summed in the same order, so plans are identical
- measured (Node, desktop): the 149-item audit wardrobe plans 8 months in about 225 ms (was 423); plan hash identical with and without the speed-ups
- speed: `jacketClean` checks the parts of `clothesHard` that the jacket and one piece decide alone outside its loop (`shirtJacketHard`; the pants' color and shoes), so a wardrobe with no clean outfit no longer tries every pants x shirt x tie (the code review CR-3-06, 12 suits, 40 shirts, 40 ties over 227 workdays: sneakers only 3,577 → 643 ms, dark shirts only 1,708 → 464 ms, normal 596 → 499); plans identical

#### Rest and rotation
- a piece never worn yet (no `last`) has full rest but creeps only from the day it was `added` (`fresh` in `outfit()`; items without `added` as before): the -1e5 "never" counted as 300 days of creep, so a new suit scored 155 against 74 for one rested six weeks and two new brown suits (brown group) beat `JKT_SAME` on consecutive workdays (the owner, 2026-11-16, 22-23 Nov; now 92, the pair split by a navy suit); `PLAN_RULES` 22; `tests/freshrest.test.js`
- rest bonuses keep creeping up past their caps (`over(i,from)`: 0.01 a day in `rb`, 0.05 for shirts and trousers, 0.2 for jackets, up to 300 days), so a piece always a point short of another still comes round (on the owner's wardrobe 4 of 11 trousers and 2 of 4 socks were never planned without it)
- a tie's rest creeps too, capped: `+Math.min(TIE_CREEP_MAX, over(x,30)*TIE_CREEP)` (9, 0.03 a day after 30 days), well under `TIE_GAP`, so a tie still never repeats within three workdays (on the 40-tie audit wardrobe over 12 months: 9 ties never planned before, 0 now; scores unchanged)
- odd trousers rotate on a long rest (`ptRest`, up to 60 days, since blazer days are weekly) and shoes avoid yesterday's pair when another suits
- a jacket pinned to one of the next 3 workdays is held back before its day
- a jacket out of season (`seasonFit` −15: a cold-weather suit on a warm day) is planned only when there is no other (`offSeason`)
- a suit whose only acceptable shoes were worn yesterday (a black suit the day after black shoes) loses 10 at the jacket choice (`shoeBound`), so another suit goes first

#### Repeats
- jackets rotate by rest and avoid the previous workdays' colour group (`jacketGroup`, `colourRepeat`); the same group as the workday before costs `JKT_SAME` (60)
- the same-group jacket cost is scaled by how avoidable a repeat is (`topGw` in planner.js, 2026-11-14, the audit's walkthrough: three grey suits and one brown, brown planned 35 of 94 suit days against 19-20 for each grey, every other suit day): ((other jackets of the day's kind) / (that group's own))³, at most 1, so there a grey after a grey costs about 2 and the four suits rotate evenly (23-24 each; with a suit every workday 31 each of 124, a grey after a grey on 61 days, the fewest even rotation allows); a wardrobe with no group bigger than all the others together keeps the full `JKT_SAME` (the owner's: the same six-month jacket sequence before and after); `PLAN_RULES` not bumped (nothing changes for the owner; a lopsided wardrobe takes it after the week's locks); `tests/jacketcolour.test.js`
- gunmetal jackets are in the grey group (`JKT_GROUP`; the owner's "mid grey worsted" is based on gunmetal), after a dark pinstripe then black, and light then mid grey, on consecutive days (2026-11-14)
- any two very dark jackets running are "dark again" (`darkJkt`: `DARK_JKT` or brightness under `JKT_VDARK` 34 of 255: black 22, dark navy 31; navy 37 and charcoal 62 are not)
- measured on the owner's wardrobe over 124 workdays: jacket-group and tie-colour repeats 1 → 0, scores and jackets worn unchanged; the nine-tie test wardrobe meets 3 tie-colour squeezes in six months (held at 3 in `tests/jacketcolour.test.js`)
- shirts and ties do the same (`shirtRepeat`, `tieRepeat`; white and light blue shirts only mildly; dark shirts count as one look and black/charcoal/grey/silver ties as one, `shirtLook`/`tieLook`)
- a tie colour never repeats on consecutive workdays: `tieRepeat` must outweigh the tie's rest (`tests/jacketcolour.test.js`)
- the same tie is not planned again within three workdays (the owner's choice, 2026-11-13): `tieRepeat` costs `TIE_GAP` (18) for the same tie the day before, two back and three back (`prev3`); tie colours keep their two-day rule; `tests/planner-repeats.test.js`
- the very tie of the workday before costs `TIE_NEXT` (20) more (fix round 2, 2026-11-15): with three ties every tie is within three workdays and rest decided, so the day before's tie came back on 33 of 101 workdays (more after a tie swap); now 1; also the next fixed workday's tie (`aheadTie`); `tests/planner-rules.test.js`
- the same jacket colour group with the same shirt look and tie look as either of the last two workdays is a whole-look repeat (`lookRepeat`)
- each jacket remembers its last two outfits so its tie and shirt colour vary (`comboRepeat`)
- `comboRepeat` counts at most `COMBO_CAP` (12) for a tie that isn't the workday before's (`comboCost` in `outfit()`; the code review CR-3-03): with two suits and two ties it fell on the other tie and outweighed `TIE_NEXT`, so yesterday's tie came back on 41 of 103 workdays (A A B B), now 0; 3 and 4 ties stay at 0; `tests/planner-repeats.test.js`
- the same tie with the same shirt colour group (`shirtGroup`, white and light blue included) on any of the last `PAIR_DAYS` (10) remembered workdays costs `PAIR_GAP` (5), under any jacket (`pairRepeat`, `pairs` in `planSim`, worn days included; the owner, 2026-11-16: a pink shirt and the black-and-pink tie on 15 Nov came back on 24 Nov under another suit, 0.9 points ahead; on his plan to 31 Jan such repeats 6 → 0, average score unchanged); `PLAN_RULES` 23; `tests/pairrepeat.test.js`
- the next fixed day's colour is avoided, and the suit before a blazer day avoids the colour of the blazer that is due (`fixedAhead`)
- `fixedAhead` returns the tie and shirt fixed on each of the next 3 workdays (`fx`: sumUps with `w` 0-2 workdays ahead and the day number `n`; picked, moved there, worn or locked; a day marked "Not worn" left out)
- each fixed tie costs as the last three workdays' do (`aheadTie`): the next workday's as the day before's (`tieRepeat`), two ahead as two back (same tie `TIE_GAP`, colour 2), three ahead as three back (same tie `TIE_GAP`)
- each fixed shirt (`aheadShirt`): `shirtRepeat` as the day before for the next workday, as two back for the one after; its very shirt `NEXT_SHIRT` (40) more on the next workday and on any later one within `settings.laundryDays` calendar days
- the look-ahead was found when an outfit moved from Nov 23 to Nov 24 and Nov 23, planned afresh, took the same black tie and check shirt; measured: a tie swapped with the week locked took a tie locked 2-3 workdays later 35 of 88 times, now 1; `tests/planner-repeats.test.js` (`realPlanner`: the fixed test plan never changes)

#### The look in the plan
- the planner picks the pants, shirt and tie for good looks (PLAN_RULES 26, 2026-11-17, the owner: a planned outfit ranked 457th of 507 on the score card, "picked because the pink tie had rested 25 days"): in `chooseClothes`, among the outfits inside the window (`QWIN` of the best `clothesScore`, unchanged) that repeat nothing (`realRep`), the look counts in two parts (`lookReadCost`; planner.js has its own `lookCost`), each beyond `LOOK_FREE` (3) points; rest and the softer repeats then choose as before
- the look is the score card's rank value, `dialLook` (Harmony + Texture + Occasion), of the jacket, pants, shirt and tie with the shoes, belt and watch `lookLeather` gives them from all the day's pieces in rotation (shared with `rankLooks`, so the planner's look of an outfit is the rank's own: `tests/lookplan.test.js`)
- part 1, each piece's own best (`LOOK_PAIR` 3 per point): an outfit costs what it falls short of the best outfit any one of its pieces (shirt, tie, pants) makes in the window, so whatever rest brings round is worn the best way it can be (the pink tie with the shirt it suits) and no piece is held back for being plainer than another
- part 2, the best rested outfit (`LOOK_K` 2 per point): the bar is the best look among the outfits whose tie, shirt and pants have all rested `LOOK_RESTED` (0.75) of their type's turn (pieces in rotation x 7 / workdays a week; pants on blazer days unless no jackets are worn), so the piece just worn doesn't set it; this part fades for an outfit with a piece rested over its turn, to nothing at two turns (`due`, from `over(i,0)`: a never-worn piece counts from its `added`)
- why two parts: with the whole look's shortfall alone (any weight that moved the rank much) a white woven shirt took 12 of 40 workdays in `tests/rotation-pause.test.js` ("no shirt hogs the rotation"), and the dress-code wardrobe without jackets wore one pair of pants 54 times and another 3; with a shirt-relative term alone the ties hogged instead (17 of 40 never planned on 149 items)
- `realRep`: the same tie within three workdays or its color the workday before (`tieRepeat` 15+), the shirt of the workday before or after (the two before and after with more than three shirts), a tie or shirt fixed ahead (`aheadTie` `TIE_GAP`+, `aheadShirt` `NEXT_SHIRT`+), `pairRepeat`, `lookRepeat` 20+, `comboRepeat` 15+; when every outfit in the window repeats something the window is chosen as before, without the look (the dress test's small wardrobe, three ties: unchanged)
- a formal day, a wedding or a funeral is chosen without the look (the occasion's own rules: a navy tie went to a wedding for its contrast), and a wedding's window is `FORMAL_QWIN` too (a well-rested navy tie still won it within `QWIN`; `tests/occasions.test.js`)
- speed: `planLook(day, sd, S, B, W)` returns the day's look function; `colRun.lw` keeps a `lookLeather` per set of pools and `colRun.look` each outfit's look for the run (key: pools, season, occasion, the four ids); only the window's repeat-free outfits are looked at; 8 months plan in 1.55-1.65x the time (Chromium, interleaved: 149 items 275-295 -> 427-460 ms, 300 items 807-821 -> 1316-1370 ms, realistic 34-37 -> 50-51 ms)
- measured (Chromium; a bench outside the repo planning 6 months from 2 Nov 2026, nothing worn before, a formal day, a wedding and a funeral): the planned outfit's look rank (median of rank / outfits the day allows; share of days in the top tenth) realistic 41% -> 28%, 5% -> 13%; dress full 42% -> 30%, 9% -> 13%; dress full without jackets 40% -> 27%, 9% -> 18%; a 149-item wardrobe 30% -> 17%, 19% -> 32%; the 300-item replay wardrobe 22% -> 13%, 25% -> 44%; agent8's replay wardrobe 34% -> 30%, 21% -> 24%; the awkward and small test wardrobes unchanged (their windows rarely hold an outfit that repeats nothing); 12 months on a wardrobe new on 1 Nov: realistic 45% -> 29%, 149 items 28% -> 14%
- the tail is the jacket: the light grey suit's and the tan blazer's best outfits rank low among all the day's jackets, and rotation still wears them (realistic 90th percentile 79% -> 62%)
- Harmony / Texture / Occasion on the realistic 75.6 / 63.0 / 84.6 -> 78.6 / 68.8 / 84.4; the old score 99.57 -> 99.64 realistic, 99.42 -> 99.48 on 149 items, no hard days anywhere
- repeats over those 6 months: tie within three workdays, the tie the workday before, the look again 0 -> 0 wherever they were 0; a tie with the same shirt color within 10 workdays agent8 6 -> 2 (12 months 28 -> 7), awkward 67 -> 59; a tie color the workday before agent8 2 -> 1, dress full 1 -> 0; more: awkward's same tie within three workdays 9 -> 13 (its windows nearly always repeat something, so the look never touches those days), the same shirt two planned workdays running dress full and its no-jacket variant 0 -> 1 each, and over 12 months on 149 items one tie color and one tie-with-shirt-color repeat, both on the formal day
- rotation: pieces never planned unchanged except the 300-item wardrobe's ties at 6 months (1 -> 4; at 12 months 0 -> 0, its pants 5 -> 4); each piece's longest wait (workdays, median / longest) realistic shirts 11/12 -> 11/19, ties 16/26 -> 19/25, dress without jackets pants 6/7 -> 10/11; wear counts spread wider (realistic shirts 13-18 -> 10-25 wears in 130 workdays)

#### Favorites in the plan
- `settings.fav` (fav.js) is read through `favRead()` (memoized on the object); with no favorites nothing here runs and plans are identical (measured on the 8 bench wardrobes, 6 months: every plan fingerprint unchanged)
- a favorite piece: `rest` and `over` in `dayPools` count its days × `FAV_REST_K` (1.25), so its rest target is a fifth shorter (a tie's 25 days, 20); `since` stays the real days (the wash, yesterday's shoes) and so does the `rest` written on the outfit's pieces (Freshness, the Why); `FAV_TIE` (1) in `argBest` and `chooseClothes`' final order makes it win a tie or a near tie (within a point; .01 left a favorite tie at 0-18% more wears on the bench wardrobes)
- a favorite look due (`planSim`: `favW`, `favN`, `favPick`, `favDay`): after `FAV_LOOK_REST` (15) workdays since it was last worn, picked or planned, at most one favorite look in `FAV_WEEK` (7) days (also looking at picks and locks in the coming 6 days, `favAhead`), only on a workday from today on whose record pins nothing (`favFree`: no ids, lock, pick, skip, kind, trip, piece taken off, occasion or formal day, vest choice, "Not worn"); its jacket of the day's kind, all pieces in rotation, the shirt out of the wash, each piece in season, and no repeat by the usual rules (yesterday's jacket or a jacket held for a day ahead, `colourRepeat` ≥ 30, `tieRepeat` ≥ 15, `aheadTie`, the shirt of the last workdays or fixed ahead, `aheadShirt`, `lookRepeat` ≥ 20); the longest rested due look first
- the day is then planned with the three pinned through `day.fav` (merged into `force` in `dayPools`) and kept only when it is that look and `rateOutfit` finds no real mistake, else the rest counts are put back and the day is planned as usual; the outfit carries `favDue` (not enumerable)
- `pairRepeat` (the same tie with that shirt color within 10 workdays) and `comboRepeat` (the jacket's own last two outfits) don't stop a favorite look: they mark down the very pairing it repeats (with `comboRepeat` the 300-item wardrobe's favorites came back every 45-50 workdays, its jackets' last outfit being the favorite itself), and Harmony's tie-on-shirt part is 1 for a favorite pair unless the tie disappears into the shirt (`favPair` in `dialHarmony`)
- measured (a bench outside the repo, 6 months from 2 Nov 2026, the 8 bench wardrobes, 3 favorite looks taken from the plan and 3 favorite pieces worn about a third as often as the rest, a locked week, a picked day, a formal day, a wedding and a funeral): without favorites every plan's fingerprint is unchanged; the looks came back on their own every 14-37 workdays (most 15-23) on realistic, agent8, dress full, the 149- and 300-item wardrobes, at least 7 days between favorite days the planner chose, none on a locked, picked or occasion day, no real mistake; the small and awkward test wardrobes (2-3 suits) already wear those looks every few workdays, so the planner brought them back once
- favorite pieces alone: dress full without jackets tie +27% shirt +31% pants +24%; dress full tie +7% shirt +17% suit +18%; realistic tie +27% shirt +21% suit 0; agent8 tie -5% shirt +28% suit 0; 149 items tie +50% shirt +67% suit +29% (a suit among four alternating by color group barely moves); look rank, score and pieces never planned unchanged or better; repeats unchanged on ordinary workdays (the new ones: the funeral's black tie two days after a favorite with it, a lock from before, and the awkward wardrobe's same tie within three workdays 13 -> 16-17, whose windows nearly always repeat something)

#### Colours and harmony
- steel blue is a mid-tone shirt, not a `darkShirt` (cutoff 95)
- `HARM`: black, charcoal, grey and silver ties are classic with navy, charcoal and grey jackets (charcoal and grey with brown, charcoal with tan)
- `BLAZER_TROUSERS` has mid blue under tan, brown and grey; black trousers lose 20 only where the jacket's own list lacks them (they are listed under grey)
- `settings.darkShirts` (`darkMode()`: 'some' default, 'often', 'never'; Settings → Schedule → Dark shirts, `setDarkShirts` in settings.js): 'often' scores a dark shirt under a light jacket like a white one and also allows a mid-tone jacket (L ≥ 80) at least 40 lighter than the shirt; 'never' plans one only when there is nothing else
- socks may pick up the shirt's or the tie's colour (`sockEcho`: their colour groups, neutrals left out; +4 in `sockScore`, behind the trousers' colour)

#### Trousers and shoes
- trousers by the day (`botFits` in `outfit()`): a formal day, a wedding or a funeral takes dress trousers; a blazer day takes dress trousers or chinos, and jeans only when `settings.dress.jeans` (Settings → "Jeans with a blazer", off by default, shown while jackets are worn); a day without a jacket takes any kind
- shoes by their style (`SHOE_FIT` in `shoeScore`, by `shoeCtx`: suit, dress trousers, chinos or jeans): an oxford goes with anything; a derby −4 and a loafer −8 with a suit; boots −60 with a suit, −20 with dress trousers; sneakers −60 with either
- combat boots: `shoeScore` gives `COMBAT_FIT` (6) with camouflage pants and under `SHOE_LINE` with anything else (the score's Shoes check says "combat boots go with camouflage pants"); worn only when picked (`hpShoes` in `dayPools`, used by `chooseLeather` alone, so the clothes are still chosen against the planner's own shoes); no belt match needed (`beltFit` 0, like sneakers); plans without them are unchanged
- Today's shoes picker offers combat boots ("Casual · pick by hand") only while the day's pants are camouflage (`handPool`), else says "Combat boots go with camouflage pants: pick those first." (`combatAway`); other pants or a jacket picked while they are worn take them off (`bootsOff` in pickscore.js, used by `pickItem`/`pickRec`; `handPool` is in pickscore.js too); `tests/combatboot.test.js`
- a swap keeps the hand-picked pieces in the slots that follow it (`keepHand` in pickscore.js, before `bootsOff`; 2026-11-18, the owner's coyote canvas belt on a camo day was refused: the belt swap freed the shoes, the planner never chooses combat boots, took black calf shoes that suit neither the camo pants nor the canvas belt, and dropped the belt, so `beltless` refused it)
- a pick from the picker on a day marked worn (`o.frozen`) is recorded as what was worn (`correctPast(slot, it, true)`, "Corrected."); it did nothing, silently, unless "Correct what you wore" was on; `tests/planner-rules.test.js`
- the tagger is offered the style 'combat' and the colour coyote only when the request names 'combat' (core.js `TAG_TYPES`; worker/tag.js `OPT_FLAGS`, `knowsCombat`, `CB_COLS`, `COMBAT_LINE`), so an older app's request is as before
- sneakers come only with chinos or jeans, where a white or neutral pair suits any colour (`SNEAKER_COLS`) and needs no matching belt (`beltFit` 0); `tests/casual.test.js`, `tests/planner-pairs.test.js` (figures from the owner's backup over six months of planning, not kept in the repo)
- with a suit, formality wins (the owner's decision, fix round 3, 2026-11-16, W2-17-02): suede costs `SUIT_SUEDE` (30, was 15) and the watch's strap and metal count `SHOE_WATCH` (half, every day) in the shoe choice; a brown strap and a gold buckle had put suede loafers with a suit on 82 of 101 suit days, now 12 on tester 17's wardrobe (black 48, oxblood 48, suede 27 of 125 workdays; it was 19 / 0 / 106)
- yesterday's pair costs `SHOE_AGAIN` (25, was 7) when another pair suits: more than the halved strap and metal swing (20), less than no matching belt (30); one pair was worn two days running 87 times in 125 workdays (`tests/planner-rules.test.js`)

#### Occasions and dress code
- `occOf(rec)`: `formal` stored as `rec.formal`, `wedding` / `funeral` / `casual` as `rec.occasion`; rules in `OCC` and `formalTop`/`formalShirt`/`formalTie`
- an occasion's suit outweighs rotation and the one clearly right suit is held back on the days before
- `casual` = no jacket, tie, clip or vest: `o.top` is null and the shirt is judged against the trousers
- a formal day takes the white shirt: `formalShirt(s, k)` counts the colour and pattern `FORMAL_SHIRT_K` (3) times in `clothesScore` and in the shirt shortlist (French cuffs once), and a formal day or a funeral picks among outfits within `FORMAL_QWIN` (3) of the best, not `QWIN` (8), so a French-cuff light blue shirt no longer outscores white
- a wedding picks within `FORMAL_QWIN` too (PLAN_RULES 26, with the look in the plan)
- `outfit()` follows the dress code: without jackets every workday is planned like `casual` (`noJ`, `day.nojkt` for `clothesScore`), with a tie when ties are worn; without ties (`noT`) no tie and so no clip is picked, and a pinned or locked tie is ignored
- a formal day, a wedding or a funeral is still dressed in full from what is owned (`full`), whatever the dress code; a day marked "No jacket or tie" has neither, also for someone who wears ties
- measured on the test wardrobe (9 jackets, 5 trousers, 8 shirts, 9 ties): each of the four dress-code combinations plans complete outfits scoring 96+ with no hard check

#### Laundry
- `settings.laundryDays` (default 1, 0 = off; Settings → Care): in `outfit()` a shirt worn within that many days is in the wash and left out (`washing`, `washed` in `pool()`), unless there is no other shirt; a forced shirt stays
- on a formal day, a wedding or a funeral the occasion's shirt outweighs the washing (`tests/weather.test.js`)
- a lock whose shirt was worn within those days is dropped, when another shirt is free

#### Locks, taking off, waistcoats
- `rec.lock` (the pieces the app showed for one of the coming days): `pool()` returns the locked piece for a slot unless the day forces another or skips it; a locked jacket sets the day's kind and counts as fixed in `fixedAhead`
- the whole lock is dropped when one of its pieces is not in the day's pool (deleted, out of rotation, the other jacket kind) or the forecast has turned against it (suede shoes on a rainy day, a piece out of season)
- a kept trip's lock (`rec.trip`) is never dropped that way (the code review CR-3-01): a piece gone lets go of its own slot only (shoes take their belt and socks), rain against packed suede or a packed piece out of season keeps it and the day's note says so ("Rain is forecast and the packed brown loafers are suede…", `tripOff`); `tests/planreview.test.js`
- rain wins over a repeat, not over a real shoe mistake (the owner, 2026-11-17 and 2026-11-24): suede (and nubuck) is never planned on a rainy day while another pair that suits is left, even yesterday's pair again; when only the suede suits (black calf under a brown suit, or patent the other pair) the suede is worn, with the rain note (`RAIN_SUEDE` 60 in the shoe choice: more than `SHOE_AGAIN` 25, less than `NO_SHOES` or patent's -200; it was 25, then 1000, which put black calf under a brown suit and patent in the rain: the code review R3 N1); a pick or a kept trip's suede stays
- on a rainy day `shoeOk` (dayPools) leaves suede out while the day has a smooth (not suede, not patent) pair, so the jacket and clothes choice steer to a jacket and pants a smooth pair suits (a navy suit with black calf, not the brown suit with suede); dry days plan byte for byte as before (the bench wardrobes, 65 workdays dry and rainy: 0 hard shoe days, and suede in the rain only where nothing else suits); `tests/planreview.test.js`
- the rain note says why suede is worn: picked for the day, packed, or "suede shoes are the only ones you have for today"; `tests/planreview.test.js`
- the notes about the jacket are kept apart when made (`jnote`, `o.jn`, not enumerable) and `takeOff` drops only those with the jacket (CR-3-07: a word filter also dropped "None of your shoes suit brown pants.")
- taking a piece off for the day (the owner's ask, 2026-11-13): `rec.off = {top, tie}`; `outfit()` plans as usual and `takeOff(o, off, untake)` removes the piece at the end
- `takeOff`: the jacket takes its waistcoat with it, a suit's trousers stay (`o.bot` = the suit, `o.suitDay` false, `o.suitBot` true), a blazer, tie or clip taken off is not counted as worn (`untake` restores `last`); sets `o.off`, `o.offKind`; `frozenOutfit` sets them from `rec.off`
- with a suit's jacket off its pants have ⇄ (the owner, 2026-11-16: "my pants are locked and I can't swap them out"): other pants are a pick (`force.bot`, kept like any pick); `outfit()` hands them to `takeOff` (`sep`), which wears them and untakes the suit, so the suit is neither worn nor counted (rest, care, Insights: the record's ids) while the new pants are; `o.offSuit` is the suit, and the pants picker offers its own pants first ("The suit’s own pants"; choosing them drops the pick, `suitPants`); Suggest another pins the best other pants (`swapSlot`); the jacket back on is the whole suit again (`setOff` drops `force.bot`); a frozen day says "Suit" taken off from `rec.kind`; `tests/takeoff.test.js`
- with other pants worn on a suit day (`sep`) the day's notes are made again for the outfit worn (`sepNotes`: `outfitNotes` with no jacket, those pants and the day's shirt, tie, shoes and belt; a packed suit's own note left out), so none speaks of the suit's trousers (the owner, 2026-11-17: "None of your shoes suit mid gray worsted pants." under his multicam pants); the pieces chosen are unchanged; `tests/takeoff.test.js` (`sepnotes`)
- the metal note names the metals as people say them (`metalWord`: "other" is gunmetal): "The silver watch and the gunmetal belt buckle are different metals." (was "The watch (silver) and belt buckle (other) …", 2026-11-17); analysis.js still finds it by "different metals"
- `needsJacket`/`needsTie` (core.js) ask for neither on a day with a piece off, so no 'Complete outfit' penalty; `scoreAdvice` never offers to swap a suit's trousers
- no waistcoat without a tie (the owner, 2026-11-13: "No one should ever wear a vest and no tie"): `outfit()` sets `vest` only with a tie, whatever `vestMode` or `rec.vest`; `takeOff` drops the waistcoat with the tie; past days keep what was recorded
- Today's vest row (`vestRow`, today.js) is hidden on a day with no tie, so its + can't add one; a jacket worn without a tie is always the `suit-open` master, never `vest-open`; `tests/takeoff.test.js`

#### No learning from changes
- the planner does not learn from swaps, picks or corrections (removed 2026-11-15 at the owner's ask: "I don't want to gum it up"); it never reads `settings.learn`, and a plan is exactly what it was with no votes
