- the votes earlier versions kept (`settings.learn`) are deleted once per start, after the first pull (`dropLearn`, history.js; `whenPulled` in start.js): the removal is stamped like any setting change (`kh` '-'), so it wins the per-setting merge on every device and in the worker; a phone on an older version may add votes again, which nothing reads, and the next start drops them; `tests/stats.test.js`
- don't bring it back without the owner's ask

#### Score (`rateOutfit`)

- the uniform blouse (2026-11-17): its Pants check is "goes with camouflage pants" (`camoPants`; a real mistake otherwise) and its Shirt check "goes over a T-shirt"; the harmony dial counts the T-shirt, blouse and camouflage pants as one uniform (no "sits close to the jacket", no jacket-and-pants pair), the occasion dial takes boots as the uniform's shoes, and the planner's notes skip "darker than the jacket" under it; the picker lists it last with "Goes with camouflage pants" when the day's pants aren't (history.js `handOk`); no PLAN_RULES bump (it is never planned)
- the score dials (2026-11-17; the planner reads only the look, Harmony + Texture + Occasion, through `planLook` since PLAN_RULES 26: "The look in the plan"): `dialHarmony` (shirt against jacket by lightness gap and `shirtScore`, tie against shirt by contrast and `TIE_ON_SHIRT`, tie against jacket by `HARM`, contrast and an accent echo, pants `pairScore`, pocket square `psEcho`, socks `sockScore`, metals, how many colors), `dialTexture` (surfaces and pattern scales), `dialFreshness` (each piece's `rest` over its type's cycle), `dialVariety` (the latest PAIR_DAYS workdays) and `dialOccasion` (shoe finish and style, shoe color for the pants, belt, tie and jacket formality or the occasion's own rules, the season); each returns `{v, why}`
- the sixth dial, Interest (`dialInterest`, 2026-11-18, the owner: the rank's #1 was "the most basic possible suit"; his pick from the mockups in `C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\interest\`): a color echo (30: a tie color within `INT_ECHO` 12/18 ΔE00 and `INT_ECHO_L` 20 lightness of the jacket's, its second or the shirt's color), two patterns that work together (25), a jacket with character (20: not navy, charcoal, gray or black; a patterned basic .7), a shirt beyond plain white (15), shoes that aren't black (10); no stretch; `DIAL_KEYS` has six (the card's row is six columns, the ring and names shrink to fit 320 px)
- `dialLook` (the rank and the planner's look term) = Harmony + Texture + Occasion + `INT_W` (.5) x Interest x `dLin(Harmony, INT_GATE 65, 80)`: Interest counts only as Harmony allows; tuned on the owner's Nov 22: the gray-white-black look fell from 1st to 26th, the "Navy" (ink aubergine) pinstripe with light blue and a burgundy repp tie rose from 12th to 1st, the top 5's lowest Harmony 79; `tests/scoredials.test.js`
- Freshness counts a piece as fully fresh after `FRESH_FULL` calendar days of rest (14; shoes 7) or its type's own turn (`dialCycle`) if that is shorter (2026-11-17, the owner: with 35 shirts the turn was 49 days and a shirt worn 12 days ago read a quarter fresh, Freshness 70 on a fresh look); `tests/scoredials.test.js`
- Occasion on an ordinary suit day (no formal day or occasion): any suit suits the day (at least .85, a dark one up to 1 by `formalTop`); only camouflage (the uniform) scores 0; the strict dark-suit scale (`formalTop`) is for formal days and the occasions (2026-11-17, the owner: "A warm taupe suit isn't formal as a suit?"); `tests/scoredials.test.js`
- a dial's parts are 0-1 values with weights (`dialSum`); its words are made only for the card (`fast`/`core` skip them in a rank run); the raw share is stretched from the top by `DIAL_K` (Harmony 2, Occasion 1.8, Freshness 1.4) so a planned month on the test wardrobes spreads about 40-100 (realistic: Harmony 38-97, Texture 42-100, Occasion 57-100)
- Texture: `surfOf` (0 smooth to 1: a knit or grenadine tie, tweed; the weave pattern `SURF_PAT` or the material `SURF_MAT`/`SURF_TIE`, whichever is rougher; no material counts as smooth) gives 42 + 40 Ã— the spread + 20 Ã— the roughest, plus the patterns (one +18, two kinds +16/+24, one kind at two scales +6, one kind at one scale -30, three -40)
- `rankLooks(pools, cur)` ranks the day's outfits on the look only (`dialLook` = Harmony + Texture + Occasion, without pocket square, socks and clip): jackets of the day's kind Ã— pants (the suit's own) Ã— shirts Ã— ties, shoes, belt and watch per jacket and pants as `chooseLeather` does without rest; only combinations breaking no rule count (`clothesHard`, shoes that suit, a matching belt, no patent, the season); measured: 36,750 outfits in 194 ms at 4x CPU, the realistic wardrobe's 507-957 in 2-6 ms
- socks in the dials (2026-11-17, with the sock patterns): a bold pair (`sockBold`: argyle, stripes, dots, other) is one more pattern for Texture, the quiet knits (ribbed, birdseye, nailhead, herringbone, clock) count as plain; a patterned pair's second or third color picking up the shirt, tie or jacket lifts Harmony's socks part; light socks with dark pants (the Socks rule) ring Harmony
- `rankLooks` also returns up to 3 swaps (one per slot: shirt, tie, pants, shoes; belt and socks only to mend a broken rule) that rank higher, or with a broken rule those that mend it (`fix`)
- `rateOutfit` returns `{score, label, checks:[{label, pts, max, why, hard}], raw, hard:[labels]}`
- the score takes points only for things that can be put right, not for taste: every quiet shirt colour scores 10 (`QUIET_SHIRTS`), a dark shirt under a light jacket too, any listed shoe or trouser colour 10, socks in the shoes' colour 5
- a real mistake in the clothes is not planned while another choice avoids it (H7, fix round 2): `clothesHard` (planner.js, built from the same rule tests as `rateOutfit`'s hard checks) is the hard checks the jacket, trousers, shirt and tie decide, plus trousers with no suiting shoes (`shoeOk`); the jacket rotation picks takes its turn only when the day's trousers, shirts and ties make a clean outfit with it (`jacketClean`, stopping at the first), and when the clothes window's best is hard the best clean combination is taken however far down; `tests/planner-rules.test.js`
- black shoes under a brown, tan or olive jacket, or other shoes with a black suit (`shoeClash`), cost `SHOE_CLASH` (60, was 50) in `shoeScore`, so they always reach the -50 line of the score's Shoes check (hard), `shoeOk`, `clothesHard` and `NO_SHOES` (the code review CR-3-08: the trousers' own points left them at -44 to -48, full marks in the score); the Shoes reason names the jacket's clash first ("a black suit needs black shoes", "black shoes don't suit a tan jacket"), then the pants', then the style ("boots don't go with a suit")
- a casual top picked by hand is judged only by "the same color as the jacket" (`shirtJacketHard`, rateOutfit's Shirt check; the code review CR-3-09: a navy sweater under a charcoal blazer scored 69)
- `tests/planreview.test.js` (realPlanner) holds the code review's planner findings, with a hand-written verdict table for shoes against the jacket rather than the planner's own functions
- in the shoe choice, shoes that don't suit the trousers (`shoeScore` ≤ -50) cost `NO_SHOES` more, so they lose to a pair with no matching belt (30, not a mistake): khaki chinos with black calf shoes at 69 while oxblood loafers suited them
- a check marked `hard` is a real mistake and caps the score at `HARD_CAP` (69): shoes that don't suit, three bold patterns or two of one type and scale, trousers the odd jacket's colour, a belt that doesn't match the shoes, a dark shirt under a dark jacket, shirt the jacket's colour, a black tie on a black suit, light socks with dark trousers, one colour head to toe, patent shoes, a piece out of season
- a missing piece (shirt, trousers, shoes; jacket or tie only when `needsJacket(o)` / `needsTie(o)`) adds the hard check 'Complete outfit' and the label 'Incomplete'
- 'Shirt and trousers' is checked on every day without a jacket that needs none; the Metals reason leaves the tie clip out when ties are not worn
- 'Variety' from `o.ctx`: the same look −3, the same tie −2 or tie colour as the workday before −1, the same tie as three workdays ago −1, the same shirt −2, yesterday's shoes −1
- 'Watch strap': a leather strap against the shoes' leather group; 'Weather' whenever the climate is known: a piece a season off −1, out of season hard
- `scoreAdvice(o, pool)`: every single swap of shirt, tie, odd trousers, shoes (with their best belt), belt, socks, watch or clip is scored; `fixes` = the best swap per slot that raises the score (up to 3), `best` = the score reached by taking the best swap again and again, `stuck` = the checks no swap improves
- measured on the owner's wardrobe: planned days 96–100 (80 of 114 at 100) with no hard check, random outfits 79% with a hard check; `tests/scorefix.test.js`
- with a suit: suede scores 0 of 5 on 'Leather formality' (was 2) and a strap that doesn't match the shoes 4 of 5 (2 on other days), so the score card no longer offers suede loafers as an improvement on black oxfords (fix round 3)
- the reasons speak a piece's own colour (`spokenCol` = `primaryCol`, an owner's colour by its name through `prettyCols`), never the colour it behaves like (`planCol`): a "Dusty pink" suit was a "tan jacket" (W2-17-04); the rules themselves still read `planCol`
- the Metals reason names only the pieces worn with a metal (`metalsWhy`: "watch and buckle metals differ" on a vest day, W2-17-10)
- 'Belt' is left out when no belt in rotation matches the day's shoes (as with no belt at all): a gray belt that suited none of the black shoes took 15 points off every day (W2-14-08); the planner's note still names the belt that would match
- the funeral note says "No plain black, charcoal or navy suit…: the plainest dark one was chosen" when the suit chosen is dark but patterned (a charcoal glen check), not that there is no dark suit (W2-17-09)

### `analysis.js`

- analysis-worker.js is a module worker since 2026-11-24 (`{type: 'module'}`; index.html's notes): it imports merge.js, core.js, planner.js and analysis.js and reads their names through their bridges
- analysis-worker.js also ranks the score card's day (a message with `rank`: the pools' items, the outfit's ids, the settings and the day; answers `{rank: id, res}` from `rankLooks`); scorecard.js keeps that worker alive, What to buy starts its own
- "what to buy" analysis (no DOM; runs in `analysis-worker.js`, a Web Worker): `runAnalysis(list, T, weeks, progress, onCat)` → `{v:2, base, cats:[{key, title, verdict, own, notes, recs}], top, occasions}`
- `horizonKeys(T,weeks)`: the workdays from tomorrow to `weeks`×7 days ahead (today is left out, as on Insights, which counts the same days: stats.js `plannedDays`)
- `evalPlan(list,T,weeks)` plans the next `SHOP_WEEKS` (26) with the real `dayRecs` (history, occasions) and measures it: score, problem notes, jacket rest, colour/tie/look repeats, shoes worn two days running, first-choice shoe days, pieces never planned, points lost per check
- `shopList` counts pieces on the way and pieces away being cleaned (`item.away`) as owned; pieces out of rotation don't count in the plan
- but an idea matching a piece out of rotation (its `shopKey`: type, colour group, pattern class) is never suggested (`shopShelved`, added to the skip set with `settings.shopSkip`; 2026-11-18, the owner: "If I have the purple tie, just not in rotation it shouldn't recommend me a purple tie"); `tests/shop.test.js`
- suits, blazers, trousers, shirts and ties: candidates are every colour in `SHOP_COLS` × pattern class (`clothesCandidates`), ranked without simulating (`rankClothes`)
- ranking: fit = how many owned jackets/shirts/ties make an outfit within `QWIN` of that jacket's best (`compatBase`/`compat`), gap = a colour group (`shopGroup`) or pattern class (`shopClass`) not owned, staples first (`STAPLES`), a bonus for partners that are never planned
- ranking by simulation alone is noise in a full wardrobe (the user's got "another brown suit" first)
- the best of each category (one per colour group and class, two per colour) are then simulated for the facts (`simFacts`): days planned, the score of its outfits, two sample outfits, problem days and short-rest days fixed; an idea planned under twice or scoring 1.5 below the usual is dropped
- solid and the fine weaves are one class for jackets (the planner treats them alike): a plain suit idea names the fine textures not owned yet (`shopDetail`)
- shoes, belts, socks, tie clip, cufflinks and coats (`smallCandidates`) are each simulated and ranked by their direct effect; shoes whose owner's pair only lacks a belt get the belt as the idea; `shoeNotes` says what the plan leans on
- a patterned pair of socks (2026-11-17, the owner: "yes, when it fits"; `sockIdea`): with chinos or jeans in rotation, ties, and no bold pair owned, an argyle and a striped pair on the casual pants' colour (its own when socks come in it) picking up the two colour families the ties have most; kept when planned on at least max(2, 3%) of the workdays and costing no points; always tier 'variety' (an idea, never a fix), ranked by days planned; `shopClass` of socks is plain, argyle, stripe or motif
- tiers: `fix` (problem days or 2+ short-rest days gone), `better` (a check no longer marked down), `gap` (a staple), `variety`, `optional` (everything in a category that is full: 2+ pieces never planned, or each planned less than once in five weeks); category verdict needed / worth / covered
- two fixes for the same days are not both top picks: the best is taken, the rest re-checked as if it were bought (same section: kept as alternatives with `dup`; coats, belts and cufflinks: dropped)
- `occasionCheck` dresses one formal day, wedding, funeral and no-jacket day and lists what is missing
- `settings.shopSkip` (keys `type|colour group|pattern class`, synced) are left out of every check
- the check follows the dress code: `compatBase` leaves out the types it excludes, the Ties (or Suits and Blazers) sections are skipped, no tie clip is suggested, reasons don't count ties, and excluded pieces are not listed as never planned
- without ties a shirt idea is ranked by the jackets it works with, a suit or blazer idea by the shirts
- shoes, belts, socks and coats are checked against the real plan whatever the dress code
- without jackets `compatBase` has one stand-in jacket, `NOJ` (`{id:'nojacket'}`), with every pair of trousers, scored like the planner's no-jacket day (`clothesScore(null,…)` with `day.nojkt`); `NOJ` is never named or counted as an idle piece
- without jackets shirt and tie ideas are ranked by the trousers (and shirts or ties) they suit, trouser ideas by the shirts (and ties); trouser candidates are made for every colour (`clothesCandidates`)
- `tests/dress.test.js` checks the ideas without jackets, with and without ties
- what is owned (`ownMix`) shows an owner's colour by its own name and swatch, not as the group of the colour it behaves like ("tan / khaki 1" for a Dusty pink suit, W2-17-04)
- `occasionCheck` (fix round 3, W2-17-09): the metals point once (the Metals check's reason is left out when the planner's note says it), and "Ready" only when the occasion's own check (Formality, Wedding, Funeral) is full ("Ready · 95" next to "Smooth leather shoes suit a wedding")
- a shoe idea says "A second … pair lets each rest between wears" only when the owned pairs of that colour are planned on a quarter of the days or more; else how often they are planned and how often the new pair would be (it was said of an oxblood pair planned once in 125 days)

### `shop.js`

- the "What to buy" button on the Wardrobe tab (`shopButton`, `#shop-btn`) and its sheet (`#shop-sheet`/`#shop-backdrop`, `openShop`/`closeShop`/`renderShop`): slides up from the bottom over the whole page like the piece picker; Escape, the backdrop, × and changing tab close it
- sections: top picks (`shopGo` opens the idea in its section), one per category with what is owned, a verdict and its ideas, occasions, hidden ideas
- an idea is folded to its picture, name and first reason; opened it shows every reason, sample outfits drawn with `figureSvg` from the owner's pieces, "Bought it" and "Not for me"
- "Bought it" (`shopBought`) adds the piece as bought, on the way (`order`+`paused`, optional `arrives`) and checks again; "Not for me" (`shopHide`) adds its key to `settings.shopSkip` and hides it at once
- swiping the sheet down closes it, like the item sheet: its header (`.shop-top-head`, with a grabber) is a handle at any time, its content only while scrolled to the top; it closes past a quarter of its height or on a flick, else springs back (`shopSwipe`; `tests/shopswipe.test.js`)
- the sheet starts 10px below the status bar (`max(12px, var(--sat) + 10px)`, as the item sheet), not at its edge, so its grabber isn't at the edge of iOS's pull-down zone
- `shopSwipe()` installs the swipe listeners the first time the sheet opens (from `openShop`, once: `shopSwipeOn`), and the grabber is its own element (`<i class="shop-grab">` in the header), so a change to either selects only the tests that open the sheet
- the sheet and its backdrop slide and fade away on closing (`visibility` switches off after the transition; not `.on` = no pointer events); the handler only sets an inline transform and lets the CSS transition finish from there
- `renderShop` during a swipe (`shopDrag`) is put off until the finger lifts (`shopStale`): a redraw removes the element the touch started on and its moves stop coming
- the summary says "N colour combinations" and "N days with a warning" (the planner's notes), not "looks" and "deductions", which meant other things on Insights; a suit idea names the shoes its sample outfits wear (analysis.js)
- the result is kept on the device (`meta` key `shop`: `{sig, at, res}`) and shown without checking again while `shopSig` is unchanged and it is under a week old; sections appear as the worker finishes them (`{cat}` messages)
- `shopSig` signs each item by what the planner reads (fix round A, CR-4-12: `shopPlanned`, the payload's `strip` less names, brand, notes, details and stamps, hashed) and the days off by their dates (`offDays()`, not the holiday list's monthly `fetched` stamp): a new photo, a rename or the monthly holiday fetch made a large wardrobe check again for 40-60 s; the new signature made every kept result stale once; `tests/shopstay.test.js`
- `shopSig` holds the wardrobe and every setting the planner reads: laundry days, dark shirts (`darkMode()`), jeans with a blazer, holidays, days off and the occasions marked from today on (else a change left the check up to a week stale); `|nojacket` / `|notie` stay last
- the progress line says about how long is left once three checks are done and over 8 s remain (`shopLeft`, from `shopBusy.t0`; W2-18-03: a 300-item wardrobe waited 40-60 s with only "5 of 20"); the check itself is no faster
- its controls use data-act (act.js; C2-8, 2026-11-22), registered as shop.js's last statement: `shopOpenTap` (Insights' card), `shopCloseTap` (×), `shopAgainTap`, `shopGoTap` (a top pick), `shopRecTap` (an idea's head), `shopFormTap` (Bought it), `shopCancelTap`, `shopBoughtTap` (Add as on the way), `shopHideTap` (Not for me); the idea's key is in `data-rec` (the idea's box keeps `data-key`); "Show again" keeps its `data-unhide` listener on the sheet

### `detect.js`

- stripe/check detection from photos (canvas)
- `usableSett(i)`: the item's `sett`, unless it is a band reading of a `MOTIF_PATS` pattern (an older reading); a weave pattern (`texPat`) takes a texture or a figure (motif) reading; drawing and `patSummary` use it
- `usableSett(i)` = `ownSett(i)` (the rules above) or, without one, the item's own pattern's look (`lookOf`, `lookSett`, 2026-11-15): on any kind of item, in the item's own colours (main = `colOf`, second/third = its colours else a step lighter or darker, lighter/darker = ±`LOOK_STEP` L*); texture → `v:4` with `rel` from `LOOK_REL`, stripes/checks → `v:2` (a check's `main` crossing lines are left out), motif → `v:3`; each carries `look` (a signature) and `tr`; kept per colours and recipe (`lookMemo`); the item's own reading always wins
- a band reading (`v:2`) on a suit, blazer or pants whose widest band is over `SETT_TAG_DE` (12) ΔE00 from `colOf` is drawn in the selected color (`settToTag`, inside `usableSett`; 2026-11-15, the owner's checkered blue pants, "washed out"): that band becomes `colOf`, every other band keeps its luminance ratio to the ground (linear light) and its a*b* offset; shirts, ties, motif and texture readings and readings near the tag are untouched (the same object); kept per reading and color (`settTagMemo`); every drawing and the painter's tile and key follow; `tests/settag.test.js`
- measured on the owner's pants (Heathered navy #27344f, L* 21.8, C 18.5; reading #616870 / #93a1b2): drawn mean before → after, ΔE00 to the tag: item sheet swatch 26 → 3.7 (L* 51 → 27), mannequin 25.3 → 3.0, painter tile 22 → 1.3, Today's swatch 29 → 7.7; his greige windowpane pants (tag #9a958f, reading ground #72645e) 18.3 → 0.3; no other item of his changes
- `settSig(s)`: a reading's signature for photo keys (`comboSig`): band colours, or the motif's grid, ground, figures and colours
- the full sett (`analyzeSett`/`settFromCanvas`): every band of one repeat in its own colour (per-step medians in Lab, merged until neighbours differ by ΔE 5; a 1–2 step band lying between its neighbours is their blurred edge), the direction, the lines crossing the other way (the residual after the main bands, projected; thread colour = 2 × pixel − band), and the scale (`cr`); stored as `item.sett` `{v:2,a,bands:[[hex,w]],cross:{d,bands:[[hex|null,w]],r},cr,tr,src}`
- `analyzeSett` stays on the page (it needs the canvas); `settFrom`'s cache (tagging.js `settMemo`) keeps it from running again for the same pictures
- `period()` takes the shortest whole fraction of a repeat that matches nearly as well (a clean pattern also matches at 2–3 repeats)
- `analyzeSett` finds the directions on a 320px copy and measures on a sharper one (up to 900px) so fine stripes keep their width
- repeats are found in colour (`periodCh` over L*, a*, b*): a tattersall's red and green lines of one lightness stay apart
- crossing lines are looked for after a moving average is taken out (`detrend`: light falloff and folds are not lines) and their repeat is at least 4% of the length (not the weave texture); they are searched and marked in colour too (each step's kind is its strongest change: lighter/darker/bluer/…)
- directions are found from a colour-difference image (each pixel's Lab distance from the cloth's median, so a light-blue line on khaki linen counts even at the same lightness), with no coherence gate; `opt.straight` (every type but ties, from `settFrom`) tries only upright and level, ±6°
- the repeat is searched with and without the profile's slow drift and both candidates are scored on the drift-free profile (`corrAt`); without a plain repeat the drift-free one counts only above 0.75 (a plaid tie with no full repeat in view stays whole)
- the sharp copy is read in 8 strips running along the lines (`stripProfiles`): a draped cloth's lines wave, so one straight projection smears them; the repeat comes from all strips together and each strip is shifted into line (`stripShifts`, `foldBins`)
- sparse lines (windowpane, pinstripe) whose repeat correlates under 0.6 are found one by one (`linePeaks`/`lineModel`: kept only where about half the strips have them, gaps varying under 25%), and every pixel is lined up on its strip's nearest line, so unevenly spaced panes still fold into one repeat; the test case is the user's linen windowpane blazer (`tests/fixtures/blazer-windowpane*.jpg`)
- with a sharp photo and a close-up, the close-up's colours are shifted so its ground matches the photo's (the photo usually has a white wall to set exposure), and the swatch takes the close-up's scale (`cr`), since it sits beside it
- a narrow band (under 20% of the repeat, a pinstripe, a pinline) takes its strongest step's colour (if 5 ΔE or more from the ground) and its width at half contrast; each crossing line keeps its own thread colour (alike ones merged); the bands are measured without the pixels under the scarcer kind of crossing line (a gingham's white stays white)
- speed: `binMedians` sorts typed arrays numerically, `labGrid` works out `rgbLab` inline with its sRGB curve from a table (`LAB_LIN`), the crossing-direction search builds each angle's projection once, `periodCh` runs on typed arrays; readings identical to before over 94 cases, `tests/sett.test.js` 36 s → 17 s
- the old brightness-only stripe reader (`detectQueued` → `item.stripes`) runs at Confirm only for a card with no close-up whose full reading found nothing and wasn't turned down (`q.settReject`); old items keep their `stripes`, and "Read pattern from photo" (`redetect`, itemsheet.js) still uses it for items with no close-up or box

### `srgb.js`

- sRGB <-> linear written once (CR-8-09, 2026-11-17): `srgbLin`/`linSrgb` (0..1), `srgbLin8` (a byte -> linear), `linSrgb8` (linear, clamped -> 0..255); loaded before mannequin.js on the page and first in asm-worker.js; in `ASSETS`
- used by draw.js (`figStripeLift`, `figLin`, `figGam`), mannequin.js (the buttons), asm-watch.js, asm-pocket.js and asm-gl.js (`asmGlTail`); the same arithmetic as the copies they replaced, so drawings, swatches and pictures are bit for bit the same
- the copies inside the painter's code fingerprint went with C3's repaint (2026-11-24): asm-color.js `asmCmBtnCol` is `linSrgb`/`srgbLin`, `asmCmBuild`'s `gam` is `linSrgb8`, assemble.js `ASM_LIN` is built from `srgbLin8` (the same arithmetic, so the same numbers; `asmCmBuild`'s `hexLin` reads the float32 `ASM_LIN` table and stays); today.js, tagging.js, core.js and detect.js keep theirs (other areas)
- its four names are in no picture key (asm-color.js `asmCmCode`) although the fingerprinted painter calls them (the code review R8 N2, 2026-11-24; `ASM_LIN` is keyed by its table's values, the output curve `linSrgb8` and `asmCmBtnCol`'s calls are not): `tests/asmcode.test.js` pins srgb.js's code (`SRGB_PIN`, a hash of its declarations without comments or spaces) and fails on any change with "change it only together with a planned repaint": bump `ASM_CM_PAINTV` or add the four names to `asmCmCode` (either repaints every look on every phone), then update the pin; a comment-only edit keeps the pin
- the photo tagging files use it too (Round B, 2026-11-17): tagging.js's own `sLin`/`linS` went, measure.js, migrate.js and pattern-read.js call `srgbLin8`/`linSrgb8`, the same to the last bit on both engines (`tests/colormath.test.js` compares them over every byte, gained bytes and 200,001 linear values)

### `mannequin.js` and `mq/<kind>.js` (Today's outfit figure)

- Today's figure is a traced mannequin (2026-11-14, the owner's choice from a prototype, `C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\mannequin-proposal\`): one vector torso per colour-coded master, in exactly the painted picture's pose and square frame; it shows until the day's painted picture is ready, and wherever `figureSvg` is drawn (Shop)
- the pocket square on the mannequin (`pocketSvg`, `.fig-pocket`): the square fold's strip where the painter puts it (asm-pocket.js `asmPocketQuad`), its print (draw.js `pocketDef`, the tie's tiles at .45), its border along the top, the lit fold edge, darker into the pocket, a thin shadow on the welt; jacket poses only
- `draw.js` `figureSvg(o, prefix)` picks the pose with `figKind(o)`: `asmCmKind(o)` (asm-color.js); `vest-open` (a waistcoat without a tie, no master exists) is the `vest` mannequin with the tie's place in the shirt
- an outfit with no colour-coded master gets the pose its pieces would have: a jacket `suit`/`vest` with a tie, `suit-open` without; no jacket `shirt-tie` or `shirt-open`; missing trousers are plain dark grey (#4A4E55), a missing belt dark brown (#3A2A22), a missing shirt plain off-white (`FIG_NO_SHIRT`); the earlier drawn figure (figure.js) was removed 2026-11-14 once every pose shipped
- poses: `vest` (waistcoat, jacket and tie), `suit` (jacket and tie, with the tie clip; blazer days too), `suit-open` (jacket, no tie: the master's open collar, throat and buttoned placket; `.fig-open` is on the skin group of an open-collar pose), `shirt-tie` (no jacket, a tie: shirt, trousers, belt and buckle, the hand on the tie), `shirt-open` (no jacket, no tie)
- the shirt masters: the shirt's sleeves (pieces 14, 18/19) are the turned parts in the shirt's cloth (`suitB` is the shirt there); the trousers are two legs split at the fly (the middle of the trousers on each row), each with its own copy of the trousers' cloth (`leg<i>`, base id `pants`, `figFabric(bot,'bot')`, `FIG_SCALE.bot` 1.6) turned the way the solved positions run down that leg (the painter's leg flow, asm-color.js `asmLegsAt`); the belt in its colour (`.fig-belt`), the buckle the master's own metal in 5 tone bands (`.fig-buckle`)
- the shirt's visible buttons are drawn where the painter draws them (`ASM_CM_BTN` in asm-color.js, copied into the tracer as `BTN`, kept only where the master's shirt is): pearl mixed 55% with the shirt's colour (`shirtColor`), a rim, four holes and a soft shadow (`.fig-shirtbtn`)
- a master without a tie numbers a cuff 81 (suit-open's cuffs are pieces 72 and 81): the tracer takes cuffs from pieces 60-89 there, 60-79 with a tie
- `mannequin.js` `outfitMannequinSvg({kind, suit, shirt, tie, bot, beltColor, vest, clip, shirtColor, bg, idPrefix, ariaLabel, fab})` draws it: viewBox 390x390, no bottom fade (the figure reaches the frame like the painted picture)
- each pose's data is `mq/<kind>.js`: `MQ_RAW[kind]` holds it as a JSON string (a string loads far quicker than the same object literal), parsed once by `mqData(kind)`; 65-94 KB raw, 29-40 KB gzip a pose (about 177 KB gzip for all five, the office included); loaded by index.html and in sw.js `ASSETS`, so it works offline and nothing is fetched for it
- `tools/mq-trace.py <kind>` makes it on the PC (numpy, scipy, opencv, Pillow: `C:\code\geo3d\.venv`), read-only on `C:\code\watch-test\colormaster\out-<kind>\` (zone masks, `uv-u.png` solved pieces, `light.png`, the master photo) and the Sapiens normals in `C:\code\watch-test\geo3d\sapiens-2b\<kind>-cc\`; re-run it whenever a master's masks, pieces or maps change, then bump `CACHE`
- outlines: OpenCV contours smoothed along the curve, Douglas-Peucker, Catmull-Rom cubics with sharp corners kept, coordinates in tenths
- a control point never reaches past a third of its own segment (2026-11-14, the owner's "jagged spikes coming out of the neck"): beside the long straight run of the frame's top edge between the shoulders the tangent was 20 units long and the silhouette overshot the corner into thin spikes along the crop, on the jacket poses
- at the frame (the neck at the top, the trousers at the bottom) each mask's last `EDGE` (6) px are its line 6 px inside, and it is traced padded by `PAD` (16) px of repeated border, so an outline leaves the picture square and its corners lie outside it; `tests/mannequin.test.js` checks the top rows of every pose drawn at 2x
- the silhouette, waistcoat, shirt and buttons from the zone masks; sleeves (pieces 14/15, 18/19), lapels (43/44) and collar leaves (45/46) from the solved pieces inside the jacket mask; cuffs from pieces 60-79; the tie's knot and blade from pieces 80/81
- every garment piece is filled from the day's items (`figFabric` patterns); pattern ids `suitB` (body and waistcoat), `suitL`/`suitR` (the lapels, viewer's left and right), `shirt`, `tie`; each other part's copy is `sp<i>`, `cuff<i>`, `tie<i>`, turned to the part's stripe angle (sleeves, cuffs and tie: the direction of constant u in the solved positions; lapels and collar leaves: their long axis)
- a banded stripe jacket (a pinstripe) is drawn at 1.6x `figFabric`'s scale (about the painter's stripe count); checks (a windowpane comes out about 7 panes across, as painted), twills and solids at 1x
- a striped jacket's lines get the painter's lift (`figStripeLift`, `FIG_STRIPE_MIX` .3): where the reading names each band's colour (`patternSpec.across`) the line is taken that share of the way from the ground to it in linear light, as the painted picture's stripes read; the measured line colour alone nearly vanished on a dark cloth
- kept ids and classes: `bladeP` (the blade, drawn with `<use>`), `lapL`/`lapR`, `.fig-vest`, `.fig-clip`, `.fig-watch`; the hands are one traced skin group `.fig-skin` (no `.fig-hand-l/r`); no `<use>` inside `<clipPath>` (the silhouette and skin clips are plain paths)
- the form shading is one small lossless WebP (195 px, 2 viewBox units a pixel, about 7 KB): black where the cloth is in shadow, white where it is lit, from the master's brightness over each zone's lit level times `light.png` R, blurred at 2048; the browser scales it up smoothly, so no SVG blur filter is needed
- the shading takes the figure's share of each pixel (1 - `alpha-background`), so nothing of the mannequin's own covers the wall: outside the figure it is clear (it used to tint the wall light grey)
- behind the figure is the painted pictures' office (the owner, 2026-11-14, so the switch from the mannequin to the painted picture isn't jarring; mockup `C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\mannequin-background\`): `.fig-office`, the pose's `office`, a 200-pixel JPEG (q 82, about 3 KB) scaled up smoothly over the whole frame, in the light and dark themes alike; `outfitMannequinSvg({office:false})` draws the plain `bg` colour instead
- the office is made by `mq-trace.py` from the painter's background (asm-color.js `asmCmBgKey`; on the PC `C:\code\watch-test\colormaster\bg\office-plate-desk-extended.png`, or `--bg=<file>`): when that background changes, re-run every pose and bump `CACHE`
- the pose's soft wall shadow is baked into its office, as the painter makes it (asmCmPrep `bgShadow`): everything but the wall, moved 34/20 px down-right at 2048, three box blurs of radius 50, the office multiplied by 1 - `ASM_CM_BG_SHADOW` (.22) x shadow in linear light; `wallShadow` in the data is its deepest share (.22); one picture to draw, not a shared office plus a shadow layer (that measured twice the extra draw time)
- speed with the office (2026-11-14, the same method): 56 ms against 45 ms without, about a quarter more (a whole-frame picture to scale); the poses are 2-3 KB gzip larger each (177 KB for all five, was 167)
- lit edges from `light.png` G and the normals, the collar shadow from `light.png` B, seams and creases ("ink") from a difference of Gaussians on the master photo; the hand's cast shadow from the skin and watch masks
- the hands are photo-like (the owner's choice): the master's skin in 9 tone bands, the inner ones softened by a 0.6 blur clipped to the skin (only the hands' own box is filtered)
- the watch is always the master's steel watch in 7 tone bands (the owner's rule, as the painted picture), whatever the item's metal, and with no watch item too (the wrist under it is not traced)
- the tie clip sits where the painter puts it (assemble.js `asmBarDraw`, measured by the tracer on the tie mask: 60% down the visible tie, clipped on from its left edge, 0.8 of its width), in the clip's colour; never on a waistcoat day
- `FIG_HOTSPOTS`, `FIG_VIEWBOX` and `mqHas`, read by nothing, were removed (CR-8-08, 2026-11-17); the tracer still writes each pose's `hot` centre points into mq/<kind>.js
- the shirt buttons' colour is the painter's own `asmCmBtnCol` (CR-8-04, 2026-11-17; `btnCol`): the hand copy read the pearl's linear values as display values and drew the buttons up to 12 levels darker; without asm-color.js loaded the same rule with the pearl in display values (239, 236, 231)
- speed (2026-11-14, Chromium CPU, 4x throttle, 390 css px at DPR 3, decode and draw on a 1170-pixel canvas, median of 7): the earlier figure 107-124 ms, the prototype mannequin 152 ms, the shipped mannequin 95-101 ms; the prototype's blur on its five vector shading layers was the extra, the shading picture replaced it
- `tests/mannequin.test.js`: each pose renders with the items' fabrics, the steel watch, the clip rule, the trousers' legs, the open collar's buttons, no `<use>` in clip paths and its size; a day with no master gets the nearest pose; a speed budget (150 ms on the test machine's canvas, where the mannequin takes about 24 ms); `KINDS` there lists the poses
- the painter's own cloth on the mannequin (2026-11-15, the owner approved the mockups in `C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\mannequin-closeup\` and `fix-2\`): draw.js `figCloth` picks a jacket's (zone 'jacket') or, on the shirt masters, trousers' ('trousers') fill by `figWay`: drawn, lift, block, hb or wt
- `figWay`: route bands or tile, a band/motif/figure reading, or a stripe or check pattern → drawn exactly as before (byte-identical SVG; shirts and ties are never touched)
- `figWay`: the item's own close-up (a `data:`/`blob:` URL, never a link) and `asmCuWants` → `block`, the painter's `asmCuTile`; a herringbone (`asmSuitWeave`) → `hb` (H2)
- `figWay`: darker than `FIG_DARK` (.035, the painter's `ASM_CM_LOOK.dark`) and `asmWtWants` without `asmCuWants` (his Black: close-up turned down by `asmCuDark`) → `wt` (B2, `asmWorstedTile`, ribs unchanged); other dark non-striped cloth → `lift` (the drawn tile's colours times k in linear light, `figLiftHex`); a lighter cloth without a close-up stays drawn
- the dark-cloth lift: the tile's mean held at k x the tagged colour (`figLiftK`, `FIG_LIFT`: luminance .008 → 2.6, .023 → 1.5, .030 → 1.35, linear between, 1 from .035; calibrated on the painted Black, Grey Herringbone and Darkest Brown jackets); striped cloth gets none; applies to block, hb, wt and lift alike
- H2 (`figHbTile`, `FIG_HB`): a clean herringbone of 24 columns x 48 ribs a tile, rib swing .8, the close-up's yarn (luminance only) at .35 with its slubs damped to .3 (wrapping blur R 12), mean k x tagged (1.5 on the Grey Herringbone); the raw x3 block read streaky, almost denim, under the mannequin's flat shading
- the block's repeat is the painter's: `T.mm` x `ASM_PX_MM` x the master's `pxmm` x 390/2048 viewBox units (73 units for a close-up tile, 14.6 for the worsted); `FIG_SCALE` and the pinstripe `fab` multiplier don't apply to it
- its pixels come from the figure's real width (`figScale`: `#stage`'s css width, at most 688, the Duo upright, over 390, times the DPR up to 3, in quarters), capped at the tile's 256 texels; one JPEG data URL (q .9) per block (`figTileUrl`, wrapping area average)
- in the SVG one base `<pattern id="…cuT">` (trousers `…cuB`) holds the `<image>`; every piece's pattern (`suitB`, `suitL/R`, `sp<i>`, waistcoat; `pants`, `leg<i>`) is an empty `<pattern href="#…cuT">` with its own `patternTransform` (`figCuSpec`), so the image is written once (a herringbone twice: its sleeves' own image at the tile's `slv`); pattern href inheritance renders in Playwright's WebKit, inline and as an `<img>` (`tests/mqcloth.test.js` checks it under `npm run test:webkit`)
- making a block takes 0.5-0.6 s at 4x CPU (the close-up patch search; the worsted 0.16 s), so `figureSvg` never waits: it draws the drawn tile (lifted when dark) and `figCuGet` makes the block on `requestIdleCallback`, kept in `FIG_CU` (24 entries; key `figCuKey`: id, `colOf`, shade, the close-up's length and tail, `ASM_CU.v`, `ASM_WT.v` for wt, the way, k, `pxmm`, the scale, `FIG_CUV`); `asmCuTile`'s own memo (`asmCuMem`) is shared with the swatch and the painter
- when a block is ready today.js `figCuDone(id)` swaps Today's `<svg>` in place (then `applyZoom`) only while the day shown wears that item as its jacket (or trousers with no jacket) and shows the mannequin; with Settings open it sets `todayStale` and Today is drawn with the block when Settings closes; Shop's sample figures use a block only once cached
- drawing only (`settings.pictures` 'drawn') makes the same blocks offline: only the item's close-up is read, nothing is fetched (`tests/mqcloth.test.js` watches the network)
- measured on the built code (2026-11-15, the three suits from `plan.json`, sheet `C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\mannequin-closeup\built\`): jacket mean Y before → after → painted: Darkest Brown .0241 → .0334 → .0323, Grey Herringbone .0371 → .0285 → .0283, Black .0109 → .0181 → .0178
- speed with blocks (same method as the prototype: 4x throttle, 390 css at DPR 3, decode and draw on a 1170 canvas, interleaved, 3 rounds x median of 7; the machine was busy, rounds spread ±25%): drawn → block Darkest Brown 130.8 → 149.4 ms (first round 101.2 → 99.7), Grey Herringbone 94.3 → 91.7, Black 58.2 → 57.3; the block costs about nothing beyond noise, as in the prototype (+0-3.5 ms); the SVG grows by the block (7 KB brown, 38 KB herringbone, 2 KB black)
- `tests/mqcloth.test.js`: the rule per item, the k values, the striped jacket byte-identical, the block written once, drawn first then swapped, cache keys, Settings open, drawing only with no request, pattern inheritance rendered
- the drawing is no longer sent anywhere: the OpenAI outfit photo made from the earlier drawing (Settings → This day's photo, `generateRender`, `figurePng`, `FIG_CROP`, the worker queue) was retired 2026-11-14 (the owner)

### `draw.js`

- procedural fabric patterns (`fabricDef`), `figureSvg()` glue for the torso figure, accessories, wardrobe thumbnails (`itemThumb`)
- the casual garments' drawings (2026-11-16): `casualMarks` lays each flat in the folded shirt's manner on one long- or short-sleeved body (`CASUAL_D`, right side drawn and mirrored by `casMir`): T-shirt crew neck and hems, polo knit collar and two buttons, henley three-button placket, sweater ribbed neck/cuffs/hem, sweatshirt V insert, hoodie hood, drawstrings and kangaroo pocket, quarter-zip stand collar and zip, cardigan V front, five buttons and pockets, chore jacket point collar, five buttons and three patch pockets; sheets in `C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\new-garments-built\thumbs-new-types*.png`
- the casual garments' cloth (`casualTile`, from `fabricDef` for any `HANDPICK_TYPES` item, before the switch): stripe = Breton stripes across in the second color (else navy on a light top, off-white on a dark one), heather = light and dark flecks, cable = upright twisted ropes a shade lighter; sweater, quarter-zip and cardigan show a faint V-stitch knit (`CASUAL_KNIT`), a chore jacket a faint canvas, jersey and fleece plain; in the 16 px swatch at `CASUAL_DOT` (.55) of the drawing's size (`swatchSVG` takes them like FABRIC)
- cargo pants (`isCargo`): `pantsMarks` adds `cargoPockets` (a bellows pocket with a flap and a snap on each thigh, `.cargo-pocket`) over the chinos twill; `tests/garmentdraw.test.js` also checks the existing types' drawings byte for byte against origin/main's draw.js
- the uniform blouse (`blouseMarks`, from `casualMarks`; 2026-11-17, the approved mockup `C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\ocp-blouse\ocp-mockup.html`): the long-sleeved body, a folded-down collar over a tan T-shirt's crew neck, a covered placket, angled chest pockets with flaps, the name and service tapes (the item's own text, core.js `blouseTapes`, in spice brown on tan tapes, `BLOUSE_TAPES` the colors) and the chest tab with the item's rank (`OCP_RANK_SVG`: bars, the oak leaf), shoulder pockets with a hook-and-loop field, a pen pocket, cuff tabs; cut pieces take the cloth at other offsets (patterns that `href` the cloth's); `i.rolled` (Today's row on a rolled day) ends the sleeves above the elbow in a roll
- `comboSig` (photos.js) includes an item's sett and `patternText` (Claude's description), so such an item gets its own photo key

#### Fabrics

- an item's `sett` is drawn first: its bands, the crossing lines over them at half opacity, at its angle (ties) or snapped (others); a swatch shows at least one repeat, the figure keeps the repeat's share of the item's width (`fabricDef(..., 'fig')`)
- a tie's read stripes (`v:2` bands) and figures (motif) have no white sheen line: one per repeat, turned with the pattern, it lay almost level and showed as light grey bands across the item sheet's swatch
- a motif `sett` is drawn as a 2×2-cell tile (two colours alternate like a checkerboard; `corner` figures where four cells meet; `alternate-cells` in every other cell), turned 45° for a diagonal grid; swatch scale from `cr` (cells in the close-up), figure scale from `tr` (cells across the item)
- a `figure` is drawn from its bitmap, its rows' runs of 1 as rects, at the figure's own proportions; cells are `T` wide and `T*aspect` tall, offset rows shift half a cell, and a figure crossing the tile's edge is drawn again on the other side
- a plain wool (suit, blazer, trousers with no pattern) on Today's figure (`'fig'`) and in small swatches (`'dot'`) has a fine, faint grain (`FINE_TWILL`: a 1.25 / 1.6 unit tile at .045 / .05), and no grain under a stripe or check there (2026-11-15, the new-user audit: the generic grain, a line every 4.5 units on the figure, read as a diagonal stripe; three testers took a plain navy suit for a striped one; chosen to keep texture below a pattern, as the owner did with the painter's twill ribs and the suit drawing's cloth scale); the drawings and the item sheet's swatch keep the generic grain
- a windowpane's or a jacket check's small swatch (`'dot'`) is a 7 / 8 unit tile, about two panes across the 16 unit square (at 18 one pane filled it and read as plain)
- cufflinks with a colour that isn't silver or gold are drawn with an enamel face in it in a metal rim (`.link-face`); a tie clip's swatch is its colour, shiny when it is a metal colour (`swatchSVG`: it read `i.metal`, which clips don't have, so it was always silver)
- `texTileSmooth` (Today's shirt swatch, the owner's choice "Smooth + thread grain"): 24 px a cell, a wrapping Gaussian (`TEX_SMOOTH_SIG` .32 of a cell) and a narrow smoothstep, so the figures have rounded anti-aliased outlines, plus a faint diagonal thread grain (`TEX_GRAIN` ±3.5%); herringbone and twill readings and every other swatch are unchanged

#### Traced drawings (no photo)

- every item without a photo is drawn as a shape traced from a product or item picture (2026-11-13, the owner's asks); the tracing tools are not in the repo
- compare a new drawing alone beside the others at 260 px, not only over its photo
- the colour and pattern pickers show the traced drawing (`cpFig` in colpick.js is true for every type): without a photo the "As drawn" panel is the item's shaped drawing (`itemThumb(drawn)`), not a flat square; with a close-up or photo the photo leads and "Drawing" is a tab (`view:'draw'`, `cpViews`/`cpSeg`); redrawn on every tap, nothing saved until Use; the figure fits the panel's height (`.cpfig`); `tests/colpick.test.js`
- the oxford (`SHOE_OX`, `drawShoePair(…, 'oxford')`; the unused `drawOxfordSide` was removed, CR-8-08): traced from the item picture of the owner's black calf oxfords on 3x crops, toe right, back shoe 14% darker, tan linings; box 0..96 x 0..48.6
- every shoe style but the sneaker is that traced pair (the owner chose adapting the oxford's tracing over making pictures): `drawShoePair(item,P,style)` lays the style's own paths from `SHOE_ST` over `SHOE_OX` (`drawShoeSide` picks the style): derby facings, tongue and laces; loafer apron and penny strap; chelsea boot shaft (6 units lower in the thumbnail), gusset and pull tab
- the sneaker (`SHOE_SN`, `drawSneakerPair`; the owner: "a normal pair of Nike sneakers"): traced from Nike's photo of the black Air Force 1 '07, mirrored so the toes point right; sole and tread in the second colour (else #f4f2ec); no Swoosh or lettering (Nike's marks)
- the combat boot (`SHOE_CB`, `drawCombatPair`, 2026-11-17, the owner: "really nail the trace and detail"): traced from the product photo of his Rocky S2V boots in coyote, the pair three quarters from the front like the sneaker, toes right; the silhouette from the photo's pixels (saturation mask), split into the pair and the front boot along a boundary placed on 2-3x crops; laces placed on 2x crops and snapped to the photo's lace pixels; the sole's and the uppers' tone bands posterized from its lightness; welts, nylon panels, seams, 4 speed hooks and 4 grommets placed on crops; no S2V logo or Rocky marks
- the combat boot's nylon (cordura) panels are drawn on every pair, in a fine weave a little darker than the upper; its sole is the second colour, else the upper `CB_SOLE_DARK` (.14) darker (the photo's sole is about 12% darker than its suede); box 0..92 x 0..86, drawn at translate(2 -14) inside the shoes' translate(2 22), so it fills 8..94 of the thumbnail
- the combat boot's tracing tools and its overlays on the photo are in `C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\combat-boot\` (tools/, shots/trace-*.png); the photo is never committed
- on the dark theme a shoe has a faint light edge (`--shoe-edge`)
- the belt (`BELT_D`, `drawBelt`, box 0..96 x 0..44.6, at translate(2 28) in the thumbnail): traced from the item picture of the owner's oxblood cordovan belt; buckle frame and prong in `METAL_TONES` of the belt's metal
- the buckle frame is one U-shaped path open on the right, its right bar hidden under the strap's tail (an outline there showed as a thin line), its bottom bar 20 px deep
- the suit (`SUIT_D`, `suitMarks`; the owner: "trace his arms pose, his legs, everything"): the whole suit as worn in Brooks Brothers' photo of their navy stripe suit, gaps between arms and body open (`fill-rule=evenodd`), right hand in the trouser pocket; shirt, a tie in the suit's colour darkened and a pocket square in the V; head and hands left out

- the blazer (`BLAZER_D`, `blazerMarks`; the owner's pick, head and hands at his request): the model in Brooks Brothers' photo of their hopsack sport coat; only the coat takes the cloth, skin, hair, polo and trousers keep fixed tones; box X=(x-835)*.052+50, Y=(y-40)*.052 in the 1648x2000 photo; routed in `itemThumb` by `i.type==='blazer'` only

- the shirt (`SHIRT_D`, `shirtMarks`; the owner's pick): folded, traced from Brooks Brothers' photo of their black pinpoint oxford shirt, the tie and label left out; cloth at `SHIRT_FAB` (.55, `shirtFabric`); lines lighter than the cloth on a dark shirt, darker on a light one

- the traced watch follows `watchOpts`: metal tones, dial colour, a leather strap in the item's colour, fluted, smooth or engraved bezel, three hands (date or none) instead of subdials; the old top view `drawWatch`, unused, was removed (CR-8-08, 2026-11-17)
- the tie (`TIE_D`, `tieMarks`; the owner's pick): laid flat, narrow end rolled beside the blade, traced from Brooks Brothers' solid rep tie in blue (the black's roll folds didn't show); cloth at `TIE_FAB` 1 (`tieFabric`: a repp stripe or dot about 6 across the blade; 1.5 gave 4, too large); the full swatch (`itemThumb(i,true)`) is unchanged
- trousers (`PANTS_D`, `pantsMarks`): traced from the product photo of grey office trousers the owner sent; the space between the legs open from below the crotch (the owner's ask); cloth at `PANTS_FAB` 0.22 (`pantsFabric`; waist to hem is about 42 in against the suit drawing's 34)
- jeans and chinos keep their twill (`trouserMarks`, clipped to the trousers): jeans gold double topstitching and copper rivets at the pockets; chinos a slanted pocket seam

### `dress.js`

- the dress code's Settings switches and the waistcoat default (loaded after app.js)
- Settings → Schedule → Dress code (`renderDressUI`, `#cfg-dress`, rows from `DRESS_ROWS`): two switches (`.dress-sw`, `role="switch"`, 56×44px), "Jackets (suits and blazers)" and "Ties", each with one line of help
- `setDress(k, on)` saves `settings.dress`, releases the locked week (`clearLocks`) and re-plans Today when Settings closes
- `setDress` redraws the switch and saves before releasing the locks (2026-11-15: a tap then a swipe away lost it)
- `newSuitVest()`: a new suit's Vest row (Add card) is "Matching vest" when `vestMode` is 'always' and jackets and ties are worn, else "No vest"
- with jackets off the Blazer days group and the Suit vest row are hidden (`#blazer-group`, `#vest-row`, class `dress-hide`) and a note says so
- `dressSummary()` gives the Schedule category's summary ("no ties", "no jackets", "no jackets or ties")
- `dressWhyUnused(i)`: why trousers, a shirt or shoes are never worn in a wardrobe without jackets (stats.js `whyUnused`)
- every suit saved by the app carries `vest:'yes'` or `'no'` (Add card, editor, "Bought it"), and a new suit defaults to no waistcoat
- `migrateVests()` (start-up, and after a sync brought data in: `dressSynced`): once per wardrobe, every stored suit with no `vest` value gets `vest:'yes'` (saved with a new stamp, so it syncs) and `settings.vestDef = 1` is saved with the settings; the flag is synced, so another device receives it with the suits and has nothing to do
- with backup on, the suits are changed only right after a sync that went through: stamping this device's copy first would let an out-of-date copy win over a newer one from another device; until then `hasVest` reads the suits the old way, so the plan is the same while a device waits (or is offline)
- it reads the stored wardrobe (IndexedDB), not `items`: tests that put items straight into `items` are left alone
- an empty wardrobe is not flagged at start-up (one from before may still arrive by sync or from a backup file); a wardrobe with no suit from before is flagged when an item is added (`dressNewItem`, from `save()` and `shopBought`), without a new stamp on the settings
- `dressSynced(m)` also drops `settings.vestDef` and `settings.dress` from memory when the merged settings don't have them (`onSyncedData` spreads the stored settings over the ones in memory, so a key the stored ones lack would stay)

### `tagging.js`

- photo tagging is six files (Round B, CR-2-17, 2026-11-17: tagging.js held eight jobs), listed in sw.js `ASSETS`; the code moved line for line, nothing renamed; the facts below stay under this heading
- their places in index.html (the restructure S3a, 2026-11-26): tag-request.js after srgb.js (before the painter and app.js), measure.js after act.js (with the ui kit, before add.js), migrate.js, own-lists.js, tagging.js and pattern-read.js in that order after dress.js; tagging.js and pattern-read.js still read the Add files a lot (S3c's `card-changed` announcement is what lets them move earlier)
- tag-request.js: what goes with every tagging request (`tagExtras`), the sharp copies kept on this device (`keepTagImg`, `tagImgOf`, `keepCloseImg`, `sharpCloseOf`, `texSig`), the corrections (`recordTagResult`, `tagStatsCard`) and the costs (`benchEstimate`, `describeEstimate`, `readEstimate`)
- migrate.js: the start-up remakes (`upgradeCloseups`, `upgradePhotos`, `fixBlackBackdrops`, `recolourSpecSetts`) and the day's remake cap (`REMAKE_DAY`, `remakeLeft`, `remakeSpend`)
- measure.js: white balance and paper (`wbRead`, `closeRead`, `paperOf`, `paperCopy`), measuring (`measureCloth`, `measureCard`, `measureParts`, `sharpness`) and the colour verdicts (`colourVerdicts`, `verdictMode`, `aiSignals`, `applyMeasured`, `measureAndApply`)
- tagging.js: Claude's answer onto the Add card (`takeExtras`, `takeMaterial`), one reading per card (`readBegin`/`readEnd`/`readAbort`), tagging again (`refineFromCloseup`, `refineApply`, `undoRefine`) and the card's notes (`tagNotes`)
- own-lists.js: own colours and patterns (`addCustomColor`, `addCustomPattern`, `openColForm`, `openPatForm`, `renderCustomUI`, `removeCustom`) and own materials (`addOwnMaterial`, `matOffer`, `matGuessUse`, `renderOwnMatUI`)
- pattern-read.js: the pattern readings (`readSett`, `settFrom`, `specSett`, `claudePattern`, `readPatternWithClaude`, `settFromSpec`, `motifFromSpec`, `texFromSpec`, `rereadSett`) and camouflage (`camoFromPixels`, `camoFromSpec`)
- the six are plain scripts in one global scope with no top-level code but declarations: a function is found at call time whichever file holds it; a top-level `const` must only read names from files loaded before it
#### Tagging requests and sharp copies

- the material (2026-11-15, the materials audit): app.js `normTag` keeps Claude's `material` (`matTag` -> `{key, conf, why}`) when it is on the type's list or the person's own (any confidence), or a new one the worker flagged `new:true` at medium or high with a valid name (`{…, new:true}`); `takeExtras` -> `takeMaterial` fills the card's Material only with a high or medium one on the list, only when it is empty or still holds Claude's own earlier suggestion (tagging again from the close-up), never one chosen by hand (`q.matSet`), and marks it (`q.matAI`, said under the field by wardrobe.js `matRow`); `tests/materials.test.js`
- own materials (2026-11-16, the owner's approved design; `tests/ownmaterials.test.js`): a new material is kept as `q.matNew`, never filled; Fabric & fit shows, under Material, the dashed offer (`.offer.matnew`, add.js `matExtras`) "None of the materials fit: Claude calls it **cupro**, <why> (<conf> confidence)." with "Add cupro as a material" (tagging.js `matOffer`): it goes into `settings.ownMaterials[type]` (`addOwnMaterial`, sorted, at most `OWN_MAT_MAX` 20 a type, saved and synced), is chosen as by hand (`matSet`, Fabric & fit checked), the note "Now one of your materials: it’s in the list for every shirt you add, on all your devices." (`.matadded`, `q.matAdded`) and the toast "Cupro added to your shirt materials." with Undo (off the card and out of the list again). core.js `ownMatName` (lower-case letters, spaces, hyphens, 2-24 characters, at most 3 words), `ownMats(type)`, `matsFor(type)` (the list + own: every dropdown, `matAlias` so import and search take an own one by name, `typeTidy`), `matTypeWord` / `matTypeOne` for the sentences. Tag requests send `ownMaterials` (`ownMatsForTag` in `tagExtras`), the reader its type's; no extra Claude call. The planner and painter know nothing of them: no season (`matSeason` ''), painted plain (`asmCmMat` reads only words it knows; unchanged, it is in the picture code hash)
- Claude's low-confidence guess (2026-11-16, the owner's addition): a material on the list (or own) at low is kept as `q.matGuess`, never filled; under Material a quiet line "Claude’s guess: wool · <why if 60 characters or less, else can’t be sure from a photo>" with "Use wool" (`.matguess`, `matGuessUse`: `setQField`, so chosen by hand and Fabric & fit checked)
- suits, blazers and pants (`MAT_ASK`) with no material: Fabric & fit's mark is "!" (`warn`, but it does not open first or count as unsure) and its summary "Material not set" (· Claude’s guess: wool, or · Claude calls it cupro); it never blocks Confirm; once a material is set the mark follows the usual rules
- photo tagging on the app side (tag-request.js, loaded before app.js since the restructure S3a; nothing runs at load): `tagExtras()` adds to every tag request the owner's colours/patterns (`settings.custom`), the latest 20 corrections (`settings.tagFixes`; an item never gets its own), and on a second tag the close-up and the measurement
- tag requests carry `wb` (was the photo white-balanced) from `tagExtras` and `/tag-many`, and `/pattern` from `claudePattern` (one flag for both pictures: the card's photo correction); a saved item's re-read and Ask Claude send none (unknown)
- the tagger gets a sharp copy (`TAG_MAX` 2048px, JPEG `TAG_Q` 0.92; group photos 2576px), kept on the device in Cache Storage `dw-keep-tagimg` (`keepTagImg`/`tagImgOf`; sw.js never deletes `dw-keep-*` caches) so an item can be re-tagged from the same picture
- the kept sharp photo is signed with its item photo (`keepTagImg(id,url,photo)`, header `x-ph`); `tagImgOf(id,it)` gives nothing for another photo; Replace photo keeps a new copy and drops `pbox`, Rotate turns the copy and `pbox`, a crop or `upgradePhotos` signs it again (`resignTagImg`); `cropSource` (item-photo.js) still takes any copy (its own similarity test)
- the sharp close-up (`keepCloseImg`/`sharpCloseOf`, `closeimg/<id>` in the same device-only cache, `CLOSE_MAX` 1536, JPEG `TAG_Q`) is kept when an Add card is saved (`closeTagImg`) and when the editor adds a close-up (`replaceTexture`, item-photo.js)
- "Read pattern from photo" (`rereadSett`) and the colour picker's Ask Claude (`colAskImgs`) send the sharp close-up; it is stored with the synced close-up's signature (`texSig`), so a replaced, removed or undone close-up never sends an old copy; another device sends the synced one; `tests/sharpclose.test.js`
- the synced close-up is 1536 px (`TEX_MAX` in add.js, 2026-11-13, the owner's ask; it was 640, where a fine twill aliased into a false check on the owner's mid grey blazer): every device gets the sharp close-up
- `upgradeCloseups` (at start-up after `recolourSpecSetts`, once: `localStorage.tex_up` '1536'): a close-up saved at 640 is made again at `TEX_MAX` from this device's sharp copy (none, nothing to do), in the old close-up's colour (each channel's linear mean matched, gains 0.6-1.6), and the sharp copy is kept again under the new signature; a toast says how many; `tests/sharpclose.test.js`
- the two remakes share `linMean64` (a picture's linear mean on a 64x64 copy) and `matchMean` (each channel's gain held to 0.6-1.6), with `sLin` / `linS` (code review CR-2-15)
- `fixBlackBackdrops` stays within the day's remakes (`remakeLeft() < 2` waits, `remakeSpend(2)`: a photo and its small copy) and sets `black_bd` only once nothing is left (code review CR-2-12)
- adding a close-up tags again with both photos (`refineFromCloseup`): fields the owner set by hand (`q.edited`, set by `setq`) stay, the rest take the new answer, and the card says what changed
- the close-up's answer never changes the type (offered: "Make it suit", `q.refineType`), its colours and pattern are fitted to the card's type, a main colour the close-up's measurement clearly contradicts (`refineContradicts`: measured ≥8 ΔE nearer the old colour) is kept and said with "Use …" (`q.refineKept`), and the changes have Undo (`undoRefine`, `q.refineUndo`; audit 2026-11-15)
- an iPhone out of memory for pictures refuses new canvases (`getContext` gives null; desktop browsers never do): the likeliest cause of a tester's very first item failing (2026-11-16, Chrome on an iPhone with 50 tabs open, a textured shirt with a close-up; reproduced by refusing new canvases at random points on her build: Confirm stuck in 49 of 200 runs, 33 of them freed by "Bought, on the way", as she saw; on the fixed code 0 of 200, no failed save, no uncaught error); `texTileUrl`/`texTileSmooth` (draw.js) give null and cache nothing without a context, `cropSwatch` gives null (the item saves without a new swatch), each Add card is drawn on its own (`cardSafe`: a card that throws while drawn becomes `plainCard`, with Confirm, and is reported), `refineFromCloseup` measures inside a try; `tests/canvasrefuse.test.js` refuses the 1st to the 40th new canvas; the harness is `C:\code\dw-material-out\stress-mem.js` (her photo cut from her screenshot, seeded variations of the server's answers)
- an Add card is drawn again whenever a reading ends, however it went (2026-11-16, a tester could save only after tapping "Bought, on the way": a step that threw after `q.refining`/`q.patternReading` was cleared left Confirm switched off until something else redrew the card): `refineFromCloseup` catches the reading and hands the answer to `refineApply` inside a try; `readPatternWithClaude` applies its answer in try/finally with `renderAdd`; `specSett` returns null for an answer it can't draw (the pixel reader takes over); `retryTag` (safety.js) likewise; `tests/addcards.test.js`
- `readPatternWithClaude` sends nothing for a card no longer in `queue` (saved or skipped while the close-up's tagging ran)
- one reading of each kind per Add card (code review CR-2-03 / CR-2-10, 2026-11-17): `readBegin(q, 'refine'|'pattern')` gives the reading an AbortController (kept beside the card in `READS`, a WeakMap, not saved with it), a new close-up calls off both earlier readings, `readEnd` says whether the answer is still the card's own (only then is it applied and `q.refining` / `q.patternReading` cleared), and `readAbort` (Skip: add.js `skipCard`; Remove close-up: `dropTexture`) aborts what is in flight; the worker gives a tester's count back on the abort (499)
- while `cardReading(q)` the Texture match section shows no Replace, Retake, Remove or "Read color and texture", only "You can replace the close-up once Claude has read it." (add.js `addCkSecs`, `.ckcuwait`); `tests/tagreads.test.js`
- tagging again and the color picker's Ask Claude send the card's type (`ask(…, {forType})`, code review CR-2-04): app.js `normTag(parsed, model, forType)` fits the colors, the runner-up, the pattern and the material to it; `type` stays Claude's answer (offered as "Make it suit")
- `ask(…, {out})` puts that call's own failure in `out.err` ({note, why, net}; code review CR-2-07): the first tag, Try again (`tagFailed(q, out.err)`), tagging again and Ask Claude read their own reason, not the shared `aiError` / `tagWhy` / `tagNetLast` (still set for the Add page's note); a failed tagging again says "Tagging again from the close-up didn’t work: <why>. The first tags stay." (`REFINE_FAIL` without a reason)
- `claudePattern` keeps a camouflage answer (`j.camo`; code review CR-2-01: it was paid for and dropped), so `camoFromSpec` gets Claude's blotch size
- `benchEstimate(n,{img,close})` prices the sizes really sent (`TAG_MAX` and `CLOSE_MAX`, 4:3); the colour picker's Ask Claude shows its cost from it

#### Owner colours, patterns and corrections
- owner colours/patterns: `settings.custom` (synced), applied by `applyCustom` in core.js; keys `own<letters>` / `own-<slug>`; each has a base (`colBase`/`patBase`): the planner reads `planCol(i)`/`patOf(i)` so it behaves like the base, while drawings and brightness (`lumCol`) use the colour's own shade; Settings lists them, removable when unused (`renderCustomUI`)
- the card shows confidence, one-tap runner-ups (`altColor`/`altPattern`), the measurement, and offers a new colour (Claude's `newColor`, or ΔE over `FAR_DE` 12) or pattern (`newPattern`) through `newColForm`/`newPatForm`
- "Add as a new color" opens on the shade Claude's name says when there is no close-up measurement (`newColHex`, `nameHex`: "dusty pink" → pink, the whole name then its words from the last), else the photo's measurement, else the tagged colour (W2-17-05: it opened Claude's "dusty pink" over the tagged cream)
- the new colour / pattern sheets show their error in the sticky bar, above the button (`.cnbarerr`, W2-16-05: above the bar it was cut through by its edge)
- the colour picker's families: hue 200-298 is a blue (`colFamily`, W2-16-11: deep blue, 293, was under "Reds, pinks & purples"); lavender (302) and purple stay with the purples
- the Add card's offers open the new sheets (2026-11-15): "Add as a new colour" (Claude's `newColor`, or a close-up over `FAR_DE`) is `openColForm` → `openColNew` with Claude's name and the measured shade; "Add this pattern" is `openPatForm` → `openPatNew` with Claude's words and `guessPatBase`; the inline `newColForm`/`newPatForm` are gone
- an own pattern may carry `look` (Claude's drawing recipe from /describe: `{layout, tr, tex | across (+ along) | motif}` in colour roles), kept by `addCustomPattern` (`cleanLookApp`) and synced with `settings.custom`; `applyCustom` keeps it on `CUSTOM_PAT`
- Settings → Your colours & patterns has "+ New colour" / "+ New pattern" (`#cust-newcol`, `#cust-newpat`) and draws each pattern with its look (`swatchSVG` on a sample item)
- corrections: saving an AI-tagged card records changed fields (`recordTagResult`: `settings.tagFixes`, last 60; `settings.tagStats` counts) and the stats page shows the accuracy (`tagStatsCard`)
- taking Claude's own suggestion of a new colour or pattern (`q.newColor` / `q.newPattern`, saved as an owner's colour or pattern of that name) is not counted as a correction (W2-17-16)

#### White balance and measuring
- white balance: `wbRead` (gains, whether a white reference is there, its brightness); a close-up with its own reference (white paper) is corrected and exposure-set from it (`texGains` in add.js), otherwise like its item photo
- a white reference in an item photo must be one solid area (half its pixels with 3+ reference neighbours) at least 1.15x the lightness of the picture's darker quarter: a khaki or beige cloth's scattered highlights are not white (taking them turned khaki grey); `settCanvas` also needs it to cover 3% of the frame before setting exposure
- `paperOf(it)` → {ref, warm} (2026-11-15): `ref` = `closeRead` found paper in the close-up; `warm` = no paper and the item photo's looser `wbRead` gains have blue ≥ `PAPER_WARM` (1.08) × red; dim light can't be told (the phone brightens every picture); kept in memory by the close-up's and photo's signatures (`PAPER`, `paperNow` reads it), nothing stored or synced
- `paperCopy(it)`: the close-up sent to /describe (the sharp copy, `sharpCloseOf`); with paper it is white-balanced from it first (`applyWb` with `closeRead`'s gains) and goes with `wb:true`, without paper as taken with `wb:false`: close-ups added in the editor are stored as taken and the sharp copy is never corrected, so `wb:true` would otherwise be untrue
- a close-up's white reference must be a solid patch set apart from the cloth (`closeRead` = `wbRead(c,{strict:true})`, `wbSolidStats`/`wbSolidOk`: a 2 px-eroded core over `WB_CORE`, `WB_GAP` brighter than the cloth's median, under `WB_TRANS` in between); used by `measureCard`, `texGains`, `closeTagImg`, `settFrom`/`settFromSpec`/`motifFromSpec`/`texFromSpec` and colpick; item photos keep the looser test
- `settCanvas(…,strict)` takes the close-up's white balance; the exposure may still be raised from the cloth's own white threads, never lowered
- measuring: `measureCloth` gives the median Lab of the close-up (or of Claude's `clothBox` in the item photo); `nearestCols` compares by ΔE2000 (`dE00` in core.js) against `calibLab`, the median of the owner's own measured items of that colour once there are two (`item.meas`), else the swatch
- `measureCloth` leaves out pixels far from the cloth joined to the region's edge (`measEdgeCut`, at most `MEAS_EDGE_MAX` 45%: the background, a collar) and returns `spread`, `edge` and the kept pixels (`px`, not enumerable)
- lightness counts half without a white reference, and a third on a close-up without one (`measKL`, `MEAS_KL_CLOSE` 3): the phone exposes any cloth to a mid tone
- `calibLab(c,type,skip)` learns from plain cloth only (`plainCloth`: a solid or a one-colour weave, `ONE_COL_WEAVES`, with no second colour) and never from the item itself
- a patterned cloth (`multiCol`: stripes, checks, motifs, paisley, tonal check) is measured colour by colour (`measureParts`/`measSplit`: the close-up's pixels split in two, seeded by the band reading's colours; the ground against the main colour, the stripe against the second); a one-colour weave is measured whole; the tagger is told the ground (`measHint`, `groundNear`); saved in `item.meas.parts`
- `sharpness(c)`: fine over fine + coarse detail at 400/100 px; under `BLUR_SHARP` (0.25) the card says "The close-up is too blurry to read the weave: retake it?" (`.blurnote`); the owner's phone close-ups are 0.19-0.64, the walkthrough's web crops mostly under 0.25

#### Colour recognition
- colour recognition on every item (2026-11-14, the owner's design): `measureAndApply` after the first tag, tagging again, Try again and Claude's pattern answer runs `measureCard` → `colourVerdicts` (one per colour) → `applyMeasured`; `tests/measure.test.js`
- a verdict's confidence (`measConf`, 0-1): how much nearer the measured colour is than the tagged one (ΔE00 gap), how near at all, the cloth's evenness (`spread`), the close-up's sharpness, a white reference, the photo agreeing with the close-up (`m.photo`, from `clothBox`), and how many of the two colours the owner's own plain items calibrate (`cal`)
- confident (`MEAS_AUTO`) and from a close-up or a white reference: the Add card changes the colour and says "Changed navy → charcoal (measured from your close-up)" with Undo (`q.auto`, `undoMeasured`, `.measauto`); an undone change is never made again (`q.autoUndo`)
- unsure (`MEAS_FLAG` up to `MEAS_AUTO`, gap 3+, within `FAR_DE`): "Measured: charcoal?" with "Use charcoal" (`.measalt`), the tag unchanged; `measuredAlt(q)` is the main colour's verdict
- a photo-only measurement doesn't count for an offer: the owner's dark mink suit measured nearest black from the photo, deep taupe from the close-up; fixtures `tests/fixtures/suit-mink*.{jpg,png}`
- a field the owner set by hand (`q.edited`) is never changed or asked about
- calibration (2026-11-14, read-only copy of the owner's backup, 42 items with a close-up): nearest measured colour = his tag 40% (walkthrough 25%); no confidence was 95% right (his pale pink herringbone shirt read white at 0.69, above every right one), so `MEAS_AUTO` 0.75 changes nothing on his wardrobe; flags on 4 of his 42 correct main colours where the old "Use …" offer came on 13
- Claude's own signals are kept from the same answer (no extra call): `aiSignals` → `q.tagAI` / `item.tagAI` = {col (the colour Claude gave), conf {type, primaryColor, pattern} high/medium/low, alt, altPat, seen (Claude was told the measurement)}; tag-many's one confidence counts for all three; the planner isn't sent it (shop.js `strip`)
- the worker's confidence stays high/medium/low (no number added): the existing answer already carries it
- the verdict weighs them (`verdictMode`, `aiFor`), main colour only and only while the field still holds Claude's answer (`tagAI.col`): Claude low with the measured colour as its runner-up → changed from `AI_LOW_AUTO` 0.55 (close-up or white reference; the note adds "Claude wasn’t sure"), asked from `AI_LOW_FLAG` 0.25
- Claude high and no white reference → never changed, asked only from `AI_HIGH_FLAG` 0.6; medium, no signals, or a runner-up that is a third colour → the measurement alone (`MEAS_AUTO`, `MEAS_FLAG`); `tests/aiverdict.test.js`
- a runner-up that agrees with the measurement is not evidence by itself: tagging again with the close-up tells Claude the measurement and asks it to name the measured colour as altColor when it picks another (`measuredText`, worker/tag.js)
- calibration of the Claude signals (2026-11-14, mocked answers over the owner's 42 close-up items and the walkthrough's 24): 0 wrong changes when Claude, right, says high or medium; Claude low on his pale pink herringbone shirt would turn it white (the one wrong change); an estimate until items carry `tagAI`
- a white reference is not weighted further than `measConf` already does: only 2 of his 42 close-ups and 1 of the walkthrough's have one, too few to calibrate
- a pattern the tag missed: a sharp close-up of cloth tagged solid with bands 12+ ΔE00 apart and two repeats suggests stripes or a check (`missedPattern`, `.measpat`), never sets it

#### Pattern readings
- full patterns (`readSett`/`settFrom`): for a striped/checked tie or shirt (`SETT_PATS`, or any cloth Claude gives a `weave` for) the sett is read from Claude's `patternBox` in the sharp photo, else the close-up (direction then from Claude's `weave.direction`), else Claude's own `weave.colours`; Claude's `weave.repeatsAcross` sets the figure scale; the card says what was read and can switch to the simple pattern (`toggleSett`); saved as `item.sett`, `item.pbox`, `item.weave`, `item.patternText`
- suits, blazers and trousers read their stripes and checks too (`SETT_PATS`); fine weaves (herringbone, birdseye, nailhead, sharkskin, twill) and houndstooth keep their drawn tiles; with both a sharp photo and a close-up showing 1.5+ repeats, the photo gives the direction and scale and the close-up the bands (`src:'photo+closeup'`); on the figure a jacket or shirt is ~200/180 pattern units across, so a 30-across pinstripe repeats every 6.7
- before a pattern is read, a photo with a white reference is exposure-corrected too (`settCanvas`, at most 1.6x in linear light), so an underexposed photo doesn't read every band too dark; a tie's scale (`tr`) comes from its own photo, a jacket's or shirt's from Claude's count
- `readable(t,p,w)` = `settable` or `claudeOnly`: an Add card reads (and saves) a motif or shirt-weave pattern with Claude once its close-up is in, not only band patterns
- `claudeOnly` patterns (motifs, shirt weaves, tie weaves) never use the pixel band reader; the item sheet's "Read pattern from photo" shows for any `claudeOnly` pattern (itemsheet.js)
- a stripe reading that contradicts the tag is not used (`settCheck` in `readSett`/`rereadSett`): crossing lines on a jacket's or trousers' stripe, or level stripes read from the item photo, turn it down (`q.settReject`, the card says why: `.settrej`); level from a close-up or Claude it is turned upright; a shirt's crossing lines are dropped; a tie keeps any reading (a plaid tie is often tagged stripe)
- `settFrom` keeps each input's reading (`settMemo`, by the card's canvases): the same reading is not worked out again on every re-tag or toggle (1.8-2.5 s each at phone speed)
- Claude reads the pattern itself once a card has a close-up (`readPatternWithClaude` → worker `POST /pattern`, `claudePattern`): every band of a repeat each way with its width, the pane shape and the repeats in the close-up and across the item, with the noise (seams, moire, uneven panes) left out; saved as `item.patternSpec`; the pixel reader (`settFrom`) is the fallback; the editor's "Read pattern from photo" (`rereadSett`) asks Claude too
- `settFromSpec` draws Claude's answer with colours from the photos: each named thread matched to its own pixels, Claude's hue where the pixels read grey (a light-blue line in khaki linen), a quarter of the ground's lightness mixed into each line (crossing threads, a suit's pinstripe)
- `settFromSpec` matches the close-up's exposure to the item photo only inside the saved pattern box (`pbox`); with no box the close-up keeps its own colours (a whole-photo median took in the wall and turned a navy pinstripe lavender); if the measured ground still lands over 15 ΔE00 from Claude's ground, it drops the exposure match, and failing that uses Claude's colours
- `settFromSpec`'s ground is the median of the whole close-up only when Claude's ground covers 70% of the cloth or more; below that it is the pixels nearest Claude's ground colour: the owner's half-navy, half-blue repp tie read slate (#344a71) for #1e2347, the median falling between the two
- a thin line (all its stripes together 10% of the repeat or less) takes, of its nearest pixels, the half furthest from the ground: blurred with the ground in the close-up it read light grey
- `recolourSpecSetts()` (once per rule change, flag `localStorage.spec_recolour`, now '3') recolours saved Claude-read patterns from the saved `patternSpec` and close-up, without calling Claude; the user's suit photos are `tests/fixtures/suit-pinstripe*.jpg`
- the owner's navy & white & grey stripe tie had Claude's structure corrected by hand (2026-11-12: white, grey, white touching), in its `patternSpec`, so a recolour keeps it

#### Camouflage
- camouflage (2026-11-16, the owner's MultiCam pants were drawn plain: no band, motif or weave reading holds irregular blotches with no grid) is a pattern of suits, blazers, pants and shirts (core.js `PATS_BY` and the worker's `SUIT_PATS`/shirt list and prompt); `camoPat(p)`: the pattern, an own pattern based on it, or an own pattern named or described as camouflage (`CAMO_RE`: camo, multicam, ocp, woodland…)
