- core.js `camoField(N, shares, seed)`: a seamless N x N map of colour indexes (index 0, the largest share, is the ground): each other colour a smooth noise field over `CAMO_OCT` octaves on a wrapping lattice (no 48-cell layer: it made pixel-step edges), and from the smallest share up each takes the pixels where its field is highest until it covers exactly its share; seeded, kept per arguments (`camoMemo`); shared by the drawings and the painter
- a camouflage reading is `item.sett = {v:5, camo:{cols, shares, seed}, tr, cr, src}` (cols largest share first); detect.js `ownSett` uses it only on a camouflage and nothing else on one; without one `usableSett` falls back to `camoLook(i)`: the item's own colours (main as the ground, second, third, a step darker and lighter; `CAMO_SHARES`, `CAMO_TR` 3 tiles across), seeded by the item's id; `settSig` has a `c…` signature (the painter's key)
- reading it (tagging.js): camouflage is `claudeOnly` (never the band reader); `camoFromPixels({tex, full, box})` clusters the close-up's middle (15% margins: the owner's close-up had floor tiles down one side) at `CAMO_SAMPLE` px in Lab with hue weighted `CAMO_AB` (4) times lightness, finely (`CAMO_FINE` 10) then merged (within `CAMO_MERGE` ΔE00, then the smallest into its nearest) down to `CAMO_K` 6 or Claude's count, clusters under `CAMO_MIN` 3% dropped: coarse clusters averaged MultiCam's sage green and pinkish tan to grey; `camoFromSpec` (the worker's layout camouflage) takes Claude's colour count and blotch size (`blobsAcrossItem` / `CAMO_BLOBS` 4 = tiles across) with the colours from the pixels, else Claude's own colours; `readSett` and `rereadSett` fall back to the pixels
- the worker's `/pattern` names layout `camouflage` but has no camo object in its schema (2026-11-18): the camo object added 2026-11-16 (`PCAMO`) was the schema's sixth optional object and Claude's API refused the whole schema ("The compiled grammar is too large"), so every pattern reading failed with a 502 for two days (found on the owner's brown foulard tie); a camouflage answer is a 422 naming the layout and the app reads its colors and shares from the pixels (`camoFromPixels`); the worker still cleans a `camo` object if one ever comes; `tests/camouflage.test.js` checks the schema sent has no camo and at most five optional objects (tests stub Claude, so nothing else would notice a schema the API refuses: check a schema change against the real API with one small text call)
- `claudePattern` sets `patternFail` when the server answers an error (5xx, 401/403) or the request fails, and the item sheet's "Read pattern from photo" then says "Claude couldn’t read the pattern right now (the server had a problem)" instead of "No clear pattern found"
- drawing: draw.js `camoTileUrl` (a `CAMO_PX` 224 px seamless PNG, edges softened by a one-pixel wrapping blend; null without a canvas) in `fabricDef` (a small swatch about one tile, on the figure `tr` tiles across); the painter routes camouflage on a jacket, waistcoat or trousers to `camo` (`asmCamoTex`: the same map, `ASM_CAMO.T` 256, the tile `ASM_SHIRT_MM / tr` wide); a shirt goes through its usual tile
- camouflage v2 (2026-11-16, the owner: "why does it look like trash", and "I don't want to use the close up as the paint for camo"): colours come from the item's tags, never the photo (detect.js `camoTag`: main, second, third, a step darker and lighter of the main; the accents muted `CAMO_MUTE` .3 toward the main's a*b*; a reading, the pixels' or Claude's, gives only the shares by lightness rank and the seed; the lightest held to `CAMO_LIGHT_MAX` .12, the darkest to `CAMO_DARK_MAX` .18, the main always the ground; `CAMO_RANK` without a reading; `CAMO_TR` 2 tiles across): his shirt's close-up measured grey and drew a coyote-brown shirt lavender, and his pants' lightest cluster (cream plus highlights, 25%) drew them washed out; shapes: `camoField` looks each field up through a slow wrapping displacement (`CAMO_WARP` .09: organic edges, not round blobs) on a finer lattice (`CAMO_OCT` from 5 cells: more, smaller blotches); `camoImage(N, cols, shares, seed)` (core.js) softens the edges with two wrapping box blurs (`CAMO_SOFT` .005 of the tile) and adds a faint grain (`CAMO_GRAIN`), shared by `camoTileUrl` (`CAMO_PX` 320) and `asmCamoTex` (`ASM_CAMO.T` 384); `CAMO_V` 2 in the tile and look keys; how close it gets to the real cloth is as good as the tagged colours (his "Multicam tan" #bdb8b4 is a light warm grey, his "Coyote brown" #4d3310 a dark chocolate)
- a uniform (2026-11-16, the owner: "the military requires you to wear both together"): planner.js `patsOf` counts camouflage pieces worn together as one pattern (no "same type and scale" clash in `patternScore`, `clothesHard`, `rateOutfit`), the shirt-and-pants same-color and contrast terms skip a camouflage pair (`camoUniform`), and a camouflage shirt or pants with a plain piece, or a camouflage shirt under a jacket that isn't, costs `UNIFORM_SPLIT` (40) in `clothesScore`; `PLAN_RULES` 20; on his wardrobe over three months the shirt and pants come only together
- a camouflage shirt is painted with the pants' camouflage painter (`asmCmRoute` 'camo' for the shirt zone, `asmCamoTex`), not the shirt's drawn tile (2026-11-16, the owner: "why do they look different": the tile route turned the same colors pinkish and gray)
- the planner: `PAT_TYPE.camouflage` 'camo', scale 3 (bold and large); `formalTop`, a funeral's and a wedding's jacket lose 60/60/40 on camouflage; `PLAN_RULES` 17; the import reads camo, multicam, ocp, woodland as camouflage (kit.js); the pattern picker files it under Figures
- the owner's MultiCam pants (2026-11-16): his own pattern "Camouflage (OCP/MultiCam)" was moved from base other to camouflage and his colour "Multicam tan" from light grey (the planner paired them as light grey pants) to khaki, and the pants got a reading from their close-up; `tests/camouflage.test.js`
- OCP (2026-11-17, the owner: "one fixed OCP look" for his uniform blouse and OCP pants): core.js `isOcp(i)` (a camouflage blouse, pants marked `camo:'ocp'` or with an own pattern named or described OCP, Scorpion or MultiCam) gets detect.js `ocpSett`: `OCP_CAMO`'s five colors and shares (tan ground, green, brown, light sand, dark brown) whatever was tagged or read, `ocp` set, its seed carrying `OCP_V`
- core.js `ocpImage(N, seed)`: the Scorpion look, finer and more broken up than `camoImage` (the owner: the generic camouflage was too big and blobby): green and brown areas wider than tall (`ax`) with fragmented edges (`warp`), light sand islands and dark brown flecks mostly inside and beside them, a fine vertical streak in every edge (`vs`), a one-pixel soft edge and a thread grain; each color its share by a histogram threshold (no sort); seamless, kept per seed (`ocpMemo`); matched to his photo at 1 px per mm (`C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\ocp-blouse\shots\gen-compare*.png`)
- draw.js `ocpTileUrl` (512 px, `OCP_PX`) in `fabricDef`: a drawing's garment stands for `OCP_DRAW_MM` (560 mm), so a tile is drawn at its real share; Today's figure as the generic camouflage (`tr`)
- the uniform blouse uses the combat boot's `camoPants(b)` (core.js) for its camouflage-pants rule

#### Motifs
- a motif answer (`layout:'motif'`: a small figure on a grid, a neat/foulard, dots) becomes `sett = {v:3, motif:{grid, ground, figs:[{shape, place, size, cols, names, centre, centreName}]}, a, cr, tr, src:'claude+pixels'}` (`motifFromSpec`, chosen by `specSett`): each colour from its nearest pixels, Claude's hue kept where they read grey, the close-up's exposure matched to the photo only when there is a box on the cloth (else the photo's wall washes it out)
- `MOTIF_PATS` (foulard, polka-dot; detect.js) never get a band reading (`readSett`, `rereadSett`): a band reader turns a neat into a diagonal plaid; without Claude they are drawn with the generic tile
- a motif figure can be any shape (`shape:'figure'`): Claude draws it as a bitmap (`bitmap` rows of 1/0, kept as `figs[].bmp`, with a `name`); the grid's cells can be taller or shorter than wide (`cellAspect` → `motif.aspect`) and every other row can be shifted half a cell (`rowOffset:'half'` → `motif.off`)
- a shirt's two-colour figured dobby (the user's grey chevrons on white, `tests/fixtures/shirt-chevron-closeup.png`) is a motif, not a texture, drawn at full contrast

#### Shirt weaves
- shirt weave textures (`TEXTURE_PATS`/`texPat` in detect.js: dobby, woven, oxford, twill, herringbone…): `/pattern` layout `texture` gives a one-repeat bitmap (`grid` rows of 1/0, 1 = raised), kind, two tones and contrast; `texFromSpec` saves `item.sett {v:4, tex:{kind,grid,lighter,contrast,rel}}`, `rel` being the close-up's lightness swing (`texContrast`, light falloff removed by a box blur) as a share of its lightness
- the texture is drawn in the tagged colour (`texTones` in draw.js: swing softened to 0.4× and capped at 7 L*, average kept at the tag, a white shirt's ground darkens slightly) as a soft image tile (`texTileUrl`), on the figure no finer than 6 units a repeat; only for shirts, and `usableSett` drops a texture reading once the pattern is no longer a weave (or a band reading on a weave)
- a texture read as `herringbone` or `twill` is drawn with the woven tiles (`herringboneTile` in draw.js, columns of slanted lines turning at each column edge), not Claude's bitmap: the user's blue shirt came back as one V that tiled into horizontal zigzags; contrast from `rel`, a swatch at most 20 column pairs (Claude counted 60 chevrons); figured weaves keep the bitmap
- a herringbone stripe's plain bands come from Claude's bitmap (`texPlainBand` in draw.js: columns the same all the way down; all 1 = a shiny satin band), drawn after every twill column, alternating column / band / opposite column / band as on the user's pink shirt (`herringboneTile(…, band)`); the worker prompt tells Claude to mark them
- a texture's `aspect` (a repeat's height ÷ width, from Claude) stretches its tile: Claude drew the user's tall dobby diamonds as a square bitmap
- a texture isn't sent to the photo generator (plain shirts share one photo per colour); textured shirts with a close-up but no reading are listed and read under Settings → Outfit pictures → "Painted plain" (asm-color.js); the user's diamond-dobby close-up is `tests/fixtures/shirt-diamond-closeup.jpg`

#### Ties
- woven and tonal-check ties are read by Claude (`TIE_WEAVES`/`tieWeave` in detect.js, in `claudeOnly`): before, nothing read them and the tie painter drew them plain; the owner's charcoal & grey woven tie came back as a motif (diagonal grid, 40 across) and paints like his close-up; a pure weave (layout `texture`) gives a `tex` reading; `tests/tieweave.test.js`
- `usableSett` takes any reading for such a tie and `specSett` makes a `tex` reading for a tie as for a shirt: the owner's black & charcoal tonal check came back as an 8x8 diamond dobby (32 across) and is painted as a tone-on-tone grid; the faint larger check over it is not drawn (a weave answer has no second layer)
- `asmTieFor` (assemble.js) takes `s.tex`, and `asmTieTile` draws it with `fabricDef(…,'asmtie')`, the weave's swing `TIE_TEX_K` (2.6) times the shirt's (at the shirt's it read about 3.5 L*, nearly flat); `TIE_TEX_K` also strengthens a twill or herringbone in `fabricDef`'s twill branch
- a tie's twill or herringbone repeats at most `ASM_TIE_TEX_MAX` (50) times across, about 4 px a repeat: Claude counted 80 ribs on the owner's burgundy twill tie and at under 3 px they blurred into flat colour
- so a tie's weave shows on the phone (the 2048 picture shrunk about 1.75 times): a figured weave repeats at most `ASM_TIE_FIG_MAX` (22) times across, and on a very dark tie (L* under `TIE_DARK_L` 20) its swing is `TIE_DARK_X` (1.55) times `TIE_TEX_K` (draw.js, generic weave branch only); chosen from four variants at phone size as subtle but visible; the owner's black tonal-check tie read flat black before
- `TIE_TEX_K`, `ASM_TIE_TEX_MAX`, `ASM_TIE_FIG_MAX`, `TIE_DARK_L` and `TIE_DARK_X` are all in `asmTieSig`
- on a tie no ground lightness is mixed into a line (the quarter mix is for a line whose threads cross the ground's): it darkened the repp tie's blue stripe and turned a thin white edging grey

#### Patterns and photo keys
- tagging again keeps the pattern as `normTag` fitted it to the card's type (`aiFields`, 2026-11-24, the review's R2 N1): `aiFields` fitted it again to Claude's type, so a shirt's oxford, end-on-end, gingham or chambray that Claude called a suit, blazer or pants came back Solid (42 of 60 shirt patterns); a first tag's `normTag` already fits Claude's own type, so nothing changes there; `tests/tagreads.test.js`
- a striped or checked shirt, or one with a motif reading (`shirtPatterned` in photos.js), gets its own photo key; plain and finely woven shirts share one photo per colour
- the words and drawings that described a read pattern to the retired outfit-photo generator (`patternPrompt`, `settText`, `motifText`, `patternDiagram`, `outfitRefsFull`, `trimRefs`, the watch's turned references) were removed on 2026-11-14 (the owner: "remove the leftovers too")

### `app.js`

- an ES module since 2026-11-24, its code unchanged, importing nothing; its bridge is its last lines (nothing above can throw at load: the database's failures are its promises'); `flt`, `queue`, `view`, `sel`, `dbCur`, `dbConn`, `dbWiping`, `connReady`, `aiError` and `aiModel` have setters, since other files and tests assign them
- `ask` is handed over with `Object.assign`, so a test no longer replaces it: tests/addcards, aiverdict and measure run the real `ask` and answer its `POST` in `window.fetch`
- `afterDom(f)` goes by its own flag (`domDone`, set by a `DOMContentLoaded` listener added when app.js runs), not `document.readyState` (2026-11-23): while deferred scripts run readyState is already `'interactive'`, so reading it would run `f` before the later scripts; `afterScripts` (the `load` event, `readyState==='complete'`) is unchanged
- `ask()` sends the code in the `x-app-token` header only (no body `appToken`) and takes the worker's answer as it is (the old Workers AI text shape with code fences is gone: the worker parses it); test stubs of `POST /` answer the fields at the top, not `{response: …}` (code review CR-2-14)
- the busy pill says "Claude is reading the color and texture…" for `/describe` with `what:'both'` (`busyLabelFor`, code review CR-2-09)
- app.js was split on 2026-11-14 (the audit's recommendation) into classic scripts loaded in its place, in this order: `app.js` (state, IndexedDB, the busy pill, the tab bar's fit, `ask`, colour helpers, photo loading, the precision swatch), `add.js` (the Add page), `itemsheet.js` (the item sheet, its photo editor, the Wardrobe grid), `history.js` (past days, locks, the piece picker, occasions, the score card), `today.js` (the month's plan and snapshot, Today's rows, figure and overlays, the pieces box, zoom, swatches, swiping, `renderToday`), `month.js`, `photos.js` (photo keys, kept photos, the realistic photo, item pictures, the queue), `settings.js` (Settings and its categories; days off, weather, toasts, dates and the theme in daysoff.js, weather.js, toast.js, locale.js, theme.js), `start.js` (`show`, the page moves, the start-up chain, the service worker)
- `dbWiping` (CR-7-01): set by about.js `wipeDevice` while it deletes the database; a connection asked for then waits (`dbHeld`; a connection that opens meanwhile is closed at once): the page reloads once the database is gone, and a delete that fails lets them open (`dbUnhold`); a refusal instead was an unhandled rejection in WebKit
- start-up is `startUp()` (start.js; Round B, CR-7-09: it was one 2,000-character line run when IndexedDB answered, which could be before the scripts after start.js had loaded): index.html's last script, `<script>startUp()</script>` after about.js, calls it once every script has run; its steps in order: `loadStored` (the four reads), `applyStored`, `startChecks` (plan rules check, old-record fix-ups), `show('today')`, `startJobs` (setup, weather, holidays, kept photos → `appReady`, photo fix-ups, safety); any throw or unreadable store goes to `startFailed`; `afterScripts` (the load event) and `whenPulled` keep their timing
- a new file goes into index.html (before the `startUp()` line) and sw.js `ASSETS` in load order
- nothing a script runs at load may reach a name declared in a later script (function declarations are hoisted only within their own file); the split was checked mechanically for that (only `applyTheme` → `renderToday`, guarded by `window.appReady`, crosses)
- no `typeof X==='function'` (or `!=='undefined'`) guard for a function or `const` from an earlier script: it can never be false once that script has run, and it turns a rename into a silent no-op (ESLint's `no-undef` doesn't look inside `typeof`); Round B (CR-7-10) deleted 223 of them; the painter's files (assemble.js, asm-*.js) keep theirs, since its code fingerprints hash the functions' text and every picture key would change
- a guard on a name from a LATER script stays unless the guarded code can't run before that script (wave C2, 2026-11-22): what can run before script N is the top-level code of scripts 0..N-1, the callbacks they register (listeners, timers, promise chains, `backOn`) and their act.js handlers, going only through functions those scripts declare; callbacks handed to `afterScripts` / `whenPulled` / `afterDom` and `startUp()` run after every script
- history.js … ui.js keep 77 such function guards and 11 `!=='undefined'` ones (2026-11-22): a tap, a touch on Today, Back or the start-up sync's timer (sync.js, 300 ms after `connRestored`) can reach them while later scripts are still loading (`show`, `renderToday`, `openSettings`, `settingsSummaries`, `renderConnUI`, the picker)
- IndexedDB storage: items, day history, settings; generated photos in `meta` as `render:<combination key>`
- `txs` and `atomic` reject on `onabort` too (`txFail`): a transaction aborted by a full disk used to settle neither way, so the Add card just stayed; the rejection is the browser's error marked `dwStore`
- a lost database connection is opened again (2026-11-14, a tester's iPhone 17 Pro: the sample wardrobe, Confirm on Add and every save did nothing, silently, and each tap of the sample left a navy suit card on Add): iOS Safari can close a page's IndexedDB connection after the app was in the background, and every `transaction()` then throws `InvalidStateError` at once, which `storeFailure` didn't count; `txs` and `atomic` run through `dbRetry` (`dbLive`/`dbOpen`, `dbConn`, `dbDrop` on the connection's `close`/`versionchange`; `dbGone`: InvalidStateError, UnknownError, "connection/closing/closed"): opened again and retried once, else rejected marked `dwStore` ("your phone closed the app's storage. Close the app fully and open it again.", `storeFailText`); `db` stays the first opening; `save()` (add.js) wraps `saveCard()` and says any failure (the card stays) instead of rejecting; `kitSaveCards` takes a card that didn't save off Add and stops; `tests/safety.test.js` (also on WebKit)
- `loadImg` revokes its object URL once the picture has loaded
- working on it: every POST to the worker's AI endpoints (`/` tagging, `/tag-many`, `/pattern`, `/render` and item pictures, not `mode:'queue'`) is caught by a `fetch` hook (`busyLabelFor`) that shows `#busy`, a spinning pill above the tab bar, with what is running (`busyStart`/`renderBusy`); photos waiting in the background queue show there too
- `/describe` shows "Claude is finding the shade…" or "Claude is working out the weave…" (`busyLabelFor`)
- `.spin` is the ring, also in "Generating photo…", the Add card's busy state and "Claude is reading the pattern…"

#### The tab bar

- the bottom tab bar is compact so the picture and the tiles fit on one screen: 44px buttons (`nav button`), and it keeps only `--navpad` (the home-indicator inset minus 18px) below them; `--navh` is its height, used by the body padding and the toast
- it grows upward (`--navx`, its buttons taller) until its top is one `--gap` below Today's below-the-waist tiles, so nothing peeks out between them (`fitNav`, after each render, on resize and when Today's size changes); at most `NAV_MAX` (58, so the tallest bar is 102px), never on a screen where the tiles already reach it; the natural top is computed, not measured, since the height animates
- the fit is measured only on a day whose photo has loaded (before that the photo has no height and the bar jumped to its cap and stayed); that measurement is kept per screen size (localStorage `nav_fit2:<w>x<h>`) and used on days with only the drawing (whose Generate button pushes the tiles lower) and on weekends; the photo's `onload` refits; old `nav_fit:` values are dropped
- the fit is always taken as if the row had three tiles: with four (trousers, a coat) a picture tile is narrower and, its 4:3 picture following its width, shorter, which made the bar taller on those days; `fitNav` works out the three-tile height from each tile's picture shape (drawn tiles keep their height)

#### The precision swatch

- `tools/visit-measure.js` measures Today's picture on a phone end to end against the real worker (2026-11-14, the owner's ask): iPhone screen, 4G, CPU 4x, a first open, swipes and a reopen, frame by frame (drawing or picture, and when), read-only on the server; run it with DW_WORKER and DW_TOKEN (from the User variable DW_APP_TOKEN) and `--pages`; measured that day: reopen picture at 2.2 s (with the app), swipes 54-110 ms, no drawing, for days the PC built; a brand-new phone 74 MB and the first picture at 42 s; `--then "label=<checkout>;…"` adds reopens served from other checkouts (a comment added inside a painter function there stands in for a painter or master change: every look gets a new key): measured after such a change, without `asmStale` the drawing on every day, then the phone's own picture 2.8-11 s later; with it the outfit's last picture at once (swipes 64-112 ms), never the drawing
- Today shows the same outfit's last picture while its current one isn't here (`asmStale`/`asmShown` in assemble.js, used by `renderFor`; 2026-11-14, the owner: "I'm seeing it swap every time I swipe"): a painter, master or lighting change gives every look a new key; kept pictures carry `lid` (`asmLookId`: master + `comboKey`), set when kept and, for pictures kept before, when `asmFor` meets them; building ahead and "is it painted" still use the exact `asmFor`; `tests/asmcolor.test.js`
- `shrink()` draws every picture on white first (2026-11-14, the owner's socks: a transparent cut-out saved as a JPEG had a black backdrop); `fixBlackBackdrops` (tagging.js, start-up after `upgradePhotos`, once: `localStorage.black_bd`) makes white the edge-joined black of a photo saved before whose edge is nearly all black, unless the item itself is dark (colour luminance under 70); `tests/photoedit.test.js`
- the precision swatch (`item.swatch`, 128px, `cropSwatch`) is cut from the fabric close-up when there is one (`q.texCanvas`, white-balanced; the Add inspector's zoom/position sliders work on it), else from the item photo; `item.swSrc` says which ('tex'/'photo'); the editor re-cuts it when the close-up is added, replaced, removed or undone (`refreshSwatch`)
- a close-up swatch takes its lightness from the tagged colour (`matchLight`: each pixel keeps its lightness above/below the crop's median and its hue; near white the swing narrows so threads stay under paper white): a close-up shot in dim light made a white shirt grey; changing the colour (Add card `setq`, editor `sete`) re-cuts it
- `texSwatches()` re-cuts existing close-up swatches once (localStorage flag `tex_swatch2`)
- `swatchSVG` (draw.js) shows the close-up swatch for an item with `swSrc:'tex'` (stats); others stay drawn from the tags, never cut from the item photo
- every drawn small swatch uses `fabricDef(…,'dot')` (draw.js), a scale that reads at that size: two repeats of a band sett (T 8 of 16), three motif cells, two and a half weave repeats with 1.7× contrast

#### Wording and accessibility

- Today's pieces card says "1 piece"; the backup status, restore and roll-back messages say "1 item"
- plurals: "1 point behind" (stats.js `pts`), "planned about once" (analysis.js), "1 item", "1 band", "1 different look", "1 of 1 day locked"
- file pickers are `<input type="file" class="vfile">` inside their label (visually hidden, still focusable: with `hidden` the keyboard couldn't reach One item, Several items, Restore file, Replace photo, the close-up); `#photo-url` and `#cfg-city` have `aria-label`s

#### Tests

- `tests/ui.test.js`: dialogs (`askConfirm`), Settings wording and folds, the accent's contrast, the checklist's place, and no sideways scroll of the pages and setup steps at 320/390/626px in both themes
- `tests/addcards.test.js`: the quick bugs of the 2026-11-13 audit
- in a cloud container Playwright may not be in the worktree's `node_modules`: run with `NODE_PATH` set to the folder that holds the preinstalled one (`npm root -g` shows it; it has been `/opt/node22/lib/node_modules` and `/opt/node-tools/node_modules`); on the owner's PC it is in `node_modules`

### `back.js`

- the phone's Back is one history stack (Round B, the code review's CR-7-08, 2026-11-17: it was three systems, start.js's `appHist`/`appKind`/`appPend`/`appTop`…, itemsheet.js's `itemHist`/`itemBack` and the colour sheet's `colBackFirst` listener, each reading the others' flags); loaded right after app.js, in sw.js `ASSETS`; the only file that calls `history.pushState`/`history.back` or listens to `popstate`
- `BACK` holds our entries, oldest first, at most two: the item sheet's (kind 'item', `histPush`/`histDrop`) and one app entry whose kind says who holds it ('set', 'sub', 'setup', 'pick', 'shop', and the borrowers 'ask', 'cu', 'rd', 'kit', 'nps'); `backKind()` is the app entry's kind ('' with none)
- each page or sheet registers what Back does: `backOn(kind, f)` (start.js 'sub'/'set' → `pageBack`, itemsheet.js 'item', setup.js, history.js 'pick', shop.js, ui.js 'ask'/'cu', colpick.js 'rd', kit.js, add.js 'nps'); a kind with none does what 'set' does; the colour sheet, with no entry, is asked first (`backOver`), and `backLand` runs when our own step back lands (the item sheet's entry asked for meanwhile)
- the API: `backPush(kind)` (make the app entry, or take it over), `backDrop(kind)` (take it away with our own step back, if kind holds it), `backBorrow(kind)` → whose it was, `backReturn(kind, was, fromBack)` (a borrower closing), `backHold(keep, f)`, `backSettled()` (a promise while our step back is on its way), `backWanted()`
- the history calls are exactly those of the three systems before (proved at the PR by logging every `pushState`/`back` and popstate on 91 walks (438 steps an engine) through every page, sheet and overlay with Back and Escape, Chromium and WebKit, against main: the same calls and the same landings)
- `appHist`, `appKind` and `itemHist` are kept (`backSync`) only for the tests and the console; the app asks `backKind()`
- an ES module since 2026-11-23 (strict; `<script type="module" src="back.js">`): `BACK`, `BACK_ON`, `backPop`, `backSync` and the rest of its state are its own; its last lines bridge the API the classic scripts call (`backKind`, `backOn`, `backOver`, `backLand`, `backPush`, `backDrop`, `backBorrow`, `backReturn`, `backHold`, `backSettled`, `backWanted`, `histPush`, `histDrop`) and the three flags as getters with no setter (a test can read `appHist`, not assign it), and export the same names
- a new function another file calls must be added to back.js's bridge lines, to its `export`, and to its row in `tests/bridge.test.js` (`BRIDGE`, and `GROWN` with the reason, since the row is on main)

### `act.js`

- an ES module since 2026-11-24, its code unchanged, importing nothing; its bridge (`ACTS`, `ACT_FIRST`, `acts`, `actsFirst`, `actTaps`) is above `actListen()`, its first top-level call; a tap during load is still passed over until `DOMContentLoaded` (`actsIn`)
- a control tapped before its file has registered its handler (the scripts are deferred: the page takes taps while they load) is passed over, not a page error (`actsIn`, false until `DOMContentLoaded`; 2026-11-23); after that a name nothing registered is a page error as before
- such an early tap is dropped: the gear before settings.js, the theme button before theme.js and a tab before start.js do nothing; a tab once start.js has run is kept for after start-up (start.js `pageWant`); today.js `todayTabTap` keeps a guard on `show` (start.js, later) for the Today tab between the two
- the gear tapped before start-up has drawn Today is only noted (settings.js `setOpenTap`, `setWant`; start.js `pagesOn`) and `showWanted` opens Settings once Today is up (`setWanted()`), after the tab asked for; opened at once, Settings showed the defaults and a change made there (a workday toggled) was saved over the stored settings: laundry days, dark shirts and the rest went back to their defaults (found 2026-11-23, on main too), and Back there threw (`backOn('set')` is start.js's)
- a paste or a drop before start-up has drawn Today is passed over (add-paste.js `pasteUp`): `openAdd` is start.js's (a page error before it has run) and the stored wardrobe isn't read yet; a drop still has its default prevented, so the browser doesn't open the file
- what the markup's controls do, without inline handlers (Round C, wave C2-2, 2026-11-18; the plan is `C:\code\cr-2026-11-16\MODULES-PLAN.md`): markup names a handler (`<button data-act="dayTap" data-i="3">`) and act.js runs `ACTS.dayTap(el, ev)`; loaded right after back.js, in sw.js `ASSETS`; a leaf (it names no other script's names)
- a handler is a top-level `function name(el, ev)` in the file that writes the markup: `el` is the element with the attribute (an inline handler's `this`), `ev` the event (its `event`); it reads its arguments from `el.dataset` or the control (`el.value`, `el.checked`), never from text compiled into the markup
- each file registers its handlers once, as its last statement: `acts({dayTap, picsPick})`; a name registered twice, or one that isn't a function, throws at load; names are app-wide, so name a handler after its control (`dayTap`, `cityPick`), not after the function it calls
- top-level functions, not arrows written inside `acts({…})`: an edit to a handler then selects only the tests that run it; only a name added or removed is top-level code
- one attribute per event type, so one element can answer several: `data-act` is the click, `data-act-<type>` any other (`data-act-change`, `data-act-input`, `data-act-keydown`, `data-act-keyup`, `data-act-pointerdown`; `data-act-focus`, `data-act-load`, `data-act-toggle` for the three that don't bubble); these nine are the types inline handlers used (no `submit`: nothing used it); a new type is added to `ACT_TYPES` (it bubbles) or `ACT_OWN` (it doesn't)
- one listener per type on `document` stands in for bubbling (`actFire`): every element on the event's path (`composedPath`, as it was when the event was sent) with the attribute runs its handler, innermost first, as nested inline handlers did; a disabled control's handler doesn't run; `focus`, `load` and `toggle` are heard in the capture phase and run only the target's own handler
- a handler's return value is ignored: an inline handler that ended in `return false` calls `ev.preventDefault()` instead (a handler that happens to return a function's `false` must not start preventing the default)
- an inline handler's `event.stopPropagation()` becomes `ev.stopPropagation()`: the handlers further out are skipped, and so is every later listener on `document` and `window` (act.js calls `stopImmediatePropagation` for it: before, the event never reached them)
- the one difference from an inline handler: a listener added with `addEventListener` to an element between the control and `document` runs before the handler, not after, and if it stops the event the handler never runs; the element listeners that stop an event of a handled type today (2026-11-18): the no-photo sheet's Escape (`#nps-sheet` `keydown`, add-sheet.js `npsSwipe`), the compare view's zoom and arrow keys (checklist.js), and Today's after-swipe click guards (today-gestures.js; capture listeners, which held inline handlers back too): check them when a control inside them moves over
- `actsFirst({…})` registers handlers that run in the capture phase, before every listener below `document`: only for a handler that relied on running before such a listener; it also runs before the tap guards (add.js's and setup.js's capture listeners on `document`, added later), and when it stops the event nothing else sees it; nothing uses it yet
- the tap guards still hold a `data-act` click back: they stop the click in `document`'s capture phase, so act.js's bubble listener never hears it
- a handler that throws, or a name nothing registered, is a page error (`reportError`), as an inline handler's was, and the handlers further out still run
- a control and the elements around it that stop its events move over together: an inline handler has already run by the time a `data-act` handler inside it could stop the event
- an element that is not in the document gets no `data-act` handler from `el.click()` (an inline handler ran there)
- a `data-act` element that isn't a control by its tag (`ACT_CONTROL`: button, link, input, select, textarea, summary) needs a click listener of its own, as its inline handler was: without one a phone's tap on it is no click (C2-4, 2026-11-18, found on the sheets' backdrops: Chromium's phone emulation handed a tap on What to buy's backdrop, 5px above the sheet, to the sheet's head, and Playwright's WebKit sent no click for a tap on Settings' backdrop; Safari on an iPhone sends a click only to an element with a click handler of its own, and `document`'s doesn't count)
- `actTaps(root)` (act.js) gives every such element under `root` an empty click listener (`actNone`; twice on one element is once): act.js calls it once for index.html's own markup (it loads after the markup), and a file that writes a `data-act` onto a `<div>`, `<span>`, `<li>` or the like calls `actTaps(container)` after writing it
- index.html has no inline handler (C2-4, 2026-11-18; 63 before): each control's handler is in the file that owns what it calls, registered in that file's `acts({…})`: theme.js `themeTap`; settings.js `setOpenTap` (the gear), `setBackdropTap`, `setDoneTap`, `catTap` (`data-cat`), `vestPick`, `darkPick`; start.js `addBackTap`, `wardAddTap`, `tabTap` (`data-page`); today.js `todayTabTap`; add.js `addPhotosPick` (`data-multi` on "Several items"); add-sheet.js `addManualTap`, `npsCloseTap` (the ✕ and the backdrop); add-paste.js `linkKey`, `pasteTap`, `linkAddTap`; kit.js `importOpenTap`, `tourAgainTap`, `homeAgainTap`; history.js `pickBackdropTap`, `replanTap`; colpick.js `colBackdropTap`; shop.js `shopBackdropTap`; item-view.js `itemCloseTap` (Done and the backdrop), `itemStepTap` (`data-n`); pack.js `packOpenTap`; daysoff.js `countryPick`, `dayOffAddTap`, `occAddTap`; weather.js `cityKey`, `citySearchTap`, `myLocTap`, `unitsPick`; care.js `laundryPick`; photos.js `itemPicsTap`; ui.js `advTap`; sync.js `syncNowTap`; backupfile.js `backupSaveTap`, `backupFilePick`; snapshots.js `snapFocus` (`data-act-focus`), `rollBackTap`; about.js `updCheckTap`, `updNowTap`, `fbSendTap`, `delAskTap`; setup.js `setupAgainTap`
- `tests/actsindex.test.js` (Chromium and WebKit, touch): every one of index.html's controls tapped, changed, focused or typed into, with the functions the markup called wrapped on `window`: the first one that ran, its arguments, that it ran before `document` heard the click, Enter's default prevented, the file fields emptied, and what the app did where the real function can run; it finds controls by id, class or place, so its taps give the same 178 lines on the app from before the move; its backdrop taps are what fail without `actTaps`
- `tests/actsindex.test.js` also reads the markup: index.html's `data-act` elements that aren't controls by their tag are the six backdrops, and a script that writes a `data-act` onto such an element without calling `actTaps` fails it
- tests that found a control by its `onclick` text find it by its `data-act` and `data-*` instead (`#cfg-workdays button[data-i="5"]` in `tests/replay/scripts/W1-5-03.js`)
- the first controls on it (settings.js `dayTap` and `picsPick`, daysoff.js `occClear`, weather.js `cityPick`): Settings' Workdays and Blazer days buttons (`data-list`, `data-i`), the Outfit pictures choice (`data-act-change`), an occasion's × (`data-day`) and a city search result (`data-i`, its place in `_cities`)
- `tests/inlinehandlers.test.js` is a ratchet: `LEFT` holds how many inline handlers (` on<type>="`, comments included) each root script and index.html still has (409 on 2026-11-18, from 414); a file over its number fails, and so does one under it until the number is lowered, so a count only goes down; a file not listed has none; when two branches lower one file's number the merge takes the lower; asm-color.js and asm-gl.js (1 each) wait for C3 (the painter's fingerprint)
- its list of checked quoted values (`CHECKED`) also only shrinks: an entry whose handler is gone fails
- kit.js, setup.js and checklist.js have none left (C2-6, 2026-11-18: 22, 19 and 5 before)
- C2-7 (2026-11-22) moved Today over: today.js (20), history.js (7), scorecard.js (4), missed.js (4), month.js (3), start.js (1) and app.js (1) have no inline handler left
- today.js's handlers: `dayStepTap` (‹ ›, `data-n`), `wornTap`, `wxSetTap` and `wxRetryTap` (the weather links: they call, then `ev.preventDefault()`), `listTap` (the pieces box's head), `listFoldTap` (the open list's ground: `foldListTap(ev)`), `rowTap` (a row's button and its unfolded picture, `data-s`), `pickTap` (⇄ and the pocket square's +, `data-s`), `offTap` (− and the "Taken off" row's +, `data-slot`, `data-on`), `vestTap` (`data-on`), `addOneTap` (`data-type`), `tileTap` (`data-s`), `belowPicTap`, `swatchTap` (`data-s`), `figLoad` (the picture's `data-act-load`)
- history.js's: `pickItTap` (a piece in the picker, `data-slot` and `data-id`; an empty `data-id` is "None: I didn't wear one"), `pickCloseTap`, `pickNextTap`, `resetTap`, `pastEditTap`; scorecard.js's: `scoreTap` (the card's head and its open panel), `scoreSwapsTap` (the swaps' ground: stops the tap), `scoreFixTap` (a swap, `data-slot`, `data-id`: stops the tap, then wears the piece); missed.js's: `missNoTap`, `missPickTap`, `missOneTap` (`data-day`), `missOkTap`; month.js's: `monthTap` (the box's head and "Fold away"), `dayCellTap` (`data-t`); start.js's: `reloadTap`
- the Add card's dropdowns (app.js `S(list, value, field, at)`) write `data-act-change="cardSel"`, the card's `cardAt(q)` and `data-f`: the handler `cardSel` is in add-card.js, which owns `setq` and is `S`'s only caller (app.js names no later file's function)
- `offTap` keeps its slot in `data-slot`, not `data-s`: with `data-s` the keyboard's focus would move from − to the "Taken off" row's + after the redraw (`keepFocus` finds a control by `data-s` when its other `data-*` changed), and it did not before
- a tile under the figure is found again by its `data-s` (today.js `pickPiece`), where it was found by its handler's text
- Today's `<div>`s that take a tap (`rowTap` on a row's picture, `belowPicTap`, `listFoldTap`, `scoreTap` on the panel, `scoreSwapsTap`): `renderToday` calls `actTaps($('#today'))` right after writing the page, inside `keepFocus`'s draw; scorecard.js writes two of them and relies on that call
- none of C2-7's needs `actsFirst`: the month's after-drag and after-move click guards (today-gestures.js) are capture listeners on `#today`, which hold a `data-act` click back as they held an inline one; today-picture.js's click listener on `#today` (a tap on the picture closes the open swatch) leaves buttons alone, so it running before `swatchTap` changes nothing
- `tests/todayacts.test.js` (Chromium and WebKit, touch, the real planner): every kind of control C2-7 moved, used as a person does, with its effect; nesting (a swap leaves the card open, a row leaves the list open); › and the Add card's Style dropdown keep the focus by keyboard; each tap-taking `<div>` has a click handler of its own (an inline one before, `actTaps`' now); it reads none of the handlers' attributes and passes on the app from before the move (47 checks an engine there and here)
- Playwright's WebKit sends a click to those `<div>`s with or without `actTaps` (tried 2026-11-22), so a real tap in a test does not prove `actTaps` was called: `tests/todayacts.test.js` records which elements were given a click listener (`__ownClick`) instead
- tests find a piece in the picker by `.pick-it[data-id="…"]` and its slot by `data-slot`, an Add card dropdown by `select[data-f="type"]`
- `keepFocus` (app.js) finds a `data-act` control again after a redraw as it found an inline one by its handler's text (C2-5, 2026-11-18; `focusActs`, `focusAt`, `focusFind`): the same `data-act…` names and the same `data-k` / `data-c` / `data-s`, then every `data-*` equal (its arguments); when no control has every `data-*` equal, one with a `data-k` / `data-c` / `data-s` is still found by those, as before, and one without them is not found (an inline handler whose arguments changed wasn't either)
- an event a test makes up must bubble, as the browser's own does (`new Event('change',{bubbles:true})`): one that doesn't never reaches `document`, where an inline handler on the control itself ran (`tests/addcards.test.js`, `tests/photoedit.test.js`)
- so a `data-act` control's `data-*` are its identity for the keyboard's focus: a state kept in a `data-*` on it (one that changes between redraws) needs an `id`, or a `data-k` / `data-c` / `data-s`, for the control to keep the focus
- C2-5 (2026-11-18) moved the Add card, the "Add without a photo" sheet, the item page, its photo tools and the Wardrobe's type chips over: add-card.js (26), add-more.js (4), add.js (2), add-sheet.js (1), item-photo.js (9), itemsheet.js (9) have no inline handler left
- the helpers of other files that those screens call wrote inline handlers from the code they were handed until their own files moved: app.js `S` (C2-7), wardrobe.js `matRow` and `ivOn` (C2-8), ui.js `closeupBtn` and tagging.js `tagFailNote` / `tagNotes` (C2-9); none is left
- `tests/additemacts.test.js` (Chromium and WebKit): every kind of control C2-5 moved, used as a person does (taps, the real dropdowns, typing, the crop's slider and drag, a file chosen, Remove close-up without touch), with the effect each had; it reads none of the handlers' attributes and passes on the app from before the move (102 checks there and here); the Wardrobe chip and the item page's dropdown keep the focus by keyboard
- C2-9 (2026-11-22) moved the rest of the non-painter files over: ui.js (10), tagging.js (12), calendar.js (11), account.js (7), about.js (5), remind.js (3), own-lists.js (3), photos.js (2), pattern-read.js (2), safety.js (2) and dress.js (1) have no inline handler left (58); settings.js, daysoff.js and weather.js had none already
- ui.js registers `askNoTap` / `askYesTap` (a question's buttons), `connLinkKey` (`data-act-keydown`: Enter in the invite field connects, its default prevented), `connGoTap`, `connOwnTap`, `connOffTap` ("Connect" in a section that needs the server), `addConnTap` (under Add's "Several items") and `cuBtnTap` (a close-up button on a touch screen)
- `closeupBtn({label, cls, mode, cloth, aria, act, remove})` (ui.js) takes no code any more: `act` is the caller's own attributes, written out in full by the caller (`' data-act-change="photoCuFile" data-id="…"'`), `remove` the name of its registered Remove handler (`'photoCuRemove'`, `'cardCuRemove'`) or ''
- without touch the attributes go on the label's file field (its `change` runs the handler); on a touch screen they go on the button with `data-act="cuBtnTap"`, `data-mode`, `data-cloth` and `data-rm`: a button never fires `change`, so `cuBtnTap` reads the name back (`el.dataset.actChange`, `el.dataset.rm`), finds the handlers in `ACTS` and opens `closeupPick` with them
- a close-up's file handler is `function(el, ev, f)` and hands the file on with `cuGot(el, f, use)` (ui.js): the chooser's file `f`, or without touch the field's own first file, after which the field is emptied; the five of them: add-card.js `cardCuFile`, item-photo.js `photoCuFile` (the item page's Close-up, Add close-up, Retake close-up and the Look beta's on an item), colpick.js `cnRetakeFile` / `rdRetakeFile`, look.js `lkCuFileQ` (the Add card's, read at once)
- `closeupPick(o)` itself still takes functions (`file`, `remove`): colpick.js's reading and the tests call it directly
- tagging.js `tagNotes(q)`, pattern-read.js `settNote(q)` and safety.js `tagFailNote(q)` take only the card (no `qh(q)` text): their buttons carry `cardAt(q)` and find the card with `cardIx(el)`, as add-card.js's do
- tagging.js registers `tagRefineAgain`, `tagRefineUndo`, `tagPatternAgain`, `tagSet` (an offered type, color or pattern: `data-f`, `data-v`), `tagMeasUndo` (`data-f`), `tagNewCol`, `tagNewPat`; pattern-read.js `settTap`; `tagFailNote`'s "Try again" uses add-card.js's `cardRetryTag`
- safety.js registers `nostoreAgainTap` ("Try again" on the page shown when the wardrobe can't be opened: the page loads again)
- account.js registers `acctMyLinkTap`, `acctCreateTap`, `acctCopyTap`, `acctLinkFocus` (`data-act-focus`: the new link's field selects itself) and `acctPurgeTap` / `acctReissueTap` / `acctRevokeTap` (the invitation's id in `data-id`), after `acctStart()`
- about.js adds `delDeviceTap`, `delAllTap`, `delCancelTap` (the delete card's buttons)
- calendar.js registers `calConnectTap`, `calReadTap`, `calOffTap`, `calGuideTap` (`data-g`), `calKeepTap` / `calDismissTap` / `calUndoTap` (the event's id in `data-id`, escaped with `esc`; `jsq` no longer used there) and Today's `calReviewTap` / `calPackTap`
- remind.js registers `remindSwTap`, `careRemSwTap` and `remindTimePick` (`data-act-change`); dress.js `dressSwTap` (`data-k`): a switch's handler reads which way to turn from the switch's own `aria-checked` (the inline handler had the answer written in when drawn: the same value)
- `remindSwTap` calls `remindOn()` with no argument: `remindOn(time)` would take act.js's element for a time
- own-lists.js registers `custNewTap` (`data-kind` 'colour' / 'pattern') and `ownMatRmTap` (`data-t`, `data-m`, on the button as on its row); the own colors' and patterns' Remove keeps its own listener on `#custom-list` (`data-rm-kind`, fix round A)
- photos.js registers `itemPicLoad` (`data-act-load` on an item picture: `picBg` with its `data-k`); a picture that loads while its markup is not in the document yet gets no call (an inline `onload` did): `picBg` runs on the next draw's load instead
- the dependency ledger gained the files' edges to act.js, pattern-read.js / safety.js / tagging.js -> add-save.js (`cardAt`, `cardIx`), tagging.js -> add-card.js (`setq`) and tagging.js -> own-lists.js (`openColForm`, `openPatForm`): calls that were text in the markup before
- `tests/inlinehandlers.test.js`'s lint no longer asks for more than 40 quoted values in inline handlers (none left after C2-7, C2-8 and C2-9; the two painter files' handlers quote nothing): it asks for at least as many as its checked list holds, which is empty now
- a file field redrawn away while its picker is open still gets its handler (act.js `actFileGone`, C2-9): the click that opens the picker (the field's own or its label's, heard in `document`'s capture phase) gives a file field with `data-act-change` a `change` listener of its own, once (`ACT_FILES`), which runs the handler only when the field is no longer in the document by then; on the page `document`'s listener runs it as before
- why: the picker stays open for as long as the person takes, and a redraw meanwhile (another card read, a sync) replaces the field; an inline handler ran on the old field and the file went to its card or item (found by id); without this the file was dropped silently (the Add card's and the item page's close-up fields without touch, and since C2-5 the item page's Replace photo, also on a phone)
- a test that sets a file on a field it took off the page itself (`setInputFiles` opens no picker, so no click) gets no handler: `tests/paperstatus.test.js` marks the old paper line (`data-old`) instead of removing it, and waits for the item page's redraw before it takes the field
- `tests/restacts.test.js` (Chromium and WebKit, touch, then without touch for the file fields): every kind of control C2-9 moved, used as a person does (taps, Enter, typing, a file chosen), with the effect each had: the question's buttons, Connection, the owner's invitations against a made-up server, About's delete card, the reminder switches and time, the calendar (Connect, ✓, ✕, Undo, the toast's Open, Today's Review and Packing list, a guide, Read now, Disconnect), the dress code, the own lists, the Add card's notes on the second and third of three cards, the close-up chooser from the card and the item page, an item picture's load, and "Try again"; it reads none of the handlers' attributes and gives the same 96 lines on the app from before the move
- `tests/acts.test.js` (Chromium and WebKit): the first controls by real taps (it reads none of the handlers' attributes, and passes on the app from before the move); the same markup built with inline handlers and with `data-act`, the same clicks and keys to both, and the same handlers run in the same order (nesting, stopPropagation, preventDefault and `return false`, a handler that removes its element or throws, a disabled button, five event types on one field, focus, toggle, load); unknown and doubled names, `actsFirst`; every name in the app's markup registered and every registered name used
- C2-8 (2026-11-22) moved the Packing list, Laundry and cleaning, cost per wear, the Wardrobe's search, sort and grid, What to buy and Insights over: pack.js (14), care.js (13), cost.js (10), wardrobe.js (11), shop.js (9) and stats.js (5) have no inline handler left; each file's section lists its handlers
- wardrobe.js `matRow(type, cur, on, ai)` and `ivOn(id)` write `data-act` attributes now: `on` is the control's attributes, `matOn(q)` on an Add card (`matCardPick`, `data-card`) or `matOnItem(id)` on the item sheet (`matItemPick`, `data-id`); it was the start of a call (`setQField(` / `setItemField(`) compiled into the markup
- a handler another file's markup uses is registered where its markup is written: care.js's `careDueTap`, `careBandTap` and `careBandKey` are in markup today.js puts on Today, stats.js's `emptyAddTap` / `emptySampleTap` (`emptyAdd()`) also on the empty Packing list, cost.js's `cpwCardField` on the Add card
- Today's cleaning banner (`#cband`, a `<div role="button">`) gets its tap listener from care.js `careBandSoon` (`actTaps` on the picture's frame), which today-picture.js `swApply` runs after every draw of Today
- its tap now runs after `#today`'s own click listener (today-picture.js: a tap on the picture closes an open swatch), not before: the listener stops nothing and the banner's question reads only the plan, so the result is the same
- the Wardrobe's search field stops Escape (`wardSearchKey`, `ev.stopPropagation()`) without `actsFirst`: no `keydown` listener sits between the field and `document`, and the `document` and `window` listeners that close things on Escape are all added after act.js's
- `tests/packcare-taps.test.js` (Chromium and WebKit, touch, the real planner): every kind of control C2-8 moved, used as a person does (taps, typing, the real dropdowns, dates, Enter and Space on the banner, Escape in the search), with the effect each had; it reads no handler attribute and passes on the app from before the move (the same 63 checks an engine there and here)
- tests and replay scripts that found a Wardrobe box by its `onclick` find it by its class (`#wardrobe .giopen`, with `data-id`), and the Material dropdown by `select.wmat`

### `add.js`

- the Add page is eight files (Round C, 2026-11-17: add.js held every job), loaded in this order right after back.js in index.html and listed in sw.js `ASSETS`; the code moved line for line, nothing renamed; the facts below stay under this heading
- add.js: white balance and the card's pictures (`applyWb`, `cardPics`, `toggleWb`), close-ups (`addTexture`, `dropTexture`), One item (`addFiles`, `stopRead`, `refineAgain`, `patternAgain`), the double-tap guard (`ADD_CALM`), `needType`/`errToHand`/`skipCard`, Several items (`addMulti`, `multiFail`, `multiAgain`), `addNoteText`, `renderAdd` and `addBackOnline`
- add-paste.js: a pasted image or link, photo links (`fetchPhoto`, `addFromUrl`, `addLink`, `pasteFromClipboard`) and drag & drop
- add-card.js: a field changed (`setq`, `shirtColFix`), `tagSwInner`, `photoPair`, the shade rows (`shadeRow`, `stepShadeQ`/`matchShadeQ`, `stepShadeE`/`matchShadeE`), `cardSafe`/`plainCard`, the checklist (`CK_FIELD`, `matExtras`, `ckLr`, `ckCheck`, `addCkSecs`, `ckLeftLine`), `formCard`, `confBtn`, `busyCard`/`multiErrCard`/`errCard`/`typeCard`, `unreadNote`
- add-more.js: How many (`qtyRow`, `stepQty`), Bought on the way (`orderRow`, `setOrder`), the Arrives date (`arriveVal`, `setArrives`) and `checkArrivals`
- add-sheet.js: `addManual`, Today's "Add one" type (`addFor`, `renderAddFor`) and the "Add without a photo" sheet (`npsOpen` … `npsSwipe`, `backOn('nps')`)
- sheet-swipe.js: `sheetSwipe`, the swipe down of every sheet but What to buy's
- the Add card's controls use data-act (act.js, C2-5 2026-11-18): each carries its card's id as `data-card` (add-save.js `cardAt(q)`, beside `qAt` and `qh`) and its handler finds the card's place with `cardIx(el)` (= `qAt(id)`, as the inline `qh(q)` did); not `data-q`, which marks the card itself (`[data-q]` counts cards)
- add-card.js registers `cardSkip` (×, Skip, Cancel, Dismiss), `cardSave` (Confirm, `confBtn`), `cardStop`, `cardMultiAgain`, `cardByHand`, `cardRetryTag`, `cardMatOffer`, `cardMatGuess`, `cardCheck` (`data-sec`), `cardCuRemove`, `cardRdStart`, `cardPatTile` (`data-v`), `cardTypePick` (the "What is it?" dropdown, `data-act-change`), `cardSwatch` and `cardField` (`data-act-input`, `data-f`), and the shade row's `shadeStep` / `shadeMatch`
- `shadeRow(o, src, id, canMatch)`: `src` 'q' (an Add card, `id` its id) or 'e' (the item page's item); it was handed the two handlers as code
- add-more.js registers `cardQty` (`data-d`), `cardOrder` (`data-on`: what the button said when drawn) and `cardArrives`; add.js `cardWb` and `saveAllTap`; add-sheet.js `npsCancelTap`
- the dependency ledger (`tests/deps.json`) gained the six files' edge to act.js (`acts`) and add-card.js -> own-lists.js (`matOffer`, `matGuessUse`: the call was text in the markup before, which the ledger can't see)
- a handler that calls a function taking an optional argument is its own small function (`npsCancelTap` → `npsCancel()`, `cropApply` → `applyCrop(id)`): act.js hands every handler `(el, ev)`, which `npsCancel(fromBack)` would read as Back
- add-save.js: `cardReading`/`cardReady`, `saveAll`, `savedTitle`, `cardOf`/`qAt`/`qh`, `save`, `saveCard`
- add-keep.js: the cards kept when the app is closed (`ADDQ_*`, `addCardRec`, `addKeep`, `addKeepSoon`, `addRevive`, `addRestore`, `addWatch`)
- the eight are plain scripts in one global scope: only declarations at the top, besides the document listeners (the double-tap guard's click in add.js; paste and drag & drop in add-paste.js), added in their old order, and add-sheet.js's `backOn('nps')`; no top-level initializer reads a name from a later file, and each top-level `let` is written only in its own file (`tests/globals.test.js`)

- Add asks "One item" (`#add-one`, `addFiles(files)`; paste and drag too) or "Several items" (`#add-group`, `addFiles(files,true)` → `addMulti`)
- several photos as "One item" each get their card at once and are tagged `ADD_PAR` (3) at a time (`addFiles`)
- fabric close-ups: `TEXTURE_TYPES` (core.js: suit, blazer, pants, shirt, tie) can have a second photo `item.texture` (a close-up of the cloth, `TEX_MAX` 1536px); a card for those types shows "Step 2 · Fabric close-up" (`texSlot`/`addTexture`), optional
- Step 2 · Fabric close-up shows the 3-step white-paper card (item-photo.js `paperSteps`) in place of the paper sentence and the "Tips for a good close-up" link (2026-11-15); on a touch screen Add close-up / Replace close-up open the close-up chooser (ui.js `closeupBtn`, Remove close-up inside it, calling `dropTexture`), without touch they are the file window and Replace has its Remove button
- Step 2 has a second button directly to the right of Add close-up (`.rdpair`, colpick.js `rdAddBtn`): "Add close-up and read texture/color" (plain beside the filled Add close-up, its words on two lines at phone width), which opens the close-up chooser (mode 'read', "Close-up for Claude") and then reads it; with a close-up it is "Read color and texture" beside Replace close-up; a close-up added through it skips the automatic re-tag (`addTexture(i,f,true)`: measured, not re-tagged; the owner: no second paid call)
- an item card on Add has a × in its top right corner (`.qx`, 44px, aria-label "Close") that does what its Cancel / Skip at the bottom does (`skipCard`), so a long card can be dropped without scrolling down (the owner, 2026-11-15); `tests/wardrobetap.test.js`
- an Add card keeps one close-up canvas (`q.raw.tex`, the larger of `TEX_MAX`/`CLOSE_MAX`), and Confirm lets go of the card's canvases (`q.raw`, `q.big`, `q.texCanvas`) once saved
- an Add card's item photo is made from its sharp copy (`raw.full`, `TAG_MAX`) shrunk to `PH_MAX` (`cardPics`); made from the 800px measuring copy `q.big`, every new item's photo was 800px (audit 2026-11-13)
- automatic white balance on new Add photos (`wbGains` in tagging.js, `applyWb`): gains from the brightest near-neutral pixels (≤35% saturated, 0.5–85% of the frame), capped at −15%/+18% per channel, skipped under 2%; a card's close-up gets its item photo's gains; the card has Show original (`toggleWb`, originals kept in `q.raw` until saved); close-ups added later in the editor are not corrected
- the "As tagged" swatch (`tagSwInner`) splits when there is a close-up: drawn colour on the left half, the close-up on the right (`.swsplit .swtex`)
- the Add card's swatch box reads "Fabric swatch · From the photo / From the fabric close-up: zoom and move it to a clean patch of cloth", with Zoom, Left–right, Up–down
- the Add card's and the editor's Vest choice show "No vest" unless the suit has one (`hasVest`)
- Confirm and "Save all" are switched off while Claude reads a card (`cardReading`: `q.refining` or `q.patternReading`), and `save`/`saveAll` return meanwhile: saved during the reading the card kept the weaker tags and the pattern call was still paid for
- bought, on the way: the Add card has "Bought, on the way" (`orderRow`/`setOrder`) with an optional Arrives date (`setArrives`); `save()` stores it paused; the grid tag reads "On the way · <date>", the open item can change the date (`setItemArrives`, itemsheet.js) or "It arrived: put in rotation"
- `checkArrivals()` (start-up and `newDayCheck`) puts items back in rotation the day after their date, with a toast; it also brings back pieces that were away being cleaned (`item.away`): `cleaned` = the date they were due back, and the toast says they are back (`careBackText`)
- the Add tab's note while not connected (`addNoteText`): "Photos won't be read automatically. Paste your invite link in Settings → Connection, or add items by hand."
- one save per card (audit 2026-11-15, C2): Confirm calls `save('<card id>')` (`cardOf`: an id, or a place for the sample/import/tests), `q.saving` makes a second tap do nothing and switches Confirm off; `saveCard(q)` takes the card itself and returns true; Skip/Cancel/Dismiss are `skipCard(id)`; `saveAll` runs once at a time (`saveAllOn`, the button reads "Saving…") over the cards by id, last first
- every handler drawn on an Add card finds the card by its id when tapped (CR-1-08, fix round A): the markup calls `qAt('<id>')` (`qh(q)`), the card's place in the list at that moment (-1 once gone), never the place it had when drawn; `cardOf(k)` takes an id or a place, and `setq`, `setQField`, `stepQty`, `ckCheck`, `setOrder`, `setArrives`, `toggleWb`, `addTexture`, `dropTexture`, `stepShadeQ`, `matchShadeQ`, `refineAgain`, `patternAgain` go through it; element ids (`qconf-<i>`, `qty-n-<i>`, `tagsw-q<i>`, `swatch-img-<i>`) are still the place at the last redraw; `tests/cardid.test.js`
- "How many": the item and its copies are written in one `txs('items')` transaction (a kill after "Confirm 12" saved 3)
- every "How many" copy keeps the sharp tagging copy and the sharp close-up on this device under its own id (`keepTagImg`/`keepCloseImg` for each, CR-1-04: only the first had them, so crop, rotate and the readings used the 1536 px photo on the others); `tests/cardid.test.js`
- a type change on the Add card drops a material the new type can't have (itemsheet.js `matTidy`, also in `saveCard`; CR-1-06: a jacket read as tweed and made a tie was saved "Other · tweed"); `tests/cardid.test.js`
- Undo of "Saved" gives the card its canvases back (kit.js `kitUnsave` → `addRevive`, as after a kill; CR-1-03: it came back without its Fabric swatch box, and a close-up reading did nothing); `tests/cardid.test.js`
- waiting cards survive a kill (H1): meta `addq` holds every card's form (`addCardRec`: no canvases or in-flight state), `addpic:<id>` its photo, sharp copy and close-up, written `ADDQ_SOON` (100 ms) after a change (`addKeepSoon`, from `renderAdd`, `setq`, `stepQty`, `setQField`), at once when a photo's pictures are made (before the reading), and on `visibilitychange` hidden/`pagehide` (`addWatch`); never while the sample/import runs (`kitQuiet`) and not before `addRestore` (from `safetyStart`) has read them back; restored cards (`addRevive`) get their canvases from the kept pictures (no Show original), a card cut off mid-reading says "The app was closed before this photo was read." with Try again (nothing is read again by itself), a card whose id is already in the wardrobe is dropped, orphan `addpic:` records go; a toast "The N cards you hadn't saved are back on Add." with Open; `tests/addcards.test.js`
- new cards (photo or by hand) go to the top and are scrolled into view (`addShowCard`, again when the first photo's card is read)
- a photo card keeps its photo whatever the request does: only a file that can't be opened (`q.err='open'`) or is under `ADD_MIN_PX` (32; close-ups `TEX_MIN_PX` 200) is refused, with "Fill it in by hand" (`errToHand`) and Dismiss; WebKit's offline emulation also blocks blob pictures, so there it shows "couldn't be opened" (a harness artefact)
- a card nobody read (not connected, or the reading failed) asks "What is it?" first (`needType`, `typeCard`): no Confirm, Save all skips it and says so; choosing the type measures the colour from the photo (`measureGuess`); a photo added before the connection landed offers "Read it now" (`unreadNote`)
- back online, cards whose reading failed for want of a connection (`q.tagNet`) are read again by themselves (`addBackOnline`, the `online` event)
- Several items: the photo's card has Cancel (an AbortController); a failure keeps the photo on the card with the reason in plain words (`multiFail`: offline, the server's `message`, never a code) and Try again (`multiAgain`, the same photo) / Dismiss; the summary counts pairs ("2 pairs of trousers", `countWord`), adds up photos picked together, and says when the 40-item cap (`MULTI_CAP`) was reached
- no model name anywhere a tester reads: the note says "Photos are read automatically…", cards "Tagged by Claude." (`aiLabel`, app.js)
- the Saved toast names the item (`savedTitle`: its name, else its colours with the type, "Saved: Grey suit."); Undo of a Save all clears the "Saved N items" line (kit.js `kitUnsave`)
- "Bought, on the way" refuses a past Arrives date (`setArrives`); the close-up step says to tap the paper on the camera screen before taking the picture; a belt's metal is "Buckle" on the card and in the item sheet
- one Arrives rule for the Add card and the item sheet (`arriveVal`: '' / 'past' / the date; `ARRIVE_PAST`): the item sheet's date field starts at today (`min`) and `setItemArrives` refuses a day gone and keeps the date it had (CR-1-07: it took one and put the piece in rotation at once); `tests/pickerfocus.test.js`
- fix round 2 (2026-11-15 re-audit, Fixer 2; `tests/addcards.test.js`):
- in-flight flags are never kept (`ADDQ_SKIP` has `refining`, `patternReading`, `stopped`): `addCardRec` keeps a close-up reading cut off as `refineCut`, a pattern reading as `patternCut`; restored, the card says so with "Read the close-up again" (`refineAgain`) / "Read the pattern again" (`patternAgain`), nothing read by itself; Confirm and Save all work (N3: it stayed on "Tagging again with the close-up…" with both off for good); old records' flags are converted in `addRevive`
- a restored card's close-up is its `raw.tex` again with `texDone` (already corrected: `texGains` and `closeTagImg` don't correct it twice), so it can be read again
- the card is kept at once when its photo is read, when a close-up reading starts or ends, when a pattern reading ends, on a Try again or Stop, on a How many tap or a field change (`queueMicrotask(addKeep)` in `setq`/`stepQty`) and before a Confirm's save (not during Save all); other changes after `ADDQ_SOON` (100 ms; it was 400)
- `renderAdd` no longer closes Settings (a reading returning or `addBackOnline` shut it mid-use); `show()` and `saveSettings` close it
- Add's note while not connected (`addNoteText`): "Checking your invitation…" while `inviteChecking`, "Your invitation is checked once you're back online…" while it waits offline, else "Photos won't be read automatically…"
- Undo of "Saved" puts the cards back and keeps them (`addKeep`) before taking the items out (kit.js `kitUnsave`): a kill between leaves the item, and the kept card of a saved item is dropped
- Several items: the photo is kept before it is read (`multiPic`, a JPEG of the 2576 px copy, in `ADDQ_PICS`; `multiErr` is kept too); restored it says "This photo wasn't read. The app was closed before this photo was read." (`multiCut`) with Try again; one that failed for want of a connection (`multiNet`) is read again on `online`
- Several items: a type the app doesn't plan (a hat) is left out and the summary says so (`oddWord`; `normTag` made it a suit); the server's "Try again later, or add the item by hand." is cut from a failure (`multiFail`); Dismiss of the last photo clears the "Each item…" line; `countWord` says "4 watches"
- a photo being read (one item, or Try again in safety.js `retryTag`) has Stop (`stopRead`, `q.abort`; `ask(img,{signal})` in app.js, an `AbortError` sets no `aiError`): the card keeps its photo with "You stopped the reading." (`STOPPED`) and Try again
- a double tap on Confirm: a trusted tap within `ADD_CALM` (450 ms) and 40 px of a Confirm tap on Add whose card was saved is dropped (capture listener, armed in `saveCard`; any other tap ends it): the list closed up under the finger and the second tap opened "Import from a spreadsheet"
- a hand card's close-up says "it is saved with the item" (no split swatch) and flags a blurry one (`texSlot`); a restored card says "Colours corrected for the light." without Show original (`wbWas`)
- with the invitation ended the read-again buttons stay away: no "Read the close-up again" / "Read the pattern again" (tagging.js `tagLive`), no Try again on a "Several items" card (`multiErrCard`); the note names the ended invitation (`addNoteText`)

- (re-audit 2026-11-15) Add's note says "Checking your invitation…" while a link is checked (`inviteChecking`) and names an ended invitation; with the invitation ended a card gets no Try again (safety.js `tagFailed`, `tagFailNote`) and no "Read it now" (`unreadNote`, `typeCard` use `isConnected()`)

#### The Add page layout and its sheets (2026-11-15, the owner's choice: variant A of `C:\code\audit-2026-11-13\add-page-mockups\`, icon 1)
- under One item / Several items a long full-width button "Add without a photo" / "Pick the type and colors yourself" (`#add-manual`, `.addlong`) with the hanger-and-pencil icon (`.aic`, the tiles' 1.8 line); "Other ways to add" keeps the link box and "Import from a spreadsheet" (`#add-import`) as its last row; the old "Add without photo" row is gone
- "Add without a photo" (`addManual`) opens its card in a bottom sheet, `#nps-sheet` / `#nps-backdrop`, inside `#add` so the card keeps Add's styles: grabber (`.nps-grab`), the icon and title in `.nps-head`, × (`.nps-x`), the card in `#nps-q`, Confirm and Cancel pinned in `#nps-bar` (Confirm keeps the id `qconf-<i>`)
- the sheet's card stays in `queue` (found by its id, as every card); `npsId` says which card the sheet holds; `renderAdd` leaves it out of `#queue` and draws it with `npsDraw` → `formCard(q,i,true)` (no card ×, no `.btns`); a card no longer in the queue (saved, skipped) closes the sheet (`npsDraw` → `npsClose`)
- one card at a time: `addManual` while the sheet holds a card shows that card again; a second card for a test: `queue.unshift({id,err:'open'});errToHand(id)` (a list card)
- top `--ivtop`, from 600px `max(4vh, var(--ivtop))` (below the status bar, as the item sheet), at most 640px wide and centered, slides up from below (transform transition), z-index 1100/1101 (under the color, reading, close-up and kit sheets and questions)
- closing without saving (`npsCancel`): ×, Cancel, the backdrop, Escape, a swipe down (`sheetSwipe`: the header at any time, the card while scrolled to the top) and Back (`npsBack`); a changed card (`npsSig` differs from `npsSnap`, taken at opening: the kept form `addCardRec` and the close-up) asks "Discard this item?" (Keep editing / Discard, `askConfirm` danger); untouched it closes at once and the card goes
- Back: the sheet borrows Add's entry (back.js `backBorrow('nps')`, kept in `npsWas`; `backOn('nps')` → `npsBack`); on a changed card Back makes the entry again (`backPush('nps')`) for the question to borrow, so Back closes the question, the next asks again (`tests/backgesture.test.js`)
- the page around it is inert while it is up (`npsInert`: header, tab bar and #add's other children; ui.js `layerInert` keeps that when a layer over the sheet closes); `body.nps-on` lifts the toast above its bar
- leaving Add with the sheet up (`show` → `npsLeave`, before the 'sub' entry is dropped) closes it; a changed card stays on Add as a list card, an untouched one goes; a card brought back by Undo or after a kill is a list card too
- after Confirm the sheet closes and kit.js's "Saved: <name>." toast with Undo shows; `tests/addsheet.test.js` (Chromium and WebKit, touch, 390 light and 690 dark, a stand-in status bar)
- `sheetSwipe(el,{head,body,backdrop,live,skip,close,slide,spring,fadeBack,drag})` (add.js) is the one swipe-down of every sheet but What to buy's (CR-1-09: four copies were one each): the no-photo and import sheets, the color, pattern and reading sheets (colpick.js `cpSwipe`) and the item sheet (itemsheet.js `ivSwipe`, from `openItem`); installed once per sheet (`data-swipe`); its handle at any time, the rest only while `body()` (else the sheet) is at its top; a mouse, trackpad or Pencil drags it by the handle (Pointer Events, a 4px dead zone, captured once it moves; not from a button, link or label); never from a text field or `skip`; `slide`: it slides away from where it was let go (`.cpgoing`), `spring`: an animation back, `fadeBack`: the backdrop fades back by an animation (else its transition is held while the sheet follows); shop.js `shopSwipe` is still its own (Fixer 5's file in fix round A)

#### The Add card checklist (2026-11-16, the owner's approved redesign; checklist.js)
- an item card (`formCard`, a list card or the no-photo sheet's) is a checklist: its title ("New item · check and confirm" and `savedTitle`, list cards only), "N of M checked" with a bar (`.ckprog`), then two columns from 600px (`.ckcols`): the photo pair, Show original, "Claude filled these in. Check each part, or fix it.", `tagNotes` and Texture match on the left; Type, Colors, Pattern, Fabric & fit (Details for non-fabrics), More on the right
- `.ckprog` is a direct child of the card, `position:sticky` (top 4px on the page, 0 in `#nps-q`), with a soft bottom shadow: it stays pinned the whole way down the card, over both columns from 600px
- sections come from `addCkSecs(q,i)` ({k, n counted, warn, mark, title, sum, body, open}); M counts Type, Colors (when the type has color rows), Pattern (fabrics), Fabric & fit (when the type has a field there) and Texture match only once a close-up exists (4 → 5); More is not counted; there is no separate close-up item
- `q.checked` {tex, type, colors, pattern, fabric} (kept with the card, left out of `npsSig`): "Looks right" / "Fine as is" (`ckLr`, `.cklr`, `aria-pressed`, `ckCheck`) checks a section, folds it and opens the next unchecked one; a field changed by hand checks its section (`CK_FIELD` in `setq`, material in wardrobe.js `setQField`)
- marks: checked ✓; unsure ! (`q.conf` below high, or for Colors a new-color offer, an automatic or flagged measurement; for Pattern a new-pattern offer; Texture match a drawing off the cloth); Fabric & fit ✦ while it holds Claude's material (`matAI`); else ○; sections Claude was unsure about open first (with nothing unsure, the first part not checked yet); Texture match always opens
- Texture match before a close-up: "Not yet · add a close-up to compare", True color in 3 steps (`paperSteps`), then the primary "Add close-up and read color, texture & material" with its cost inside (`.ckreadadd`, `rdStart('q')`: the chooser in mode 'read', then the reading; 2 lookups for testers) and under it the outlined "Add close-up" (`closeupBtn`, `.ckadd`, `addTexture`: re-tags)
- Texture match after: the comparison (checklist.js), the match bar with Match close-up / Undo (which also checks the section), the white-paper line with Retake close-up, the blurry note (no-photo cards), Replace close-up (the chooser with Remove close-up on touch) and, without touch, Remove close-up (`.ckrm`), then Read color and texture (`rdAddBtn`, `rdCostLine`, `rdDoneHtml`) and Fine as is
- Pattern: Claude's pick (`q.aiTag.pattern`) and its second choice (`q.altPattern`) as drawn tiles (`.ckpt`, `role=radio`, `swatchSVG(patVar(...),true)`; "Your choice" when another is set), then "All patterns" (the picker, `patBtn`)
- Fabric & fit: metal / buckle, cuffs, vest, season, kind, style, leather, the watch's fields, Material with Claude's suggestion (`matRow` … `q.matAI`: "Material suggested by Claude"), and the Fabric swatch zoom / position box (`.sw-box`)
- More: Name, Brand, Notes (no longer behind "More details"), Bought, on the way (`orderRow`) and How many (`qtyRow`)
- the footer line "N left to check · you can still confirm" / "All M checked · ready to confirm" (`ckLeftLine`, `.ckleft`) over Confirm and Skip / Cancel (in `#nps-bar` on the no-photo sheet); Confirm is never blocked by unchecked parts, only while Claude reads or it saves (`confBtn`)
- from 600px a list card is wider than the page's 560px column (`min(100vw - 32px, 880px)`, centered)
- the old "Step 2 · Fabric close-up" (`texSlot`) and the card's "More details" fold are gone (`moreRow`/`toggleMore` deleted in fix round A)
### `itemsheet.js`

#### Its files (Round C, 2026-11-17: split by job, no behavior change)
- three classic scripts in itemsheet.js's old place, in this order in index.html and sw.js `ASSETS`: `item-photo.js` (`PH_MAX`, the small copy `PH_SM`/`phSm`/`makeSmall`/`smallPhotos`, the photo tools row `photoTools`, the white paper `paperSteps`/`paperHint`/`paperLine`, `setPic`/`picUndo`, replace, rotate, `undoPhoto`, zoom & crop `cropSource`…`applyCrop`), `item-view.js` (the sheet: `editing`, `wardList`, `noMotion`, its Back entry, `itemShown`, `itemSynced`/`itemGoneTell`, `renderItem`, `openItem`/`closeItem`/`itemFocusBack`/`dropItem`, the swipe down `ivSwipe`, ‹ › `stepItem` and the sideways swipe and keys), `itemsheet.js` (`E`, `phEdit`, the item page's body `itemBody`, `ivSummary`/`ivDetailSum`/`ivCareSum`, the edits `sete`, `TYPE_FIELDS`/`matTidy`/`typeTidy`, `patSummary`, `redetect`, `setItemArrives`, `togglePause`, `clearStripes`, `rm`, the swipe between type chips `wardTypes`/`stepType`, `wardPic`, `renderWardrobe`)
- every line of the old itemsheet.js is in exactly one of them, in its old order (only the file headers are new); each `let` sits in the file that assigns it (`editing` in item-view.js; `phEdit`, reset by `itemBody` and `renderWardrobe`, in itemsheet.js), so `tests/globals.test.js` gains no cross-file write
- the item page's controls use data-act (act.js, C2-5 2026-11-18), the item by its id in `data-id`: itemsheet.js registers `itemPick` (the `E` dropdowns, `data-act-change`, the field in `data-f`), `itemDetails`, `itemReadPat` (`data-how` 'reread' or 'detect'), `itemGeneric`, `itemArrives`, `itemPause` (`data-want`), `itemDup`, `itemDelete` and the Wardrobe's type chips `wardChip` (`data-t`); item-photo.js `photoFile`, `photoRotate`, `photoCrop`, `photoCuRemove`, `paperTipTap`, `cropDrag` (`data-act-pointerdown` on `#cropbox`), `cropZoom`, `cropApply`, `cropDiscardTap`
- `cropDrag(box, e)` takes the frame as act.js hands it (an inline handler's `event.currentTarget`; under one listener on `document` that would be the document)
- a field's name is in `data-f`, not `data-k`: `data-k` alone finds a control again for `keepFocus`, and the same dropdown of another item must not take the focus
- `setFlt(t)` (app.js since S1a, 2026-11-25; it was itemsheet.js's) sets the Wardrobe's type filter `flt`: itemsheet.js's chips' handler and `stepType` and wardrobe.js's All chip call it
- what runs at load is only item-view.js's `backLand`/`backOn` and the two swipe blocks (the sheet's in item-view.js, then the Wardrobe's in itemsheet.js, so their keydown listeners are added in the old order), and none reads a name from a later file

- the Wardrobe editor can replace, rotate, zoom-crop and undo an item's photo without touching its tags (`photoTools`, `setPhoto`)
- the photo tools are one row right under the pictures, always shown (2026-11-15, edit-photos-mockups option B, the owner's go; the "Edit photos" fold, `togglePhotoTools` and `phEdit.open` are gone): Replace · Rotate · Crop · Close-up (`.phtools`, `.ptbtn` with `.pt-replace`/`.pt-rotate`/`.pt-crop`/`.pt-closeup`), each a drawn icon (`PT_ICON`) over a short label, 58px tall (3.625rem), as many columns as buttons; full names for screen readers ("Replace photo", "Rotate the photo a quarter turn clockwise", "Zoom and crop the photo", "Replace close-up" / "Add fabric close-up": `aria-label`, on the file input for the label-wrapped ones); no photo: "Add photo" alone (plus Close-up); not a fabric: no Close-up; `tests/editphotosb.test.js`
- the row's Close-up is `closeupBtn` (ui.js, which takes `aria` for the name and markup in `label`): on a touch screen the close-up chooser (Take / Choose / Remove / Cancel); without touch the file window itself, with "Remove close-up" as a quiet 44px button under the row (`.ptmore .ptrm`)
- every photo change has Undo in its toast, and the editor's Undo button is gone: "Photo rotated.", "Photo replaced. …", "Photo cropped." (Apply), "Crop saved." (left without Apply), "Close-up added.", "Close-up removed."
