- the materials update (2026-11-15, the materials audit): `ASM_CM_MAT` gained velvet [1.2, 1.45] (deep folds, a strong pile sheen), sheen [1, 1.3] (mohair, silk blend, polyester blend), tropical [1, 1] (tropical wool), stretch [1, 1.2] (a performance stretch shirt); corduroy, moleskin, camel hair, melton, loden and alpaca are soft; jersey a knit; seersucker, a cotton suit or blazer and a cotton tie matt like chinos (a cotton tie had the silk shine); the new cloths are read from the material only (a description could name any of them); changing `asmCmMat` changed every look's key (the code fingerprint), so the pictures repaint once
- a plain corduroy or seersucker jacket, waistcoat or trousers with no close-up to paint from is routed `cord` / `sucker` (`asmPileKind` in `asmCmRoute`; a close-up's own patch wins, `asmCuWants`) and painted with `asmPileTile` (`ASM_PILE`: corduroy 12 rounded wales down the garment over 30 mm with the pile's streaks; seersucker 4 repeats of a flat and a puckered stripe in the one colour over 24 mm; light swing `amp` as `ASM_WT`'s); its key carries `/pcord` or `/psucker` (`asmCmWeaveTag`); striped seersucker keeps its read bands (no pucker yet); velvet has no texture, only its light
- a tie's sheen lies where the tie itself is lit (`ASM_TIE_LIT`, `asmTieLit`: none below .7 of its lit level, so the knot's dimple and the folds shape it), times the material's highlight strength; it was one even band down the blade
- filtered tile reads (`asmCmMips`, `asmCmRead`, `ASM_CM_MIPS` 4, `ASM_CM_LOD0` 2; the shader's `tileRead`/`tileLv`, `uTO[5t+L]`, the levels stacked to each tile's right in `asmGlAtlas`): a pixel's 2x2 or 3x3 read points each take the tile level whose texels are as far apart as the points (blended between two levels; exactly as before while the points are under 2 texels apart); at the tie, the shirt's tile (solved, sleeves and the woven 3x3), read trousers along the legs and the suit cloth's band tile; the single-point reads (an upright shirt, a turned cuff's one point) are as before
- a `${...}` or a `//` comment pasted into asm-gl.js through a script needs care: the shader is one template string per line of many statements (see the bullet below)
- a comment inside a line of asm-gl.js cuts off the rest of that line (the file's lines hold many statements): it dropped `uJl` and every dark jacket differed from the CPU; `tests/asmgl.test.js` caught it

#### Cloth from the close-up (2026-11-14; the owner: his brown suits "just look bland", approved from before/after sheets of every item it changes)
- a jacket, waistcoat or trousers routed `flat`, `twill` or `plain` whose item has a close-up (`asmCuWants`) is painted with a tile cut from that close-up (`asmCuTile` → `cloth[z].suit`, in `asmCmJob`; `asmCuTag` in `asmCmSig`); striped, checked, herringbone, tweed and linen cloths keep their own painters, and an item with no close-up is painted as before
- `asmCuPatch`: the largest clean square of cloth on a 420 px copy (all cloth, light even within `ASM_CU.even`, no line over `ASM_CU.edge` times the cloth's usual: seams measured 10-24 times, clean cloth 4-7), the sharpest such window; none clean: no tile
- `asmCuTile1`: the square at 256 texels, its light flattened at 1/`ASM_CU.flat` of its side, made to tile without mirroring (`asmCuWrap`), its detail laid on the item's tagged colour (the tile's mean is that colour; the close-up gives texture only), drawn `ASM_CU.zoom` (3) times life size, since a fine weave at life size is under a picture pixel
- (the owner: "what happened to medium gray, it looks like a disaster") flattening at a seventh of the square left the close-up's soft blotches and crease shadows in the tile, which repeated as a patchwork; hence `ASM_CU.flat` 18
- (the owner: "Half strength for medium grey") a cloth whose pattern or description says fine or worsted (`asmCuFine`) shows its grain no stronger than `ASM_CU.fine` (.5 swing); other cloths show theirs as in the close-up, brought up to `ASM_CU.show` when fainter
- (the owner: "Leave the current painter for black solid color suits") cloth darker than `ASM_CU.dark` is not taken from the close-up (`asmCuDark`): his black suit lost its ribs and went plain
- a new close-up or a retagged colour repaints the item's looks by itself (the tag holds the close-up's length and tail, the key the colour); a change to `ASM_CU` or the functions repaints all (they are in `asmCmCode`); tests/asmcu.test.js
- (the owner, same day: a new Light Grey close-up "painting without any texture") `asmCuPatch` counted a well-lit light grey's light threads as white paper (27% of the picture, scattered), found no all-cloth window and the suit fell back to flat colour; paper is now a solid area (`ASM_CU.solid` of its 13 px surroundings) brighter than `ASM_CU.paper` times the picture's 30th percentile of light
- (the owner, same day, from bench sheets of his Grey Herringbone at full, double and triple strength: "put the herringbone on triple") a jacket, waistcoat or trousers routed `herringbone` with a close-up takes the close-up cloth too, at `ASM_CU.hb` (3) times life on the body, lapels and waistcoat and `ASM_CU.hbs` (2) on the jacket's sleeves (his choice from mocks with single, double and triple sleeves; the tile's `slv`, applied per pixel by `suitCol` where `geo.seg` is 1 or 2 and by the shader's `uSlv`), whatever `asmCuFine` says; without a close-up, or read in bands, it keeps the herringbone painter; sheets in `C:\code\watch-test\light\cu\hb`
- the bench's sheets for this are in `C:\code\watch-test\light\brown3\show`, `cu\show` and `cu\r2`

#### A figured shirt from its close-up (2026-11-14; the owner, from 2048 mockups: "Move the repeat finder into the app", then "Build it with the strict confidence test")
- a shirt routed `tile` with a close-up (`asmCrWants`: not herringbone, twill, end-on-end or a band reading; a printed pattern is taken too) is painted with ONE WHOLE REPEAT cut from the close-up when the finder is sure (`asmCrTile` in `asmCmJob`, replacing `asmCmTile`'s drawing; `asmCrTag` in `asmCmSig`); else it is drawn as before
- why not the suits' close-up cloth (`asmCuTile1`): blending a square's edges cuts and doubles a regular figure (the first mock had brick-like seams, figures near black and rows drifting with the camera's angle)
- `asmCrFind`: the cloth's light with the lighting divided out, its self-similarity over the middle window, the two shortest strong non-parallel peaks as the repeat's steps (a brick layout: a rectangle two cells tall), the steps nudged until the 7 x 7 cells round the middle, laid over each other, are sharpest
- the strict confidence test: `per` (the share of the cells' contrast the averaged cell explains) at least `ASM_CR.per` (.6) and `sd` (the averaged cell's strength) at least `ASM_CR.sd` (.12); the figures as first measured are superseded by the bullet on his shirts below; tone-on-tone weaves are too faint (sd) and stay as drawn
- `asmCrTile1`: the cells averaged into one along the pattern's own rows and columns (straightened), the ground (the lighter 55%) set to the tagged colour, the figure keeping its own colour against it; life size, never finer than `ASM_TEX_MIN_MM`; an ordinary fine shirt tile ({t,TW,TH,mm,fine}), so the CPU painter and the WebGL shader paint it with no change of theirs
- (the owner, the same day, sending his lavender print's close-up: "why didn't this one pass the strict test") it had been left out by name because the first finder tiled the weave under it; the finder was wrong, not the close-up: the search stopped at W/7 (its sprigs repeat 70 px apart) and took the two shortest peaks (the threads, 1 mm)
- the finder since: the search reaches W/`ASM_CR.D` (4) on the light smoothed over `ASM_CR.smooth` px (figures stay, threads go); the candidates are the shortest of the strong peaks; every pair is tried and the pair whose pile explains most wins (of those within `ASM_CR.near`, the smallest cell); the score is cross-checked between alternate cells of the pile, so a pile of few large cells is not flattered
- (the owner: "how come they turned the pattern sideways?") a print on a diagonal grid was sampled along its diagonals and laid level; the tile's cell is now upright: its sides are the shortest repeats within `ASM_CR.tilt` degrees of level and of upright (whole-number sums of the two steps, nudged again), so a cell may hold several repeats (`cr.cells`)
- (the owner chose "sharpen the figures first") each cell of the pile is lined up on the others (`ASM_CR.align` px, then half steps, twice) before they are averaged: unaligned, the print's sprigs were soft dashes; the tile is up to `ASM_CR.TWmax` texels wide for a large cell
- on his shirts now: "White & grey & white dobby" (per .95, sd .31) and "Lavender & navy micro-print neat" (.90, .37) pass; the rest fail on sd (.06 or less: White woven .75 / .06, Check .70 / .04) or on per
- the bench's prototype and sheets: `C:\code\watch-test\light\patch-repeat.js`, `cu\dobby`, `cu\fig`; `tests/asmcr.test.js`

#### Phones
- faster masters on a phone (the owner's ask; first use was 15.5 s on a simulated phone, 2.6 s with the setup kept): `asmCmWarm` (end of `asmAhead`) warms coming masters in the background as setup-only jobs (`job.setupOnly`, assemble.js `asmBuild`), once a session, not on save-data or 2G; `tests/masterspeed.test.js`
- the PC builder's shared setups (`GET /blob`, `cmsetup/` records) and 2048 copies of the masters (`master2048`) are no longer asked for (CR-8-06, 2026-11-17): `asmCmSetupBlob`, `asmCmBlobOf`/`asmCmBlobRead`, `asmCmSetupFetch`, `asmCmMasterCopy`/`asmCmMasterCopyRec` and their keys were deleted; a phone downloads the 2880 master and works its setup out once (kept on disk); none of them was in a code fingerprint, so no key changed
- `asmData` is out of `asmCmPrepCode` (still in `asmCmCode`): the worker swaps its own decoder, so including it made page and worker setup keys differ; `tests/asmcode.test.js` skips it
- a phone asks for a look's picture on the worker first (the owner's ask, from when the PC builder uploaded them: the phone side was slow); `asmCmEnsure` downloads the master, masks and parts (2.8 MB) only to paint a look with no picture there
- a look with no picture on the worker is painted on the phone at once, coming looks too (until 2026-11-16 a coming look waited 10 minutes, `ASM_PC_WAIT`, for the PC builder)
- a master's setup (`asmCmPrep`) is kept on every device, phones included, packed (`asmCmPack`/`asmCmUnpack`: the large arrays gzipped, the master's pixels decoded again): about 145 MB in memory at 2048 -> about 5 MB, read back in about 0.6 s at phone speed against about 23 s to work it out; `job.diskCache:false` skips it

#### Uploads from the computer
- the PC builder (`npm run assemble`, tools/asm-prebuild.js, its watcher `C:\code\assemble-watch.ps1` and `assemble.ps1`, the `--gpu` path) was removed on 2026-11-16 by the owner's decision: phones paint each look on their graphics chip (about 90 ms) or CPU, so nothing paints looks ahead on the computer any more; its tests (`tests/asmprebuild.test.js`, `tests/asmgpu.test.js`, the builder part of `tests/drawnonly.test.js`) went with it
- this computer's daily upload cap (2026-11-14, the owner: "limit the pc to only upload 300 items per day"): `tools/upload-budget.js` counts every `PUT /render` from `asm-cm-upload.js` in one ledger for all checkouts (`<home>\.dw-upload-budget.json`, a lock file, the UTC day; `DW_UPLOAD_LIMIT`, `DW_UPLOAD_LEDGER`)
- a limit for one day only (2026-11-14, the owner: "lift the upload cap for today"): `node tools/upload-budget.js --today <n>` writes today's limit into the ledger (`--today off` takes it out; no argument prints today's count and limit); every tool reads it while the ledger's day is today (`limitNow`, `today`), and the usual limit is back the next UTC day by itself; the worker's own daily write allowance still applies
- cm-upload reserves its whole batch first (exit 4, nothing uploaded); tests give every script a temporary ledger; `tests/uploadbudget.test.js`
- every new upload a PC tool makes (`PUT /render`, `PUT /blob`, any write to the worker) takes `budget.take(n)` from `tools/upload-budget.js` first
- `tools/fill-material.js --apply` saves approved materials to the wardrobe (its header said nothing was saved); each item's field stamps are worked out by core.js `itemFields`, read from the file, so only `material` takes the new stamp and a field changed on a phone and not yet synced still wins (CR-8-12, 2026-11-17; `tests/fillmaterial.test.js`)

### `asm-gl.js`

#### Routing
- the colour-coded painter's per-look pass on the graphics chip (WebGL2), ported with the owner's approval after the iPhone bench: about 90 ms a look on a phone chip against 0.45-1.5 s on the CPU at 2048; loaded after asm-color.js (index.html, asm-worker.js); in `ASSETS`
- `asmCmBuild` first calls `asmGlTry(job, hit)` (hit = `asmCmSetup`, shared by both painters); a covered look with working WebGL2 paints on the chip (`{url, gl:true, ms, renderer}`), else it returns null (why in `asmGlNot`) and the CPU paints
- the worker reports WebGL2 (`{ready, gl}`, `asmGlProbe`); without it (iPhone before iOS 17) `asmRun` has a covered look (`asmGlOnPage`) drawn on the page's WebGL2 by `asmRunPage` (assemble.js)
- (CR-8-02, 2026-11-17, the owner: fix now) the page only uploads and draws: the worker sets the master up on its CPU or from disk and packs its textures (`asmGlPrepOff`: `asmGlPack`, `asmGlGeoPack`; transferred, sent only when the page holds another master), the page draws with a small part of the setup (`asmGlPage`, `lite`: size, folds, tie geometry), the worker finishes the read-back pixels (`asmGlFinOff`: `asmGlTail`, then the watch and pocket square)
- before, the page's first look of each master worked the setup out on the main thread (25 s blocked at 4x CPU, the reviewer's `repro-page-setup.js`) and kept a second copy of it; now the longest main-thread task is about 0.1-0.16 s at 4x CPU (cold and from disk) and the setup lives only in the worker
- the page's upload is in steps with a pause between (`asmGlUploadSlow`, `asmGlUploadSteps`; `asmGlUpload` runs the same steps at once and finishes with `gl.finish()`)
- a look the setup shows isn't covered after all, or a failed page draw, is painted by the worker's CPU (`{...job, gl:false}`), never on the page's CPU; the same picture either way (output hashes of worker GL, worker CPU, page draw and page CPU unchanged)
- the drawn pixels are read back without holding the thread (`asmGlRead`: a pixel buffer, a fence polled on short timers, then copied out; a plain `readPixels` where fences are missing): the plain read waited 1.5-4 s for SwiftShader's draw on the page; the same bytes
- a lost context: that look is painted on the CPU and the chip retried after `ASM_GL_RETRY_MS` (60 s); no WebGL2 at all is remembered for the session (`asmGlNone`)
- (CR-8-03, 2026-11-17) one ordinary error while painting on the chip (a bad tile, a bug in one look's cloth) paints that look on the CPU and keeps the context (`asmGlFail`; what may have been half uploaded is uploaded again); only a lost context or the same error again within `ASM_GL_RETRY_MS` with no picture between lets it go
- a GL state let go is released for good (`asmGlRelease`: textures, framebuffer and programs deleted, `WEBGL_lose_context`), in `asmGlLost`, on a size change in `asmGlInit` and in the idle release (`asmGlFree`)
- the switch: Settings → Outfit pictures → Advanced (owner only), "Graphics-chip painting" (`asmGlUI`, `asmGlSet`, `localStorage.asm_gl` '0' = off), passed in the job as `job.gl` by `asmCmJob`; the line under it says how the last picture was made and how long it took (`asmGlNote`)

#### Coverage
- one rule, `asmGlWhy(job, P)` ('' = covered, else the reason; `asmGlCovers`); without P it checks the job only (the page's routing)
- every master (`ASM_GL_KINDS`: suit, vest, suit-open, vest-open, shirt-tie, shirt-open) and every jacket cloth, tie, shirt and trousers the routes give are covered; only no WebGL2, a lost context, the switch off, `job.look === false`, a size over `ASM_GL_MAX` (2880, the masters' size) or no background picture go to the CPU
- a picture over `ASM_SIZE` is scaled to 2048 exactly as asmCmBuild does
- reasons that can still occur on bad input: a missing garment, tile or geometry, tiles over `ASM_GL_TILE` (4096) alone or stacked, a small-figure grid over 8x8, a herringbone with soft column edges (`ASM_HB_SOFT`, 0 today), more than `ASM_GL_CUFFS` (15) turned cuffs, a polyline over 256 points or in a creased sleeve, trousers folds that don't fit a byte, a waistcoat cloth not the jacket's (`asmGlSame`), a patterned belt

#### CPU and GL agreement
- a GL picture has the CPU picture's key ("look + code"); asm-gl.js is not in `asmCmCode`, so a GL change must keep the two within 2 levels (`tests/asmgl.test.js`)
- every random cloth detail goes through `asmH32` (`Math.imul` and `>>> 0`, which a shader's uint multiply reproduces bit for bit); the GLSL is written by `asmGlHashGlsl`
- a detail read at a cloth position goes through `asmCellV` / `asmCellT`, which fade a cell into its neighbour within `ASM_CELL_FADE` (.02 of a cell) of its edge: float64 on the CPU against float32 on the chip flipped details at hard edges (iPhone: up to 8 levels); with the fade the largest difference is 1-2
- faded reads: the twill/herringbone fleck, the linen's threads, a herringbone's grain cells, yarn breakup and floats (`ynOf` in `suitCol`), a small woven figure's thread crossing
- the sleeves pick upper arm or forearm in whole numbers on both painters (`asmTubePartQ`, lines as `uQ` uniforms); `asmTubeTabs` keeps the float `asmTubePart`, so the kept setup's key (`asmCmSrcSig`) is unchanged
- the tweed's cell `floor(i/lsc)` comes from float64 via the tables texture, since float32 could pick the next cell on the shirt masters (`pxmm` 116/174)
- a polyline segment ends exactly where the next starts, so a point beyond a bend ties and the first segment wins on both painters

#### Shader
- the outer garment (`uOut`) is the jacket on the jacket masters and the trousers on the shirt masters (folds' lighting only, as on the CPU); `uvC` is its cloth position (the shader's `uvAt`: solved piece, legs, tube, lapel, flow)
- the twill's squeezed-rib softening (`ASM_CM_AA`) is `uAA`; band tiles (`asmSuitTex`) are read at four points a pixel as `asmCmAt` does, with the stripe breakup and dark-ground shading added in the shader; a striped jacket takes neither cloth look nor lighting (`uJm.w`), like the CPU's `e.keep`
- the waistcoat is the jacket's own item (`asmCmItems`) painted with the jacket's cloth; the cloth is read once a pixel and `outer()` applies each garment's light: two whole readings made the program too big for SwiftShader (lost context), a loop over the two garments was 3x slower
- one program per kind of master (`asmGlVar`, built once per context by `asmGlUse`): SwiftShader runs every branch, and one program for all made a suit-master look about twice as slow
- ties (`asmTieFor`) and shirts (`asmCmTile`) read from tiles are bilinear, so no CPU change was needed; without `P.tieGeo` a tile tie is its colour on both painters; turned shirt parts are indexed in the lighting texture (`asmGlCuffs`, `uCu`)
- polyline sleeves (`asmPolyAt`, arms bent less than `ASM_CREASE_DEG`) are `polyAt`; the trousers' position is `asmLegsAt`; the tie clip (`asmBarDraw`) and shirt buttons (`asmCmButtons`) stay CPU passes after read-back

#### Textures
- the master is packed once (`asmGlPack`) and uploaded (`asmGlUpload`), the solved positions separately as RG16UI (`asmGlUploadGeo`)
- WebGL2 guarantees only 16 sampler units: the jacket's, tie's and shirt's tiles share one stacked texture (`asmGlAtlas`), linen and tweed share unit 14 (re-uploaded when `G.clothK` changes), a small figure's grid is a uniform array (`uWg`), the output is bound to no unit, the tables texture holds the legs, `ASM_LIN` and polyline points, and the shirt masters reuse the lighting texture's dead channels (`asmGlLegsOk` checks the fold shifts fit)
- texture memory at 2048 (`asmGlBytes`): about 197-199 MB, 229-231 MB on a waistcoat day (the waistcoat's own texture)
- on SwiftShader (software) at 2048 a look takes about 0.6-1.8 s; a real phone chip is the bench's ~90 ms

#### Tests
- `tests/asmgl.test.js`: each `LOOKS` row painted both ways at 512 in SwiftShader, max difference at most 2 and mean under 0.1; plus no WebGL2, a lost context and retry, the switch, the page's routing, the real worker's chip, and the sleeves' part rule
- rows carry `has` tags (the cloth a row must have) so a row can't quietly turn plain; `ASMGL_SIZE=2048`/`2880` paints at a phone's or the masters' size, `ASMGL_ONLY=<regexp>` picks rows
- the test derives stand-in vest, open and shirt masters and their solved maps in the page from the two fixtures (no real masters)
- `lab/gl-bench.html` (the iPhone bench) compares the live asm-color.js and asm-gl.js; `tests/labbench.test.js` asks for at most 2 levels

### `asm-worker.js`

- the Web Worker that paints the pictures (see `assemble.js`); in `ASSETS` in sw.js
- a module worker since C3 (2026-11-24): assemble.js `asmWorker` starts it with `new Worker('asm-worker.js', {type: 'module'})`, and its `import './srgb.js'` … `import './asm-ocp.js'` lines run the eight files in the order its `importScripts` did; each file's bridge puts its names on the worker's `globalThis`; its own `asmData` and `asmJpeg` (an OffscreenCanvas) and the save counters around `asmCmPack` and `asmCmDiskPut` are written through the bridge's setters (those four are `let`s), so the painter's own calls reach them; its ready message says whether WebGL2 works in it (`gl`); a worker file or import that can't load or doesn't parse fires the Worker's `error`, and the page paints (`asmWorkerDown`), as when `importScripts` threw
- it takes `{id, job}` and answers `{id, res}` or `{id, error}`; a worker without WebGL2 also takes `asmRunPage`'s steps `{id, job, glPrep:{key, gk}}` (answered with transferred textures) and `{id, job, glFin:{o, ms, renderer}}` (CR-8-02); the PC builder's own messages (`{setup}`, `{probe}`, `{encode}`, a job without its master's files, `job.hk`), the CPU-only `asm-worker.js?nogl=1`, `asmGlTry`'s `job.raw` and the raw-pixel answer (`res.px`) were removed
- it counts the setups it is still saving to disk (`asmCmPack` and `asmCmDiskPut` wrapped; neither is in a code fingerprint) and tells the page `{saving: n}` a moment after each change, so the idle release never cuts a save off (CR-8-01)

### `sw.js`

- one version at a time: install caches every `ASSETS` file into `CACHE`, then cache-first; works offline
- why: network-first with a per-file race mixed old and new files on slow connections after a release
- add new app files to `ASSETS` and bump `CACHE`
- look.js (the Look beta) is in `ASSETS` (2026-11-17)
- install fetches with `cache:'no-cache'`, so unchanged files are a 304
- a new version waits (no `skipWaiting` at install) until the next start or `{type:'skipWaiting'}` (about.js `updateNow`); `{type:'version'}` answers the version
- `cache:'no-store'` requests are left to the network; `sw.js` is in `ASSETS` so the page can read its own version
- an `ASSETS` file requested under another folder (an offline deep link) is served from the cache, or the page came up unstyled
- it is found by the end of the path (`/deep/mq/vest.js` → `mq/vest.js`), so the files in sub-folders come too (CR-5-05: offline, a deep link had no figure poses)
- **a deep link goes to the root** (the restructure S5-0, 2026-11-26; the owner's OK: offline the address bar then shows the root): a page asked for under another folder of sw.js's (`/DailyWardrobe/some/path`, `…/x/index.html`) that the network answers with a 404 or not at all gets a 302 to the app's root (`ROOT`, sw.js's folder, not the site's), its query kept and its `#…` too (a redirect without one keeps the link's: an invitation's `#invite=` survives); a page that is there under a folder (lab/gl-bench.html) is shown as before
- why: index.html answered at the deep path ran a module twice, its tag's copy under the deep path and the root's for its imports (a file from the cache has the root's address, the base its imports resolve from); on main bus.js ran twice there, and step 5's imports would have run core.js twice (the app didn't start in the pilot)
- online on GitHub Pages a deep link was GitHub's 404 page and is now the app at the root (measured on both engines under `/DailyWardrobe/`, `C:\code\s5-0-lab\deep.js` and `deepwk.js`; Playwright's WebKit opens no page offline with `setOffline`, main or branch, so its offline case stops the server instead)
- `tests/sw.test.js` appends a run counter to every script it serves (`__runs`): from a deep link, online and offline, the app is at the root with the query and `#…`, and every page script ran once; main's sw.js fails both (a 404 online, bus.js x2 offline)
- a Web Worker asked for with a query is answered from the cache like any file (the PC builder's bypass for `asm-worker.js?nogl=1` is gone)
- icons are 256-colour palette PNGs; the manifest has `id` "./" and separate `any`/`maskable` entries; one `theme_color` (#101820, the dark header in both themes)
- `tests/assets.test.js`: every `ASSETS` file exists and every needed file is listed (one missing breaks every update)
- `tests/sw.test.js`: reload stays put, a bump prompts and waits, Update switches, a waiting version runs on next open, takeover from the old network-first worker

### `worker.js`

- the pocket square in the tagger (2026-11-16): worker/tag.js lists its colours, patterns (`border` in `PATTERN_LIST`) and materials always (/describe takes it), but the tag's type enum and rules name it only when the request says `types:['pocketsquare']` (`knowsPq`, `tagRules(pq)`, `tagTypes(pq)`, POST /, /tag-many, Workers AI): an older app reads an unknown type as a suit

- worker.js is the entry (the route table `ROUTES`, `serve`, `corsFor`, the handlers fetch/queue/scheduled, `export { RenderHub }`); the routes live in ES modules in `worker/` (2026-11-14): `tag.js` (POST /, /tag-many, the prompt and schema, the Workers AI fallback), `pattern.js`, `links.js` (GET /fetch), `render.js` (POST /render, `RenderHub`), `items.js` (item pictures), `records.js` (GET/PUT/DELETE /render, /blob), `holidays.js`, `backup.js` (the backup's versions, parts, snapshots, syncLite), `photostore.js` (photos stored once, their clean-up, /sync), `people.js` (`whoIs`, plain errors, caps, invitations, feedback, account deletion), `remind.js` (Web Push)
- each module imports what it uses from the others by name; the modules import each other in cycles (only functions cross at load), and none imports worker.js: a constant read at load time by another module would hit the cycle's TDZ, so keep load-time tables inside their own module
- Wrangler bundles the modules on deploy (`main = "worker.js"`; checked with `npx wrangler@4.158.0 deploy --dry-run --outdir <dir>`), and the deploy workflow also runs on `worker/**`
- tests that read the worker's text (prompts, lists) take `require('./workersrc.js')()`: worker.js and every worker/ module; tests/impact.js records worker/ modules function by function like worker.js (`workerFile`, `workerFiles`) and follows a changed constant across the worker's files
- Cloudflare Worker; every endpoint needs the owner's `APP_TOKEN` or a tester's invite code (`whoIs`, worker/people.js); with `APP_TOKEN` unset every route refuses (403), the three tagging routes included (they used to run open)
- the router is the table `ROUTES` (`'<METHOD> <path>'` → `[handler(req, env, json, who, cors), what to say when APP_TOKEN is unset]`): `fetch()` resolves the caller once and hands `who` to the handler; no handler checks the token itself
- a switched-off tester may still `DELETE /account` (`whoIs` returns `offWho`, the router lets only that route through); every other route answers 403 "invite off"
- only `POST /` tags a photo: any other address not in `ROUTES` answers 404 (a `POST /holidayz` used to spend a Claude call), a known address with the wrong method 405
- `POST /` reads the token from the `x-app-token` header before reading the body; the body's `appToken` is no longer accepted (the app has sent the header since 2026-11-12); it reads at most 15 MB of body and stops there (`TAG_LIMIT`, `readCapped`) → 413
- `fetch()` wraps the router (`serve`) in a try/catch: anything a route throws (a KV write over the day's allowance, a Durable Object restarting) answers JSON 503 `{error:'server', message}` with the CORS headers (`SERVER_DOWN`), and the error goes to the log; Cloudflare's error 1101 page had no CORS headers and every call showed "Failed to fetch" until midnight UTC (2026-11-13 audit)
- `ALLOWED_ORIGIN` may be several origins separated by commas: the matching one is echoed in `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` (with `Vary: Origin`), any other origin gets 403
- CORS answers carry `Access-Control-Max-Age: 7200` (`corsFor`): a phone keeps a preflight's answer 2 hours instead of asking before nearly every call
- `scheduled()` (cron `*/15 * * * *` in wrangler.toml) runs `sendReminders` (worker/remind.js) and the daily photo clean-up (`photoGc`, worker/photostore.js)
- the `queue()` consumer (queue `wardrobe-renders`, binding `RENDER_QUEUE`; names kept from the outfit photos so the bindings need no change) only finishes account wipes (`{wipe}`, `runWipe` in worker/people.js; an error retries after a minute); any other message, such as an outfit-photo job left from before 2026-11-14, is acked and dropped
- a Worker may make 1,000 calls to Cloudflare's own services (KV, Durable Objects, queues) a request on the free plan, and 50 fetches to the outside (the "about 50 KV calls" written here before was the outside cap; CR-6-04, 2026-11-17); the free plan allows 1,000 KV writes and 1,000 lists a day for everyone together, which shapes the caps and the list-free reads below
- not done (CR-6-04, Low): Roll back (`?snap=`), an older app's `GET /sync` and the clean-up's `refsInUse` read one KV value per photo in one request, so they break above about 1,000 photos (about 330 items with a photo, a small photo and a close-up); a "lite" Roll back that fetches photos with `?ph=` would lift it
- every handler takes `(req, env, json, who, cors)`; `'* /sync'` and `GET /fetch` are in `ROUTES` without wrappers (2026-11-17)

#### Deploy

- deployed automatically by `.github/workflows/deploy-worker.yml` (Wrangler, config in `wrangler.toml`) when a merge to `main` changes `worker.js`, `worker/**`, `merge.js` (the merge rules the worker imports), wrangler.toml or the workflow; it creates the queue if missing, so the token needs Queues edit
- it needs the repo's `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` secret and `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` / `WARDROBE_KV_ID` variables; without them it prints a `::warning::` that the live worker was not updated (it was a quiet notice) and the user deploys by hand; the workflow has `permissions: contents: read`
- its test step is `npm test`: on `main` that selects nothing when the merged branch was tested (the map carries the fingerprint of `main`'s files) and every test when it was not, so a normal deploy runs no test and needs no browser
- the step "Which tests will run" (`node tests/run.js --changed --list`) decides: Chromium is installed only when the answer is not one of `tests/impact.js`'s two "nothing runs" sentences (`tests/deploy.test.js` checks they still match)
- WebKit is installed too when that answer's `WebKit: …` line (tests/run.js, #551) is not "… do not run": the deploy of #557 (2026-11-17) selected everything (main's map unstamped) and its 11 WebKit halves failed with "Executable doesn't exist" (the workflow had installed Chromium only)
- when a browser is needed, the system packages (`npx playwright install-deps <browsers>`, apt) get two minutes (five with WebKit) and may fail with a warning, and the browser download is tried three times; no step uses `--with-deps`: on 2026-11-11 the runners could not resolve the Ubuntu mirrors for hours and deploys failed in apt although no test was going to run; WebKit's halves do not start without its apt packages, so then `npm test` stops the deploy
- the downloaded browsers are cached (`actions/cache`, `~/.cache/ms-playwright`, keyed by Playwright's version and the browsers)
- the system packages come from the same .deb cache as the full test run (`tools/ci-browser-deps.sh`, its own key for `chromium` or `chromium webkit`), installed after the browser download; apt only on a miss, two minutes (eight with WebKit)
- Run workflow with `check` (`gh workflow run deploy-worker.yml --ref <branch> -f check=true -f only=<name>`) runs the tests and browser installs without deploying (the Deploy step has `!inputs.check`), in its own concurrency group; `only` runs the files named like it with their WebKit halves (both browsers installed) instead of the change's selection
- the checkout has the whole history (`fetch-depth: 0`): garmentpaint.test.js reads an older commit with `git show`, and a shallow checkout failed two of its checks ("fatal: bad object", the deploy of #557)
- the workflow installs with `npm install --no-package-lock`: a `package-lock.json` written on the runner is a new file and made the selection run everything
- is the current worker live: `DELETE /render?key=not-a-key` with the app's Origin answers 405 from the worker since 2026-11-17 (the route went, CR-6-14) and 400 from the one before; it deletes nothing on either

#### worker/tag.js

- the new garments in the tagger (2026-11-16): `OPT_TYPES` (tee, polo, henley, sweater, sweatshirt, hoodie, qzip, cardigan, chore) and `OPT_KINDS` (trouserKind cargo) are offered only to an app that names them in the request's `types` (`knownTypes(body)`; core.js `TAG_TYPES`): POST / (`tagSchema(…, known)`, `tagRules(known)`, `materialExtra(known)`), /tag-many (`multiPrompt(known)`) and Workers AI (`tagWithWorkersAi(env, image, known)`, `promptOf`); an older app reads an unknown type as a suit
- the uniform blouse in the tagger (2026-11-17): 'blouse' joins `OPT_TYPES` (offered only to an app that names it in `types`; core.js `TAG_TYPES`), its words in `OPT_WORDS` (a military uniform coat such as OCP: tapes over angled chest pockets, shoulder pockets, not a chore jacket), its color row, pattern line (camouflage, solid, other) and materials (nylon/cotton ripstop, cotton ripstop, cotton twill, polyester/cotton, cotton), equal to core.js
- without `types` every request the worker sends is byte-identical to before (`PROMPT`, `TAG_RULES`, `MATERIAL_EXTRA`, `MULTI_PROMPT`, the schemas): `tests/tagger.test.js` compares the single tag, the picker's two calls, /tag-many and Workers AI with `tests/fixtures/garmentworker-old.json` (recorded by `node tests/garmentworker-calls.js --write`; record again only for a deliberate change to what an older app is sent)
- the prompt is built from pieces (`promptFor(known)`: `PROMPT_HEAD`, the type words `OPT_WORDS`, `colorText(known)`, `PROMPT_PATS`, the pattern lines `OPT_PAT_LINES`, `PROMPT_KINDS`, cargo, `PROMPT_TAIL`); a known type adds its words, color row, pattern line and material row (`matTypes`), and any new garment adds 'heather' and 'cable' to the pattern enum (`tagPatterns`: `PATTERN_KEYS` = `PATTERN_LIST` + `OPT_PATTERNS`; `PATTERN_LIST` stays the older app's enum)
- the lists hold the new types always: `TYPE_LIST` (corrections, own colors, /describe, pattern.js), `TYPE_COLORS`, `PATTERN_BY_TYPE`, `MATERIALS_BY` (all must equal core.js); /describe's tables, `MATERIAL_RULES` and its pattern enums (`PATTERN_KEYS`) list them for every app (a description names its item's type, so an older app is never given one back)
- the new materials' tiers: fleece, waffle knit, piqué, canvas, duck canvas, french terry plain to see (`MAT_SEES`); slub cotton, loopback cotton, shetland wool likely (`MAT_LIKELY`); a polo's "pique" is read as piqué (`cleanMaterial`)
- schema size with every new garment: plain tag 5896 → 6020 characters (under the freeColors 6233 the API compiles); the picker's calls grow by the new enum values only (freeColors 6233 → 6357, freePattern 5813 → 5937), not yet compiled by the API
- when the pocket-squares branch is on main too, its `knowsPq(body)` is `knownTypes(body).includes('pocketsquare')`, the type joins `OPT_TYPES` and its words join `promptFor`'s pieces

- photo tagging via the Claude API (secret `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, optional `CLAUDE_MODEL`); falls back to the Workers AI binding `AI`
- single tag (POST /): `tagSchema(custom,true)` adds confidence, `altColor`/`altPattern`, `newColor`/`newPattern` ({name, why} or null) and `clothBox`; effort medium; an optional `closeup` is sent as a second image; `measured` and `corrections` become prompt text (`measuredText`/`correctionsText`); `custom` is checked by `cleanCustom`
- `/tag-many`: several items in one photo; Claude lists every item with a confidence and a rough box in thousandths; the app crops each into its own Add card
- Claude calls are cached (`tagWithClaude`, 2026-11-13 audit; the fixed text used to come after the photos, so nothing was cached): `system` is the fixed instructions (`CLAUDE_SYSTEM` + `opts.prompt`: `TAG_INTRO` + `TAG_RULES` + `SINGLE_EXTRA` for one item, `MULTI_PROMPT`, `PATTERN_PROMPT`) with the first `cache_control`, then the owner's colours, patterns and corrections (`opts.owner`) with the second; the photos and the per-call text (`opts.text`: close-up note, light wording, measurement, the colour/pattern picker's extras, `/pattern`'s item type) come last
- Claude gets the field rules without the JSON template or "no code fences" (`TAG_RULES`, from "type —"; structured output answers); Workers AI still reads `PROMPT` whole, template included; `PROMPT`'s JSON template lists `trouserKind` and `shoeStyle`
- the light wording follows the app's optional `wb` boolean on `POST /`, `/tag-many` and `/pattern` (`lightText`, `wbOf`): `true` = the app white-balanced the photos from a white reference (take colours as shown), `false` = sent as the camera took them (judge as in neutral daylight, allowing for the light's cast), absent = the safe wording (judge as in daylight, allow for a cast only where the photo clearly has one); no other prompt text says the photos are white-balanced or how to judge colours (the old wordings contradicted)
- `cleanTag` keeps `weave.repeatsAcross` up to 80 (a cap of 12 drew a pinstripe jacket's stripes, asked for as 20–40, about 2.5x too far apart)
- each Claude call has a 3-minute limit (`CLAUDE_MS`; running out answers 504) and one retry on 429, 529 or 5xx, after the `retry-after` (at most `CLAUDE_RETRY_MAX` 20 s) or `CLAUDE_RETRY_MS` (2 s; `env.CLAUDE_RETRY_MS` overrides it, the tests set 0); a 4xx is not retried
- a dropped connection (`fetch` throws) is not retried (code review CR-2-11, 2026-11-17): the API may already have taken and billed the request; it answers 502, the count goes back, and the log says "the connection dropped … not retried"
- the accessory values the app has (code review CR-2-06): an app that names any new garment in `types` (`accOpts`, the rule of `tagPatterns`) gets band rubber and nato, metal two-tone, leather nubuck and braided in the schema (`ACC_ENUMS`) and the rules (`ACC_METAL`, `ACC_LEATHER`, `ACC_BAND`); an older app and the picker's calls (`freeColors` / `freePattern`, near the API's limit) are asked as before; app.js `normTag` already took them
- `measuredText` tells Claude a nearest measured color that is a listed key or an own one (`KEY_RE`: own keys may hold digits, ownrot1; code review CR-2-05); the app no longer sends the parts' colors (`measHint`: the worker never read them)
- /tag-many, /pattern and /render read their body with `readAiBody` (code review CR-2-08): a content-length over `AI_BODY_MAX` (15 MB, as `POST /`) is refused before reading, at most that much is read (`readCapped`), then JSON; /tag-many and /pattern check the photo and close-up with `badPics` (`POST /`'s data:image rule) before anything is spent or sent (/pattern allowed 20 MB, /render 20 MB, /tag-many 15e6 characters read whole)
- a reading the app closes on (the app killed, the connection dropped) stops: `POST /`, `/tag-many` and `/pattern` pass `req.signal` to `tagWithClaude` (`opts.signal`), which answers 499 `CLIENT_GONE`, and the handler gives the count back (wrangler.toml's `enable_request_signal`, the default from the 2026-11-17 compatibility date; without it the signal never fires and nothing changes)
- the log line of every Claude call has input, cache read, cache written and output tokens
- the Workers AI fallback is `tagWithWorkersAi`: any failure (including the licence step `AI.run(model, {prompt:'agree'})`, inside a try) answers 502 and gives the tester's tag count back
- `COLOR_HEX` (each key's hex) = core.js `COLORS` and `PATTERN_BY_TYPE` = `PATS_BY` (2026-11-15, for /describe; `tests/colors.test.js` checks both)
- the pattern list (`PATTERN_LIST`) must stay in sync with `PATS_BY` in core.js, and the per-type colour lists `TYPE_COLORS` / `DIAL_COLS` with `COLS_BY` / `DIAL_COLS` in core.js (`tests/colors.test.js` checks it)
- sock patterns (2026-11-17) are offered only to an app that names 'sockpat' in its `types` (`OPT_FLAGS`, `knowsSock`; core.js `TAG_TYPES` sends it, the picker's freeColors/freePattern calls don't): then the pattern enum adds `SOCK_NEW_PATTERNS` (argyle, ribbed, clock), the rules get the socks' pattern line (`SOCK_PATTERNS`), the second/third colours as the pattern's (`SOCK_SECOND`, `SOCK_THIRD`), socks leave the "solid" line (`SOCK_KINDS`), and `toeColor` (`SOCK_TOE`; a plain string in the schema, required, `cleanToe` keeps a known key or 'none'; never an accent), on the single tag and /tag-many; an older app's request is byte-identical (tests/tagger.test.js)
- coat patterns (2026-11-17, the Look beta) are offered only to an app that names 'coatpat' in its `types` (`OPT_FLAGS`, `knowsCoat`; core.js `tagTypesNow` sends it only while the beta is on): the rules' kinds line loses "overcoat, raincoat: solid" (`COAT_KINDS`) and gains `COAT_PATTERNS`; the enum needs nothing new; an older app's request is byte-identical; `tests/lookworker.test.js`
- `DIAL_COLS` is exported for /describe's finish half (worker/describe.js)
- the material suggestion (2026-11-15, the materials audit): the plain single tag (not the picker's `freeColors` / `freePattern` calls, whose schema is near the API's limit) asks for `material {key, confidence, why}` (`materialSchema(null)`: the key a plain string, so one schema serves every type) with `MATERIAL_EXTRA` after `SINGLE_EXTRA` in the cached prompt (the rules `MATERIAL_RULES` and every type's list)
- `MATERIALS_BY` (worker) = core.js `MATERIALS_BY` (`tests/materials.test.js` checks it)
- `cleanMaterial(m, type, patterns, closeup)` holds an answer to the audit's tiers whatever Claude said: a cloth plain to see (`MAT_SEES`: linen, seersucker, corduroy, velvet, tweed, flannel, denim, jersey, grenadine, waxed cotton, hopsack…) may be high; a good bet (`MAT_LIKELY`, plus silk, wool, cashmere and cotton on a tie) medium at most with a close-up and low without; anything else (wool vs blends, cashmere, cotton blend, polyester…) low; socks always low; a key that only repeats a pattern is `none`; a type without a list gives no material; `cleanTag(t, {closeup, ownMaterials})` applies it; `tests/tagger.test.js`
- own and new materials (2026-11-16, the owner's approved design): `MATERIAL_RULES` lets Claude give the plain name of a material on no list when it can clearly tell (cupro); `cleanMaterial(m, type, pats, closeup, own)` passes such a name on with `new:true` (an owner's own without it), medium at most (socks low), when `matName` accepts it: lower-case letters, spaces and hyphens, 2-24 characters, at most 3 words, never a pattern or weave word (`PATTERN_LIST` and more), a leather (`NOT_MAT_LEATHERS`, renamed from `LEATHERS` 2026-11-17: core.js `LEATHERS` is the shoe leathers), a color key, a non-answer, or prose (`NOT_MAT_PROSE`: "ignore the rules"); those stay `none` as before. The app sends `ownMaterials {type: [name]}` with the plain single tag and the reader's 'both'; `cleanOwnMaterials` keeps types with a list, at most 20 sanitized names each, none already standard, and `ownMaterialsText` puts them in the owner's cached text (the picker's calls leave them out). Old apps ignore `new` (they take only keys on the list) and an app sending none works as before

#### worker/pattern.js

- `/pattern`: the woven pattern as data (`PATTERN_SCHEMA`/`PATTERN_PROMPT`, effort high): `across`/`along` bands `{role, name, hex, w}` (shares of a repeat), `stripeDirection`, `repeatsInCloseup`, `repeatsAcrossItem`, `paneAspect`, `ignored`, `description`; tidied by `cleanPattern`; 422 when there is none
- motif: `layout:'motif'` with `motif {grid: straight|diagonal, ground {name,hex}, figures [{shape (MOTIF_SHAPES), placement: cell|corner|alternate-cells, colours (1, or 2 alternating), centre (square-with-dot), sizePct}]}`; `across`/`along` are null; `repeatsInCloseup`/`repeatsAcrossItem` count grid cells
- the pattern lists `TEXTURE_KINDS` and `MOTIF_SHAPES` and the 1/0 bitmap cleaner (`bitmapOf`) are one copy in `worker/patternbits.js` (a leaf module, 2026-11-17, code review CR-2-15), imported by pattern.js and describe.js
- a motif figure's placements (`MOTIF_PLACES`: cell, corner, alternate-cells) and a texture's contrasts (`TEX_CONTRASTS`: faint, subtle, clear) are in patternbits.js too (Round B, 2026-11-17), used by both routes' schemas and cleaners; the schemas serialize the same, so the requests to Claude are byte-identical
- the `description` /pattern and the single tag's `weave` ask for is one sentence for the owner and the app's painter, naming the cloth's weave and finish in a tailor's words (fine worsted, flannel, linen, twill, herringbone, seersucker, corduroy); no prompt asks for text for the retired photo generator (code review CR-2-13 / CR-10-16); its words are read by assemble.js `asmClothOf` / `asmLinen` / the micro-weaves, asm-color.js `asmCuFine` / `asmCmMat`, today.js `ovlGrain` and photos.js `lookSig` (comments in both prompts name them)
- a pattern reading is logged as "pattern read by Claude" (`what`), not "tagged"

#### worker/describe.js

- `POST /describe` (`handleDescribe`, 2026-11-15): `{what:'colour'|'pattern', text, type, field, colours, pattern, custom, closeup?, wb?}`; the words trimmed, `<`/`>` removed, 1-120 characters (else 400) and wrapped in `<description>`; `type` in `TYPE_LIST` (a pattern only for a type with patterns); the body read to 16 MB (`readCapped`)
- the model is `env.CLAUDE_DESCRIBE_MODEL || 'claude-sonnet-5-5'` (the owner's choice; tagging keeps `CLAUDE_MODEL`); a colour at effort low, max_tokens 4000, a pattern at effort medium, 8000; adaptive thinking left on (Sonnet 5.5 refuses `thinking:{type:'disabled'}`), structured output, the refusal fallback (`fallbacks:'default'`)
- the system text is the fixed rules and tables (`descTables`, built once from `COLOR_LIST`, `COLOR_HEX`, `TYPE_COLORS`, `PATTERN_LIST`, `PATTERN_BY_TYPE`; byte-identical, first cache point, about 2,100 tokens, over Sonnet 5.5's 512-token minimum) then the owner's colours (with their bases) or patterns (`ownerText`, second cache point); the item and the words go last
- the call is tag.js `claudeCall` (split out of `tagWithClaude`: headers, fallback, retry, time limit, stop reasons, token log), with no image required
- `cleanDescribeColour`: a hex in the words wins, else Claude's must be `#rrggbb` (422); `plansAs` outside the type's list → `baseFor` (core.js `colBaseFor`'s rule), never `own…`; `existing` outside the type's list and the owner's for the type → nearest by ΔE00, and `dE` is given; any kind but colour/several answers `{kind, ask}` only (still counted: it was a paid call)
- `cleanDescribePattern`: `plansAs` in `PATTERN_BY_TYPE[type]` (else the words' guess, else other); a weave look answered `solid` moves to the type's first weave (`WEAVE_PREF`: dobby, woven, nailhead…); `look` tidied by `cleanLook` (grids of 1/0, 2-16 each way, both tones; bands roles only, shares; motif up to 3 figures; `tr` 0.2-80); nothing drawable and plansAs other → 422
- a tester's lookups are counted (`describe` cap, `spend` before, `unspend` on any non-200); plain words for testers (`PLAIN.describe`)
- `what:'both'` (`describeBoth`, 2026-11-15, the app's "Read color and texture from close-up"): the color and the pattern call run side by side (`Promise.all`), each with its own tables, schema, effort, cleaner and ΔE rules; the answer is `{colour, pattern, model, lookups}`, a half that failed given as `{error, detail, status}` (plain words for testers, `plainFor`); both failing answers as one failed lookup (the colour half's status)
- `what:'both'` may come with no words when a close-up is sent: each half then gets the stand-in description "the cloth in the close-up" (`NO_TEXT`) and a line to read the close-up itself (`NO_TEXT_LINE`); no words and no close-up is 400; a type without patterns is 400
- in a 'both' the pattern half also gives the material (2026-11-15; no third call, still 2 lookups): its schema adds `material {key, confidence, why}` with the key a plain string (`patternSchema(own, mats)`; it was that type's list as an enum until 2026-11-16, when own and new materials came; no anyOf, tested against the colour and pattern schemas' sizes), the owner's own materials for the item's type (`ownMaterials[type]`) in its owner text, its cached text is `descTables().both` (the pattern tables + `BOTH_MATERIAL`: with no words every cloth is a pattern answer and corduroy, seersucker, velvet or linen go in material), and the answer's `pattern.material` is `cleanMaterial`'s (never the item's pattern or the one read); a single pattern lookup keeps its own schema and text
- a tester's 'both' counts 2 lookups (the owner's decision; `spend(…, 2)` against the per-tester and all-testers caps) and each failed half gives 1 back; a tester with 1 left gets 429 with `short:true` and "Reading color and texture takes 2 lookups and you have 1 left today." (`BOTH_SHORT`), before Claude
- `tests/describe.test.js` (Claude mocked); `tools/describe-eval.js` holds the design's 45 cases with their accept rules, NOT in npm test: it runs only with `DW_WORKER`+`DW_TOKEN`, or `--direct` with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (each case a paid call; about $1 for `--runs 2` on Sonnet 5.5)
- the Look beta's "Read with Claude" (2026-11-17) is a 'both' with `look:true` (an older app never sends it, so its requests are as before): `src:'photo'` says the picture is the whole item, not a close-up (`lookPhoto`, `NO_TEXT_PHOTO`, `NO_TEXT_PHOTO_LINE`); `src:'closeup'` is worded exactly as the old reader
- with `look:true` a type without patterns (`LOOK_FINISH`: shoes, belt, watch, tieclip, cufflinks; else 400 as before) gets the colour half and a finish half (`finishRules()`, built at the first call; `finishSchema`, effort low, 2000 tokens): `material {key, confidence, why}` held to the type's keys (`cleanFinish`: another kind's key is none), a watch's `dial`, a belt's `buckle`; the answer is `{colour, pattern:null, material, dial, buckle, model, lookups}` (a failed finish half as `finish:{error…}`); still 2 lookups for a tester, each failed half given back; `tests/lookworker.test.js`
- the colour arithmetic (`rgbLab`, `hexLab`, `dE00`) is `worker/colormath.js` (a leaf module, Round B CR-2-15, 2026-11-17), imported by describe.js (`hexLab as labHex`) and by tools/describe-eval.js (`loadColormath`); core.js keeps the app's copy, and `tests/colormath.test.js` checks the two give exactly the same numbers

#### worker/links.js

- `/fetch` downloads photo links for the app (`fetchLinked`)
- it refuses anything but a public host (`publicHost`: no IP address in any spelling, no `localhost`, no single-label or `.local` / `.internal` / `.lan` / `.home` / `.corp` name), follows redirects by hand (5 at most) checking each stop, checks the page's share image the same way, and stops reading at its caps (15 MB photo, 600 kB page)
- a link that can't be reached (dead domain, refused connection: `fetch` throws) answers 502 "That link could not be reached…"
- a tester's links are counted (`fetch`: 100 a day, 300 for all testers; CR-6-11, 2026-11-17), before anything is fetched; over it the refusal's sentence is in `error`, which is what add.js `fetchPhoto` shows ("Today’s limit of 100 photo links is used up…")
- only photo types come back (`IMG_TYPE`: jpeg, png, webp, gif, heic, heif, avif); an SVG (it can carry script) answers 415 "That link is not a photo."

#### worker/render.js

- `POST /render` (`handleRender`) makes item pictures only (`{mode:'item'}`, worker/items.js); anything else answers 410 "Outfit photos are no longer made", before the OpenAI key is checked; without `OPENAI_API_KEY` an item request answers 501
- its body is read with tag.js `readAiBody` (15 MB, refused by content-length before reading; code review CR-2-08)
- the OpenAI outfit photo was removed on 2026-11-14 (the owner: "remove the leftovers too"): the prompts (`RENDER_PROMPT`, `BACKDROP`, `WATCH_KEY`, `NO_WATCH`, the swap prompts, `MASTER_NOTE`), `outfitPhoto`, the queued jobs (`queueRender`, `job:`/`jobrun:`/`jobfail:`, `runRenderJob`, `jobClaim`, `failJob`, `keepMade`), `GET /render-queue` and `GET /render-wait` (`queueStatus`, `waitForRenders`, `tellHub`); outfit photos kept from before are still read and written with `GET`/`PUT /render` (records.js)
- OpenAI calls have a 5-minute limit (`OPENAI_MS`)
- an item picture whose save is refused for the day's KV writes (`limit exceeded`) is still handed to the app (`stored:false`, `ITEM_LOCAL`: kept on that phone only), its write count goes back, the item count stays (the picture exists); after that, and after any route's refused write (worker.js calls `noteKvFull`), that isolate answers item pictures 429 `kv-limit` (`ITEM_FULL`) before paying OpenAI, until the next UTC day (`kvFullUntil`, a WeakMap per KV binding); 2026-11-15: a batch of 5 was paid for and every picture thrown away
- `RenderHub` (binding `RENDER_HUB`, class kept, so no Wrangler migration) is one Durable Object per person (`hubOf(env, ns)`: `renders` for the owner, `renders:u:<id>:` for a tester); it holds a tester's daily counts (`/use`, `/used`) and the "switched off" mark (`/off`, `/wipe`); its photo-wait and job-claim parts went with the outfit photo
- job records a hub kept for the retired queue stay in its storage until that person's `/wipe`; they are a few bytes each and nothing reads them
- the save lock (CR-6-01, 2026-11-17): `POST /lock {op:'take'}` → `{ok, tok, last}` or `{ok:false, busy}`; `{op:'give', tok, ver}` lets go (only the holder's `tok`) and keeps `ver` = `{rev, saved}` as `last`; a lease of `LOCK_MS` (30 s) in memory, so a save whose request was killed blocks that person's saves for 30 s at most
- `last` is handed to the next save for `LAST_MS` (2 minutes) and kept in memory only (no storage write): KV may show an older version for about a minute in another location, and an object is evicted only after over a minute idle; `/wipe` forgets it (the owner's "Delete everything" leaves KV at rev 0)
- the logs object (CR-6-03, 2026-11-17): one more RenderHub, `renders:logs` (`logHub(env)`), keeps the feedback and error reports: `POST /log {op:'add'|'get'|'drop', kind:'fb'|'er', days, max, items | id}`, a list per UTC day (`log:<kind>:<day>`) and an index `log:<kind>` = `{day: count}`, one log operation at a time (`serial`); apart from the owner's hub, so his `/wipe` keeps them
- the lock, the logs and `/wipe` need no new binding and no migration: the class `RenderHub`, the binding `RENDER_HUB` and the `v1` SQLite migration are unchanged in wrangler.toml
- a save that runs past the lease no longer writes (R6 N1, 2026-11-24: one past 30 s let a second save in, both were answered rev N+1, one change was lost): `{op:'hold', tok}` renews the lease for its holder and answers `{ok:false, lost:true}` once another save took the lock or it was given back; backup.js `lk.hold()` asks it only when `HOLD_MS` (10 s) passed since the take or the last renewal, after each photo put (photostore.js `putPhotos`' `keep`) and before the daily copies and the backup are written; a lost lease is answered 409 busy (the app asks again), the backup count and the backup's write given back (`leaseLost`), its photos left to the daily clean-up; a hub that can't be asked goes on as before
- measured (`tests/savelease.test.js`, `C:\code\d-server-lab\kvmeasure.js`, main beside the branch): the same KV puts, reads and deletes on 19 saves (80 puts), 2 hub requests per ordinary save as before; a save writing 12 photos for 96 s makes 6 renewals (hub requests, no KV write); the CR-6-12 replay (old apps against this worker) passes with the same KV writes per step
- an older app gets that 409 like any other and asks again (`C:\code\d-server-lab\oldapp409.js`, 2026-11-24: the builds 26cd8b1, 7a28f96 and c4a549b and today's app each saw one 409 then 200, both phones' pieces on the server and the phone, no error or toast, "Backed up today")

#### worker/items.js

- `/render` POST with `mode:'item'`: a product picture of one item from a text description via OpenAI image generation, or an edit of the item's own photo (`ref`), stored under the given key (`itemPhoto`), with a 5-minute limit (`OPENAI_MS`); made at once (an old app's `queue:true` is ignored)
- the key (`renderId`) and the item's own photo (`dataUrlToBlob`) are checked before anything is counted or paid (CR-6-06, 2026-11-17): a key that isn't a photo key or a photo that doesn't decode answers 400; before, a bad photo threw after the counts were taken and a missing key paid for a picture that was never kept
- one test for "the day's KV writes are used up": `kvLimit` (items.js); worker.js uses it as `kvFull`

#### worker/records.js
- `GET /blob` (`getBlob`): the raw bytes kept as `blob:<key>` (a master's setup packed for phones); `PUT /blob` went on 2026-11-17 (CR-6-14): its writer was the PC picture builder, removed 2026-11-16; today's app no longer reads them (CR-8-06), apps before it in the field still do; the blobs kept are still read
- photos are saved per outfit combination (`comboKey()` in photos.js: the shirt by base colour only; the jacket/tie/clip/watch by how they look; plus the vest) as `render:c<16 hex>` in KV (no expiry) and in `meta`, so a combination is never generated twice
- GET/PUT `/render?key=` share a photo between devices; `GET /render?list=1` lists every kept key (a failed list answers 503)
- the copies kept per date (`render:<YYYY-M-D>`, 2026-11-07/08, 90 days) are read only: `GET /render?day=` (`renderId(o, true)`), which apps before #523 (2026-11-17) still ask and move to its combination; today's app no longer asks, and they expire by February 2027; `PUT /render` takes a combination key only (2026-11-17)
- `DELETE /render` went on 2026-11-17 (CR-6-14): no app or tool asked for it; it answers 405
- photos under test keys (`c7e57…`) expire from KV after 7 days (`renderTtl`)
- `PUT /render` reads at most 4 MB of body (`readCapped`, `RENDER_MAX`; 413 past it) and `PUT /cm-shared` at most 200 KB (CR-6-07, 2026-11-17)
- a kept photo's stamp `t` more than `RENDER_AHEAD` (10 minutes) past the server's clock is kept as that limit, and one kept so before loses to any upload (CR-6-07: a record a wrong clock stamped years ahead could never be replaced)
- not done: `GET /render?list=1` is still one KV list per app start per person (a read cannot replace it without an index document that costs a write per photo)
- the app asks for it once per start at most (both of its readers share one request) and not again for the day once refused (photos.js `workerList`)
- KV writes per item added, option A (the owner's choice, 2026-11-15): the swatch is a 128 px JPEG (app.js `cropSwatch`, `SW_Q` .8, about 6-8 KB of text; the PNG was about 38 KB) and data strings under `PH_MIN` (16384) stay inline in the backup (sync.js and worker/backup.js); the photo, the 480 px `photoSm` and a close-up stay their own `ph:` values
- measured on the lab (real worker, a tester's phone, one sync per item): a suit with a close-up 5 → 4 writes, a shirt without one 4 → 3, shoes (no swatch) 3 → 3
- old records stay as they are: PNG swatches already stored as `ph:` values are still referenced and read (nothing is cut again or re-uploaded); an edited old item's swatch under `PH_MIN` goes inline and its old `ph:` value is left to the daily clean-up; an older app still sends small photos by reference in `photos` and the worker takes them (`isPhotoOld`, `PH_OLD` 256)
- either order of deploy works: an older worker splits the app's inline swatch out as a `ph:` value as before (no saving until the worker is deployed); `tests/backup-storage.test.js`
- not done: making `photoSm` device-only (option B, not chosen: needs saving it without a new stamp and a merge that keeps the local copy, else devices re-upload each other's items forever)
- the owner's colour-coded masters are shared with testers (2026-11-14): `cm:shared` = `{keys, t}` lists exactly the render keys that are master assets; owner-only `GET`/`PUT /cm-shared` (`handleShared`), and a PUT with the same keys writes nothing
- a tester's `GET /render?key=` falls back to the owner's `render:<key>` only when they have no copy of their own and the key is in `cm:shared` (`sharedKeys`); nothing else of the owner's ever falls back (outfit pictures, item photos, backups, snapshots, jobs)
- `cm:shared` is read at most once a minute per isolate (`SHARED_MS`, a `WeakMap` per KV binding); a `PUT /cm-shared` updates that isolate's copy at once
- a tester's `GET /render?list=1` adds the `cm:shared` keys to their own (one cached read, no extra list; the list call still counts once against their `list` cap, the fallback reads count nothing)
- a tester's `PUT /render` of a master key acts on their own copy only (it then shadows the owner's for them alone)
- `GET /blob` reads the owner's `blob:<key>` for everyone: every blob is a master's packed setup, written only by the owner's PC builder; if a blob ever holds anything private, this must change
- `tests/sharedmasters.test.js`: the fallback and its limits, the list, blobs (read; `PUT /blob` 405), the 60 s cache, cm-upload writing the list once, and a tester's page painting a suit look against the real worker

#### worker/holidays.js

- `/holidays?country=XX`: public holidays from Google's public holiday calendars, for countries Nager.Date lacks (the app asks Nager.Date itself first)
- when no calendar answers, a built-in list (`BUILTIN_HOLIDAYS`, `source:'built-in'`): Kuwait (2026-11-15, the new-user audit: KW answered 404 on every start), the fixed national days and the Islamic holidays on their expected dates, named "(expected)" (Kuwait announces the exact days off shortly before); dated to the end of 2028: extend it each year (`tests/server-limits.test.js` fails when it reaches under ~10 months ahead)

#### worker/calendar.js

- `POST /calendar {url, tz, days?, fresh?}` (`handleCalendar`, 2026-11-17): reads a calendar subscription link for the app (a browser can't: no CORS) → `{from, to, tz, events:[{id, title, start, end, allDay, time, location, days}], more}`, the events touching today to `CAL_DAYS` (60) days ahead in the person's zone; owner or tester, like every route
- the link is a secret: it comes in the POST body only (a GET with ?url= is 405), is never written to KV, logged or echoed (errors are fixed sentences, `CAL_MSG`; a failed fetch logs only "timeout"/"network"); event descriptions, attendees and the calendar's own UIDs never leave the server (ids are `calHash` of UID and instance day)
- only https on a public host (`calUrl`: webcal:// and http:// read as https://, links.js `publicHost`, no user:password), redirects followed by hand and each checked (5 at most), `CAL_MAX_BYTES` (6 MB) read, `CAL_FETCH_MS` (15 s); a 401/403/404/410 says the link no longer works, a page that isn't BEGIN:VCALENDAR is 422
- no KV reads or writes: an answer is kept in memory for `CAL_CACHE_MS` (10 min, keyed by a hash of who, the link, zone and day; `fresh` skips it), and each person may cause `CAL_RATE_N` (6) real reads per 10 minutes per isolate (then the last answer from memory, or 429 "read several times just now")
- the parser (`calParse`, `calEvents`): folded lines, escaped text, VALUE=DATE all-day (DTEND exclusive), DATE-TIME in UTC, a TZID (IANA; Outlook's Windows names via `WIN_TZ`; else the calendar's VTIMEZONE standard offset; else the person's zone) or floating, DURATION, STATUS:CANCELLED left out, RRULE DAILY/WEEKLY/MONTHLY/YEARLY with INTERVAL, COUNT, UNTIL, BYDAY (with ordinals), BYMONTHDAY, BYMONTH, EXDATE and RECURRENCE-ID overrides (the moved instance keeps the id of the day it replaced); a timed event under 24 hours is its start day (a gala till 1 a.m.)
- speed (the free plan's CPU time): only the properties used are parsed (`CAL_PROPS`), a non-repeating event outside the window is dropped as it is read, zone offsets are kept per quarter hour (`tzMemo`); measured on the owner's PC: a 1.4 MB calendar of 3,000 events about 8-13 ms warm, the fixture 0.5 ms (the first call in an isolate about 12 ms more for Intl); a dedicated small calendar, as the walkthrough suggests, is the safe case

- the parser lives in calparse.js (2026-11-25, moved unchanged: calHash … calEvents), an ES module worker/calendar.js imports and the app's cal-worker.js imports; the deploy workflow runs for it (`.github/workflows/deploy-worker.yml` paths); POST /calendar is otherwise unchanged for older apps (its answers byte for byte main's, `tests/calfeed.test.js`)
- `POST /calendar/feed {url, tk?, etag?, lm?}` (`handleCalFeed`): the feed's own bytes streamed back unparsed (`text/calendar`, with `x-cal-etag`, `x-cal-lm`, `x-cal-tk`, exposed by CORS), or 204 when the calendar answers 304 to the etag / last-modified passed on; the same link checks and redirects as POST /calendar, the same per-person count (`CAL_RATE_N`), no KV reads or writes, the link never logged
- not an open proxy: only a body that starts as a calendar (`BEGIN:VCALENDAR`, read from the first bytes) is relayed, else 422 with nothing of it; only the person's own link: `x-cal-tk` is an HMAC of who and the link under the owner's secret (`calTicket`), and a link read without its ticket (Connect, a phone's first read) counts against `CAL_NEW_N` (4) per `CAL_NEW_MS` (1 h) per person in the isolate
- a feed over `CAL_FEED_MAX` (40 MB) is refused (413, "This calendar is too large to read…") when its length says so, and fails the read when it grows past it unannounced: never cut silently
- measured (`tests/calfeed.test.js`, Node on the owner's PC, per read): the relay 0.14 ms at 0.05 MB, 0.16 ms at 1.4 MB, 0.28 ms at 6 MB, 0.7-0.9 ms at 24 MB; POST /calendar's parse 2.4, 23, 98-117 and 78-86 ms there (24 MB is cut at 6 MB)

#### worker/backup.js

- `/sync`: KV binding `WARDROBE`; the backup's `{rev, saved}` is kept as the KV key's metadata, so `?v=` and the PUT conflict check don't load the backup
- `kvMeta(env, key)` reads a key's metadata with `getWithMetadata(key, {type:'stream'})` and never reads the value, so `syncVersion` (every sync) and `keepSnapshots` (every save) don't list KV; a KV without `getWithMetadata` (the test mocks) lists
- `GET /sync?lite=1&since=` (`syncLiteGet`) reads only the small value; `GET /sync?ph=` (`syncPhotos`) returns photos; `POST /sync` (`syncLitePost`) merges the delta by the app's rules (`mergeLite` = merge.js `mergeStateBy`, the app's own rules), keeps the daily copies (`keepSnapshots`) and writes once; nothing new → nothing written; an older worker answers POST 405, and the app only POSTs after a `lite:1` answer
- `mergeLite` is merge.js `mergeStateBy` with the server's options (`MERGE_SERVER`: `num`, `lax`, `Date.now`, `drop: sampleOnly`); `liteSig` is merge.js `stateSig(s, true)`; exported for `tests/fieldmerge.test.js` and `tests/mergeshared.test.js`
- backup.js imports `../merge.js` (`import '../merge.js'`, side effect only) and takes `stamp` (as `stampOf`), `mergeStateBy` and `stateSig` from `globalThis.DW_MERGE` (Round B, CR-6-12: its ~130 copied lines `mergeItemLite`, `mergeDayLite`, `mergeSettingsLite`, `mergeSplitLite`… are gone); Wrangler bundles merge.js into the worker
- the owner's "Delete everything" keeps `wiped` = {at} (people.js `WIPED_KEY`; not in any family `wipeSome` deletes); `syncLiteGet` reads it for the owner only, after the `same` answer, and sends it as `wiped`
- the stamps from wrong clocks (#485) include the field stamps: `clampStamps` reads a stored `fs` / settings `kse` stamp ahead back at the save time (`fsClamp`, `fs.u` kept equal to the clamped stamp), `dropFuture` leaves out a sent record or settings with one ahead (`fsOver`, `kseOver`); the app's sync.js `clockFix` drops a restamped record's `fs` and puts `kse` stamps ahead back to now; `put()` stamps with core.js `nextStamp` and `itemFields` raises it above the stored copy only when it isn't
- the server keeps and hands out no sample piece he hasn't made his (fix round 3, W1-3-04: the 14-15 Nov apps uploaded the whole sample, and every phone joining got it with "You're trying a sample wardrobe"): worker/backup.js `sampleOnly` (`sample` without `sampleMine`) leaves them out of `GET ?lite` (`items` and `idx`) and `mergeLite` drops them, so the next save cleans the backup (one write, part of a save that changes something anyway); day records naming them stay, read as pieces deleted since
- `/sync` takes backups up to 48 MiB (`BACKUP_MAX`); KV holds 25 MiB a value, so one over 20 MiB (`PART_MAX`) is kept in parts: the first under the key, with `parts:n` in its metadata, the rest as `<key>~<rev>~<i>`, written before the key; reads (`storedResponse`, `partedStream`) stream them back part by part, daily copies copy them (`copyStored`), a new version deletes the old version's parts (`dropParts`), "Delete everything" takes `wardrobe:v1~…` too
- a PUT over 20 MiB is never parsed whole (`putParted`, `BIG_HEAD`): sync.js sends `data` last so the worker reads `baseRev` from the first bytes; an older app (data first) is answered 413 "update the app"
- such a PUT is saved only through `scanPhotos` (its photos cut out, the rest parsed and saved by `putWhole`: the record check, the write budget); one whose photos can't be cut out, or whose rest still isn't a small backup, is refused 413 (CR-6-02, 2026-11-17: it was stored in parts as it came, past `recCheck` and the write cap, and left a backup with no index that every POST answered 422 `legacy`); no app sends one (its photos are plain base64)
- `tests/rollback.test.js`: a 30 MB backup whose one photo can't be cut out and a 21 MB one with none are refused; a backup kept in parts before is read whole, handed back on a conflict, copied with its parts and replaced by a small one; 51 MB is refused
- a backup stored inline (the whole data, or with #284's index `{"rev","saved","index","data"}`, read by `readHead`/`loadSync`) is still read and is converted to the photos-stored-once form at its next save (worker/photostore.js); an older app's `PUT /sync` has its photos hashed out by `splitPhotos`
- a tester's backups: 30 a day (`EXTRA_CAPS.backup`; 300 for all testers together), counted on each save and given back on a conflict; over it, 429 `{what:'backup'}` "Today’s 30 backups are used up. Your changes are kept on this phone and backed up tomorrow."
- every answer that saved nothing gives the backup back (CR-6-05, 2026-11-17): a PUT's body is parsed before anything is counted (bad JSON: 400, uncounted), and photostore.js `handleSync` gives it back on any other non-2xx and on a thrown error, as `syncLitePost` does; before, 30 cut-off uploads used up a tester's day
- stamps from wrong clocks (fix round 3, 2026-11-16): a save's records, days, settings and deletion marks dated more than `CLOCK_AHEAD` (10 min) past the server's clock are left out (`dropFuture`), so crafted marks dated years ahead delete nothing (W2-13-03); what the backup holds from before is read with such stamps at its own save time (`clampStamps(data, saved, saved+CLOCK_AHEAD)`), the same in `syncLiteGet` and `syncLitePost`, stored so at the next save
- the daily copy's day is the app's `day` unless it is more than a day away from the server's UTC date, ahead or behind, then the server's (`keepSnapshots`; a clock a year ahead kept `snap:2027-11`, one behind `snap:2020-01-01`: CR-6-08, 2026-11-17); tests that save on several days use days around today (photostore, snapshots, accounts)
- one save at a time per person (CR-6-01, 2026-11-17): `saveLock(env, ns)` takes the person's RenderHub lock (render.js `/lock`) after the body is checked and the backup counted, around the version read, the check and the write, in `syncLitePost` (`syncLiteSave`) and `handleSync`'s PUT (`putWhole`, `putParted`); it is given back in a `finally`; before, two saves from one version were both answered `{rev: N+1}` and the later write dropped the other's change (20 of 20 races at 15 ms a KV call)
- the lock busy for about 3 s (`LOCK_WAIT`) answers 409 `{error:'conflict', busy:true}`; KV showing an older version than the hub's `last` (a stale read elsewhere, `lockStale`) answers 409 too; every app since 2026-11-07 answers a 409 by asking again and reads nothing from its body, POST and PUT alike
- the lock fails open: no `RENDER_HUB` binding, a hub that throws or an answer it doesn't know → the save goes ahead unlocked, as before
- the lock costs no KV read or write (the version check is the save's own `syncVersion` read) and 2 hub requests a save tried (a few more while one waits); a race now writes 4 KV values for two saves instead of 6 (the day's copies once)
- a new version's `saved` is never the version before's (`nextSaved`: at least `m.saved + 1`): two forks once shared one `{rev, saved}` and the losing phone's quick check said "nothing new"
- `POST /sync` with a `baseSaved` other than the stored `saved` at the same rev (a fork from before the lock) answers 409; the app asks again since its version, gets everything (`syncLiteGet`'s `all`) and sends what the backup lacks; a save with no `baseSaved` (or 0) is taken as before
- `tests/savelock.test.js`: 20 races of two saves (0 lost, distinct `saved`, 4 KV puts), an older app's PUT racing a POST, a stale read, a fork, a held lock (409 busy after about 3 s, the backup given back; only the holder lets go; free after 30 s), the lock given back after a conflict, a refused record and a failed write, the hub down, no binding, and "Delete everything"
- a backup whose new photos pass the day's writes stores the photos that fit and is refused 429 `{what:'backup', write:true}` ("…N of M new photos are saved…", `writeBudget`), so sync.js pauses until the next UTC day; the stored photos are skipped next time (`phThere`), and the save finishes then
- the daily copies carry the item count of the version copied in their metadata (`keepSnapshots(…, n)`, `n`; no extra write), and `?snaps=1` lists it (W2-14-03); `syncLitePost` and photostore.js `putWhole` pass it

#### worker/photostore.js

- photos stored once (2026-11-13): each photo is its own KV value `<ns>ph:<hash>` (the hash `phHash`; the value the photo's JSON string; metadata `{t}` = when written), and the backup is `{"rev","saved","pv":1,"revs","data"}` with every photo in `data` as `dwph:<hash>`, metadata `{rev, saved, pv:1}` (`pvText`, `pvMeta`, `isPv`); the owner's is about 137 KB
- a save (`syncLitePost`, an older app's `PUT` → `putWhole`) writes only the photos not stored yet (`putPhotos`: a photo is written again only when the clean-up has it in view, `ph:gc`'s `cand`/`gone`) and then the small value; photos first, so a failed photo write never leaves a backup pointing at nothing
- the daily and monthly copies (`keepSnapshots`) copy the small value with `pv:1`, so they share the photos
- the old form is rebuilt on request (`pvResponse`, streamed 8 photos at a time, `{rev, saved, data}` only): an older app's `GET /sync` and its 409, `?snap=` (Roll back), and `putParted`'s 409; `storedResponse` sends any `pv` value that way
- `?ph=` reads each photo from its own key; `?lite=1` reads the small value (`loadAny` = `loadSync` for a backup still stored inline)
- conversion: a backup stored inline is converted at its next save: all its photos are stored, its daily copy is written in the small form (`storePhotos` gives `keepSnapshots` the old version by reference), then the new version; nothing is converted on a save that changes nothing
- a PUT over 20 MiB has its photos cut out of the bytes unparsed (`scanPhotos`: a `"data:` string of `PH_MIN`+ characters with no escapes, hashed over its bytes) and is saved like any other; if what is left is still over 20 MiB (or a single photo is), it is refused 413 (2026-11-17, CR-6-02; it was kept in parts)
- the daily clean-up (`photoGc`, from `scheduled()` in the quarter-hour at 23:00 UTC, `gcDue`; the owner and every tester in `invites:index`): a photo is deleted only when neither the backup nor any kept copy refers to it (`refsInUse` reads every `pv` value; an inline copy refers to nothing; an incomplete list deletes nothing), it was seen so at two runs a day apart (`ph:gc` `{cand: {hash: first seen}, gone}`), and its own `t` is over a day old (`GC_AGE`); it reads each photo again before deleting, holds the bytes, reads the backups once more after and puts back any now referred to; at most `GC_MAX` (200) a run
- it also converts kept copies stored inline, `GC_SNAPS` (2) a run, monthly ones first, keeping their expiry, once the backup itself is converted (`convertSnaps`)
- measured on a copy of the owner's backup (98 items, 191 photos, 16.74 MB): a later save of the day 16.89 MB written → 136 KB; a day's first save (backup + daily copy) 33.8 MB → 273 KB; 42 copies at steady state about 726 MB → 22 MB (the photos 16.5 MB once); the converting save writes 191 photos + 3 values (194 KV writes, once); an older app's GET still gets the identical 16.74 MB; the clean-up with 42 copies about 2 ms
- don't roll the worker back past this change without converting the backups back: an earlier worker would hand `dwph:` references to apps as photos (nothing is lost, the photos stay in `ph:`)
- `tests/backup-storage.test.js`: saves and dedupe, copies and Roll back, an older app's GET, PUT, 409 and a 25 MB PUT, conversion from inline and from #284's index, the clean-up (never a referenced photo, also one only an old copy refers to; two runs; a save meanwhile; a race put back; inline copies converted), account deletion

#### worker/people.js

- `whoIs(env, token)` is the one place that says who is calling, from the `x-app-token` header: the owner when the token's SHA-256 equals `APP_TOKEN`'s (`sha256Hex`, `sameText`; never `!==`) → `{id:'owner', role:'owner', ns:'', caps:null}`; a tester when KV has `invite:<sha256 hex of the code>` = `{id, name, caps, created, off}` → `{id, name, role:'tester', ns:'u:<id>:', caps, hash}`; `off` → 403 `{error:'invite off', message}`
- storage per person: every KV key of a person's data starts with `who.ns`: the backup (`<ns>wardrobe:v1`), `<ns>snap:*`, `<ns>render:*`, `<ns>ph:*` (and, until they expire, `<ns>job:*`, `<ns>jobfail:*`, `<ns>jobrun:*` left by the retired outfit-photo queue); the owner's ns is the empty string, so the owner's key names are byte for byte what they were (`tests/accounts.test.js` asserts the exact names)
