- keys that belong to nobody's namespace: `invite:<hash>`, `invites:index` (the list of invitations, so `GET /invites` reads one document instead of listing KV), `feedback:log` and `errors:log` (written before 2026-11-17, still read until they expire), and `use:<id>:<day>` (the count, only without the Durable Object)
- `GET /render?list=1`, `/sync?snaps=1`, `?snap=` and roll back see only the caller's keys
- the one exception: the owner's colour-coded master records listed in `cm:shared`, which a tester reads and lists as their own (worker/records.js)
- daily caps, testers only (`DEFAULT_CAPS` `{tag:40, pattern:10, item:20}` per UTC day, `utcDay`; an invitation's own `caps` override them, `cleanCaps`): tag = `POST /` and `/tag-many`, pattern = `/pattern`, item = item pictures; the outfit-photo cap (`photo`) went with the outfit photo on 2026-11-14, and a `photo` number in an older invitation is ignored
- a tester's first 7 days after the invitation was made (`FIRST_WEEK_MS`): the AI limits (`DEFAULT_CAPS`), writes and lists twice (`FIRST_WEEK_X`, in `whoIs`); uploads, feedback, backups, reminder changes, error reports and photo links are not doubled (the code always did this; the docs said "every limit", CR-6-15); `/me` gives `firstUntil`, and Connection says until when
- colour and pattern lookups (`describe`, `POST /describe`, 2026-11-15): 40 a day per tester (the owner's number; 80 the first week) and 150 for all testers together (`ALL_CAPS.describe`, about $1.50 a day on Sonnet 5.5 at most); `CAP_WORDS` "colour and pattern lookups"
- more caps the app does not show protect KV's daily writes (`EXTRA_CAPS`): 60 photo uploads (`PUT /render`), 20 feedback messages, 30 backups, 20 reminder changes (`push`) a day per tester
- every KV write a tester causes is counted (`write` in `EXTRA_CAPS`: 200 a day, 400 in the first week; `ALL_CAPS.write` 450 for all testers together, so the owner always has over half of the free plan's 1,000): each new `ph:` photo a backup stores and the backup itself (`writeBudget` → `putPhotos`' `allow`), its daily copies (`keepSnapshots` returns its writes, `countWrites`, never refused), `PUT /render`, reminder changes, an item picture (1) (feedback and error reports since 2026-11-17 go to a Durable Object, no KV write; `DELETE /render` is gone); `spendEach` takes several counts for one call or none, `spendUpTo` as many as are left
- KV lists are counted too (`list`: 30 a day per tester, 60 the first week, 150 for all testers): `GET /render?list=1` and `/sync?snaps=1`
- all testers together have a daily total (`ALL_CAPS`: tag 150, pattern 40, item 60, upload 300, feedback 100; the `TESTER_DAILY_TOTAL` variable, JSON, changes any), counted like one more tester under the id `all` (hub `renders:u:all:`, or KV `use:all:<day>`) after the tester's own cap; over it the call is refused in plain words (`all:true`) and the tester's own count goes back; the owner is never counted; set budget limits on the OpenAI and Anthropic accounts as well: this only bounds what testers can spend
- over a cap: 429 `{error:'limit', what, cap, used, message, detail}`, the message a plain sentence ("Today’s limit of 20 item pictures is used up. It starts again at midnight UTC.", `CAP_WORDS`); `detail` repeats it because the app's tagging errors show `detail`
- `spend(env, who, what)` counts before the AI call and answers null or `{status, body}`; `unspend` gives the count back when the call fails, both the tester's and the `all` count
- the counting is in the tester's `RenderHub` (`POST /use {what, n, cap, day}` → `{ok, used}`, kept in the object's storage as `use` = `{day, n:{…}}`; `GET /used?day=`): no KV write per AI call; without the `RENDER_HUB` binding `useCount` falls back to KV `use:<id>:<day>` with a 3-day TTL
- a tester's answers are in plain words (`plainFor` in the router's `json`, `viewer` = who asked): an `error`, `detail` or `message` that names an API, a provider, a secret, a binding, the worker, KV, billing or a dashboard (`TECH_RE`) becomes a plain sentence chosen by the route (`PLAIN`: photo, tag, pattern, sync, other; `error` becomes `server`), and the original goes to the worker's log; sentences already written for people (limits, sizes) are left alone; the owner sees everything
- invitations, owner only (`handleInvites`): `POST /invites {name, caps?}` → `{id, code, name}`, the code 32 URL-safe characters from 24 random bytes, shown once, only its hash stored; `GET /invites` → `{invites:[{id, name, caps, created, off, used}], day}`; `DELETE /invites?id=` switches one off (`MAX_INVITES` 30 active)
- switching off: the tester's hub is told first (`POST /off`), so capped calls stop at once (`spend` answers 403); the `invite:` record is rewritten with `off:true`, which every other route sees as soon as KV shows it (up to a minute in another location)
- `POST /invites {reissue: id}` (owner, fix round 3, decision 1): a new code for an active tester's same account (same `id`, so the same `u:<id>:` backup, caps and first week); `invite:<new hash>` gets the old record, `invite:<old hash>` becomes `{replaced:true}` (no id or name), the list entry takes the new `hash` and keeps the old ones in `prev` (deleted with the tester: purge, DELETE /account); 3 KV writes; a switched-off invitation gets 400
- a replaced code is refused everywhere, DELETE /account included (no `offWho`), as 403 `{error:'invite off', replaced:true, message}` (worker.js `deny` passes `replaced`); the app tells it once as "Your invitation link was replaced by a new one…" (sync.js `endedTold`)
- `DELETE /invites?id=&purge=1` (owner): switches the invitation off, deletes everything under `u:<id>:` (the queue finishes a large one), their feedback and today's count, and takes them off the list; the code stays refused ("switched off")
- `GET /me` → `{id, name, role, caps, used, day}` (`handleMe`, `usedOf`; the owner: `caps` null and `used` all 0)
- `POST /feedback {text, version, where}` → `{ok:true}` (text 1–4,000 characters); `GET /feedback` (owner) → `{items:[{t, name, text, version, where}]}` newest first, the last 180 days, 500 at most
- feedback and error reports are kept in the logs object (render.js `logHub`, CR-6-03, 2026-11-17): each one KV document read, added to and written back, so three testers sending at once were all told `{ok:true}` and 1 of 3 was kept; now no KV write per message (the `write` count is not taken), and what `feedback:log` / `errors:log` hold from before is shown with it until they expire; without the binding (plain test mocks) the KV document as before (`logItems`, `logAdd`)
- `DELETE /account {confirm:'delete'}` → `{ok:true}` deletes only the caller's: a tester's `u:<id>:*` keys (their `ph:` photos included), their `invite:` record and line in `invites:index` (the code stops working), their feedback, their hub's storage and their push subscriptions (`dropPush`); the owner's own families only (`OWNER_FAMILIES`: the backup, `snap:`, `render:`, `ph:`, the masters' packed setups `blob:` and their list `cm:shared` (since 2026-11-17, CR-6-10: they stayed behind), and the retired queue's `job:`, `jobfail:`, `jobrun:` while any are left), never `u:*`, `invite:*`, `invites:index`, the feedback or the error reports
- a tester's deletion also takes their entries out of the logs object (`POST /log {op:'drop', id}`) and the old KV documents
- `wipeSome(env, ns, budget)` deletes a part (`WIPE_NOW` = 300 KV calls in the request, `WIPE_PART` = 500 a queue message; they were 30 and 40 from the wrong "about 50 a request", CR-6-04) and the queue finishes the rest a part per message (`{wipe:'owner'|'u:<id>:', n}`, `runWipe`); without the queue the answer carries `more:true` and the same call deletes the next part
- for the owner to check (CR-6-14): once the Cloudflare dashboard shows no `job:` keys left, `job:`/`jobfail:`/`jobrun:` can leave `OWNER_FAMILIES` (3 KV lists per owner wipe) and `TECH_RE`'s `RENDER_` can go
- a tester's `DELETE /account` writes `invites:index` first, then deletes `invite:<hash>` (2026-11-16, the audit's W1-10-01: with the day's writes used up the code went first and the list's write failed, so the app said "nothing was deleted", its retry "already deleted", and the backup stayed for good): refused there, nothing has changed; once the code is gone, a failure in the rest is sent to the queue with a delay to just after the next 00:00 UTC (`resetDelay`, at most 12 h) and answered `{ok:true, later:true}`
- the queue's wipe refused for the day's KV allowance sends itself again with that delay (worker.js `queue`) instead of `retry` a minute later (retries are few)
- Revoke (`DELETE /invites?id=`) writes the `invite:` record before telling the hub (W1-10-02: with the writes used up the hub was off and the record not: readings refused, backups taken, the list "active" for good)
- a KV write refused for the day on any route but `/sync` answers `KV_FULL_OTHER` ("…used up, so nothing was changed. Try again after the daily reset"), not the backup sentence about changes kept on the device (worker.js); `tests/backup-storage.test.js`
- `tests/serverchecks.test.js` (2026-11-17, the code review's round A): the logs kept whole when testers write at once, the reminder cron keeping a phone that switched on meanwhile, the large PUT with no photos, backup counts on answers that saved nothing, item pictures checked before counting, kept photos stamped ahead and capped bodies, "Delete everything" taking `blob:` and `cm:shared`, photo links counted and SVG refused, the dead routes, no garbled characters in the server's files
- `tests/accounts.test.js`: the owner and two testers against one mocked KV, queue and a Durable Object per name: isolation of sync, snapshots, photos and lists; the outfit photo's 410 and the gone queue routes; the owner's exact key names; caps on item pictures, readings and patterns (429, clear the next day by moving `Date.now`, refunds, KV fallback); invitations, revoking, `/me`, feedback, account deletion; the hardening; and the app (invitation link, Connection page, limit messages)
- the owner's path is covered by the existing suites `sync`, `snapshots`, `render`, `render-sync`, `prefetch`, `paste`, `tagging`, `multi`

#### worker/remind.js

- the morning reminder: `GET /push?endpoint=` → `{key, on, time}` (key null while the server has no VAPID keys), `POST /push {endpoint, time, tz, dates}` switches a phone on or updates it, `{endpoint, off:true}` off; one KV document `push:subs` (`PUSH_MAX` 200); a tester's changes are counted (`push`, 20 a day)
- `POST /push` changes or switches off only the caller's own subscription (`mine`: same push address and `x.ns === who.ns`); knowing another account's push address is not enough
- `sendReminders` (from `scheduled()`): each phone whose time, in its own time zone (`localNow`), falls in the run's window, on a date the app gave, once a day (`last`), gets a push with no body signed with the VAPID keys (`vapidAuth`: an ES256 JWT, `aud` the push service's origin); 404/410 drops the subscription
- the cron reads `push:subs` again just before writing it and makes only its own changes: `last` of the phones it sent to (when their push address and time are unchanged) and the removal of those the push service called gone (CR-4-03, 2026-11-17: the pushes take seconds, and a phone that switched on or changed its time meanwhile was overwritten); one KV read more on a run that sent something, no extra write; a phone's change within the milliseconds between that read and the write can still be lost (KV has no compare-and-swap)
- the window (`dueDay`): a run covers the local clock from the run 15 minutes before to now, so a reminder at 23:46–23:59 fires at the 23:45–00:00 run (for the day before; a window `[at, at+15)` ran past midnight and never fired), one in the hour the clocks skip fires at the run that jumps over it, and the repeated hour sends it once (`last`); `tests/workerfix.test.js`
- records coming in (fix round 3, 2026-11-16, W2-13-02): worker/records.js `recCheck` refuses a backup (PUT /sync, in `putWhole`, on every path since `putParted` refuses what its scan can't cut, 2026-11-17) or a delta (POST /sync) holding a record the app doesn't make, by the same shapes as core.js `cleanRecord` (plus the backup's own `dwph:` references), with 400 `{error:'bad record', what, message:"The backup was not saved: it holds a record the app doesn’t make (…). Update the app and try again."}` and the backup count given back; photos sent with a backup must be whole pictures (`PIC_RE`, `picBytesOk`); a stored backup is not checked again (the app cleans what it reads)
- PUT /render keeps only a whole `data:image/jpeg|png|webp;base64` picture (`PIC_RE`), `sig` cut to 2,000 and `model` to 80 characters (W2-13-06)
- POST / takes only a whole base64 picture as `image` (and `closeup`), refused before anything is spent: "javascript:alert(1)" became a Claude call (W2-13-07)
- worker/tag.js `cleanTag` gives `confidence` (and `conf`) only as high/medium/low and takes `< > " \`` out of the answer's other text; the Workers AI answer goes through it too (W2-13-05)
- secrets `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` (base64url of the 65-byte public point), `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` (base64url of the 32-byte private scalar) and the variable `VAPID_SUBJECT` (the app's https address); `tests/remind.test.js`

### `README.md`

- covers what the app is, joining by invite link, privacy, hosting (GitHub Pages + the Cloudflare Worker), running the tests

## Testing

`npm run test:all` (or `node tests/run.js`) serves the repo, drives it in headless Chromium and checks rendering, swipe/layout on phone and iPhone Duo viewports, the planner against an independent rule checklist, belts/leather/metals, the outfit score, photo-less adding, pattern detection, and the worker with mocked bindings. Add a check for every behaviour you change; extra suites can go in `tests/*.test.js`.

#### Running tests

- `npm test` = `node tests/run.js --changed` (only the tests a change can affect); `--changed --list` only says what would run; `npm run test:all` runs everything
- `NO_IMPACT=1` turns the recording off; `TEST_BASE=<commit>` compares with another commit
- docs-only changes (`*.md`) need no `npm test`: they are not part of the fingerprint; README.md is not read by any test
- Safari's engine: `npm run test:webkit` (`node tests/run.js --webkit`, or `TEST_BROWSER=webkit`) runs every test on Playwright's WebKit instead of Chromium, with no map and no selection; the owner uses only Safari on an iPhone, so run it after changes to touch, focus, storage or layout
- WebKit is not preinstalled: `node /opt/node-tools/node_modules/playwright/cli.js install webkit` then `… install-deps webkit` (apt); a cloud container loses it when it is reclaimed
- the files that check Chromium and WebKit (addsheet, backgesture, backgesture-over, backgesture-item, closeupread, closeupsheet, editorrework, editphotosb, itemredesign) take `engines` from run.js (`tests/webkit.js` `engines`, fix round A, CR-10-01/02): one shared WebKit per process with the fixed plan, the painter off and the file's flags on every context, as Chromium's; they launched WebKit from playwright themselves, so its half ran the real planner and the painter; under `--webkit` only the WebKit half runs (it ran twice, the first named Chromium); WebKit's contexts are not recorded (CDP is Chromium's)
- a new test that checks both engines takes `engines` (`module.exports=async({ok,chromium,engines,…})=>{for(const [eng,launch] of engines){…}}`), never `require('playwright').webkit.launch()` itself (pickerfocus was moved over at the merge, 2026-11-17)
- the nightly run installs WebKit as well as Chromium on the shards that have a WebKit half (`install-deps webkit`, up to five minutes of apt, then `install chromium webkit`; full-tests.yml): without it those seven files failed "WebKit: the browser starts (Executable doesn't exist …)" every night from 2026-11-16, which used up the five retries
- `tests/webkit.js` stands in for what only Chromium has: CORS-free mocks (headers added, preflights answered), CDP touches (synthetic touch events, a tap also clicks, no native scrolling), and service workers blocked (with one in control Playwright's WebKit stops routing POST/PUT to the mocks)
- expected WebKit harness failures: native scrolling under a swipe (`shopswipe`, `wardrobetap`), `sw.test.js`, and "access control checks" from fake hosts asked from a Web Worker or sw.js; the first run (2026-11-12) found one real bug, in `wardrobe.test` (a shared save timer, `wardTypeT`, drops a text field's edit when the sheet is redrawn before its `change` fires)
- about.js's fetch of the page's own sw.js (the version at start-up, `?check=<n>` when Settings opens) cut off by a reload is a WebKit page error "/127.0.0.1:<port>/sw.js[?check=<n>] due to access control checks." in a context with routes (about.js catches it; Chromium says nothing); `tests/webkit.js` (`SW_CUT`, `quietSwCut`) leaves exactly that message out of every WebKit page's `pageerror` and console-error listeners, so tests need no filter of their own (Round B: `snapshots` failed "no page errors" in 10 of 15 runs alone)
- WebKit's Linux build is not iOS Safari: no safe areas, URL-bar resizing, momentum scrolling, ITP or 7-day storage eviction; the phone stays the last check for those
- ESLint 10 (`npm run lint`, `eslint.config.mjs`, `tests/lint.js`): `no-undef`, `no-redeclare` (errors) and `no-unused-vars` (a warning); each classic script is given the top-level names of the scripts it shares a scope with (index.html's scripts; each Web Worker and what it `importScripts`), read from their sources when the config loads, plus names put on `window` from inside functions and by the tests
- the pre-push hook runs ESLint on the scripts the branch changed (`--changed`, as committed, about 1 s) and refuses the push on an error; warnings show only on lines the branch touched; with ESLint not installed it says so and passes
- `tests/globals.test.js` (Node only, about a second; Round B, CR-7-07): every assignment to another script's top-level `let`/`var`/function is in its `ALLOW` list with its exact count, so a new cross-file write fails and a write that went away must be taken off the list (it only shrinks); call the state's owner instead (`planDirty`, `todayDirty`, `setWardrobe`)
- the dependency ledger (Wave C2, 2026-11-18): `tests/deps.test.js` (Node only, about a second) finds which file uses which other file's top-level names (a read, a call, a `typeof` guard or an assignment; acorn + eslint-scope) and compares the file to file edges with `tests/deps.json` (841 when it came: 835 between the 83 page scripts, 6 in the two Web Workers' rows)
- a new edge fails until it is added to `tests/deps.json` (`node tests/deps.test.js --update --add`), with the reason in the pull request; an edge that went away fails until it is taken off (`node tests/deps.test.js --update`, which never adds), so the ledger only shrinks
- merge.js, srgb.js, back.js and locale.js must use no other file's names (`LEAVES` in tests/deps.test.js)
- `node tests/deps.test.js` prints the cycles and the files outside them (70 of the 83 page scripts were one cycle when it came); `--why a.js [b.js]` lists the names a.js uses from each file
- two branches that changed one file's line in `tests/deps.json`: take either side, then `node tests/deps.test.js --update`
- `tests/scriptlist.js` (`scriptList(html)`, `scriptTags(html)` with `module`) is the one reader of index.html's script tags, used by eslint.config.mjs, tests/typecheck.js, tests/globals.test.js and tests/deps.test.js: every `<script>` with a `src` ending in .js, whatever its other attributes (`type="module"`, `defer`) and their order, so a tag written differently can't drop out of a checker
- tests/assets.test.js and tests/bridge.test.js read the page's scripts from `tests/scriptlist.js` too (2026-11-22)
- the checkers are ready for ES modules (the ES-module move, step 1, 2026-11-22): a page script is a module when its tag is `type="module"`, and `tests/modscope.js` tells every checker what a module shares, its bridge: the names it puts on `globalThis` at its top level (`Object.assign(globalThis, {name})`, `globalThis.name = …`, `Object.defineProperty(globalThis, 'name', {get, set})`)
- ESLint lints a module with `sourceType: 'module'` (strict); the other scripts and the tests' page functions know only its bridged names, so a use of a name it keeps to itself fails `no-undef`; in a module `typeof name` on an undeclared name and `this` outside a function are errors too
- the type check takes a module in the page's program as an ES module; `types/globals.d.ts` declares its bridged names (with the type of the module's export since 2026-11-24, batch T1: see the type-checking bullets)
- tests/globals.test.js: a module owns the names it bridges (the allowances keep their file through a conversion); tests/deps.test.js: a module's row is the globals it uses plus the files it imports from
- `tests/bridge.test.js`: `BRIDGE` lists every bridged name per file (back.js 16, locale.js 1 since 2026-11-23); a file's row is added by the pull request that converts it and then only shrinks; it also fails on a global use of a name a module keeps to itself (a `typeof` guard too), a module tag without `import`/`export` in the file, a bridge statement the checkers can't read, a name bridged twice or over a classic script's, an import that isn't a plain relative path to another `type="module"` page script, and a Web Worker that `importScripts` a module
- `tests/bridge.test.js` since step 3 (2026-11-23) also fails on: a row that `origin/main` already has gaining a name (main's copy of the file is read through git; a name that must be added after a conversion goes in `GROWN` with its reason; where git has no `origin/main` the check is skipped and says so); a `let` handed over with `Object.assign` (the global would be a copy); an accessor that isn't plainly `get: () => name` on the file's own `let`; a setter that assigns another name; a page script, test or tool assigning a bridged name that has no setter (`name = …`, `name++`, `window.name = …`: with a getter only the write does nothing, and a function handed over with `Object.assign` is replaced on the page while the module keeps calling its own, so a test's stub never runs for the module's own calls); a module missing from sw.js `ASSETS` under its tag's spelling
- a Web Worker's files come from `tests/modscope.js` `workerFiles(root, worker)` (2026-11-24): its `importScripts(…)`, or a module worker's `import './x.js'` lines, in order; eslint.config.mjs (a module worker is linted as a module), tests/typecheck.js (its scope resolves modules), tests/deps.test.js, tests/bridge.test.js, tests/mergeshared.test.js and tests/modtools.test.js ask it; tests/assets.test.js and tests/impact.js read the imports too
- `tests/bridge.test.js` checks a module worker: its imports are plain relative paths to page scripts with no names (the files hand theirs over on `globalThis`), it bridges nothing, what its files read there is some file's of its list (a classic file's top-level name or a module's bridge), and its writes need setters; `--plan` counts its uses of a file it imports
- the bridge check doesn't take a declaration's own initializer for a write (escope leaves names unresolved in a function with a direct `eval`: tests/sample.test.js's `const key=` read as a write of core.js's `key`)
- `tests/modscope.js` `bridged(ast).acc` gives each accessor as written (`{get, set, reads, writes}`); a write inside a string given to `page.evaluate('…')` is not seen by the bridge check
- `tests/appsrc.js` (`appSrc(ROOT, …files)`, `asScript(src)`): an app file's text as a plain script for the Node vm tests (asmrelease, clock, colormath, costwear, fieldmerge, mergeshared, rollback, settingsmerge use it): the same text while the file has no `import`/`export`, else those statements blanked with spaces (no Node flag); a default or renamed import or export throws
- `tests/modtools.test.js` proves all of that on a made-up app (`tests/fixtures/modtools`: two classic scripts, two modules, mistakes on purpose) with eslint.config.mjs's own rules (it imports the config with `?root=<folder>`) and `tests/typecheck.js` `check(ts, root)`
- not moved onto `appSrc`: tools/fill-material.js and tests/sourcerules.test.js cut lines out of core.js, merge.js and about.js by pattern (`^const …`); `export` in front of those lines needs their patterns changed with the conversion
- type checking (Round C, 2026-11-17): `npm run typecheck` (`tests/typecheck.js`, TypeScript pinned in `package.json`, about 4 s) runs tsc's checker over the plain JavaScript with `tsconfig.json`'s options (`allowJs`, `checkJs`, not strict, nothing emitted); no types are written by hand
- its scopes are read from the sources each run, as eslint.config.mjs reads them: `page` (index.html's scripts, lib DOM), `asm` and `analysis` (each Web Worker and its `importScripts`, lib WebWorker), `sw` (sw.js) and `server` (worker.js and worker/, merge.js through its import); a worker's scope and the server count only their own files, a file the page loads counts in `page`
- `types/globals.d.ts` is generated (`--update`): the names put on window/self/globalThis that no script declares at its top level (inside a function, the test runner's TEST_PLAN and ASM_TEST_OFF, a test's switch the app reads off window such as ASM_NO_WORKER); the check fails while the committed copy differs
- `tests/typecheck-baseline.json`: each file's error count per scope (281 when it came, 2026-11-17); a file over its count fails (an unlisted file counts 0), one under it is named; `node tests/typecheck.js --update` writes it afresh and refuses a higher total, so a file split that moves errors into a new file is taken; `--list [file]` prints the errors
- `npm run lint` ends with the type check; the pre-push hook (`tests/lint.js --changed`) runs it when the branch changed any script, and also refuses a baseline total above origin/main's; it reads the working tree, not the commit; without TypeScript installed it says so and passes
- `tests/typecheck.test.js` checks the ratchet's rule on made-up counts (Node only, no TypeScript)
- since 2026-11-24 (batch T1) `types/globals.d.ts` gives a page module's bridged name its real type, the module's export of it (`declare var toast: typeof import('../toast.js').toast;`): 1,750 names; the bridge itself is unchanged; a bridged name the module doesn't export, and the names put on window by tests, tools and the runner, stay `any` (16)
- the sw scope reads an in-memory copy with every name `any` (`types/globals.loose.d.ts`, never written; `loose` on the scope in tests/typecheck.js): a `typeof import` there pulls the page's modules into sw.js's program (about 1,000 errors of page code checked as worker code); the module worker scopes take the typed copy (0 errors)
- the real types found 27 new errors, all code that works (none a bug): `Date - Date` (now `.getTime()` on both sides, where each side is always a Date: `keyDate`, `todayDate`, `new Date`), `localStorage.setItem` given `nowMs()` (now `String(…)`, what setItem does itself), and JSDoc where a shape was inferred too narrowly: `labHex`'s `@param {number[]}`, `addCustomPattern`'s optional `look`, `settFromSpec`'s optional `full`/`box`/`wb`, casts in start.js's `.then` and assemble.js `asmJob`'s `j` (type-only in the painter: `asmCmCodeSig` o89az3 and `asmCmSrcSig` 14g16pr unchanged); the page went 215 → 213, the total 250 → 248
- `tests/garmentdraw.test.js` reads origin/main's draw.js through `asScript` and runs it with a stand-in `globalThis` (2026-11-24): since C3 made draw.js a module its `export` line threw "Unexpected token 'export'", and its bridge "Cannot redefine property: swN"
- `tests/modtools.test.js` shows a wrong type on a bridged name is caught: the fixture's c.js calls `label(1).toFixed(2)` (m.js's `label` gives a string) and the check must report it; with `any` it would pass silently
- the tests' access point (T2 phase 1, 2026-11-25; tests/hook.js): each page module puts the names only the tests use (474, in 66 modules) on `HOOK` in one statement after its bridge, `globalThis.HOOK={__proto__:globalThis.HOOK||null, fnA, get flag(){return flag}, set flag(v){flag=v}};`: a chain of plain objects, one per module that loaded, so `HOOK.name` finds the name in whichever module has it
- a test reaches those names as `HOOK.name` (always spelled so: tests/bridge.test.js finds the uses by that text), in page.evaluate functions and strings alike; a setter for the 33 a test assigns (`HOOK.shopBusy=false` reaches shop.js's own `let`); `'name' in HOOK` never throws
- the app never reads HOOK (tests/bridge.test.js fails if a page script, a worker, an inline script or lab/ does); the statement is names already declared, so it can't throw, a module that fails to load is just missing from the chain, and it is no file or request (sw.js ASSETS unchanged); function texts are unchanged, so the paint fingerprint is too (o89az3, 14g16pr)
- in every page the runner hands a test (tests/run.js adds `INIT` from tests/hook.js to each context) and in a vm context after `vmHook(ctx)`, the chain ends in a root that throws a ReferenceError for a name no module put there, read or written, as a bare name does; the app's own chain ends in null
- `types/globals.d.ts` declares `HOOK: any` (generated: modules write `globalThis.HOOK`); tests/modscope.js reads the HOOK statement (`hooked`) and leaves it out of `bridged`, so HOOK is not a bridged name for any checker
- `node tests/bridge.test.js --hook` lists the names the tests use through HOOK alone (ready to come off BRIDGE, T2 phase 3) and each test file still using some of them as globals; `--to-hook <file> [--write]` rewrites a file's global uses to `HOOK.name` and lists what it leaves for a person (a `typeof name`, a string with an escape, a sentence that names one)
- bridge.test.js counts a name in a string's code (page.evaluate('…'), a SETUP template) as a use, and a word of a sentence too (the safe side), except in an ok() message, a CSS name (`#shop-btn`), a one-word string (a key: `metaGet('shop')`) and a string joined after `appSrc(…)` (the vm tests' own scope)
- T2 phase 3 (2026-11-25): the 468 names the tests used through HOOK alone are off the bridge (the modules' `Object.assign`/`Object.defineProperties` statements and BRIDGE in tests/bridge.test.js); 1,282 of the 1,750 remain bridged; they stay on each module's HOOK statement, and the exports are unchanged; add-paste.js and theme.js have no bridge left
- what stays bridged: the names the app's files, the Web Workers, lab/ or the inline `startUp()` read as globals, plus six only tests use, because a test also reads them as globals on an older build that has no HOOK: `syncing` (replay W1-4-01, c4a549b), `kitEdited` (replay `_verify3-lib`), `asmCmMf`, `asmCmCodeSig`, `asmCmSig`, `asmCmSrcSig` (garmentpaint.test.js puts them on origin/main's page's HOOK)
- a name only the tests use goes on its module's HOOK, not the bridge: a test reads it as `HOOK.name`, and BRIDGE gains nothing (a row only shrinks)
- bridge.test.js `notCode` (phase 3): a string that is a file name (`'shop.js'`), a host name (`'shop.test'`) or an id (`'rd-camo'`, words joined by hyphens, no space) is no use, though each parses as code; in a string that doesn't parse, an attribute selector's value (`[data-act="setOpenTap"]`: markup) and a tab-separated record (a tests/impact.map line) are no use either; `--to-hook` leaves them as they are (on main it wrote `'HOOK.shop.js'`); checked on made-up strings in bridge.test.js
- a name a test reaches by a computed key (`window[n]`, `typeof globalThis[n]`, `'name' in window`) isn't seen by bridge.test.js: phase 3 read every one-word string naming a leaving name; folds.test.js now calls `(toggle in HOOK?HOOK:window)[toggle]()`, modload.test.js's act.js-missing check counts a name on HOOK as handed over, and its Back flags check reads `appHist`/`appKind`/`itemHist`'s accessors in HOOK's chain (`Reflect.set(HOOK,'appKind','zz')` is refused)
- restructure S1a (2026-11-25, `C:\code\cr-2026-11-16\STEP1.md`): no app file assigns another file's top-level `let`; it calls a setter the owner exports and bridges (an imported binding is read-only, so step 5 needs this): app.js `setSel`, `setView`, `setFlt`, `setAiError`, `setAiModel`, `setConnReady`, `setDbConn`, `setDbCur`, `setDbWiping`; core.js `setWeather`, `setClimate`, `setClockSkew` (and `setWardrobe`); history.js `setPastEdit`, `setSwapMsg`, `setTodayPullWait`; month.js `setMonthOpen`; scorecard.js `setScoreOpen`; today.js `setListOpen`; photos.js `setRemoteKeys`; settings.js `setSetSeen`, `setViewStale`; safety.js `setStoreToldAt`; sync.js `setRestoring`; kit.js `setKitNoMine`; add-sheet.js `setAddFor`; assemble.js `setAsmOut`
- the setters are one-line functions above each owner's bridge (`function setSel(v){sel=v}`); a call where the owner failed to load throws a ReferenceError where the assignment threw one (`a=b=c=false` became `setMonthOpen(false);listOpen=false;setScoreOpen(false)`, the order the chain assigned in)
- `tests/globals.test.js` `ALLOW` is empty since S1a: any app file assigning another file's top-level name fails it (call the owner's setter)
- each owner's HOOK statement has a getter and a setter for every `let` the tests assign (S1a: 42, `items`, `dayRecs`, `TODAY_OVERRIDE`, `cache`, `sel`, `view` …), so a test can write `HOOK.cache={}` where it wrote `cache={}`; three written names are functions with no setter (`show`, `save`, `fill`)
- `--to-hook` also rewrites the names the tests assign (those with a HOOK setter, `writeSets`) where they are assigned only: `cache={}`, `x+=1`, `x++`, a destructuring target and `window.x=` become `HOOK.…`; a read, `x.y=` and `x[k]=` stay; an assigned name with no setter and an escaped string that assigns one are left for a person; the six older-build names (`OLD_BUILD`: syncing, kitEdited, asmCmMf, asmCmCodeSig, asmCmSig, asmCmSrcSig) are left alone; a command-line flag string (`'--show'`, `CLI_FLAG`) is not code
- bridge.test.js can't see a page use of a name that the test file also declares on the Node side (dress.test.js's own `shop` hid `shop={…}` inside `q.evaluate`, which then made a new global; the cloud run caught it): such a write now goes `HOOK.shop={…}`; at phase 3 every file declaring a leaving name was read by hand, and only that one ran it in the page
- tests/globals.test.js's floor on owned names is 600 (a module owns the names it bridges: 966 on main, 655 after phase 3)
- tests/actsindex.test.js writes a step's expected call as `C('stepItem','1')` (= `'stepItem(1)'`), so the label is no use of the name; with a name off the bridge it watches the function at its handler only (`VIA`), as before
- restructure S1f (2026-11-25), the end of step 1: `tests/bridge.test.js` fails when any test or tool assigns a bridged name (bare, `window.name`, in its code or the code of its strings: `readers`' `testWrites`); a test sets app state with `HOOK.name=…` (`--to-hook` rewrites it); the one exception is `OLD_BUILD_WRITES` (garmentpaint.test.js's `asmCmMf`, written on origin/main's older build, which has no HOOK)
- `testUses` and `--to-hook` don't read as code a string searched for in another (the first argument of `includes`, `indexOf`, `lastIndexOf`, `startsWith`, `endsWith`: motif's SVG `fill="…"`), or a call's label handed straight to a helper of the file (`step(what, act, 'findCity()')`) outside a call that runs code (`SEARCHED`, `RUNS_CODE`, `CALL_LABEL`, `notCodeStrings`); a one-call string run in the page or kept in a list to be run later (folds.test.js `['score','toggleScore()','#sbox']`, tools/cssdiff.js's screens) is still code
- S1f took the six names S1a left to the tests off their bridges and BRIDGE (`addFor`, `asmOut`, `swapMsg`, `remoteKeys`, `storeToldAt`, `listOpen`: the app calls the owner's setter, the tests use HOOK); `calSummary`, `loadSnapshots`, `renderAbout` and `renderCare` (bus.js listeners now) are also used through HOOK alone and can come off next (`--hook`)
- measured at phase 3 (`C:\code\t2-shrink-lab`, main 7fc116a beside the branch): `failload.js`, each of the 66 changed modules missing and not parsing (133 cases), Chromium and WebKit: what a person sees is main's case for case; `offline.js`: the offline start as main's; the replays 86 of 86; `fp.js`: `asmCmCodeSig` o89az3, `asmCmSrcSig` 14g16pr on both engines; the type check 248 (main's 248)
- what the baseline holds (2026-11-17, all checked by hand, none a bug): DOM types (`querySelector` gives `Element`, so `.style`, `.dataset`, `.value`: about 150), objects given fields after they are made, `Date` arithmetic, numbers passed to `localStorage.setItem` and `setAttribute`, `replace` callbacks returning numbers, sw.js's `self` typed as a plain worker, asm-worker.js replacing assemble.js functions on purpose; today.js calls `toggleZoom(x, y)`, which takes no arguments (the zoom ignores where the tap was, as the owner asked)
- installing a package in a worktree whose `node_modules` is a junction to `C:\code\DailyWardrobe\node_modules`: `npm install` replaces the junction with a real folder (npm's "Removing non-directory"); copy the package into the shared folder instead, and put the junction back
- `npm run gaps` (`tests/gaps.js`): the top-level functions and `styles.css` rules no test reaches, from the committed map
- **One test run at a time on this machine** (the owner's choice, 2026-11-17; `tests/testlock.js`): every run that runs tests (`npm test`, `test:all`, `test:webkit`, a run by name; not `--list`) first takes the lock file `os.tmpdir()/dw-test.lock` (pid, start, worktree, branch), shared by every worktree
- a run that finds the lock held waits and says so ("waiting for the test run in <worktree> (<branch>, pid …, 4 min)"), looking again every 3 s; don't kill another session's run to get it
- a stale lock is taken over and said so: its pid is dead (a killed run), it was not touched for 5 min (the holder touches it every 15 s) or it is older than `TEST_LOCK_MAX_AGE` (90 min)
- after `TEST_LOCK_WAIT` minutes of waiting (5; 0 = don't wait), or when the lock can't be written, the run goes ahead with at most 8 at a time (fewer when other runs are already going ahead that way, listed as `dw-test.lock.<pid>`) and says so
- `TEST_LOCK=0` skips the lock and the busy-machine cap; `TEST_JOBS=<n>` still sets the number at once in every case
- the lock goes when the run ends, fails, gets Ctrl+C or is killed; a worker process exits when its parent goes (`disconnect`), and Playwright closes its browsers with it (tested: a run killed with `Stop-Process -Force` left no worker or browser 6 s later, and the next run took over its lock)
- a busy machine gets fewer at once: just before the tests start the runner samples the processors for 0.5 s (`busyCap`), and when more than a third are in use it runs only as many as the idle cores less one (at least 2), and says so ("the machine is already busy (60% …): 11 at a time instead of 15")
- runs on older branches don't know the lock: until a branch has merged this main, its runs neither wait nor are waited for
- **WebKit halves in `npm test` only for page changes** (the owner's choice, 2026-11-17; `webkitWanted` in run.js): the WebKit half of a file that checks both engines runs when the change touches a file in sw.js `ASSETS` other than the compute-only scripts (`PURE`: planner.js, core.js, analysis.js, analysis-worker.js, detect.js, srgb.js, merge.js, migrate.js, asm-worker.js, sw.js), or `tests/run.js`, `tests/webkit.js`, `tests/fixedplan.js`, `package.json`
- otherwise only an edited dual test file's own WebKit half runs; `npm test` prints a "WebKit: …" line saying which halves run and why; when what changed isn't known they all run
- `npm run test:all`, `npm run test:webkit` and a run by name always run the WebKit halves; `TEST_WEBKIT=1` forces them in `npm test`, `TEST_WEBKIT=0` leaves them out of `npm test` and of a run by name
- a test that runs longer than `TEST_FILE_TIMEOUT` seconds (900; 0 = no limit) fails "timed out after N s" with what it printed so far, its worker process leaves (closing its browsers) and a new one takes its place (2026-11-17: on a crowded machine single workers sat at no CPU for 20–30 min and the run never ended); the slowest test took 477 s on that crowded machine; only parallel runs (not `TEST_JOBS=1`) have the limit
- a worker process starts its Chromium while the recorder loads the worker (`bench`, eager), not when its first test asks; every test file already got one shared browser per worker process with a fresh context per file (a fresh Chromium per file measured 2.3 s against 0.37 s for a fresh context, under load); no test file launches its own browser (only `tests/replay` and tools that tests spawn do)

#### Replay tests (the 2026-11-15 audit's scripts)

- `npm run test:replay` (`tests/replay/run.js`): the audit's 84 bug replays (`tests/replay/scripts/W*.js`), each starting its own lab (`harness/lab.js`: the real worker.js on an in-memory KV, Claude and OpenAI mocked) and driving a WebKit iPhone 17 Pro (`harness/phone.js`); a script prints REPRODUCED (the bug is back) or NOT REPRODUCED (still fixed)
- not part of `npm test`, the nightly run or any push check: it needs Playwright's WebKit and takes about 60 minutes of script time (W2-18-01 alone about 5), about 10 minutes with `--jobs 8` on the owner's PC; run it after a change near a fixed bug, or before a release
- `--only W2-13-` (a prefix, or several comma-separated), `--jobs <n>` (script groups at once), `--app <dir>` (test another copy; default this checkout); a group's scripts share a lab port (verifier N: 9610+10×(N-1)) and run one after another, groups side by side
- it also runs the code review's fix-round replays, `tests/replay/scripts/CR-<reviewer>-<nn>.js` (2026-11-17): each uses a lab port group no other script uses, so each is a group of its own and runs beside the others (`groupOf`; `--list` says "its own port"); `--only CR-` runs just those
- it exits 1 on any REPRODUCED or NO VERDICT; a script that didn't pass runs once more alone after the rest and the summary names it as flaky; logs, kv files, profiles and screenshots go to the git-ignored `tests/replay/.work/`
- W2-19-09 (the text-size cap at 150%) is kept on purpose by the owner: its REPRODUCED is expected, and a NOT REPRODUCED fails the run as "kept behavior changed" (`KEPT` in run.js)
- old builds the scripts replay ("the phone was still on the old app": 26cd8b1, c4a549b, 7a28f96) are made with `git archive` into `.work/builds/<sha>` (`RP.old` in `harness/paths.js`); without that history (a shallow clone) those scripts give NO VERDICT: `git fetch --unshallow origin`
- no owner photos: `harness/make-photos.js` draws 18 photo-sized stand-ins into `.work/photos` (W2-18-01 counts how many photo items fit in one backup, so they are as large as phone photos)
- nothing leaves the machine (`harness/net.js`): Open-Meteo and Nager.Date answers are replayed from `tests/replay/fixtures/net` (`--record-net` records missing ones), every other non-127.0.0.1 request from a page or the lab's worker is refused and listed in the summary, and a script's own Node `fetch` to another host throws
- why the pages have two guards: with the app's service worker in control, WebKit sends a page's cross-origin fetch past Playwright's routes, so the browser's proxy is a local one that refuses every connection, and an init script points the page's fetch for the two data services at a local replay server

#### Parallel runner

- `tests/run.js` gives every `tests/*.test.js` file one shared Chromium (a file's `chromium.launch()`/`close()` open and close only its own contexts) and prints the slowest files at the end
- tests run several at a time: `tests/run.js` starts worker processes (`node tests/run.js --worker`; `together`, `worker`), one fewer than the processor cores, at most 16; `TEST_JOBS=<n>` sets the number, and `TEST_JOBS=1` runs them one after another in one process, printing as it goes
- why one fewer than the cores: at as many as the cores, once the suite passed 2,000 checks, a different start-up step failed each run (a 30 s page load, a click), on `main` too: the machine starving rather than a test; why at most 16: on the owner's 16-core / 32-thread PC 16 at a time took 27 s and 28 took 26–52 s with a test failing under load
- each worker process has its own local server, Chromium, recorder and Node coverage (`bench`) and runs one test at a time, so two tests never share any of them; the built-in suites of `run.js` are handed out like the files
- a test's lines are kept in its worker and printed together when it finishes (`• name`, then its checks); the parent adds up the counts and what each test used, and writes `tests/impact.map` once at the end
- the slowest tests start first, by the times of the last run (`node_modules/.cache/dw-test-times.json`, never committed)
- a file that checks both engines runs as two tests side by side (fix round A, CR-10-02): its Chromium half under its name and its WebKit half as `<file> · WebKit` (`DUAL`, `WK_HALF`, `RUNS` in run.js; a file is dual when its `module.exports=async({…})` takes `engines`); the WebKit half has no map line and runs whenever its file is chosen (`withHalves`); backgesture's two halves one after the other (about 390 s, nearly all of it waiting for sheets' slides to finish) were the whole suite's critical path; under `--webkit` a file is one test
- the Back checks are three files (2026-11-18; each half 160 s alone was the cloud run's critical path): `backgesture.test.js` (the tabs and the layers with nothing of ours under them), `backgesture-over.test.js` (over Settings and Add), `backgesture-item.test.js` (over the item sheet and the nested stacks), each a page of its own running its parts of `tests/backgesture-parts.js` (`run(parts, t)`, `PARTS`); the same 100 checks per engine, plus a "no page errors" per file; measured on the owner's PC: WebKit 47/56/59 s, Chromium 41/51/53 s (155 and 144 s as one file)
- a worker process that dies fails the test it was running, and another takes its place
- every local test server takes its port through `listenSafe(server, host)` (`tests/safeport.js`, 2026-11-18): `listen(0)` once landed on 1719 and on 6665, ports Chromium refuses (`ERR_UNSAFE_PORT`, ~70 checks failed); a port on `UNSAFE_PORTS` (the Fetch standard's bad ports, refused by WebKit and Node's fetch; Chromium refuses all but 4190 and 6679) is closed and another asked for; `tests/safeport.test.js` checks the list against Node's fetch and Chromium, and that no test or tool calls `listen(0)` itself or uses a fixed port on the list
- a promise that fails with nothing waiting for it fails the test it happened in, with its stack (`unhandledRejection` in run.js's worker process); one that fails after its test ended is reported with the name of the test before it

#### Timing and flaky tests

- after a tap, tests wait with `settle(page, ms)` (passed to every test file) instead of a fixed `waitForTimeout`: it returns once no animation runs and the scroll is still, waiting up to 2×`ms` (`DEBUG_SETTLE=1` logs each wait)
- still means 4 frames, or on WebKit also 2 frames 100 ms or more apart (fix round A; Chromium's rule unchanged): Playwright's WebKit runs a page's frames far apart at times, and its waits ran to their limit with nothing moving (140 of backgesture's 255 s of waiting on WebKit)
- a test that fails while others are running is run once more by itself after the rest (`RETRY_MAX` 5 such tests at most; more than that is not the load); only that run counts, and the summary names it ("failed with other tests running, passed by itself") and ends with "N flaky (passed on retry)"
- when the summary names a test that way, make its check wait for its condition: a test must not rely on a quiet machine (`settle`, `waitForFunction` on the condition the next check reads, a short polling loop, not a fixed time)
- speed budgets scale with the machine (`tests/pace.js`, fix round A, CR-10-03): `pace(page)` times a fixed piece of work in the page (script, canvas, an SVG decoded and drawn; median of 7) against the owner's PC (`PACE_REF` 38 ms quiet) and gives the factor (1 at the least); `mannequin` (150 ms) and `colpicklive` (12 ms, measured before the 4x throttle) allow budget × factor and say both; GitHub's 4-core runner failed the flat figures (217 against 150, 19.7 against 12)
- `holds(page, fn, arg, ms)` (tests/pace.js): true once the condition holds within ms (4 s), for a layout that settles after a timer (`nufix9`'s and `backzoom`'s 268 px checks failed on the runner when read after `settle`)
- the nightly's other timing failures, fixed by waiting for the condition (fix round A): `photoedit` waits for "Photo rotated." (the toast comes after the save), `multi` holds the slow answer until Cancel was tapped (not 1.5 s), `inviteswitch` waits for the canceled question to go before reading whether a new one came, `testerview` waits for the app's own sync to end and states the sync status in each Backup line
- fixed sleeps left in tests are only part of a gesture (a long press, the pace of touch moves, a double tap's gap) or wait to see that something does not happen
- a test that sets `items` or `dayRecs` in memory with a mocked worker that can sync (its `env` has `WARDROBE`) makes the mock answer no `/sync` (`pathname==='/sync'` → 503, as in `itempics` and `queue`), unless it tests syncing: the start-up sync (300 ms after the database opens) merges in what is saved and empties them on a busy machine
- set the worker for a page with `addInitScript` before the one page load, not on a first page followed by a reload: the first page's delayed start-up work can run before the reload (`prefetch` counted a photo downloaded twice)
- `window.appReady` is set once the kept photos are loaded (`loadRenders`, which starts `renders` afresh) and every script has run (`afterDom`, DOMContentLoaded, not the load event, which also waits for pictures): tests put photos into `renders` right after it, and read notes later scripts show
- a test that depends on today's date fixes the clock (`watchmatch` failed on the 12th of every month: the date watch's window read as a 12 numeral)
- a test's "short drag" must be slow enough not to be a flick (2026-11-17): `monthswipe` paces its 40 px drag at 30 ms a move, since `monthRelease` turns the month for over 30 px at more than 0.5 px/ms and WebKit's touch stand-in ran it in under 80 ms on a quiet machine (it passed only under load)
- a check that counts one device's sync traffic first makes every device quiet (`backup-storage`'s `quiet`, as `sync`'s) and counts only that device's requests: on the runner the older app's scheduled whole-backup sync landed in B's count ("still takes the small sync (10425.2 KB up …)", 2026-11-17)
- touch gestures take their speed from `e.timeStamp`, not `Date.now()` when handled (the shop sheet's swipe); `tests/shopswipe.test.js` gives every CDP touch its own `timestamp`, so its gestures have the same speed however busy the machine is

#### The impact map and selection

- while a test runs, the runner records in `tests/impact.map` every top-level function it runs (the app's scripts in the page over CDP coverage, worker.js and its worker/ modules in Node), every `styles.css` rule that applied to an element, the files its pages were served (`<loaded>`) and the files it read itself or ran in a Web Worker (`<any>`)
- the recorder loads the worker in Node before any test (`prepare`) and gives what ran then to every test that runs any of it: Node loads a module once per process, so its load-time code was otherwise noted for the first test that imported it only
- a test that starts a Node child process (`spawnSync(process.execPath, [tools/…])`) is recorded too: the recorder patches `child_process` (`spawn`, `spawnSync`, `execFile`, `execFileSync`, `fork`), notes a repo file in a subfolder named on the command line, and gives a Node child `tests/childrec.js` (`NODE_OPTIONS=--require`), which writes the repo files it reads when it exits; files in subfolders (`tools/*.js`, fixtures) go into the map as `<any>`; root-level files a tool serves to its own headless page are left out (the test's own pages record those function by function)
- Chromium answers no coverage request while a page load is pending and drops the old document's coverage, so the recorder reads it just before `goto`/`reload` and before a page or context closes (`recorder`); a test that navigates another way would lose what it ran before
- maps from parallel and one-after-another runs differ only in functions a timer fires (the scheduled sync, `prefetchRenders`, `lockUpcoming`); the map only adds up, so that only selects more
- the map adds up: what a test used is added to what it used before, names and tests that no longer exist are dropped (`save`)
- except a test's whole-file entries (`<any>`, `<loaded>`): when the test passed (run.js hands `save` each result's `ok`), they are what this run saw (fix round A, CR-10-06: `about.test.js` kept README.md, which it stopped reading in 50948e2, so a README-only change ran it); function and CSS names still add up (timers)
- the helper modules a test file requires from tests/ (`require('./webkit')` → `tests/webkit.js`, and theirs) are put in its entry from its source at every run (`helpersOf` in `begin`; fix round A, CR-10-08): Node reads a module once per process, so the patched fs noted a helper only for the first test in each worker process
- on Windows `node_modules` paths no longer leak into the map: `relOf` turns `\` into `/` before its filter (CR-10-09)
- `tests/sourcerules.test.js` (Node only, about a second; fix round A, CR-10-07): the rules read from the scripts as text, moved out of three page tests that therefore ran for every script change: every localStorage key, named directly or through a constant, is one Delete removes (about.js's `ABOUT_KEYS` / `aboutOwnKey` run in `vm` from its source), no `confirm`/`alert`/`prompt`, ui.js loaded and kept; `about.test.js` reads CACHE from sw.js as the page is served it, not with fs (every app change bumps CACHE)
- for a change, `git diff` against `origin/main` (committed, uncommitted and new files) is turned into the edited top-level functions and CSS rules (`jsImpact`, `cssImpact`); a test runs when it uses one of them, reads the file, was edited itself or is not in the map yet; comments and spacing select nothing
- a changed top-level constant is followed by name to the functions and constants that name it, in the scripts that share its scope (`follow`; across all the worker's files for the worker), and to the test files that name it
- a test file can leave a name out of the constant name matching with `module.exports.ignoreNames=['CACHE']` (`namedBy` in impact.js): `impact.test.js` does, since its `CACHE` lines are a made-up sw.js and the `CACHE` bump every app change needs ran that slowest test every time
- what can't be narrowed selects every test that loads the file: other top-level code, `index.html`, a new selector that no plain rule covers, an `@media` condition, a script V8 can't parse
- a top-level block called on the spot (`(()=>{…})()`) runs at load in every test, so its own functions (the listeners it installs) are recorded and selected as units, named `<block>>name` (`units`, `kids`, `flat`; `scan` lists them by compiling the block's body inside a function that does nothing)
- the block's own name (`@{<its first statement>}`) stands for its load-time code: an edit there, a listener added to it, or a whole block added or removed still selects every test that loads the file (what runs at load can reach any test, and a new block has no record to select by)
- a listener in a block is named after the code before it (`@el.addEventListenertouchmove`); two alike in one file share a name, which only selects more
- a selector new to the sheet (a rule added or its selector changed, inside `@media` or not) can only apply where its last compound matches: when an old unconditional rule's plain selector (one compound of a tag, classes and ids) asks for no more than that compound (`.head` for `.sheet .head::before`, `.card` for `.card.on`), only the tests that used that old rule are selected (`within` in `cssImpact`); with no such plain rule (`.card .dot b`, a class only styled inside `@media`), every test that loads the sheet
- a class inside `:not(…)` doesn't make a rule "for a new class" (`.card:not(.fresh)` styles the old cards); `selNeeds` reads only what an element must have, outside brackets
- a whole `@media` block added or removed counts as its rules; an edited condition is the stylesheet as a whole
- a changed `@keyframes` block selects only the tests that used a rule naming it (`animation` or `animation-name`, in the old or the new sheet; `kf` in `cssScan`): a new animation that no old rule names selects nothing by itself; any other at-rule (`@font-face`, an `@media` condition) is the stylesheet as a whole
- after `main` was merged in, `select` looks for the newest commit of the branch from before that merge whose fingerprint is in the map (`lastPass`): a test can only have turned if both the incoming change (`impactOf` from that commit) and the branch's own (`impactOf` from `origin/main`) can affect it, so the tests both select run; with a file changed on both sides (docs and the map aside), or when the incoming change selects everything, the branch's whole selection runs
- `tests/run.js`, `tests/impact.js`, `tests/fixedplan.js`, `tests/childrec.js`, `tests/webkit.js` (run.js's engines, fix round A) and `package.json` select everything (`FULL`); so does a missing map or a missing `origin/main`
- a change to a file the browser gets also runs the built-in suite "renders the outfit without errors"; `sw.js` is otherwise covered only by `tests/sw.test.js`, since service-worker code isn't recorded
- `npm test` fetches `origin/main` before comparing (`baseOf`): a cloud session's own copy can be months old with its history cut short, and then everything would run
- the fingerprint: a passing `npm test` (or whole suite; not a run by name) writes `# tested <hash>` into the map, a hash of every file's content but the map, `*.md` and `LICENSE` (`fingerprint`, the same for a folder and for the commit made from it)
- `select` trusts the map only when `origin/main`'s copy carries the fingerprint of `origin/main`'s own files; otherwise it says so and runs everything, which records and stamps anew (a map left out of a commit, an edit after the last test run, two changes merged on GitHub without a run on the result, an edit made on github.com)
- the push check is `.githooks/pre-push` (`node tests/impact.js pre-push`, `prePush`): it refuses an uncommitted map and prints a note when the commit's files were not passed by `npm test` (a note only); every test run switches it on in the clone (`installHook`: `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`, unless hooks are already set up otherwise)
- `tests/impact.test.js` checks the selection

#### Fixed plan and assembled pictures off

- the fixed plan (`tests/fixedplan.js`): `tests/run.js` puts it into every page of a test file (an init script on each context, `track` in `file()`) unless the file ends with `module.exports.realPlanner=true`; the page then has `window.TEST_PLAN`, and the app's seams take their outfits and score from it without calling the planner: `planMonth` (today.js), the score card's `dialsFor` and `rankFor` (`TEST_PLAN.dials`, `TEST_PLAN.rank`: fixed values, no worker), the calendar's `dayMarks` and `checkPlanRules` (history.js; it only calls `planRulesCheck`, the one that reads `PLAN_RULES`, without it)
- its outfits: each workday's pieces taken in turn from the pieces in rotation (sorted by id, picked by the day number), with a day's record honoured as the planner honours it (frozen past days from `ids`, `force`, `skip`, `lock`, `kind`, a casual day with no jacket or tie, the day's `vest`); the score is always 94 with four checks, so the score card still opens and folds
- why: the planner ran at start-up in every page test, so an edit to `outfit()` or `planSim`, or a `PLAN_RULES` bump, selected 101 of 103 tests; with the fixed plan (2026-11-12) `outfit()` or `planSim` 101 → 30, `rateOutfit` 67 → 29, `scoreAdvice` 58 → 16, `tieScore` 70 → 35, a `PLAN_RULES` bump 101 → 25
- `realPlanner` files check what the planner chose (e.g. `dress`, `occasions`, `weather`, `scorefix`, `replan`, `picker`, `weeklock`, `stats`); a test that calls the planner itself (`outfit`, `buildPlan`, `planWith`, `rateOutfit`) still runs it and is still selected by planner edits
- a fixed-plan file that checks one planner choice calls `buildPlan` for it (deeptaupe, khaki, rotation-pause; the code review CR-10-05: on `planMonth` the fixed plan's day-number pick passed by coincidence)
- a new test gets the fixed plan by default: if it checks which outfit, score or advice the planner gives, it needs `module.exports.realPlanner=true` (it will usually fail without it, since the fixed outfits are plain)
- the built-in suites in `run.js` get the fixed plan too, except `REAL_SUITES`: the two planner checklists, the outfit score and "renders the outfit without errors", which keeps the real planner so a planner change that breaks start-up is still caught
- a change to `tests/fixedplan.js` selects every test (`FULL`): it is put into pages, not served, so nothing records it
- assembled pictures are switched off in tests the same way: `tests/run.js` puts `window.ASM_TEST_OFF=true` into every test page (`ASM_OFF`, also the built-in suites) unless the file ends with `module.exports.assembled=true` (`assemble`, `asmahead`); assemble.js starts with `ASM_ON=!self.ASM_TEST_OFF`, and the app calls into assemble.js only through `asmLive()` (photos.js), so a test with them off runs none of it (`asmSync`, `asmLoad`, `asmKey` used to run at start-up in every test and an edit to one selected 104 of 106; now 6–7)
- `self`, not `window`: asm-worker.js imports assemble.js, where there is no `window` (`asmahead` caught the Web Worker stopping)
- Settings still calls `renderAsmUI` (it hides the Outfit pictures group while assembled pictures are off) and asks the worker only through `asmLive()`
- in the map, a test's entries for each file it ran with switched off are replaced, not added to: `fixedOf(test)` in run.js returns the files (`planner.js` with the fixed plan, `assemble.js` with assembled pictures off) and `save` drops them before adding (`true` means `['planner.js']`); otherwise old entries (or a branch's older map merged in by `merge=union`) would select such a test for every planner change for good

#### Writing tests

- old worker versions that tests sync against (`deltasync`, `photostore`) are fixtures (`tests/fixtures/old-worker/worker-<commit>.js`), not `git show`: in a shallow clone those checks skipped themselves silently
- tests that read the worker's text take `require('./workersrc.js')()` (worker.js and every worker/ module)
- test files are grouped by feature (Round B, 2026-11-17, the code review's CR-10-14): 79 fix-round and small files went into 45 homes, 257 → 178 files (e.g. `planner-rules`, `planner-repeats`, `invite`, `server-limits`, `backup-storage`, `back`, `a11y`, `colors`, `folds`, `tagger`)
- in a joined file each old file is one part, its body unchanged: `/* ---- was tests/<old>.test.js ---- */`, then `const <old>=async({…})=>{…}`; a bare old name in this file (`nufix9`, `rules8`) names that part
- the parts run one after another; a part that throws is reported as `threw (<part>): …` and the others still run (as separate files they did)
- a file joins only files with the same `realPlanner` and `assembled` (a check moved onto the fixed plan passes by coincidence, CR-10-05), and no file that takes `engines` (its other half would run the joined parts twice); the planner rules on the fixed plan are `planner-pairs`, with the real planner `planner-rules`
- a new check goes into its feature's file, not a file named after a fix round
- a test's "kill" (a reload) first stops the page keeping anything (`addRestoreState='killed'`, `addcards`'s kill checks and `nu2fix2`'s `restart`): a reload lets the dying page's handler for a cut-off reading run and keep its own record over the cut card, which a kill never does (on a busy WebKit: "The server couldn't be reached" instead of "closed before this photo was read", "Tagging again…" without "Read the close-up again")
- `snapshots` lets both devices finish their background syncs (`quiet`/`hush`, as `sync.test.js`) before the roll-back and before the other device's follow-up sync, and a failure prints both devices' wardrobes, the good one and A's workdays

#### On Windows

- the suite runs on Windows too (the owner's PC, `C:\code\DailyWardrobe`, 16 cores: 2169 checks in 42 s, 8 at a time)
- `.gitattributes` checks every text file out with LF (`* text=auto eol=lf`), since Git for Windows defaults to `core.autocrlf=true` and CRLF broke source checks that look for `\n`
- a test that makes its own git repo turns `core.autocrlf` off in it (`impact.test.js`): that repo has no `.gitattributes`, and a merge there wrote CRLF on Windows
- on Windows, read a file's executable bit from git (`git ls-files -s`: `100755`), not `fs.statSync`, which shows none there
- on Windows PowerShell, `npm` and `npx` may be blocked by the script policy: `npm.cmd` / `npx.cmd` work; install with `npm.cmd install --no-package-lock`

### Writing a change the selector can narrow

- check before running: `node tests/run.js --changed --list` says what changed and which tests it selects; "top-level code" or "the stylesheet as a whole" in that line means the change can be restructured
- new behaviour goes inside a function that is called from where it is used, not in a block that runs when the script loads: `shopSwipe()` is called from `openShop()`, so only tests that open the sheet run it
- a listener is installed by such a function the first time it is needed (a flag like `shopSwipeOn` keeps it to once), not by a top-level `(()=>{…})()` or `document.addEventListener(…)`
- don't add the call to a function that every test runs (`show()`, `renderToday()`, `closeShop()`): an edit there selects everything; call it from the narrowest function that needs it
- a new top-level `const`/`let` is fine: it is followed by name to the functions that use it
- a new constant gets its own `const` line, never appended to an existing `const A=1,B=2;`: the selector takes one statement as one change, so every name on that line counts as changed (in assemble.js a constant appended to the `ASM_VER` line selected 90 of 92, on its own line 2)
- a few tuning sets that are only read together share a line (the herringbone's `ASM_HB_*`), where an edit to one counts as an edit to all of them
- a new visual element gets its own class name (`.shop-grab`), not a new selector built from existing classes or a `::before` on an existing element: a rule for a class the app never had selects no existing test
- state for an existing element also gets a new class (`.x.new-state`) rather than new declarations in its base rule: a rule that applies on every page (`#shop-sheet`, `body`, `.card`) selects every page test when its declarations change, and rightly so
- measured on the swipe-down change: as a load-time block with `.shop-top-head::before` it selected 89 of 91; as `shopSwipe()` from `openShop()` with `.shop-grab` it selects 5; an edit inside the item sheet's `touchmove` listener went 89 → 5, a new `.ivhead::after` rule 89 → 17
